# Text RPG Framework — Build Spec v0.6

Nov 6, 2026 · @Ali

v0.6 combines the rules framework (Part 1), the turn engine with Explore mode, scenario setup and the actor-control review (Part 2), the HP and body-part system (Part 3), the save data model (Part 4) and gear (Part 5). The open questions at the end must be answered before this spec goes to a build agent; deferred items can wait until after the first build; check-on-release items are settled for now and get reviewed once the game is played. The game is for personal use only: one player, published only as a Claude artifact, with no public release.

## Part 1 — Rules framework (from v0.1)

### 1. How to read this spec

- Treat every rule here as binding. Values marked *placeholder* are tunable config values, not constants.
- Items marked **\[Proposed\]** were suggested during design but not yet confirmed. Build them so they are easy to change.
- Do not invent mechanics beyond this spec. Report any gap as an open question instead.

### 2. Core principle

Skill growth, Burden and Potential are never shown to any agent. Part 2's hard rules govern everything else about what code and Claude each own.

### 3. Character model

Skills are grouped into trees of exactly three. Example only: Tactics = Preparation, Cleverness, Logistics.

**Combat skill trees** (minimum for the first build; skill names are *placeholders*):

- Combat – Arms: light weapons, medium weapons, heavy weapons
- Combat – Ranged: light ranged, medium ranged, heavy ranged
- Combat – Unarmed: strike, grapple, improvisation; they behave the same for now (Part 5 §5). Improvisation is fighting with whatever is at hand: improvised weapons (Part 5 §7) roll it (Ali, 07/11/2026). Every improvised base has two modes (Ali, 09/11/2026): a melee **strike** and a ranged **throw** (the Throw action, like throwing a vase: reach 0 + the throw's +2, combined roll of Improvisation and the ranged skill of its class), the strike rolled with Improvisation. Unarmed skills stand in for both; Arms skills also stand in for the strike and Ranged skills for the throw (`AttackMode.standInFns`, at the usual stand-in share). Natural weapons is a creature-only skill outside every player tree (creatures' natural attacks roll it)
- Combat – Defense: dodge, block, parry
- Fortitude (Ali, 10/11/2026, MB5, MB31, MB32): endurance, resolve; the third slot is open. Resolve has the Morale function (Will group, XP factor Full); it sets max morale and rolls the morale roll (Part 3, Morale).
- Armor – Conventional (Ali, 08/11/2026, AR1–AR3): light_armor, medium_armor, heavy_armor; function armor in the Defense group; blocked for amorphous bodies. What the skill does while worn (AR4) and how it improves (AR5) are parked (D-184); equip requirements will read it (EQ1–EQ7, levels pending).

Endurance sets total HP (Part 3, Total HP). An average fighter has 50 in a combat skill; this is the design reference point. All-XXX perks apply to these trees as to any other.

**Other skill trees** (placeholders; each is its own tree):

- Perception: sight, hearing and insight (insight answers lies, Part 3). Sight and hearing are tagged with the Sense role.
- Stealth (Ali, 08/11/2026, ST1–ST19): sneaking, combat\_stealth and vanishing. Sneaking and Combat Stealth have the same use, split by engagement: Combat Stealth inside an active engagement or for an attack, Sneaking outside one. Vanishing breaks the sight of someone who already sees you, in or out of an engagement, and counts as a combat skill for the rating. Each stands in for the others at 0.5×. For the character rating, Sneaking counts as a notice skill and Combat Stealth as a combat skill. Renaming, moving or adding skills stays a data edit (a tree lists its skills; a skill's tree is read from that list).
- Speech: persuasion, intimidation, barter. No body-part role.

The trees' skills are designed with character and mob generation. A perception roll uses sight for an event perceived by sight and hearing for one perceived only by sound (the cue from computePerception); when both apply, the higher. Combined checks (sight with hearing) are to be explored later.

**First-build skill list.** The first build has these skills, by id: melee\_light, melee\_medium, melee\_heavy; ranged\_light, ranged\_medium, ranged\_heavy; unarmed\_strike, unarmed\_grapple, unarmed\_natural; dodge, block, endurance; sight, hearing; persuasion, intimidation, barter; plus the class trees (Swordsman: riposte; Sword-dancer: blade\_dance; Slime: tremor) and insight; sneaking and combat\_stealth are in. Which trees an actor can use comes from each tree's access rule (Part 4 §4, Skill tree access); the creation screen offers the trees a new swordsman can use. Natural attacks and fallback.attackSkill name one of these ids.

**Skill substitution.** Any roll or check may use another skill that shares a function with the named skill (Skill functions, below) at reduced efficiency: that skill's effective skill × SUBSTITUTE\_EFFICIENCY (0.3, Ali 08/11/2026, F4; perks can raise or lower it). Code computes every candidate and uses only the highest value after efficiency. Example: intimidating with intimidation 20 and persuasion 100 uses persuasion at 30. The named skill counts at full value, and the roll is recorded against the skill actually used.

**Typed IDs** (ID review, Ali, 09/11/2026). Every library ID is a type prefix plus a number, never a name: skills SK, functions FN, groups GR, classes CL, achievements AC, services SV, perks PK (`src/library/perkIds.ts`), items IT (`src/library/itemIds.ts`), skill trees TR (`treeIds.ts`), races RC and body types BT (`raceIds.ts`), NPC templates TP (`templateIds.ts`), and the small tables in `tableIds.ts`: body-part roles RL, healing sources HL, clock rows DU, zone kinds ZK and attack modes AM; each counted from 1 within its type. Agents see perks as `{ id, name }`; a perk move the Parser names instead of giving its id is read as its id. A minted or improvised item's story ID is built from its base item's ID (`IT2_m12_1`, `IT21_i7`); the Parser still names an improvised weapon's base by word (improvised_light, improvised_medium, improvised_heavy), and code maps the word to its item (`IMPROVISED_ITEM`). Body types now carry a display name; the Weaver sees body types as `{ id, name }`. Roles and attack modes carry display names (the `attackModes` table; a natural attack may keep its own name, such as fists). Agents see a weapon's modes as `{ id, name }`, and a mode an agent names instead of giving its id is read as its id (`modeIdOf`); natural attacks are `natural:<AM id>`. The Weaver writes a zone's kind as its ZK id from `catalogs.zoneKinds`. The prefix shows what kind of thing an ID is, so IDs of different types never collide. Ordered scales, tags and categories, body parts, NPC behaviour (activities, routines, routine sets), place kinds and story-world IDs keep their word keys: ordered scales use stable keys plus one names table, tags and categories are fixed string enums, and body parts and NPC behaviour are words the Weaver and Planner write. **Skill IDs** (Ali, 09/11/2026). Every skill's ID is fixed (`src/library/skillIds.ts`, `SK`), never its name, so renaming a skill touches only the names table (`skillNames.ts`). Data and saves store the ID; code refers to skills through `SK` (e.g. `SK.eyesight` = SK14). IDs are never reused or renumbered. A standing defense (dodge/block), a sense function (sight/hearing), an armor weight and a weapon class map to their skill through `DEFENSE_SKILL`, `SENSE_SKILL`, `ARMOR_SKILL` and `CLASS_SKILL`; no skill ID is built from text. Functions and groups have fixed IDs too (`src/library/functionIds.ts`: `FN` FN1–FN10, `GR` GR1–GR7), so a function and a group with the same display name (Defense, Ranged, Stealth, Influence) never share an ID; names live in FUNCTION_NAMES and GROUP_NAMES (functionNames.ts). Agents see each skill as `{ id, name }`, and a skill an agent writes by name or old alias is read as its ID (`skillIdOf`).

**Skill functions and groups** (Ali, 08/11/2026, F1–F8). Each skill's library entry lists its narrow functions, and a skill may have several; stand-ins read functions. Each function sits in one broad group; a 40 perk's +1 and defeat XP read the group. Current list: docs/skill-tags.md. Exception pairs (`CONFIG.NO_STAND_IN`) never stand in for each other even when they share a function: dodge and block (Ali, 09/11/2026). **Sense rolls are function rolls** (Ali, 09/11/2026): a sight roll (stealth contest, ambush notice, Look) rolls the sight function, so every skill with that function counts at full value and the higher effective skill applies (tier multiplier still applies): Eyesight for sight, Earshot for hearing (`RollRequest.fn`). **Tremor** (Ali, 13/11/2026) is no sense function: it augments Earshot, adding its true skill × tier to every hearing roll, and never reaches sight. A function roll marks only the skill that rolled. Its situational modifiers (light, Night Eyes) follow the skill that rolls, not the function (Q3, Ali 09/11/2026): a hearing roll ignores light.

- Melee group: arms (melee\_light, melee\_medium, melee\_heavy), unarmed (unarmed\_strike, unarmed\_grapple, unarmed\_improvised, unarmed\_natural)
- Ranged group: ranged (ranged\_light, ranged\_medium, ranged\_heavy)
- Defense group: defense (dodge, block)
- Health group: health (endurance)
- Will group: morale (resolve)
- Notice group: sight (sight), hearing (hearing); tremor sits in the group and augments hearing
- Stealth group: stealth (sneaking, combat\_stealth, vanishing)
- Influence group: influence (persuasion, intimidation, barter)
- No function: riposte and blade\_dance. Each joins the Melee and Defense groups directly and augments one skill with its full value on every roll: riposte augments parry, blade\_dance augments dodge (PR #270)

**Skill states** (one per skill):

| State | Counts toward Burden | Affected by Burden | Improvement roll |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Tagged | No | No | 1d100, cap 125 |
| Normal | Yes | Yes | 1d100, cap 100 |
| Flawed | Yes | Yes | 1d100, cap 75 |

A Flaw is something the character functionally struggles to improve: its cap is 25 lower (FLAW\_CAP\_PENALTY), so it trains to 75 and slows well before it. Tags and flaws move only the cap; every state rolls one d100 (no roll advantage or disadvantage). A skill that becomes Flawed above 75 keeps its raw and gains nothing more by training.

**Limits:**

- 100 is the mortal limit for raw skill (SKILL\_CAP); a tagged skill's limit is 125 (TAG\_CAP\_BONUS 25, EG16). **The improvement roll** (Skill Gain Roll Options, SG1 C, Ali 12/11/2026) is read off the tier table: margin = d100 + (cap − raw − Burden) − GROWTH\_ROLL\_NEEDED (90, placeholder), and the tier sets the gain: Success +2, Great Success +3, Critical Success +4 (Ali, 13/11/2026: each +2, then pulled back 1), plus 1 per band past it (GROWTH\_GAIN, GROWTH\_GAIN\_PAST\_CRIT, placeholders). The room left below the cap is the bonus, so the roll gets harder smoothly with no wall, and raw never passes its cap. A flawed skill's cap is 75 (FLAW\_CAP\_PENALTY 25, Ali 12/11/2026); tags and flaws move only the cap, and every state rolls one d100. A tagged skill has no Burden, and each skill's own cap is in the roll too (GROWTH\_CAP\_IN\_ROLL, SG3 a), so a tagged skill at 100 rolls like an untagged 75 and a flawed skill at 50 like an untagged 75. Natural edges do not move the roll (GROWTH\_NATURAL\_EDGES off, SG2 a; LF4: no natural roll forces a gain). Odds of any gain with no Burden: 86% at raw 25, 61% at 50, 36% at 75, 12% at 99; average gain about 2.4, 1.6, 0.8 and 0.1 a roll (the cap clips it at 99). Scaling: past the cap raw does not grow by training; perks, achievements and gear still carry true and effective skill to 150, 200 and beyond. Workaround mechanics for raw come later (D-6).
- **No cap past training** (D-3, settled by CS21): the training cap above is the only cap, and it limits raw skill alone. True skill and effective skill both go past it with no top: perks, achievements, essences, gear and augments carry a skill past 100, and the true skill past the cap is what the 120 and 140 milestones read. Conditions (wounds, fatigue, morale) are roll modifiers, not a step of effective skill.

**Three skill values** (Ali, 07/11/2026):

- **Raw skill:** what training gave. Burden, Total and skill improvement use raw skill only.
- **True skill:** raw skill + perks that raise that one skill by a flat amount and are always on (e.g. a ladder's Training milestone, +5). Ladder milestones and all-XXX perks read true skill. It has no cap of its own, so a ladder's boosts can carry a skill past the mortal limit of 100 toward the 120 and 140 milestones.
- **Effective skill** (computed for every check and roll, in order): true skill → + gear → × the skill's tier multiplier → + combined-check perks → + situational skill perks. No cap applies (D-3); conditions are modifiers on the roll.
- **Skill tiers** (Ali, 08/11/2026). Each skill has a tier on the SKILL\_TIERS scale (tier\_0, tier\_1, tier\_2), set in the library. The tier gives the skill's final efficiency multiplier (CONFIG.SKILL\_TIER\_MULT): tier 2 is 1×, tier 0 is 0.5×, and tier 1 is 0.75× (a placeholder). Every skill is tier 2 except the Slime senses skill (tremor), which is tier 0. The rating reads the skill at its tier (X18–X23).

Derived, never saved; the Character page shows true skill, with raw skill beside it when they differ.

Gear is a flat bonus to effective skill unless an item says otherwise. Standard kit (an average fighter's minimum equipment) gives one +25 per roll, shared by all kit pieces: pieces never stack (*placeholder*).

Wounds are no longer part of effective skill: the health-track level and body-part role penalties are a roll modifier type (§4, Combat rolls). They apply to rolls only, never to no-dice checks (settles OQ-15, Ali, 07/11/2026).

**Character fields:** defined in Part 4 §4 (a Core every actor has, plus Progression, Mind and Journal modules). Part 4 adds race, body type and classes.

### 4. Resolution

The dice deal the hand; skills and perks decide what the character can do with it.

**Layer 1 — event roll.** Rolled once per scenario, for the player, at scenario setup (Part 2, Scenario setup). It deals the hand: it decides the who and what of the scenario (its event, cast, challenge and gain), not how alert anyone is. Code reads it with a fixed base in place of skill (EVENT\_BASE, *placeholder*, 50):

```
margin = d100 + EVENT_BASE + net modifiers − 100
```

Only three sources may modify the roll, each its own modifier type: perks, gear and statuses (blessings, buffs, curses, debuffs) whose library entry names the event roll. A gear effect counts only when its library entry names the event roll; it may be active while only carried (Part 5 §2). Code reads the player's inventory once, at scenario setup step 2. Injury, place, difficulty, skills, terrain and tactics never modify it. It reads the normal roll table (MARGIN\_BANDS, the same bands as a skill roll; ER3), so with the placeholder base the totals (d100 + net modifiers) read as follows. Without modifiers of 25 or more, only a natural roll reaches a critical tier:

| Total (d100 + modifiers) | Tier (ID) | Stakes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Under −25 | Critical Failure (−3) | A real challenge with little gain; the harder of the two **\[Proposed\]** |
| −25 to 24 | Failure (−2) | A real challenge with little gain |
| 25–49 | Near Miss (−1) | A fair event tilted slightly against the player: gain one step below the challenge (ER2) |
| 50–74 | Success (0) | A normal event, gain matched to challenge |
| 75–124 | Great Success (1) | Good fortune: gain major, challenge none to moderate, a windfall or a chance worth taking (ER1) |
| 125+ | Critical Success (2) | The best of luck: gain extreme, challenge major or extreme, the same as Critical Failure (D-247) |

**Natural edges** apply after the bands: a natural 01–05 drops one tier and 96–100 raises one tier (CONFIG.NATURAL\_EDGE, 5 at each end); no natural roll moves two tiers (Ali, 12/11/2026: the old 01 −2 and 00 +2 are gone). The result stops at Critical Success and Critical Failure unless the player has opened tiers past them (`tierUnlocks` on perks and achievements, D-211). Each opened tier widens the natural critical range by EVENT\_CRIT\_NATURALS (5): with one opened above, 96–100 is Critical Success+ and 91–95 Critical Success; failures mirror from 01. The Weaver is told the tier with its plus or minus signs.

**Layer 2 — skill checks.** No dice. Effective skill is compared with the scenario's target number. Result is pass or fail only; the margin does not matter. The target comes from the stage plan's challenge grade through DIFFICULTY: none 20, minor 35, moderate 50, major 65, extreme 80 (placeholders). Past Extreme the table goes on for tasks beyond a human body (CR4): mortal limit 100, beyond 150, mythic 200, divine 300, each the skill that just manages it. These four are task grades only (CR12): a scenario's gain, a lock's grade and the Weaver's choices stay within none to extreme, and nothing picks them in play until D-219 says when they apply. A check passes when effective skill plus situational modifiers is at least the target. The same rule applies to every actor. Wounds don't apply (settles OQ-15). Exception, looking around (Ali, 07/11/2026, Q17): the player's observe action with a notice skill (sight, hearing) is a roll, not a check: d100 with the skill and the light modifier where the player stands, with no scene number (SE15); it learns like a solo roll at Moderate. Success or better shows everyone in the player's zone and the zones in sight of it clearly. Look around is also a weak search (SE14, Explore mode, Searching): a contest against everyone hiding within its range, one tier down.

- **Combined checks:** some perks let a check add several skills, or half of another skill.
- **Gear:** flat skill bonuses from item effects that are active (Part 5 §2–3). They enter effective skill; item stats and the rare noted roll modifiers are read separately.
- **Wounds:** superseded by Part 3, which is the latest for all HP-related items.
- **No critical rolls.** There is no separate AGG-style Critical Roll; Critical Failure and Critical Success are ordinary tiers with no special effects (critical effects are deferred).

**Layer 2b — combat rolls.** Attacks and defenses are rolls, not checks. Both use one function:

```
margin = d100 + SKILL_WEIGHT × effective skill + roll modifiers − ROLL_NEEDED
SKILL_WEIGHT = 0.5, ROLL_NEEDED = 63.5 (placeholders); d100 runs 1–100 (00 = 100)
```

Tiers on margin: below −75 Critical Failure; −75 to −26 Failure; −25 to −1 Near Miss; 0 to 24 Success; 25 to 74 Great Success; 75+ Critical Success. Margins may end in .5; compare exact values.

**Scale keys.** Standing design rule for anything in the code with levels (Ali, 07/11/2026, restated 08/11/2026 NV3): every ordered scale (tiers, band tactics, zone light and cover, stance, noise, visibility, and in turn the others) has one table in `src/scales.ts`: a row per level, lowest first, each a stable key and its display name. Code and saves use only the key, a level's order is its row position, and display names appear only in text for the player and for Claude, so adding or renaming a level edits only the table (Ali, 07/11/2026). Numbers that go with a level (a reach, a modifier) are keyed by the level's key in config, never by its position. Noise, quietest first: silent, faint, hushed, quiet, murmur, normal, raised, loud, blaring, deafening (murmur, raised and blaring added 11/11/2026). Each noise level is a loudness in decibels at 1 m from real references (CONFIG.NOISE\_DB; the library's Noise levels table gives each its reference: faint 20 dB breathing, hushed 30 a whisper, quiet 40 a library, murmur 50 low talk or rain, normal 60 conversation, raised 70 a busy tavern, loud 88 a shout, blaring 100 a hammer on an anvil, deafening 120 a thunderclap). **Hearing by real metres** (BG-57, HB1–HB8, Ali 12/11/2026; perception/noise.ts `soundDb`, `hearingOf`): a sound's decibels at 1 m less 20·log10(metres) (the inverse-square law; never nearer than SAME\_ZONE\_M, 1 m) less what it loses at each crossing between zones, along whichever route loses least (HB4, SOUND\_LOSS names table, CONFIG.SOUND\_LOSS\_DB from STC ratings): an open gap 0, a shut window 30, a shut door 30, a wall 35. A barrier loses by its kind while shut (door, gate, hatch: door; grate: open; rubble: wall), nothing while it stands open (BG-70) or is broken through; a link with no barrier loses nothing if it can be seen through, else it is a shut door; a drop link is a window; two zones with rectangles that touch on the same floor with no link lose by the edge between them (wall, window, open or low). It is heard (a sound, not who or what) while at least CONFIG.HEARING\_FLOOR\_DB (20 dB, a quiet place at the old 0.5 game share), and clearly from CLEAR\_ABOVE\_FLOOR\_DB (15, ANSI S12.60) over it (HB1). Per-zone background noise (HB2, HB3, HB6: a names table from still to crowded, a background per zone kind, a busy tavern room) is removed for now (Ali, 12/11/2026, FR1) and parked as D-231. A whisper said to someone within WHISPER\_REACH\_M (1 m) is said leaning in, from WHISPER\_LEAN\_M (0.3 m) (HB8, an estimate); a whisper not meant for you is heard, not understood. Voices do not rise in noise (HB7: no Lombard effect). In the open a normal voice is clear to 18 m and heard to 100 m (the library's Noise levels table gives every level, `openAirReachM`). A perk's `hearingDb` makes every sound that many dB louder for its owner (RD4: Keen Ear 10, about three times as far); silence stays unheard. Visibility, least first: unseen, faint, close, low, normal, high, glaring (NV1–NV6, Ali, 08/11/2026). Each is how far it is made out clearly and glimpsed, in metres, in daylight with a clear view, from real references (CONFIG.SIGHT\_M; the library's Visibility levels table): faint 0.5/2 m a palmed coin, close 6/20 m a nod or an expression, low 15/60 m someone keeping still (where witnesses stop naming faces), normal 25/200 m someone walking, high 50/500 m a fight or someone running, glaring 200/2000 m a fire or a torch at night. Perception measures them in metres (step 2 PR 2, `sightLevel` with position `seeingM`): the straight line with sight from the observer to where the event happened (GameEvent.pos, the actor's point), never less than SAME\_ZONE\_M (1 m); full out to the clear metres, partial out to the glimpsed metres. In a zone without rectangles the same zone counts as SAME\_ZONE\_M and each sight link as LINK\_PACES × PACE\_M metres, which gives the old links (`sightReach`, still in the library table): faint [−1, 0], close [0, 2], low [1, 7], normal [3, 25], high [6, 62], glaring [25, 250]. A still figure (NS2 stealthStart) is kept out to low's 15 m and a look (Q17) makes out figures to normal's 25 m. A dark zone caps sight at partial. **Words carry only by sound** (real distances, 11/11/2026): a line's words reach an observer only when its sound does, and heard clearly (Ali, 11/11/2026, BG-17: a sound heard only partly is a voice without its words; Every Sense One still carries them) (the perception's `heard` flag, `hearsWords`), so a talker seen across a field is seen, not heard; this covers the words shown, what is passed on (told), deals, quest hand-ins, NPC speech rolls at the player and Overheard. A line said beside another action (a bark) is as loud as the louder of the action and the voice. A careful look (Q17) makes out a still figure as far as a moving one (normal's clear reach), and a figure turning stealthy stays seen as far as a still one is made out (low's clear reach). Addressing everyone reaches those who hear the player's voice clearly at the volume chosen (BG-71); that check, a called order (trade) and stepping up to speak all use perception's one rule `hearsClearly`, with the listener's hearing perks (BG-88). Armor weight, lightest first: light, medium, heavy. Worn armor makes body-moving events (move, attack, perk move, break free, pick up) louder by CONFIG.ARMOR\_NOISE\_STEPS noise steps: light +0, medium +1, heavy +2 (placeholders); a stealthy posture doesn't cancel it (AN1–AN4, Ali, 08/11/2026). Tier keys, worst first: critical_failure, failure, near_miss, success, great_success, critical_success. Tier arithmetic (shifts, gaps) works on a tier's level, its row position; a roll shifted past either end keeps the end key plus `beyond`, the steps past it, shown as one + or − per step (Critical Success+). Saves store keys (schema v3; v1 and v2 saves are migrated on load). Schema v5 split hostile from opposed: older saves' opposed becomes hostile, so they play as before, and a logged more\_opposed step becomes colder.

**Tables added by the code scan** (Ali, 12/11/2026, CS7 to CS9): hit outcomes (HIT\_OUTCOMES, missed to crushing hit; src/ruleset/hits.ts names the shared checks: struck = the body was reached, met = the body or a shield, defended = dodged, parried or blocked), grades (GRADE\_LEVELS, none to divine; the Weaver, barriers, gains and locks use none to WEAVER\_GRADE\_TOP, Extreme) and minted item quality (QUALITY\_LEVELS; a level's multiplier in the quality formulas is its position + 1; Passable, Solid, Exceptional, Flawless). Round 3 added the activity attention table (ATTENTION, alert to asleep), which nothing reads yet (D-200).

Modifier types (each type diminishes within itself; values per type are deferred):

- Gear and situational skill perks: inside effective skill; flat skill perks: inside true skill (§3).
- Roll perks: flat roll points.
- Gear roll modifiers: rare noted modifiers from items, such as the weapon hit adjustment, at full value (Part 5 §3).
- Wounds: health-track level plus body-part role penalties, plus −5 while an essential part is Crippled or worse, in roll points.
- Situational: flanking, outnumbered, unaware, terrain, range, light, dodge vs missiles.
- Aim: its own type, so it never diminishes with situational modifiers.

**Stacking within a type.** Within one modifier type, bonuses and penalties are ranked separately by size. Each group stacks by the one power rule (RN1, runaway-stacking page, 14/11/2026; src/ruleset/stacking.ts powerStack): n equal items total n^p, so the k-th largest counts k^p − (k−1)^p, with no ceiling. Roll modifiers use p = MOD\_STACK\_EXP (0.5, RN2 b): the largest in full, the second at 0.41, the third at 0.32, the fourth at 0.27. The type's value is the bonus total plus the penalty total. MOD\_STACK\_EXP is a placeholder. Example: bonuses +6, +4, +2 give +8.29; penalties −8, −3 give −9.24; the type adds −0.95. Other scenarios set their own exponent: payoff pieces PAYOFF\_EXP 0.8 (RN4); the 120 perks' shares and percent augments on one skill add in full (RN3 c, p = 1); perk sleep multipliers (sleepNeed, sleepClear) multiply together in full, as the spot, armour and body type multipliers do (RN5 c; review if growth multipliers above ×1 start piling up); chance and % reductions are decided one by one as they are built (RN6 b). Earlier: the halving series STACK\_RATIO^(x−1) (Ali, 08/11/2026).

One roll point equals two skill points; write roll modifiers small.

**Tier shifts**, applied after banding: natural edges (01–05 −1 tier, 96–100 +1, never two: Ali, 12/11/2026) and tier perks. They apply to attack and defense rolls, never to the location roll. There is no clamp: a shifted tier may go past Critical Success or below Critical Failure. Narration and the packet show the nearest of the six named tiers with one + or − per step past the end: ID 3 is "Critical Success+", ID 4 "Critical Success++", ID −4 "Critical Failure−".

**Gap shifts**, applied to the gap (Part 3, Damage flow): heavy weapon vs block −1, cover, and perks that add a tier to block or dodge.

The margin bands stay open-ended: a margin of 75 or more is Critical Success, never more, unless a perk or achievement unlocks tiers past it. Otherwise only shifts move a tier past the ends.

**Unlocked tiers (D-211, Ali, 11/11/2026).** A perk or achievement may open tiers past either end on one roll kind, on every roll made as one side of a contest (roll `contest`, D-292), or on every roll (`tierUnlocks`, read by `tierReach`). On skill rolls each tier opened adds a margin band TIER\_EXTRA\_BAND (50) wider past the end: one tier up makes 125 or more Critical Success+, two make 175 or more Critical Success++; one tier down makes below −125 Critical Failure−. Natural edges and tier shifts still apply on top. The event roll reads its unlocks differently, on purpose (Ali): skill can raise a skill roll's margin, so an opened tier there is a band skill can reach; nothing raises an event roll, which must stay random, so an opened tier there widens the natural critical range instead (EVENT\_CRIT\_NATURALS). The two rules are not to be merged.

**Contests.** Every roll with two sides (perception against stealth, an ambush roll against perception, speech against the listener) uses roll() for both sides and is read by tier difference, like the attack and defense gap: gap = the opposing side's tier − the acting side's tier, after tier shifts. The acting side wins below 0, the opposing side wins above 0, and 0 is a tie. ContestResult carries the gap; what a larger gap does is not defined yet. Modifier types are those of Layer 2b except aim, stacked the same way. Wounds reach a roll through the skill's body-part role tags (Part 3, Roles).

### 5. Progression

XP drives levels and perks only. Skills grow separately, through skill improvement (section 6).

- **What XP is: the Thread** (Ali, 09/11/2026, XP lore XL1–XL3). This is backstory: no NPC or agent knows it, and nothing in play shows it (no renamed "XP", no level-up flavor). It is the design lens for deciding what gives XP and why.
  - Every life is a thread in the world's weave. Skills are the body's craft: they grow by use and stop at the mortal limit (100). XP is thread, not practice.
  - Overcoming someone ties the part of their thread that their training wound into yours, so defeat XP is the worth of the foe's skills. Hitting them, or their yielding, is the touch that ties it. A foe that flees, or one downed by someone else, slips away. A thin thread adds little to a thick one (taper). The weave doesn't care whose thread it is, so stance doesn't matter.
  - A great deed knots your own thread: achievement XP.
  - Classes are patterns a thread can follow. A class's top level is a pattern with only so many turns, and its capstone closes it. Banked XP is loose thread waiting for a pattern, and Commoner is the plainest pattern, always open. Unwritten Legend and A Nobody are threads not yet woven.
  - It is felt, never seen (no glowing strings), and nobody can see another's thread. Beyond the mortal limit (D-6) can hang off it: the body stops at 100, the thread doesn't.
  - **Test for any new XP source:** does it overcome someone, so their thread joins yours, or is it a deed big enough to knot your own? If neither, it teaches through skill marks and gives no XP.

- **XP** (Ali, 07/11/2026, XP proposal X1–X10; all numbers *placeholders*, kept small at first). Three sources, each logged on the source trail as {source, amount, turn} (B8):
  - **Defeats.** Defeats are no longer the main source of XP: achievements (below) contribute greatly, and a single one often pays more than many defeats. An NPC pays once (B4), whatever its stance (RS7), when it is downed and the player hit it at least once, or when it surrenders to the player or breaks (flees) while fighting the player: a morale break is a defeat, as for the player (CS2, Ali 12/11/2026; both turn its stance toward the player to yielded). Companions' defeats wait for companion XP. The amount comes from stats only (X18–X23, Ali 07/11/2026; Danger was dropped). Defeat XP reads each skill's **group** (Ali, 10/11/2026: there is no separate XP category): a skill counts in its first group (`groupsOf`), so attack skills count in Melee or Ranged, dodge and block in Defense, endurance in Health, the senses in Notice, stealth skills in Stealth and speech in Influence. Riposte and Blade Dance (formerly Slip Counter) join the Melee and Defense groups directly (D-158) and count in Melee. Lockpicking and Filching (the Sleight group, FL1) count like Stealth. A skill's worth is SKILL\_RATING\_AT\_50 (23.1) × (s ÷ 50)^SKILL\_RATING\_POWER (2), so doubling a skill makes it worth four times as much, with no cap (Ali, 12/11/2026, LF15; it replaced the old training-effort curve, which assumed 100 was the top and went flat from 98); s is the **true** skill × its tier multiplier (LF16), for the player and foes alike. Within each group the worths are ranked high to low and count at 1, ½, ¼…. The foe's **rating** is the sum over groups × the group's XP factor. XP factors are an ordered scale (XP\_FACTORS: None 0, Quarter 0.25, Half 0.5, Three quarters 0.75, Full 1; CONFIG.XP\_FACTOR\_MULT), and each group names its level (CONFIG.XP\_GROUP\_FACTOR): Melee, Ranged, Defense and Health Full, Stealth Three quarters (Ali, 10/11/2026), Notice Half, Influence Quarter, Speed None. Melee and Ranged are separate pools, so a foe strong in both counts both in full. **Gear in the rating** (Ali, 10/11/2026): a rating also adds GEAR\_RATING\_FACTOR (0.25) × the coin value of the gear the actor holds and wears (gear coin is priced at about one coin per skill point of power; carried spares and coin don't count). Gear counts on both sides: the player's held and worn gear is part of the player's rating in the taper, like the foe's. **Levels in the rating** (Ali, 10/11/2026, option B): every rating, the player's included, adds LEVEL\_RATING\_SHARE (0.02, a balance lever) × what the XP curve asks to reach the actor's level from 0 (`xpToReach`, the sum of `levelCost` for every step; xpToNext asks the same `levelCost`, so changing the curve changes both). An NPC's level is its template level; the player's is its full character level, free and bonus levels included (Ali). With LEVEL\_XP 100: level 4 adds 14, level 10 adds 92, level 20 adds 382. Defeat XP = (foe rating + foe gear rating) × min(1, DEFEAT\_XP\_TAPER (2) × (foe rating + foe gear rating) ÷ (player rating + player gear rating)) × DEFEAT\_XP\_MULT (1, X20). All numbers are *placeholders*, to be tuned later (X19). Example against the test village's player (rating 63, 81 with gear): a slime (rating 34) gives 29 XP, a guard (rating 126, 139 with gear) 139.
  - **Achievements**, a major XP source alongside defeats (Ali, 11/11/2026): the first slime felled pays 169 XP (reward level I) where the slime itself pays 7.7, and Blooded Fighter (10 defeats) pays another 169. Each has an id and a name and is earned once (the every-10-levels achievement repeats, one reward level higher each time, D-179). Library achievements are a data table that only code awards. Per body type: 1, 10 and 100 defeats (reward levels I, II and III; ids first\_defeat:<type>, defeats\_10:<type>, defeats\_100:<type>; "First Slime Felled", "Slimebane", "Scourge of the Slime Hordes"). Per race (human and elf for now, identical, both humanoid; library NPCs and the player are human until creation offers a choice): the same counts (ids first\_defeat\_race:<race>, defeats\_10\_race:<race>, defeats\_100\_race:<race>; "First of Elfkind Felled", "Bane of Elfkind", "Scourge of Elfkind"). In all: 10, 100, 250 and 1000 defeats (reward levels I, II, III and IV: "Blooded Fighter", "Calamity of a Hundred Blades", "Harvester of the Fallen", "Thousand-Slayer, Herald of Ruin"; D-119). Per tree, when enough of its skills (by its rating) reach 50, 100 and 150 (reward levels II, III and IV), one line per tree rating, grander the higher the rating (D-165): normal "Adept / Master / Grandmaster of <tree>", superior "Paragon / Ascendant / Transcendent Sovereign of <tree>", legendary "Chosen of <tree>", "<tree> Incarnate", "The Heavens Kneel to <tree>"; inferior trees earn none. The achievements table is treated like a scale table: saves keep only the row key and what it names, so a rename or a new reward level edits only the table. Each achievement has a reward level, an ordered scale like tiers: −1 to XV (D-179). One equation gives every level its XP, for achievements and quests alike, with no top (CS15, CS16): XP = 50 × ((p + 2) ÷ 2)³, where p is the level's place (0 = level 0, I = 1, −1 = −1). So −1 6, 0 50, I 169, II 400, III 781, IV 1,350, V 2,144, VI 3,200, X 10,800, XV 30,706 (CONFIG.REWARD_XP_CURVE, read only through rewardXp; REWARD_LEVELS in src/scales.ts; Ali, 07/11/2026). Names name what was defeated and grow grander with the feat; small feats get tame names (Ali, 07/11/2026). Progression keeps defeat counts per body type and race. Kills are counted apart from defeats (D-277): a kill is the player's blow that takes someone else to Dead, and it stays a defeat too, so both tallies move; its chain is 1, 10 and 50 kills ("A Life Taken", "Gravefiller", "Death Walks Beside You"; reward levels 0, I and II), each paying +1 max morale. Near death is read by depth, not by count (D-279): a fight survived from Critical, back up after going Down, and the survival roll passed (D-54), which earns "Woke Up Anyway" (reward level II, +10 max morale). Its twin for the mind (D-294): a fight won although your morale broke in it climbs a chain at 1, 5 and 25 ("Back from the Break", "Broken, Not Beaten", "Nothing Left to Fear"; reward levels II, III and IV), each level paying +5 max morale and one Nerve step; the fight still counts as lost for the Loser scars, as a break always does. The reward fits the deed, as a guide for whoever proposes a row (D-278): an ordeal pays what would have carried you through it (max morale, death's door, rest), and a feat done with a skill may pay that skill. New achievements come only from Ali-approved proposals (D-108); Weaver-seeded scenario achievements (X7) are dropped (Ali, 07/11/2026). Earned achievements are listed on the Achievements page (the rail's Achievements tab), not on the character card: grouped by reward level with every group open (a heading the player closes stays closed), and clicking one opens its side panel with how it is earned, the turn it was earned, its reward, and every level of its chain (rows linked by `after`) with how each is earned, its reward level and whether the player has it (src/ui/pages.ts achievementDetail, achievementLevels).
  - **Quests** pay XP when done, by their reward level, from the same XP equation as achievements (CS16): 0 50, I 169, II 400, III 781, IV 1,350 XP (QUEST_REWARD_LEVELS, rewardXp). The level is picked by code from the scenario's gain grade when the quest is accepted: minor 0, moderate I, major II, extreme III (CONFIG.QUEST_GAIN_LEVEL); IV is kept for later story quests. A defeat quest's XP is cut by the defeat-XP taper (QX2): min(1, DEFEAT_XP_TAPER × the counted foes' average power ÷ the player's power), each foe's power taken when it fell and the player's at hand-in (QX4), so easy hunts pay little to a strong player. Other jobs are not cut: their reward level already comes from the scenario's grade (QX3). Sizing quest XP to the player's level is parked (D-180). See Quests below.
- **Levels.** From level n to n+1 takes LEVEL\_XP × n XP (LEVEL\_XP 100). **Class level cap** (Ali, 09/11/2026): a class's top level is set by its tier: CLASS\_TIER0\_MAX\_LEVEL (5) + CLASS\_LEVELS\_PER\_TIER (5) × tier, at most CLASS\_MAX\_LEVEL (20) (`classMaxLevel`). So tier 0 tops out at 5, tier 1 at 10, tier 2 at 15, and tier 3 and up at 20. When the class the next level would go to is at its top level, no level happens and XP banks (Progression.xp keeps growing). Picking a class with room on the sheet takes every banked level at once (`spendXp`). The default class, **Commoner** (`fallback`), is open (unlocked, level 0) for every new character from creation, whatever the Start and race (Ali, 14/11/2026; newPlayer), and opens again for a player whose current class reaches its top level if it is ever missing, so banked XP always has somewhere to go. Its capstone (at 5, its top level), **Nobody Special**, unlocks the class A Nobody (ClassDef.unlockPerk). **A Nobody** (Ali, 09/11/2026; its Start was removed, CC3) gives Quick Study at its level 1 and the capstone **Every Fall a Lesson** at 15, its top level: losing a contest or a defended attack by exactly one tier step marks the owner's rolled skill (and the asked skill when a stand-in rolled), inside the learning window or not. A class perk the character already has is not granted twice. **Alternate capstones** (Ali, 09/11/2026): a class's capstone (ClassDef.capstone, the default) is granted when the class reaches its top level, not at a flat 20. A class can also list alternates (ClassDef.capstones), each with conditions in the tree-access form (Must / Either of / Blocked by), which can name a race, race tag, body type, class level, perk or a true skill (kind 'skill'). When the class reaches its top level, every alternate whose conditions hold at that moment is offered (Progression.capstoneOptions). While an offer stands, the default is granted dormant (inactive). On the character sheet the player either keeps the default, which makes it active (`keepCapstone`), or picks one of the offered alternates in its place (`swapCapstone`); either choice is final. With nothing on offer the default is active at once. The alternates themselves are designed in the capstone thread. There is one XP bar. A level goes to the class picked in the class tree, or else to the current class (X9; settles OQ-8). **Class tree** (CT1–CT4, src/ui/classTree.ts): the Class line on the character card (the class, its tier and where the next level goes) opens a full-screen pop-up that is also the only place to pick where the next level goes. It shows the classes the character has plus the next step from each: a class one of them opens through a capstone or level perk (ClassDef.unlockPerk) or a class level (unlockClass), and Commoner once the character has any class. Each tier is a band of its own, side by side, Common on the left and Legendary on the right (CT2 b), and an arrow joins each class to the class it opens. A gold thread is the character's own path (CT4): it joins the classes they have levelled in the order they first took a level in each (from the level log; the start's class first, marked "Started here"), turning a link it follows gold; picking another class for the next level draws the next stretch dashed until the level lands. A class not open yet is dashed and says where it comes from; a class with no outgoing link is marked Dead end, and one whose perk opens every class (Write Your Own Ending) says so. Clicking a class opens its card (story, tier, level, level perks, capstone, opens from, leads to) with "Next level goes here" on any class the character has that is not previous and below its top level; picking it sets the next class and takes every banked level at once (`spendXp`). While a scenario runs the level waits for its ending moment (EG8). With mechanics shown, the turn lists "+n XP (source)" and "Level n!".
- **Level perks:** one perk point on every even level (PERK_POINT_EVERY 2; perkPointAt in src/ruleset/levels.ts, which NPC spawn reads too), spent on perks. Some perks are trade-offs (e.g. give up a skill for a higher cap, faster training and bonuses to class skills); these come later, on their own tab (S6).
- **Skill ladders** (Ali, 07/11/2026, perk page S1–S9): every skill has a perk at each 20 points of true skill (PERK\_MILESTONE\_EVERY), with no top (CS11): perks are written for milestones 20, 40, 60, 80, 100, 120 and 140, and a milestone past the last written perk shows as reached with no perk yet (D-293 will make them rarer past 140). Reaching a milestone unlocks its perk; taking it costs one perk point (S2). Milestones 20, 60, 100 and 140 give a perk that adds a new ability; milestones 40, 80 and 120 give a perk that raises numbers, with the same +5 true skill in its skill and +1 in its group at each of them (Ali, 12/11/2026, LF13; the older +5/+10/+15 example was a mistake). A perk is kept if the skill later drops below its milestone (S4). **Milestone gating** (Ali, 07/11/2026): a milestone shows only once enough ladder perks have been taken in that same skill: 60 and 80 need one (from 20 or 40), 100 and 120 need two, 140 needs three, and the pattern goes on (160 three, 180 four …, ruleset perksNeeded). Until then it stays sealed like an unreached one. All-XXX perks do not count. **The 20/60/100/140 perks and the 40/80/120 perks** (Ali, 08/11/2026, F1–F5; docs call each by its milestone: "20 perk", "40 perk" and so on). The 20, 60, 100 and 140 perks add an ability; the 40, 80 and 120 perks raise numbers. A 40/80/120 perk has no conditions and gives +5 true skill in its skill, +1 true skill in its skill and in every other skill sharing a group with it (docs/skill-tags.md; Ali, 12/11/2026, LF1: the group, not the function), so +6 in its own skill, and one more number: one of the skill's own numbers, or +10 true skill on skills that work well with it (+10 to one, or +5 to two). Numbers stay on theme (light weapons get hit and aim, heavy weapons get damage), and a 40/80/120 perk never repeats what a 20/60/100/140 perk gives. The 40 perks are in src/library/boostPerks.ts. The 80 and 120 perks are not written yet (LF2, Ali, 12/11/2026): they keep the 40 perk's +5 own skill and +1 group true skill, but their one more number can differ from the 40 perk's. They stay empty until each skill's own perk round (LF14). Ladder perks are level perks (OwnedPerk source 'level'). Content is chosen milestone by milestone; the chosen perks and the bank of perks kept for later are in docs/perks.md. **Two views** (Ali, 07/11/2026): every perk has a Story write-up (what a player reads when choosing) and exact Rules (what code applies, the basis for building it), and the two must say the same thing. Both live in src/library/perks.ts, and docs/perks.md is generated from it. On the Perks screen the player switches the perk card between Story and Rules. A perk that is written but not coded yet (built: false) shows both views but can't be taken. Most perks are always on and apply when their condition is met; a few are moves chosen on a turn (G1). **Quirks** (Ali, 08/11/2026, name for now): a perk that is negative in nature is called a quirk (PerkDef quirk: true). None are designed yet. **Innate perks and quirks** (Ali, 08/11/2026): each race can list any number of innate perks and innate quirks (RaceDef innatePerks, innateQuirks). Every member of the race, player or NPC, has them from spawn as owned perks with source 'race'. They cost no perk point, can't be undone and don't count toward milestone gating. The Perks screen lists them under Innate. Human and Elf have none yet. When Human and Elf perks are picked, AGG's origin perks are the models (Ali, 08/11/2026, AGG comparison A4). Riposte (Swordsman) has ladder perks (its 60 perk is Answer the Blow) and blade\_dance mirrors it; lockpicking and the slime senses get none (G3).
- **All-XXX perks** (All-50, All-100, All-150; formerly mastery perks, renamed by Ali on 07/11/2026; the save key stays `mastery`): granted instantly, at no point cost, when enough skills of a tree reach 50, 100 or 150 (by its rating, D-164); the steps go on every ALL\_STEP\_EVERY (50) points with no top (CS11), a step past All-150 having no perks written yet. All three steps work alike (D-197): the tree's passive for that step plus each of those skills' part (src/library/allPerks.ts ALL_BUNDLES, STEP_PARTS). Once granted they are never removed. They replace the LLtQ category bonus.
- **All-XXX check** (Ali, 12/11/2026, LF3): runs at scenario end, when achievements are also granted, and both show in one scenario-end gains moment (its animation is with the motion work). Uses true skill (S9); the number of skills needed comes from the tree's rating (D-164). Built (D-226, CONFIG.GROWTH\_AT\_SCENARIO\_END): while a scenario runs, the player's All-XXX check is skipped at turn end and grantAchievement queues the player's achievements on Progression.pendingAchievements; when the scenario ends, closeScenario grants the queue and runs the check (src/engine/growth.ts flushGrowth). A queued achievement keeps the turn it was reached on, and its line names that turn, not the turn it was handed over (BF60). In free roam they wait for the next scenario's end too (BG-217, Ali, 14/11/2026), and its gains moment counts the roam's XP. NPCs are checked every turn. The ending turn's narration line carries one gains summary, LogLine.scenarioGains (ScenarioGains: skill raises with from and to, milestone unlocks, All-XXX perks, achievements, the XP bar from and to, level-ups), gathered across the scenario on ScenarioFrame.gains each turn; an outer scenario that ends with its inner one sums both. That line leaves out its own achievement, unlock and level-up notes, since the summary shows them.

With 100 as the mortal limit, the All-150 tier is unreachable through training alone until workaround mechanics are designed.

### 6. Skill improvement algorithm

Runs entirely in code at the end of each scenario, for the rolls the scenario pays (Scenario-end growth, below). Claude has no part in it. How a single roll gains is being redesigned (EG16, the skill-gain roll document); this section describes the code as it is.

```
Rolls are made in rounds. At the start of each round:
  Total  = sum of raw skills, excluding Tagged skills
           (Flawed skills count)
  Burden = max(0, floor((Total - Potential) / K))
  Burden holds for the round; the next round works it out again,
  so the first round's gains weigh on the second.

In each round, every skill with rolls left makes one (SG1 C):
  Cap    = SKILL_CAP (100); Tagged: + TAG_CAP_BONUS (25) = 125;
           Flawed: - FLAW_CAP_PENALTY (25) = 75
  Tagged : Margin = d100 + (Cap - Skill) - GROWTH_ROLL_NEEDED (90)
  Normal : Margin = d100 + (Cap - Skill - Burden) - 90
  Flawed : Margin = d100 + (Cap - Skill - Burden) - 90
  Read Margin on the tier table (natural edges do not move it: SG2 a, LF4)
     Near Miss or worse   +0
     Success              +2
     Great Success        +3
     Critical Success     +4, +1 per band past it (GROWTH_GAIN, GROWTH_GAIN_PAST_CRIT)
  Skill never passes Cap.
  (All-XXX checks run at scenario end, not here: LF3)

Repeat rounds until no skill has rolls left. Clear all marks.
```

- **Marks** (Ali, 07/11/2026, "Skill progression: options" SP1–SP11): a use marks the skill actually rolled (SP4) and, when a stand-in made the roll, also the skill the action asked for (Ali, 09/11/2026: both learn, each against its own window, so a skill far below its stand-in can still rise), success or failure, when the opposition is from MARK\_WINDOW (10, placeholder) below the skill's raw value up to MARK\_WINDOW above its true value (SP2; Ali, 09/11/2026, replacing ±10 of raw or true), so true-skill perks widen what teaches you. A skill not on the sheet learns from use too (Ali, 09/11/2026): it counts as raw 0 and joins the sheet with its first gain. Close calls teach too (Ali, 09/11/2026, an "or" with the window): a contest or defended attack whose final tier gap is exactly 0 marks both sides' skills whatever their values. Wins against stronger sides teach too (Ali, 13/11/2026): a contest or defended attack the player wins has no top to its window, so only the floor (MARK\_WINDOW below raw) applies; a loss still needs the full window, and so does the skill a stand-in rolled for. Passed checks teach the same way (Ali, PG2 a, 14/11/2026): a passed check has no top to its window, however hard its number; a failed one still needs the full window. A search for hidden items, gear or secrets passes when it finds every one it rolled against; a stash beats no one, so it keeps the window. The opposition is a check's target, or the other side's true skill in a contest, attack or defense; notice rolls follow the same rule (SP6). Endurance is marked by a single hit costing ENDURANCE\_MARK\_PCT (10%, placeholder) health or more (SP5). One mark per skill. Only the player keeps marks; NPC progression comes later (SP7).
- **Stacking marks** (Ali, 11/11/2026, SM1): a skill's marks are counted per engagement (or per scenario, outside one). The first mark owes an improvement roll, and so does every MARK\_STACK\_EVERY-th mark after it (config, 1: every mark owes a roll; Ali, 14/11/2026, D-318, was 2 and before that 3). Counts clear when the engagement ends or the scenario breaks (SM3).
- **Scenario-end growth** (Ali, 12/11/2026, growth proposal EG7, EG8, EG10, EG13, EG17 and the rounds rule; replaces instant skill-ups, SM2, and the per-engagement roll, SP3). While a scenario runs, marks only count: each owed roll waits (Progression.marks). When the scenario ends, it pays rolls = round(owed rolls × tier share × ending factor), plus GROWTH\_ROLLS\_PAST\_CRIT (1) per step past Critical Success on its event roll, plus GROWTH\_LOW\_LEVEL\_BONUS (1) while the character level is below GROWTH\_LOW\_LEVEL\_BELOW (5) (src/engine/growth.ts scenarioRolls, payScenario). A scenario that marked nothing pays nothing. Tier share (GROWTH\_TIER\_SHARE, by the scenario's event-roll tier): Critical Failure 0.5, Failure 0.6, Near Miss 0.7, Success 0.8, Great Success 0.9, Critical Success 1. Ending factor (ENDING\_FACTOR, judged endings by which ending it was, EG5 b): resolved 1; lost and broken off 0.75; left, escaped, interrupted and worn out 0.5; every factor is kept between ENDING\_FACTOR\_MIN (0.5) and ENDING\_FACTOR\_MAX (1.25), so no ending the Weaver writes or picks pays outside them (EG17 a). There is no scale factor (EG13 a): every mark is worth the same in a moment, a situation or an episode. The rolls are dealt to the marked skills by their owed rolls (largest remainder; EG6 a) and made in rounds (section 6). Marks, achievements, levels and All-XXX steps earned in free roam wait for the next scenario's end (BG-217). An inner scenario pays only for its own marks; the bigger scenario's waiting rolls and achievements are kept apart (ScenarioFrame.held) and come back when it resumes, or join the inner's when both end. Level-ups wait for the same moment (EG8): spendXp does nothing while a scenario runs, and the ending takes every level the XP allows. Gains show in the scenario-end gains summary (LogLine.scenarioGains); failed rolls show nothing and nothing goes to Claude (SP11). CONFIG.GROWTH\_AT\_SCENARIO\_END false brings back rolls at the end of the turn of the mark. Scaling: the payout counts rolls, not points, so it works the same at 50, 75, 100, 150 and 200; what a roll does at each skill is the improvement roll's (section 6), which stops at the mortal limit (LF4).
- **Essences** (Ali, 12/11/2026, growth proposal section 2: EG1 a, EG2, EG3 b, EG4 a, EG9 a, EG11 b, EG12 a, EG15 b; src/engine/essence.ts). An essence is a package of true skill (and later a trait, D-229) the player may take into a limited number of slots. It is a small foreign addition that sits on the Thread, never a strand of it (NM2), and it is not body: its points are true skill, count toward perk milestones and All-XXX (EG4 a), and ignore the mortal limit. **Rows:** the library `essences` table (ES ids, src/library/essenceIds.ts): a name, where it comes from (a race, a body type, or deed), a weight, the skills its points go to with weights, an optional trait and quirk (perk rows), and a story. Today's rows (Gel Patience from slimes: Natural Weapons 2 + Tremor 1; Gate-Warden's Habit: Block 2 + Earshot 1, Street Instinct: Dodge 2 + Insight 1, Haggler's Ear: Barter 2 + Earshot 1, Tavern Brawler: Strike 2 + Persuasion 1, all from humans; Hard-Won Habit from deeds) are placeholders; the content round is D-229. **Template lists** (Ali, 13/11/2026, Template Essences EN1–EN7): every NPC template carries its own essence list, worked out in code (`templateEssences`, src/engine/essence.ts) from its race and the sheet its copies spawn with (`templateSkills`). A row is on the list when its race or body type matches, or it names neither (EN2 a), and the template's weighted average in the row's skills (raw × tier) is at least ESSENCE\_FIT\_SHARE (0.4, placeholder) of its best skill (EN1 a). Because the fit is a share, a template keeps its list at every scale. A template may carry several rows and rows may be shared. Deed rows never go on a list. NpcTemplate.essences is a hand-set list that replaces the worked-out one (EN3 b); an echo (unique template) carries only its own essence, a row with from.echo, which is never worked out onto any other list, and a test checks every echo has exactly one. Today: slime Gel Patience, guard Gate-Warden's Habit, barkeep Haggler's Ear, thief Street Instinct, patron Tavern Brawler. **Sources:** a foe the player hurt (Mind.hitByPlayer) rolls once, ever, on the turn it lies dead (Mind.essenceRolled): an essence comes only from a death, never from a defeat (Ali, BF24, BG-199), on its template's list, or with no template the same rule on its own sheet (EN4; EG9 a: the source fixes the kind). A scenario that ends resolved at Great Success or better makes one appearance roll at its ending moment (TF20 b, EB1, Ali 14/11/2026): with anyone **bested** in it (ScenarioFrame.bested, dead or not), it offers **two** essences and the player takes one (EssenceOffer.pair: taking, giving or selling one lets the other go; passing one keeps the other): one from anyone bested, at random, and one from one of the ESSENCE\_TOP\_BESTED (3, placeholder) bested with the highest power; each comes from that person's list, is measured from their skill (raw × tier, EB1 b) and has its own quality roll. With nobody bested, a deed row whose points go to the skill marked most, measured from the player's true skill. **Bested** (BS1-BS3, Ali 14/11/2026; bested() in src/engine/xp.ts): defeated by the player (payDefeats), or a foe on the other side that stood down from the player's own offer to stop (persuasion, lie or bribe; offer.ts standDown), once per person, ever (Mind.bested). It is not a defeat and pays no XP; it feeds the ending's list and the Bested chain (AC237-AC241: 1, 10, 100, 250, 1000 at reward levels −2, −1, 0, I, II; −2 is a new bottom level and pays 0 XP by the one curve). Also bested (BS1 d): a guard's suspicion talked away (suspicion.ts clear, "talked"); a won persuasion, lie or threat against someone hostile or opposed to the player before the words (speech.ts resolveSpeech, BESTING\_SPEECH); and a filch from someone hostile or opposed that the Filching contest leaves uncaught (resolve.ts doFilch). Staying unseen never bests. BS4: a bested and a defeated person weigh the same in the ending draw. **Two rolls** (Ali, 13/11/2026): first the **appearance roll**: an essence appears when d100 ≤ ESSENCE\_APPEAR\_CHANCE for the source (a notable foe 100, any other foe 5, an ending at Great Success 60, at Critical Success 70; placeholders). D-274 (Ali, 13/11/2026, FS18): every beaten foe rolls once, ever; a notable foe's essence always appears, so its quality roll alone decides whether it fades. A foe is **notable** when its template is notable, it is a rolled or unique NPC, or its power is at least ESSENCE\_NOTABLE\_SHARE (2) × the player's: only a foe twice as strong or more counts by power (notable(), src/engine/essence.ts). A held essence's trait or quirk is a perk its holder owns (source essence) once, however many held essences carry it, and it goes with the last of them (FP8); their points still add. Then the appeared essence makes its **quality roll**: margin = d100 + base − ESSENCE\_ROLL\_NEEDED (100, the growth proposal's formula; the generous roll Ali picked) on the normal tier table, natural edges included; base ESSENCE\_ROLL\_BASE: a foe 80 (holds 81%: 25% common, 50% fine, 1% rare, 5% epic), an ending at Great Success 80, at Critical Success 100 (95%). Together with the appearance roll, an essence is offered 81% of the time from a notable foe, about 49% from a Great Success ending and about 67% from a Critical Success one. Threadbound item actives (Call the Toss, All or Nothing) work on the quality roll only. Near Miss or worse on the quality roll: the essence fades (logged essence\_faded; the Narrator tells it as a strand that slipped away, NarrationView.essencesFaded); Success common, Great Success fine, Critical Success rare, each step past it one grade higher (a natural high on a Critical Success is one such step; the code had stopped at rare until CS13) (ESSENCE\_GRADES: common, fine, rare, epic, legendary; past the last, ×ESSENCE\_GRADE\_PAST\_MULT each). **Points** = max(1, round(ESSENCE\_SHARE (0.07) × source skill × grade multiplier (ESSENCE\_GRADE\_MULT 1, 1.5, 2.5, 4, 6))), split over the row's skills by weight (largest remainder). The source skill is the foe's raw × tier in those skills (weighted), or for an ending the player's own true skill. **Room** (EG2): ESSENCE\_SLOTS\_BASE (1) + one per ESSENCE\_SLOT\_EVERY (7) character levels: 2 at 7, 3 at 14, 4 at 21, no top. **Choosing:** an offer waits ESSENCE\_OFFER\_TURNS (10) turns, or until a new scenario starts; /essence lists offers and held essences; /essence take N fills an empty slot; with every slot full, /essence take N give M gives up a held one first; /essence pass N lets it go for good. **Removal** (EG3 b, EG15 b): only at rest (out of any fight, no hostile in the zone); the essence is lost, and it costs ESSENCE\_REMOVE\_LEVELS (1) × the current level's XP, except the first ESSENCE\_FREE\_CHANGES (3) changes, a removal or a swap, which are free (EB3, Ali 14/11/2026; Progression.essenceChanges); the XP bar may go into debt, which new XP pays off first. **Shown:** when an offer is waiting that the player hasn't seen yet, a pop-up opens on Play once the turn lands (src/ui/essences.ts maybeOfferPop, once per offer): the essence's name, grade, story, its points as chips ("+1 Dodge", "+1 Insight"), slots used and how long it waits, with Take it (with every slot full, one "Take it, give up X" button per held essence), Let it go and Decide later; the buttons run the same /essence commands. The Character page has an Essences tile (slots used, and a violet "◆ N on offer" while one waits) that opens the Essences pop-up (CP4): slots as tiles, held essences with their chips, empty slots dashed, and On offer with the same buttons, the two offers of a pair side by side under "Take one: the other goes". The play sheet has an Essences row naming the held ones and a violet "◆ N on offer" link that opens that pop-up. The character card itself has no Essences lines, so essences show once on the Character page, in that pop-up; the ending line's gains summary lists essences offered (ScenarioGains.essences); the Narrator gets essences offered this turn as name and story only (NarrationView.essences, rule 22; the thread may be named, EG11 b, never numbers). **Achievements** (EG12 a): First Knot (I, take your first essence) and Room for No More (II, every slot full at character level 8 or higher). Scaling: points follow the source's skill, so at a source of 50 / 75 / 100 / 150 / 200 a common essence gives 4 / 5 / 7 / 11 / 14 points and a rare one 9 / 13 / 18 / 26 / 35; the grade table and the slots have no top. Past 100 essences and perks are how skills keep rising. **Essences open their skills** (Ali, 13/11/2026): a held essence that adds true skill to a skill opens that skill whatever its tree's or its own access rules (skillBlock), and taking it puts any missing skill on the sheet at raw 0 so its points count. Letting the essence go closes the skill again; the raw-0 entry stays and counts 0. A body that lacks the part a skill's role needs is D-255.
- **Essence extraction** (Ali, 14/11/2026, DX1-DX5 on the Essences page; src/engine/extract.ts, action extract). A blessing of Imbra, minor goddess of essence manipulation (D-311: gods and blessings; the blessed Start grants it), the perk Extract Essence (NM2: it works on the essence, a small foreign addition to someone's Thread; no god touches the Thread itself), lets the player, as their main action and within grip reach, take the essence of someone who is Down (Incapacitated) and has broken or surrendered (DX2 c; a willing target is D-312). No roll decides it (DX3 a), and it uses up the target's one essence roll (Mind.essenceRolled), so a later death gives none. The essence comes from the target's own list, always appears and never fades: its quality roll (base ESSENCE\_ROLL\_BASE.foe) sets the grade, and below Success gives Common (DX4 b). Its points are worked out from the target's skills first (raw × tier); then the target is left **Hollowed** (a quirk, perk source inflicted; Core.hollowed): each of the essence's skills keeps 1 − HOLLOWED\_SHARE\_MULT (2) × the essence's share (7% × the grade multiplier) of its true skill, and every skill keeps 1 − HOLLOWED\_ALL\_LOSS (10%), multiplied, for good (DX5 a; hollowedMult in src/ruleset/perks.ts). It counts as an attack on the target (DX6 a; setAttackOverrides): the target, though Down, and its bandmates who see it fully turn hostile to the extractor. No fear is paid: bandmates already paid "A bandmate goes down" when it fell, as with any blow on someone Down. The offer is an EssenceOffer with source extract. Only the player extracts, since only the player takes essence offers.
- **Passing on essences** (Ali AT75, 13/11/2026: ES1 a, ES2 a, ES3 a, ES4 a, ES5 a, ES6 a + permanent, ES7 a; src/engine/essence.ts). Only an offer the player hasn't taken can be passed on (ES1); it stays an essence and goes straight to someone (ES2; an essence carried as an item is parked as D-249). **Give:** /essence give N to a name in view; the receiver must be within grip reach and have room, and takes it in with no yes needed (ES4, a gift). **Room** (ES3): an actor with a progression record (the player, unique NPCs today; permanent vs unique is D-148), into slots by the player's rule (ESSENCE\_SLOTS\_BASE + one per ESSENCE\_SLOT\_EVERY levels); its points are their true skill (essenceSkill). **Sell:** /essence sell N to a name; the buyer must hear the order (as an item sale), not be wary, and pay the buying price for its value (trade.ts buyingPrice, one equation with items: SELL\_SHARE, haggling and Hard Bargain). **Value** (ES5): points × ITEM\_VALUE.skillPoint × ESSENCE\_VALUE\_MULT (10, placeholder): a common essence from a source of 50 / 75 / 100 / 150 / 200 is 40 / 50 / 70 / 110 / 140 coin, a rare one 90 / 130 / 180 / 260 / 350, no cap. **Buyers** (ES6): a seller (Trader class) takes it into a free slot if it has a record, else holds it (Actor.essenceStock) to sell on; a unique NPC who is no seller buys only into a free slot (Ali: "I should also be able to sell to permanent"). A sale can be refused (wary, no coin, no room); a gift only by having no room. The receiver remembers it (a memory line). NPCs selling or giving essences to the player are parked as D-250 (ES7).
- **Training actions** happen only under a tutor; designed later (SP1, SP8). Beyond the mortal limit stays later (SP9).
- **StartingTotal:** sum of untagged raw skills at creation. A record only; it never changes.
- **Potential:** the Total at which Burden begins. Can be changed later by perks or events. At creation it is the fixed POTENTIAL (300, placeholder) for every character. Any character, NPC or player, may carry its own value instead, set case by case: an NPC template's potential, or a value set on one player character.
- **K:** *placeholder*, default 20.
- **Shown** (Burden review, Ali 14/11/2026, BU3 b): Burden shows only once it is above 0, and only in a skill's pop-up on the Character page (an untagged skill: "Burden −N to improve" and the untagged total against Potential). Tagged skills never show it. Agents never see it (section 2). The rest of the review kept the rules as they are: one Potential of 300 for every Start (BU1 a), Burden linear with no ceiling (BU2 a), raw counted at face value whatever the skill's tier (BU5 a), and no rule for carried weight (BU6 b). Perks, achievements and events that raise Potential are D-317.

### 7. Player commands \[Proposed\]

Commands are handled by code and do not advance time. There is no /save: the story saves every turn (Part 4 §10), and a full export exists only in the GM debug view.

| Command | Effect |
| --- | --- |
| `/stats` | Show the character sheet |
| `/inv` | Show inventory |
| `/quests` | Show active quests |
| `/undo` | Rewind one turn: the head moves back to the previous bookmark; dice are not rolled back (Part 4 §6) |
| `/help` | Show the rules text and a recap of the events the player perceived; code only, no Claude call |
| /aim | Marks this turn's attack as aimed: a harder roll that, if it hits, lands on the body part the attack names; it never picks the target. The play screen's Aim toggle does the same. Code only; resets after the turn resolves. Goes in front of an attack command: `/aim /strike the guard at his left leg` |
| `/strike`, `/shoot`, `/throw` | Attack commands (Ali, 11/11/2026): `/strike <who> [at <body part>]`, `/shoot <who> [at <body part>]`, `/throw <what> at <who> [at <body part>]`. The words go to the Parser as "I strike …", so the Parser picks the weapon, mode and targetPart as for typed words; with no target they say how to use them |

## Part 2 — Turn engine (rev. 2 + Explore mode + scenario setup + actor control)

### Role, goal and hard rules

You are building the turn engine for a text RPG that runs as a Claude artifact app. Code owns every number and every fact; Claude calls only interpret, decide intent, voice characters, plan scenarios and narrate.

The engine must solve four problems that appear when one model plays both sides:

1. **Omniscient enemies.** Enemies react to the player's current action because the same context saw it. Fix: enemies commit to orders without seeing it (simultaneous turns), and each actor acts only on what it knows.
2. **Leaky narration.** The narrator hints at plans the character could not know. Fix: the narrator never receives hidden information.
3. **Forgotten plans.** An ambush or secret disappears between turns. Fix: hidden state lives in code, and triggers fire without asking a model.
4. **Latency and cost.** Several calls per turn is slow. Fix: parallel calls where the design allows, cheaper models where prose quality doesn't matter, and agents only in scenarios that need them.

**Ruleset (do not change its numbers):** skills in three-skill trees in the style of Long Live the Queen, with caps, Tags (+) and Flaws (−); Fallout-style perks (level perks plus all-XXX perks); AGG-style d100 roll-high with tiered results, where the dice set the situation and skill checks mitigate it. Build the ruleset behind the Ruleset interface (Resolution engine): the six core methods for real (Part 2, First-build ruleset), the rest stubbed (each method keeps its signature and returns a fixed placeholder).

**Agents.** Six Claude agents, each with one job:

| Agent | Job | Side |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Agent Parser | Turns player text into structured intent | Player-side |
| Agent Planner | Commits an engaged actor's order before the player acts (background) | Hidden-side |
| Agent Responder | Answers the player as an NPC with a secret or plan (dialogue) | Hidden-side |
| Agent Voice | Answers the player as an ordinary NPC; never sees the hidden store | Player-side |
| Agent Narrator | Writes what the player reads | Player-side |
| Agent Weaver | Plans events, casts and stages at scenario setup and stage extension | Hidden-side |

Agent Planner and Agent Responder both read OppositionView; each has its own prompt. Agent Responder and Agent Voice are the dialogue calls: one per addressed spokesperson, after the Parser.

**Hard rules**

- No model computes a roll, sets health, adds an item or decides success. A model's output is a request; the engine decides.
- The player and every NPC go through the same roll code. No player-only resolution path.
- No agent receives anything its character could not know. Views are built deny-by-default. Nothing from state.hidden reaches Agent Parser, Agent Narrator or Agent Voice.

### Architecture: one turn

Only code ever sees every agent's input and output. Planner calls run in the background between turns, before the player submits. In dialogue turns the addressed actors' dialogue calls run after the Parser, because spoken words are meant to be heard. Speech is an action: talking takes the turn's main action, for the player and for every NPC.

#### Tactical and explore turn

1. After turn N is saved, code computes turn N+1: its mode, the triggers that fire at its start, and its engaged significant actors (state.pending). If the player's zone links to a frontier with no held extension, code also starts that frontier's background Agent World builder call (Stage extension).
2. Each engaged significant actor's Planner call starts at once, in the background, with that actor's OppositionView built from saved state, up to MAX\_PLANNER\_CALLS. Explore has no engaged actor, so it runs no Planner.
3. On submit, Agent Parser receives the player's text and PlayerView. Resolution starts once the Parser returns, and never before the previous display plus BACKGROUND\_WINDOW, in every mode. An actor whose Planner call isn't back by then uses its type's combat list this turn (Fallback behavior).
4. Code validates every output. If the Parser returns a clarify question, the turn stops and turn is unchanged. Planner outputs are kept, because state hasn't changed, and apply once, when this turn resolves.
5. The engine builds every active actor's ActorView, then merges the Parser's intent with one order per actor, in this priority: fired triggers, surrendered actors' hold orders (Part 3, Morale), agent orders (dialogue call, else Planner), onAlert orders, routine orders (not engaged) or combat-list orders (engaged); rolls, applies effects, and records who perceived each event at the moment it happens.
6. If this turn called scenarioBreak, or the player moved into a frontier, scenario setup or stage extension runs now, before the save (Scenario setup).
7. The story is saved (Part 4 §10), including plan updates and this turn's logged agent inputs and outputs.
8. Code builds NarrationView from the events the player perceived.
9. Agent Narrator writes the full narration and lists the events it covered; the leak scan and fact check run; then the text is shown. No streaming.

#### Dialogue turn

A turn is a dialogue turn when the Parser's main action is a speech action (talk, persuade, deceive, intimidate, barter) addressed to one or more NPCs.

1. The Parser parses the player's text and lists the addressees: named actors, a band, or everyone in earshot.
2. Code picks one spokesperson per addressed band: the member the words are aimed at (the action's target, BG-122); else the named member; else the sticky partner (the NPC the player last spoke with this scenario) if it is an active member of that band in reach; else the band's nearest addressed member, ties by actor id. When a band is addressed without a name, no partner of it is in reach and the player can see more than one of its members, code asks "Who do you speak to: A or B?" naming only people the player can see, with no Claude call and no turn spent (OQ-27, answered 07/11/2026). At most MAX\_SPOKESPEOPLE (config, placeholder) bands get one, in the order the player's words name them; members of other bands only hear the words. Inactive actors are never picked.
3. Exchange slot, part 1: the engine resolves the player's speech. A speech check is the speaker against the other side: one roll by the player with the action's speech skill, contested separately against each spokesperson's roll with the same skill (read by tier difference, Part 1 §4, Contests). Talk and persuade use persuasion; deceive uses Lying, answered with Insight; intimidate uses intimidation; barter uses barter. Each earlier try of the same kind at the same listener this engagement costs the speaker −15 (D-154). Any of them may be substituted by another speech skill (Part 1 §3, Skill substitution). The speech event is perceived like any other event. Winning counts toward check\_passed secret reveals, sets prices with a seller (D-93), and leaves the listener Shaken or Swayed (D-154). NPCs speak to the player with a roll the same way, through persuade, deceive, intimidate or barter (D-154).
4. One dialogue call per spokesperson runs in parallel: the Responder if that spokesperson has an agenda, else the Voice. Each receives its own view plus only this turn's spoken words and its impression (Dialogue input, below). Each returns its one action for the turn: a reply (a talk action) or any other main action.
5. Exchange slot, part 2: spoken replies resolve right after the player's speech, in initiative order. A spokesperson that chose another action resolves it at its own initiative in step 6.
6. resolveTurn runs for every other actor and for the spokespeople's other actions, as in step 5 of the tactical turn; then steps 6 to 9 of that turn run.

Rules for dialogue turns:

- **Planner calls:** an addressed spokesperson's background Planner call is cancelled once the Parser returns a dialogue intent, never on a clarify question; a result that arrives anyway is logged as unused. Engaged actors elsewhere keep their Planner calls.
- **Triggers:** a turn-start trigger fired for a spokesperson is shown to its dialogue call as already committed. The trigger's order is its action, and the call's line is kept only as a bark. A mid-turn trigger may replace a spokesperson's other action before it resolves; a reply already spoken stands.
- **Barks:** words spoken alongside a non-speech main action are a bark, not speech, and make no dialogue turn.
- **Calm bandmates \[Proposed\]:** when a spokesperson's output switches its routine, calm, code-run bandmates who perceived the exchange switch to the same routine next turn. A bandmate switches only if that routine id is in its own routines list and every slot the routine reads is bound for it; otherwise it keeps its routine and code logs routine\_skip. Engaged bandmates decide through their own Planner or combat list. Calm means not engaged, no alarm armed, and no attack or alarm perceived within DECAY\_AWARE\_S (CS2).
- **Timing:** dialogue results are shown no earlier than DIALOGUE\_WINDOW (config) after submit, set at or above the Responder's usual latency, so guarded and ordinary NPCs answer in the same time.

#### Dialogue input

A dialogue call receives, from this turn, only the words the player spoke and one impression per speech action: how convincing it sounded (talk, persuade, deceive, barter) or how threatening it felt (intimidate). It never receives the player's action type, any non-speech check, the player's move, approach, stance or deferred actions. The impression comes from the player's tier; the listener's roll decides only who wins the contest. Code maps the tier to fixed words (table below; wording is a placeholder). No tier names, no numbers.

| Player's tier | Convincing (talk, persuade, deceive, barter) | Threatening (intimidate) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Critical Failure | insulting, plainly false | laughable |
| Failure | unconvincing | unimpressive |
| Near Miss | doubtful but possible | unsettling |
| Success | convincing | threatening |
| Great Success | very convincing | frightening |
| Critical Success | completely convincing | terrifying |

The tier names are the six tiers of Part 1 §4; the ruleset still owns them.

### Action economy

Being replaced by Action time below, stage by stage.
