Slots (AE1), the ranged move cost (ZP3), action slots (CO13), dashes (AE2) and extra attacks (AE4) now run on seconds of move and main time: see "Built so far (stage 2)" under Action time. The bullets below on those are kept for their history; the speeds and the rest still hold.

Settled by Ali 07/11/2026 ("Action economy: options", AE1–AE12; settles D-19, D-60, D-83).

- **Slots (AE1).** A turn is one move (a walk: 8.4 m for an unarmored human), one main action, and free things: gear ops, defenses (each after the first in a turn takes the outnumbered modifier) and barks. Perks and class skills change a slot: add one, swap one, or make one free. A fight turn stays 6 seconds whatever it holds (AE12).
- **Ranged (AE1, ZP3).** A ranged attack (a shot or a throw) costs the main action and CONFIG.RANGED\_MOVE\_SLOTS move slots (1) by default. If the attack reaches from where the actor stands, it takes its move slot first and a move goes only on the move slots left (with one move slot the move is dropped: move\_dropped, ranged\_attack); otherwise the move stands and the attack is dropped. Shoot and Move (B4, banked) would lower the cost.
- **Action slots (CO13).** Each actor gets one move and one main action a turn (now seconds of each: see Action time). A movement spends a move slot first and, when no move slot is left, a main slot, but only when no main action is planned. How far a slot goes comes from real speeds over the Fight turn duration (6 s, engine/speed `slotMetres`): a move slot walks and a main slot spent moving (a dash: the move costs both actions) runs the rest of the turn. Speeds (RD7, `moveSpeed`) come from the race (`RaceDef.speed`, m/s; AB1 c, so one body type serves a dog and a tiger): human and elf walk 1.4, run 5 (a run in kit); dragon 1.8 and 12 (a horse on the ground); slime 0.5 and 1 (placeholder); each animal its own real speeds. A future speed skill multiplies them (AB1). Worn armor slows both: a flat m/s off, then a share off what is left (`armor.speed`, default CONFIG.ARMOR\_SPEED by weight: light 0 and 0%, medium 0.1 and 5%, heavy 0.2 and 15%, placeholders), and a movement speed factor (`speedFactor`, 1 today) multiplies the result for later perks, statuses and mounts. An unarmored humanoid walks 8.4 m and a dash adds 21.6 m: 30 m in all. Without zone rectangles a fresh slot always crosses at least one link. An actor with more move slots walks farther.
- **Dash (AE2).** Anyone (player or NPC) with no main action whose move hasn't reached its zone when the move slot runs out spends the main slot to run on. No running stops a dash (dash\_blocked): the move role is off once its Crippled and Destroyed parts reach SHARED\_ROLE\_OFF\_PCT (60%) of its parts, a Destroyed part counting DESTROYED\_PART\_WEIGHT (1.2) Crippled ones (the shared-role rule, Part 3; CS8, CS24). One Crippled leg of two still dashes, one Destroyed leg does not. Leg Bolt's slow stops a dash too.
- **Order (AE3).** The move comes first unless the Parser marks it `after` ("strike, then step back"). NPCs keep move first for now (parked as D-185: the Planner would need the same field).
- **Following at a distance (BF35, Ali 14/11/2026).** A walk never ends on someone: its end keeps PLACE\_GAP\_M (0.2 m, BF71) from everyone else in the zone, with no one let off for standing near where it began; a walk up to someone stops PLACE\_SPACING\_M (1 m, arm's length) from them. Closing in on someone for a blow, a grab, tending or a filch keeps no gap from them: it stops at the reach the action needs, and when the straight-line spot is taken it steps round to the nearest free spot at that reach that its walk can get to (RING\_STEPS spots round them; position.ts walkLeg). Hemmed in on every side, the action is lost with reason no\_room, not out\_of\_reach (roomAround). The code-run opening move of a fight closes to the blow's reach the same way. A follow keeps the distance the player says (the Parser's spot `keep`): metres, `range` (FOLLOW\_RANGE\_SHARE, 80%, of the reach of the ranged weapon in hand) or `melee` (striking reach). Not said, FOLLOW\_SNEAK\_M (5 m) sneaking and FOLLOW\_M (2 m) otherwise. Farther than that, the follower closes in; nearer, it never backs off by itself. An NPC on the follow routine keeps after the player in the same zone: at range while it holds a ranged weapon, else at striking reach (engine/follow.ts followGap). The following line on Play shows the distance kept ("2 m back"). Keeping sight while following is parked as D-307.
- **Words with a walk (BF36, Ali 14/11/2026).** Words are heard from where they are said. For the player the order of their words decides: "I tell him to wait and walk out" speaks first (the Parser's `after`), "I walk to the door and call to him" speaks at the door. An NPC's words beside a move its mind chose are said as it sets off (a talk is done first; a line beside another action is said on its own before the walk), unless its move says `speakAt: "end"` (walk there, then say it). A walk up to someone to be heard (approachMove) still comes first.
- **Extra attacks (AE4)** come only from perks and class skills (Flurry: a second attack at tier shift −1), never from a high skill alone.
- **Free actions (AE5)** stay as above; a perk can make one action free a limited number of times per engagement (Dirt in the Eyes).
- **Readying (AE6)** is a perk move (Held Shot), not something everyone can do; anyone can still hold.
- **Speech (AE7, TP6 c)** in a fight is an action only when it uses a skill: persuade, deceive, intimidate and barter take their words' time as the main action; plain talk takes no time (orderCost gives it 0 s, so the turn's time stays and carries, TP4). Quick Word, once the Persuasion 20 perk, is everyone's rule (the perk is retired, PK15; the empty slot is D-325); barks persuade no one. A one-sentence line said beside a non-speech action in a fight is an exchange like talk (same spokespeople, contest and dialogue calls), but the player's action still resolves in turn order; each spokesperson's spoken reply is free too, and the action its dialogue call chose still resolves in turn order.
- **Asides (OA1, OA3; playtest feedback 13/11/2026).** Outside a fight, the player's words go with the turn's action: words said beside a deed (a buy, a give, a knock, a search) are an exchange like talk (same spokespeople, contest and dialogue calls), said after the walk over, and answered this same turn; the deed still resolves in turn order and each spokesperson's spoken reply is free (the Quick Word path, src/engine/speech.ts quickWord). Words that name no one are for the deed's target, else whoever the player was just talking with. An attack starts a fight, so its words stay a bark. A courtesy beside a real question is never the turn's words (Parser rule 2). Words for someone last seen in the zone the player walks into are said once there, never put off (Parser rule 11, legality); BG-147 widens this: anyone the player knows is about can be called to without a move, and whoever hears it answers.
- **Furnished rooms (FR1–FR5, Ali 13/11/2026).** A zone lists named furniture (Zone.furniture: a kind from the furniture kinds table FU, a name, where it stands: a compass side or corner, or the middle). It is colour first (FR1, "mostly cosmetic, so what the Weaver makes up as it goes impacts nothing"): a move can end beside a piece (spot near its id), and search and stash work where one stands; it never blocks sight, cover or movement. FR2: every zone kind has default furniture (a room a table and a bench, a cellar barrels and sacks, a yard a woodpile and a rain barrel, a square a well, a street a handcart), every layout zone lists its own, and the Weaver may replace either with a whole list (a zone's furniture, or a layout zone's by key in its place's furniture). FR3: the Narrator never moves or contradicts listed furniture and may add small unlisted things for colour, which are not in the game until the player uses one (as an improvised weapon, say). FR4: a piece on an edge (edge: true, a counter or rail) is one record and shows on the far side of that edge too (src/engine/furniture.ts furnitureIn). FR5: every zone view lists furniture with where it stands, and the map data gives each piece its point and kind so the map can draw an icon for it.
- **Free small deeds (OA4, Ali 13/11/2026).** Setting something down where one stands is a free gear op (set_down, with qty for part of a stack), like stowing: it takes stowing's time, never the turn's action. Asking to see one's stats or inventory alone (the Parser's look: stats or inv) shows /stats or /inventory with no turn passing.
- **Talking someone out of a fight (TF1, TF4–TF17, Ali 13–14/11/2026; src/engine/offer.ts).** In a fight, persuasion is an offer to stop (TF13): the player's persuade at a foe that speaks (an Intelligent race, whoever runs it: TF4) and is hostile to them, one contest as for any speech. The foe answers with its wound and fear penalties on its roll, so the health and morale bars are the will to fight and no new bar is needed (TF1). How it landed (TF15): convinced (a win by OFFER\_WIN\_TIERS 3, or OFFER\_WIN\_TIERS\_SHAKEN 2 once the foe is OFFER\_EASIER\_HEALTH Hurt or worse, or OFFER\_EASIER\_MORALE Uneasy or worse; *placeholders*, TF14), tempted (a smaller win or a tie) or unmoved (a loss). A foe run by code stands down when convinced. A foe its AI plays is told how it landed ("it convinces you", "it tempts you", "it does not move you"), in this turn's Responder or Voice impression and as self.standDownOffer in its view, and answers standDown true or false within OFFER\_OPEN\_TURNS (2); no answer is no. The listener answers for its bandmates together with it who heard the words (TF6). **A stand-down** (TF16, TF5) is a truce like a taken yield: for the rest of the scenario neither the foes who stood down nor the speaker's side as it stood (the player and everyone fighting beside them) may attack the other (an attack is wasted, reason truce), and the foes' hunt for them ends. A stand-down is its own outcome, never a defeat for either side (TF18): no XP, no essence roll, and taking a foe's offer is no loss for the player; a foe the player talks down before any blow from them is tallied talked\_down (the Blood Unspilled chain). **Talking a foe into surrendering (TF19):** a persuasion or lie the Parser marks demand "yield" asks the foe to give up, not only to stop. It needs a bigger win: CONFIG.OFFER_YIELD_WIN_TIERS (4), OFFER_YIELD_WIN_TIERS_SHAKEN (3) once the foe is Hurt or Uneasy or worse, and OFFER_YIELD_EASIER (1) less for each of: the foe is Broken, a part of it is Crippled or worse; never less than a win by one tier (placeholders). A foe cowering Broken can still be asked (canDemand). A code-run or Broken foe yields when convinced (with the bandmates who heard it, TF6), as a surrender from fear (loseNerve), so it counts as beaten; an AI-run one is told it is a demand that it surrender and answers yields (standDown does not answer it). The event is kind surrender. Foes do not yet demand the player's surrender.
- **Breaking a container open (BF31, Bug Fix Questions; base approved 14/11/2026; src/engine/resolve.ts doBreak):** the action "break" at a locked container within grip reach is the turn's action (Action times row DU37, 6 s, hard) and loud (heard like a shout). A soft container (sack, pack: ItemDef.container.soft) is cut open with no roll by an edged weapon in hand (ItemDef.edge). Otherwise it is a roll of the melee weapon in hand (bare hands: Improvised) with the lock's grade modifier, as Lockpicking uses (LOCK_GRADE_MOD); Success or better opens it and leaves lock.broken (it never locks again). Event kind break_open. **What breaks inside** (BK1-BK3, src/engine/breakage.ts): every item has a fragility (FRAGILITY: Fragile, Delicate, Sturdy, Tough, Solid, Unbreakable; ItemDef.fragility, else FRAGILITY\_BY\_KIND: consumables Fragile, accessories Delicate, armor, shields and the rest Sturdy, tools Tough, weapons Solid; charms and curios Delicate, bandages Sturdy, coin and keys Unbreakable, Threadbound items never break). Each breaks at a break roll of FRAGILITY\_BREAKS\_AT or worse (Near Miss, Failure, Critical Failure, Critical Failure−, Critical Failure−−, never; *placeholders*), so tools sit between armor and weapons. The one break roll is checked against everything inside, opened or not (BK2); a stack breaks together. A broken item stays as its broken version (BK3): tag "broken", name "broken <name>", worth BROKEN\_VALUE\_SHARE (0.1, *placeholder*) of its value, no attack, armor, healing or bonus. The Weaver may set a minted item's fragility. The Narrator gets breakOpen.broke (names once open, "something inside" while it stays shut). **Safer breaking** (BK4): rolled opens count toward Forced Entry, Steady Crowbar, Nothing Spilled (AC234-AC236, 1/10/50, names placeholders); every level's safeBreak reward moves every fragility line one tier worse. No perk yet (BK5, D-305). **Guarded** (BK6): breaking a container its owner guards is theft seen with no Filching contest: everyone outside the breaker's band who makes it out clearly catches them as caught Filching (owner FILCH\_CAUGHT\_OWNER\_STEPS colder, others FILCH\_CAUGHT\_WITNESS\_STEPS, all remember); half perceived, suspicious. Searches: an owner down or yielded doesn't guard (a body's box breaks freely); one who lets you search them (letsSearch) still guards their locked box. Open: guards suspecting locked containers (GD1-GD6). **Lies** (TF17): a deceive at a foe in a fight is an offer too, Lying against Insight. Threats stay as they are and wear down morale. **Bribes** (TF10): barter naming the coin (the action's qty) is an offer, Barter against Barter, with OFFER\_BRIBE\_MOD (10) for each doubling of the coin over the foe's price (OFFER\_BRIBE\_PRICE 5 per character level), as much less per halving (*placeholders*); the stand-down only comes once the coin is paid, and with too little coin nothing comes of it. **Can't be refused** (TF10 a): Beg for Mercy (Persuasion 100, at the last health level before going down) and Buy Them Off (Barter 100) make any win an offer that even an AI-run foe must take. **Both ways** (TF11): an NPC's persuasion or lie at the player in a fight is an offer; the player sees it in player.standDownOffers and takes it by saying so (the Parser's standDown, the offerer's id); the player's taking it beats no one. **No repeat penalty in a fight** (TF9 b). **Shown** (TF12, TF17): the Narrator gets offers on the player's speech (how it struck each listener) and a stand\_down event; the Parser sees which foes can be talked down (talkDown); in a fight the Talk ideas offer Talk X down, Lie to X to end it, Pay X to stop, and Take X's offer to stop.
- **Guards and locked containers (GD1-GD6, "Breaking things open, guards and locked boxes"; src/engine/suspicion.ts):** a guard (class Guard) whose search turns up a locked container on someone suspects them (GD2 b: a search only; on sight parked, D-306). It holds the suspicion (Mind.suspects; its agent sees self.suspects), is one step colder and remembers it (GD3 b). A code-run guard keeps close to them and asks to see inside every SUSPECT\_ASK\_TURNS (3, *placeholder*) turns. What a guard is after (GD1 b): stolen goods (stolenFrom) and the scenario's forbidden goods (the Weaver's plan.forbidden, catalog item ids, StageInfo.forbidden); what it was told about lives in its memory for its agent. Cleared (GD4 b) when the container is opened or forced open where the guard clearly sees inside: nothing it is after, the suspicion goes and the step comes back; stolen goods, caught as caught Filching (FL5, the owner learns too); forbidden goods, FILCH\_CAUGHT\_WITNESS\_STEPS colder and remembered. Or the suspect wins a Persuasion or Lying contest against the guard. Lasts until cleared or the scenario ends (GD5 a); anyone can be suspected, NPCs too (GD6 a).
- **Steady yourself (SY1, Ali 13/11/2026).** The main action steady: in a fight only (the actor is engaged), never while Broken, and not again within STEADY\_COOLDOWN\_S (*placeholder*, 30) game seconds. One morale roll (Resolve, with the worst Shaken the actor carries against it); its tier wins back a share of max morale through the composure row Steadying yourself (CP6), or, done badly, loses one through the fears row Trying to steady yourself makes it worse (FR13): STEADY\_PCT (*placeholders*) Critical Failure −10, Failure −5, Near Miss 5, Success 10, Great Success 15, Critical Success 20, and STEADY\_PAST\_PCT 5 more for each step past either end. Great Success or better also leaves the actor Mentally steady: its next morale loss is multiplied by STEADY\_BUFF\_MULT (*placeholder*, 0.5), then the buff is gone. NPCs can steady themselves too. The Narrator gets it in words (calmer, worse, no change), never points. On the play screen: Ideas offers Steady yourself in the Fight group only while the player can steady (cantSteady is null), and a violet Mentally steady chip sits beside Morale on the sheet while the buff lasts. With a broken nerve the chips stay Yield and Flee.
- **Putting things into containers (PI1–PI4; Ali 13/11/2026: "carrying equipment being one item in the inventory, and we can place items inside them").** Gear op stow with into: a container the actor carries (a sack), or an open chest or crate within grip reach that is not someone else's to guard. It is free gear, like stowing, and takes stowing's time. A container holds anything for now (inventory and container size is parked, D-254); qty puts in part of a stack, which joins a stack of its kind inside. A container never goes inside another, worn things come off first, and into a worn pack is a plain stow (CT7: the pack holds what is stowed). Taking it out again is pick\_up of that item. The inventory lists what each carried container holds (with stack counts), and the Narrator's gear list says what it is holding. NPCs can do the same.
- **No pockets** (Ali's Play comment, 14/11/2026, reversing PO1-PO3): "remove the concept of pockets and consider everything in packs outside containers accessible". Everything carried outside a container (in the worn pack, or loose) is as quick to reach as drawing a weapon, in a fight too: drawing it, using it (a draught is drunk the same turn) or stowing it costs only its own row. Only taking something out of a container one carries (a sack: gear op pick_up of an item it holds) adds the Action times row DU38 "Get something out of a container" (6 s, one fight turn, *placeholder*) in a fight. What is picked up, given, bought or filched joins the stowed items, so a worn pack holds all of them, and a pack taken off a downed body takes them all (CT7). The belt with pouches (IT46) and the bandolier (IT47), which only added pockets, are removed (PO4 a); their IDs are retired.
- **Perk moves (AE8)** (Plead, Battle Cry, Buy Them Off) are main actions of their own type: the Parser picks an owned one by name (its perkMove), and Commands and Ideas list the ones the player has. A free perk move (Dirt in the Eyes) rides beside the move and the main action and resolves just before the main action; one that fails its conditions is dropped (perk\_move\_dropped).
- **NPCs (AE9)** use the same slots and perks. The Planner still returns one move and one action; an extra slot with no choice in it (Flurry's second attack) is filled by code, and one with a choice gets a Planner field the NPC fills only when it owns the perk.
- **Restrain (BF5, Ali 13/11/2026; run 82b).** A grab that holds someone back unhurt is the main action restrain, within the grabber's grip reach (NEEDS restrain: grip; out of reach it closes in on the move slot). The grabber's grapple against the target's better of grapple and dodge; the target wins ties. Held, the target is held as by A8 Hold (heldBy, src/engine/held.ts restrain): its next move is its break-free try, and failing it loses that move. No damage and no hostility: it is never an attack, and the target feels it (touch).
- **Armor (AE10):** putting armor on or off stays one main action. **Criticals (AE11):** "lose your next action" means the next main action.

### Action time

Settled by Ali 13/11/2026 on the Action Time pages (part one https://claude.ai/artifact/X5qfW3RXXFhzV51nbmC9tr, part two https://claude.ai/artifact/7Vzne1WtdZ6DeH77xzfWVg, part three https://claude.ai/artifact/Hazzen8xGG66boCGkixaYd; AT1 to AT84). It replaces the slots of the Action economy section above as it is built, in four stages; each rule names its stage. Until a stage is built, the Action economy section still describes the code.

**Units and turns**
- **Seconds and rounds (AT35, AT74; stage 1).** Every action has a time in seconds. Short durations are counted in rounds: CONFIG.ROUND\_S (3 s, one exchange of blows) is the one lever for them, and anything may be a fraction of a round (twice a round is ROUND\_S / 2). "Round" is a code ID only: the game never shows it, only the final number of seconds ("slowed for 6 s").
- **Turns are only the player's input (AT13, AT37).** The world lives in time; a turn is when the game stops to ask the player. A turn lasts as long as the player's order (AT13 c), at least CONFIG.FIGHT\_TURN\_MIN\_S (3 s) and at most FIGHT\_TURN\_S (6 s) in a fight; outside a fight at most CONFIG.EXPLORE\_TURN\_S (30 s, Ali 14/11/2026; was 120 s, AT46), and an order with no time of its own takes the Action times table's action row (30 s). Unused time carries (AT20). Stage 2.
- **Repeating orders and chains (AT78, AT53; stage 2).** An attack or shot order repeats until the turn's time is used, and stops early when the target is out of reach after the actor's move has followed it, goes down, yields or is out of sight, or when the clock pauses (AT65). One order can name a short chain ("draw and strike"); the chain fills the turn the same way, a chain that ends in an attack repeats it, and what is not started when the turn ends is dropped.
- **Long actions lift the cap (AT45; stage 3).** While the player keeps at an action that runs by itself, or a walk, time runs until it is done or the clock pauses (AT65).

**The Action times table (AT1, AT11, AT12; stage 1)**
- One library table, `actionTimes`: per action, its seconds at a time factor of 1, whether it locks movement (AT19), its focus and stop (AT57), its output (AT27), what is left when broken off (AT6, AT28) and an optional speed function (D-248). Values for a skill-50 professional, from real references (the Part Three page cites each; a second agent checked them):
  - melee or unarmed blow 3 s (no lock, reflex); throw 3 s (locks, reflex); light bow 5 s, medium bow 6 s (locks, steady); crossbow shot 2 s and spanning its own action: light 4 s, stirrup 8 s (locks, careful; a spanned crossbow stays loaded, AT11c b), the heavy crossbow at once (TP6 f: 0 s, base effort; was 28 s all-out);
  - Battle Cry 1 s; talking 0.4 s a word (share: the words said before a break are heard), but plain talk in a fight takes none (TP6 c); barks stay free (AE7);
  - draw, stow, swap, pick up and hand over: instant, base effort (less realism, TP6 a: once 1 to 4 s);
  - a door open or close, a bar, a key: instant, and the walk goes on (TP6 b: once 1 to 4 s and you stopped at the door); pick a lock 30 s (TP5; locks, careful, kept when broken);
  - bandage 20 s (TP5; locks, careful, lost when broken, the bandage not used up); drink a potion 3 s (lost); hide 2 s (locks); look around 3 s (steady);
  - rest 60 min (locks, deep, share), sleep the night (careful, stop never, steps), wait until the time named (deep), trade 3 s (TP5: once 10 min; steady, lost);
  - armour on and off: instant at base effort, every weight (less realism, Ali 14/11/2026; once light 2 min, medium 1 min, heavy 10 min with help and twice that alone);
  - perk moves name their own time (the speech perks take talking time, Dirt in the Eyes a throw's 3 s).
- **Time factor (AT22).** An action's seconds for an actor = the row's seconds × that actor's time factor (1 for everyone for now). The backbone for D-248: a row may name a speed function whose effective skill halves its time every N points above 50 and slows it the same way below (N a lever on the row). Picking a lock is the first row on it: its speed function is Lockpicking with N = PICK\_HALVING\_PER (25), the BA2 rule it always had. Which rows name one, and what else feeds the factor, waits for the speed skills (D-248).
- **Healing draught (AT10).** It gets a healing row: 25% of health and parts up to Damaged, any time, 3 s (placeholder).

**Spending time (stage 2)**
- **Movement locks (AT19).** Each action locks movement or not; a locking action takes the same seconds from move time as from main time. Shoot and Move (B4, banked) becomes "your shots don't lock movement".
- **Leftover (AT20, TP4 b).** The time left after the actor's last finished action carries, each pool on its own, for CONFIG.CARRY\_TURNS turns (2): carried time is spent oldest first and what is left of it carries on until it is too old; each pool holds at most CARRY\_MAX\_TURNS fight turns of it (2, so 12 s; the oldest goes first). Held time and a turn where nothing finished carry nothing new (a unit test checks the cap). **Time back (TP3 a):** an action that could not happen (all the actor did with it was a wasted event because its target or thing was gone, dead, down or out of reach, or no room: resolve.ts STALE\_REASONS) spends none of its time: its main time, the move time it locked and its clock come back, and the time carries. A miss, a failed roll, a refusal, a yield or a truce keeps its cost; the walk before it stays walked. For an NPC the TP2 swap uses the time this turn.
- **Fast fighters (AT21).** Time decides how many blows fit; AE4 goes. Flurry (AT79): every unarmed strike its owner throws brings one free strike at the same target at once, costing no time and no action, one tier less sure; the free strike can't trigger Flurry again but triggers every other perk a strike does, and a free punch from another perk (One-Two) does trigger Flurry. Flurry and One-Two name Grip parts, not hands.
- **Order inside a turn (AT48).** Actions resolve in the order they finish; the fight's initiative roll breaks ties.
- **Walks carry on (AT49).** A walk not yet at its goal carries on next turn by itself until the actor orders something else; a dash runs for the turn's seconds. Built for the player (stage 3d-1): Actor.walking keeps the move; a turn whose intent has no move and no action (or only words) carries it on (resolve.ts carryOnWalk); any other action or a new move ends it; the Parser is told (rule 24), and a stop where they stand is a move to their own zone. Outside a fight a walk runs on the world clock to its goal (AT45/AT46, below); in a fight it walks the turn's move time and carries on. A way found shut ends it (BG-69). NPCs' walks carrying on wait (they get an order each turn). **Following** (BG-190): a move with spot near someone and follow set (the Parser sets it for "follow him", "tail her") keeps after them: reaching them does not end it, and it carries on toward where they are now while the player can see or hear them (resolve.ts keepFollowing); a sneak with no move sneaks along a walk (reading choice: sneaking is a way of moving). Lost, it ends and the player is told why once, the turn after (Actor.followLost; logged follow\_lost): seen going down, slipped from sight in the zone the player had them in, or gone on out of sight. The Parser reads input that only carries on ("continue", "keep following") as a turn with no move and no action (rule 24). **On screen:** while following, the action box shows the following line in place of the walk line: "Following Bren · in the Upstairs landing · 24 m · 17 s to go" (or "close behind" with no walk left), with Keep following ("I keep following Bren") and Stop here. The turn they are lost it reads "Lost Bren · they slipped out of sight in the Upstairs landing" (went down in / went on out of sight from), in the warning colour, with Look for them ("I look around for Bren"). The chips only fill the box (src/ui/doing.ts followingNow). The fake page's "follow" and "lose" show both.
- **NPCs (AT16, AT47).** Same times and rules. An NPC's Planner or code is asked for a new order only when its current action ends or the clock pauses for it (AT67); mid-action it carries on. Built: an NPC at a long action is asked again once its own coming-near check fires (stage 3d-3, below).

**Built so far (stage 2)**
- **Pools.** Each actor's turn is seconds of main time and of move time, each the turn's length plus what carried (src/engine/resolve.ts slotsOf). A fight turn lasts FIGHT\_TURN\_S for an attack, a hold or a move, else the player's order's seconds within FIGHT\_TURN\_MIN\_S to FIGHT\_TURN\_S; outside a fight (stage 3d-2, AT46) a turn lasts as long as the player's order, at most EXPLORE\_TURN\_S (120 s): its walk, its way and gear ops and its action's seconds (resolve.ts exploreSeconds; a look around is 3 s); an action with no time of its own (a hold, an untimed action), or a turn with nothing timed in it, lasts the default action row (60 s). Haggling (barter) is words, so its turn is capped. Longer actions, walks, rests and waits run on the world clock instead (AT45).
- **Costs (orderCost).** A blow, throw or shot takes its row (bows and crossbows name theirs on the item: shotTime, and a crossbow's spanTime before each shot, under the spanning row); a bandage, a drink, a hide, a look around, a trade and words that use a skill at 0.4 s a word take theirs (plain talk in a fight takes none, TP6 c); gear ops (draw, stow, swap, pick-up, taking out of a sack, a hand-over), armour on or off, doors, bars and keys are instant and cost no strain (less realism, Ali 14/11/2026, TP6 a and b: their rows are 0 s at base effort). An order with no row takes all the main time left; one with a row that ends this turn takes what is left of it.
- **Locks.** A shot or throw that reaches from here takes its seconds from the move time first; the move walks on what is left (a medium bow's 6 s leaves nothing: move\_dropped, ranged\_attack).
- **Walking.** A walk pays its seconds from the move time, then from the main time when no action is planned (a dash); metres not walked go back as seconds.
- **Order and repeats.** Pass one runs everyone's order in initiative order; then attack orders repeat in the order the next one finishes (initiative breaks ties) while main time lasts and the turn has not ended for the actor (a walk first pushes its clock on). Before each, the attacker follows its target on the move time left; it stops once the target is down, yields, breaks, is out of reach after following, or the attack came to nothing. Words said in the exchange are that speaker's main action for the turn.
- **Per round.** Defences (outnumbered after the first), Counter and a parry's strike-back count per round (the round in which the action finishes).
- **Long actions.** In a fight, an action longer than a whole turn's time (picking a lock; a row over 6 s) is a long action (src/engine/resolve.ts longAction): it takes all the time left each turn, the actor is busy (Actor.busy: row, seconds done of need) and an event of kind busy says so; it takes effect in the turn its seconds are done. The player carries it on by giving the same order again (same row; the same item and target, except a crossbow's spanning, which goes on whoever it is aimed at); any other order sets it aside. An NPC at one is not asked for a new order (plannerActors skips it, AT47) and carries it on, unless a trigger fires for it. An attack aimed at someone at a long action breaks it by the row's focus (careful and deep: any attack; steady: a hit that wounds; breakFocus); broken off or set aside, its work is kept when the row's onBreak keeps it at that progress (spanning, armour, picking), else lost (a bandage: lost, and not used up). Outside a fight (stage 3d-2, AT45) the player's action longer than EXPLORE\_TURN\_S (a rest) runs on the world clock at the end of the turn (a stretch of kind busy, posed standing) and takes effect when its time is done; one that would be refused (no coin, nothing they'd buy, armour that won't go on) is refused at once instead (resolve.ts wouldGo; buy, sell and equipArmor have a dry run). Stopped on the way, it stays busy with its seconds done and goes on when the player gives the same order again, unless its focus is deep (AT65: broken, by its onBreak); the mechanics line names it by its row ("Put on heavy armour stopped after 2 min: someone is coming at you"). The one the player is at it with (the seller at a trade) talking to them is part of it, not contact. An NPC's long action outside a fight still takes effect at once. Not yet: share, misfire and fires (no long fight action uses them); words stay said all at once in the turn they are ordered (a long line takes the turn). A rest in a fight is a long action too (deep; broken off, it heals by the share done).
- **Reaction and hesitation (AT58, AT60).** In a fight, an actor's initiative is rolled once, when its fight starts (a fight turn after a turn without one): its reaction time is ruleset/actionTime.ts reactSeconds, the Action-times equation with REACT\_BASE\_S (2 s) at a total of 50, halving every REACT\_HALVING (50) points (Actor.react). Its first fight turn starts that many seconds in (main time and walking alike); the roll orders it for the rest of the fight. Turn order: the unaware last (as before), then whoever starts first, then the roll. A believed Bluff makes the foe hesitate: its next turn starts after its reaction time again, and the mark is spent. Outside a fight the roll is fresh each turn, as before. Never Surprised is unchanged (hearing the attack coming, the owner is not caught unaware).
- **Flurry (AT79).** Built in the engine (src/engine/resolve.ts doAttack): every unarmed strike its owner throws with two Grip parts working and holding nothing, hit or miss, brings one free strike at the same target at once (no time, no action) with tier shift −1; its own free strike never sets it off again, and it triggers every other perk a strike does; One-Two's free punch sets it off. The perk is still banked (Unarmed Strike 100), so no one has it yet. One-Two's condition now names a Grip part holding nothing.
- **Rounds for perks (AT74).** Gut Feeling rolls against each hidden watcher at most once every HUNCH\_EVERY\_ROUNDS (20) rounds of game time. Timed perk marks (Leg Bolt's slow, Make Them Stumble, Dirt in the Eyes, Bluff) last PERK\_MARK\_ROUNDS (2) rounds of game time after the turn they were made (PerkMark.made).
- **Leftover.** A fight turn's finished action leaves its unused seconds (main, and move once the walk arrived) to the next turn, minus any carried seconds it did not use; holds, idles, waits, sleeps and rests carry nothing. A pool so never holds more than twice a turn (tests/cases/s269).

**Long actions (stage 2)**
- **Over several turns (AT2, AT17).** A long action takes effect when its seconds are done; until then the actor is busy and it carries on by itself each turn; ordering anything else stops it (if its stop allows).
- **Focus (AT25, AT57):** what breaks it from outside, an ordered scale FOCUS: reflex (nothing; too quick), steady (a hit that wounds), careful (any attack aimed at the actor, hit or miss), deep (also the coming-near check, AT67). Sleep is careful; readying a shot or a grand spell is steady.
- **Stop (AT26, AT57):** whether the actor may choose to stop: any (default), never (sleep), or until a share of progress (a grand spell: free to stop before 60%, committed after). Only the actor loses the choice; its focus's breaks still break it.
- **Output (AT27):** end (the whole effect when done), share (in proportion), steps (points along the way).
- **When broken off (AT6, AT28):** bands by progress, each lost (nothing happens, nothing used up), kept (the work counts later), share, misfire (names a row of a Misfires table, empty until spells exist) or fires (takes effect as if finished). Example: a fire spell 0 to 60% lost, 60 to 90% misfire (explodes in the hand), 90 to 100% fires. In a fight today only lost and kept occur: share, misfire and fires wait for rows that use them there (rest, talk and spells are never long in a fight; CS5).
- **Defending while busy (AT7).** Defences as normal, unless the action carries a tag that blocks or penalises defence; every defence roll gets the same penalties.
- **No gates (AT8, BG-107; built in stage 3b).** Rest, wait, sleep and heal items can always be started; their time and what breaks them are the cost. The healing rows' inFight flag is gone. Rest keeps its REST\_COOLDOWN\_MIN (the Part Two page kept it). In a fight the clock is already paused, so a wait or a sleep ordered there does nothing ("not while a fight is going on around you").

**Initiative (AT58, AT60; stage 2)**
- A reaction time, rolled once when a fight starts for the actor: CONFIG.REACT\_BASE\_S (2 s) at a total of 50, halving every REACT\_HALVING (50) points, times the time factor; the same roll breaks ties for the rest of the fight. Someone unaware starts only once they notice.
- "Acts last" (Bluff, Staggering Bolt) becomes hesitating: the target's next action starts only after its own reaction delay again. Never Surprised: when the owner hears the attacker coming, its reaction starts when the attack starts, and it defends as aware.

**Per-round limits (AT50, AT51, AT74; stage 2)**
- Counted per round, ROUND\_S the lever: every defence after the first within a round takes the outnumbered modifier; Counter and a parry's strike-back once a round. Gut Feeling rolls once every HUNCH\_EVERY\_ROUNDS (20). Perk marks that lasted "the next turn" (Leg Bolt's slow, Make Them Stumble, Dirt in the Eyes) last 2 rounds.

**The world clock (AT56, AT65 to AT71, AT61, AT63, AT66; stage 3)**
- One loop for every span of time an order covers (a turn, a walk, a rest, a trip, a night). Inside it the world steps: one round while a fight is going on anywhere; elsewhere everyone catches up every WORLD\_STEP\_S (60 s); actors near the player, fighting or hunting are stepped one by one, the rest jump ahead as the night does.
- **The coming-near check (AT67, AT68).** For every actor, run on what it perceives now, people outside its band only, hostility never used. It fires at the first of: contact (an attack aimed at it, words to it, a touch, a noise at WAKE\_NOISE or louder); closing (someone whose visible path ends at it, or who closes faster than RUSH\_SHARE (1.5) × their kind's walk, once their time to reach it is WARN\_S or less); a change (someone already accepted starts closing, readies a weapon, or is seen sneaking). Time to reach = (distance − STRIKE\_M) ÷ closing speed; WARN\_S = READY\_S for the posture (lying 3, sitting 2, standing 1) + DECIDE\_S (3). Accepted: everyone perceived at the start and everyone listed at a stop the actor carries on past; they fire again only on contact or a change, and stop being accepted when out of the senses or more than REARM\_FACTOR (2) × their trigger distance away. Seeing a path needs the person made out clearly. Steps are at most half the shortest time anyone perceived needs to trigger, between STEP\_MIN\_S (3) and STEP\_MAX\_S (60); noticing is rolled per second.
- **Pause or break (AT65).** The clock pauses and gives the player the turn on the coming-near check, a fight the player can see or hear, and someone seen going down, yielding or running. The action is broken only if its focus says so; otherwise it carries on (AT17). Deep breaks on any stop and on anyone not accepted coming within STRIKE\_M. A companion's words to a resting player pause it but never break it; anyone else's break it (AT69). Sleeping alone gives no warning until the sleeper wakes (AT71). What stops the clock is one rule for everyone (AT70; a player setting is parked as D-246).
- **Nothing hidden (AT61).** Fight turns start only from what the player perceives (attacked, or seeing or hearing a fight or a foe closing in); threatHere goes everywhere; asleep, the player notices only what would wake them. Built so far: fight turns (src/engine/mode.ts fightTurns): an attack on the player this turn, a foe after them (relations.ts foesAfter, the same list isEngaged uses) that they can see or hear just being there, or a fight between others they can see or hear; a foe after them out of their senses keeps normal turns, even one that traded blows with them and went off (BG-189).
- **Agent NPCs in a stretch (AT63).** Their Planner order covers the first step; then they run on code until the clock stops, and their next call is told what happened meanwhile. Unique NPCs follow their goals on code, not routines.
- **Unseen fights (AT55).** A fight the player can't perceive runs on code rules every step, with no Planner calls.
- **Walks off the road (AT24)** are trips too; their encounter odds are parked (D-245).
- **Build notes (AT66).** Timers count elapsed time at every step; engaged or hunting NPCs follow their fallback orders on coarse steps; scenario endings, leaving the ground, free-roam starts and reach and find quest goals are checked at every step, and a scenario break stops the clock; what the player is told is one summary of time spent, distance, why it stopped and what they perceived, never anything else.
- **Built so far (stages 3b to 3d-2).** The player's rest and wait outside a fight run on the world clock at the end of the turn (src/engine/resolve.ts runStretch; the check is src/engine/comingNear.ts). Each step moves the clock on (natural recovery, aging) and everyone on their routines (catchUp), and lasts at most half the shortest time anyone perceived and not accepted would need to fire the check at their run speed, within STEP\_MIN\_S and STEP\_MAX\_S. It stops on contact (an attack aimed at the player, words to them they hear, a sound at WAKE\_NOISE or louder), on the closing and change checks above, or on a fight in the player's senses (mode.ts fightInSenses). The levers are CONFIG.READY\_S per body pose (BODY\_POSES: a rest is spent lying, a wait standing), DECIDE\_S, RUSH\_SHARE, REARM\_FACTOR; the strike distance is HOSTILE\_NEAR\_M (the page's STRIKE\_M, the same 2 m). Someone whose path ends at the player is someone tracking, hunting or engaging them. Reading choice: someone newly perceived who does not fire the check is listed (accepted) at once, so steps stay long and only a change fires them again. A rest is deep: any stop breaks it and it heals by the share that ran; the mechanics line says "Rest stopped after 12 min: someone is coming at you". Stage 3c: NPCs after someone (engaged or hunting, whom catchUp leaves alone) follow their fallback orders on code each step of a stretch and of the night (resolve.ts huntOnCode), with no Planner calls: seeing whom they are after tells them where that one is; a move or search walks there at a run (a turn's dash); an attack walks up and, in reach, strikes as in a turn (doAttack), a blow a round, the steps one round while such a fight is on. A foe that reaches the player strikes no blow in the stretch: it stops (contact) and the next turn is a fight; asleep, the night stops when the foe comes into the zone (no warning, AT71). After the first step an agent-run NPC's own order is spent and it runs on code too (AT63). Walks (stage 3d-1, AT45/AT46): outside a fight, what of a player's walk the turn's move time left is walked on the world clock at a walk, step by step, to its goal (resolve.ts walkStep), stopping like any stretch; stopped on the way it carries on (AT49). A road trip keeps its own rules (TV1), and a walk through an old map's zone with no rectangle walks the turn's move time only. PlayerView.player.walking says where to and the metres and seconds still to go (stage 4); PlayerView.player.following {who, name, lost?, zone?} says whom it keeps after, or, the turn after, that they were lost and why (BG-190). Stage 3d-2: turns outside a fight last as long as their order (AT46, Pools above) and the player's long actions run on the world clock (AT45, Long actions above). The night is one stretch (AT42): it stops on a newcomer hostile to the sleeper or a foe after them reaching them (huntOnCode, its blow comes next turn); still asleep after a turn, it runs on with the people already there accepted, and the game plays the sleeper's turns until they wake or a turn moves the clock on no further (ASLEEP\_AUTO\_TURNS is gone). Stage 3d-3: an NPC at a long action in a fight runs the coming-near check each turn on what it perceives (comingNear.ts busyCheck; its watch kept on Actor.busy.watch, started the turn it first runs, standing); when it fires (contact, closing, a change) busy.alert is set and logged (busy\_alert), so next turn its own order stands and plannerActors asks it, instead of carrying the long action on (AT47); the alert is spent then. An agent-run NPC whose order covered only the first step of a stretch or a night is told how long time ran on (Mind.meanwhile, shown as self.meanwhile.minutes on its next Planner call; the Planner instructions say what it means); what it perceived meanwhile is in its events (AT63). Reading choice: NPCs outside a fight never hold a long action (theirs take effect at once), so their check during a stretch is their code orders (huntOnCode, routines) and does not stop the player's clock. Not yet: the night still steps by SLEEP\_STEP\_MIN with its own arrival rule rather than the coming-near check.
- **Sight (AT81).** Someone sneaking or hidden is noticed only on a won detection roll (sight or hearing function against stealth); someone not hiding is seen within their visibility's reach with a clear line of sight, no roll; the sight skill doesn't stretch that reach.
- **Unseen blows and bodies (BF65).** A blow or shot that hurts or downs someone is felt by them and seen by anyone who sees them, as a `struck` event by the victim (the hit, the fall or the death; its effects), even when the attack itself reached no one because the striker was hidden or unknown: the striker stays hidden exactly as the stealth rolls say (src/engine/resolve.ts struckSeen). It raises the alarm in the victim and in those who saw it (updateAlarms), and the health, part and bandmate fears count it. Someone awake who first sees a body (down or dead) it did not see fall finds it, once per body (Mind.bodiesFound): a `found_body` event, alarm, and the bandmate fear when it is one of its own (findBodies, each turn's end). Anyone struck unseen, seeing such a blow, or finding a body is startled (Mind.startled): an agent-run one gets a Planner call to react even outside a fight (plannerActors). Someone on a sleep step who is awake shows the activity `lie_awake` ("lying awake"), never asleep.
- **Where a shot lands (BG-251, Ali BF73).** A shot or throw, hit or miss, makes an `impact` event by its target, at the target's spot: heard and seen from there, never showing where it came from. It is Loud (88 dB) on armour (the part hit is covered), a shield, or a weapon that parried it, or for a miss indoors (a wall, floor or furniture); Raised (70 dB) on flesh or clothing, or for a miss outdoors (earth, grass) (CONFIG.IMPACT\_NOISE; real references: chopping wood 66-71 dBA at 5 m and pounding tent stakes 66 dBA at 15.2 m, about 80-90 dB at 1 m, 3M Noise Navigator; soft-material impacts peak 20-25 dB below metal, Brüel, so 70 dB on flesh is an estimate). It reaches those the shot itself didn't (not the target when the hit was felt); it raises their alarm (ALARM\_KINDS, the target's own too) and startles them, so they get a Planner call. A shot or throw is felt by its target only when it meets them (blocked or past): a miss reaches them by its impact. Melee makes none: a blow already sounds beside its target.

**Timers (AT15, AT30 to AT43, AT72 to AT77, AT80, AT83, AT84; stage 1)**
- In game time: an engagement ends after ENGAGEMENT\_END = the longest attack or reload time in the Action times table + one round (33 s today), with no blow and no closing in (AT72); a chase is lost after CHASE\_LOST\_S (60 s) (AT31); belief decay is three levers, tracking to aware after DECAY\_TRACKING\_S (600 s; AT85: a hunter on a trail keeps at it longer), aware to suspicious after DECAY\_AWARE\_S (120 s) and suspicious to unaware after DECAY\_SUSPICIOUS\_S (900 s) with no new sight or sound, never while hunting or engaged, and only the alertness fades (AT73, AT80; an alarm stands down after DECAY\_AWARE\_S with no new alarm); an NPC stays where its own agent put it AGENT\_STAY\_S (180 s) (AT33); a guide waits LEAD\_WAIT\_S (90 s) (AT34); an essence offer waits ESSENCE\_OFFER\_ROUNDS (200 rounds, 10 min) or until a new scenario (AT75).
- Still in turns, since they pace the player's decisions: STALL\_TURNS (never counting a turn in a fight, AT76), CHECKIN\_GAP\_TURNS, ROAM\_MIN\_TURNS, Weaver beats (AT39 to AT41), and the bookkeeping counts (AT43). ASLEEP\_AUTO\_TURNS went with the world clock: a night runs as one stretch (AT42; stage 3d-2).
- **How timers count (built).** Every stamp stays a turn number; the game keeps the clock time each recent turn ended (TURN\_ENDS\_KEPT, 500) and a timer reads the game seconds since its stamp (time.ts secondsSince). Tracking fades on its own lever, DECAY\_TRACKING\_S (AT85).
- **Scenario budgets in game time (AT83, AT84).** Moment 12 min (1 place), situation 40 min (2 places), episode 2 h (4 places), journey 3 days (8 places, new), venture 7 days (any number of places). Fights count like any other time; a long action uses the budget by its real length. A place is one location the Weaver builds (a tavern, a farmstead, a stretch of road), made of zones.

**What the player sees (AT11a, AT49; stage 4)**
- The Play screen shows what each perceived character is doing: done, or the time left. An active move order shows with its goal and time left. Only what the player perceives; seconds and minutes only.
- **Built so far (engine side).** A long action under way shows in the views as `doing` {what (the Action times row's name), done, left (whole seconds)}: on each fully seen actor (SeenActor.doing, in the PlayerView and NPC views) and on the player (PlayerView.player.doing; src/engine/busy.ts doingOf). The Parser is told (rule 23) that the same action again carries it on. The active move order waits for walks that carry on (AT49, stage 3).
- **Built (screen side).** Who's here: each fully seen person at a long action gets a teal chip with a small meter of the share done and "span a crossbow · 6 s left". The action box: the player's own long action shows above the chips ("Bandage · 15 s done · 25 s to go", a meter, and a Keep going chip that fills the box with "I keep going: bandage"; nothing is sent until Send), since only the same order again carries it on. Each turn's results: an Actions row of the long actions the player saw (LogLine.actions, built from the PlayerView before and after the turn): still going with the time to go, ✓ done, broken off (busy_broken) or set aside (busy_set_aside); one whose actor is out of full sight after the turn is left out. Times read "40 s", "2 min", "1 min 30 s" (src/ui/doing.ts secsText); the meter is drawn once and never moves by itself.
- **Built (screen side, AT49).** While PlayerView.player.walking is there, the action box shows the walk under the long action, in the accent the map uses for you: "➜ Walking to village square · 24 m · 17 s to go", the note "Carries on next turn while you only talk; any other order stops it.", and two chips that fill the box: Keep walking ("I keep walking to village square") and Stop here ("I stop and wait here"); nothing is sent until Send. A goal with no open way reads "no open way there" (src/ui/doing.ts walkLeft). On the Map the goal zone gets a dashed accent outline and "Heading here", and its card says "Heading here · 24 m · 17 s to go". The fake page's "walk <zone>" sets a walk; "stop" ends it.

### Game state model

The save is the set of pages defined in Part 4. On load, the engine builds one in-memory GameState from those pages; it is never saved as one document. Everyone is in a zone and stands at a point in metres in it (step 2, below). Each zone belongs to one place, and the stage may hold zones from several places. Knowledge lives on the observer: the guard's belief about the player is `guard.mind.knowledge.player`, and it can be wrong.

```ts
type Perception = 'full' | 'partial';
type Cue = 'sight' | 'sound' | 'smell' | 'touch';

// In-memory working state, built from Part 4's pages on load
type GameState = {
  storyId: StoryId;
  turn: number;
  mode: 'explore' | 'dialogue' | 'tactical';
  zones: Zone[];                // the stage: zones loaded now, possibly across several places
  player: Actor;                // Actor is defined in Part 4 §9
  actors: Actor[];              // NPCs and enemies
  bands: Band[];
  hidden: HiddenStore;          // never enters the Parser's, Narrator's or Voice's input
  events: GameEvent[];          // this turn's events; saved in the turn record
  agentLog: AgentTurnLog[];     // this turn's agent inputs and outputs; saved in the turn record
  rngSeed: number;
  rngState: number;
  pending: { turn: number; mode: 'explore' | 'dialogue' | 'tactical'; engaged: string[]; fired: string[] };
                                // computed after the previous turn is saved
};

type Band = {                   // a small group: a guard post, a gang, a household, the player's party
  id: string; name: string; goals: string; temperament: string;
  tactics: 'simple' | 'competent' | 'cunning';   // TACTICS keys
  lethal: boolean;   // set when the band is made, default true; an existing band keeps it (Part 2, Agent Weaver)
  relations: Record<string, 'hostile' | 'opposed' | 'neutral' | 'friendly'>;  // default stance toward other bands
};

// What a zone is (Z1–Z5, Ali, 08/11/2026; rewritten for CS3, 12/11/2026): one open space, however big: a room, a yard, a
// hall, a square, a field (a road is one zone too, see Travel). Each open space is ONE zone with a size (Metres, below),
// never split to show distance. A barrier between two parts (wall, door, gate, counter, fence, steps) starts a new zone,
// so a link is a way between two spaces, not a measure of distance. People stand at real positions in metres inside a
// zone (src/engine/position.ts, Spots in a zone), and distance is measured between positions. Every Weaver zone names a
// zone kind from the library (zoneKinds: room, corridor, stairs, cellar, street, square, gate, yard, field, woods); the kind
// fixes indoor or outdoor and gives the default cover and light, which the plan may change. An unknown kind is a bad_zone.
// Height: a zone's height is metres above the ground outside (0 when unset). Stairs change height, never distance north,
// south, east or west. The Weaver may give it; when it doesn't, code infers it from the exits (src/scenario/heights.ts): a
// link whose words go up (up, upstairs, loft, roof...) climbs CONFIG.STOREY_HEIGHT (3 m, placeholder), one that goes down
// (downstairs, cellar, below...) drops it, any other keeps it; a bare "up" or "down" counts only beside a way to climb
// (stairs, steps, ladder, hatch...), so "down the street" stays level (BG-6); a story's first zones stand at 0. The Map page shows
// one height (floor) at a time, with a height slider and stair badges, and zones on other floors straight above or below.
// Drops (SD7): a link marked drop (a window or ledge over a lower zone) is seen through but never walked: paths and
// pursuit go round (shortestPath skips it unless it asks for sight only), and the Weaver never links back up it. The player
// takes it only by saying they jump (Parser move.jump, Order.jump): they land in the zone below and take a fall. The fall is
// flat damage by the drop in metres (the two zones' heights), from CONFIG.FALL_BANDS (over 2 m 15, over 5 m 40, over 9 m 80, and past the human range over 15 m 120, over 30 m 160, over 50 m 200, about terminal speed, after which it stays;
// placeholders, real-life injury heights: under about 2 m rarely hurts, a storey or two breaks bones, past about 9 m is often
// fatal for a person). Being flat, tough or supernatural bodies can power through it. It lands on a random leg through the
// normal damage step. A Dodge roll on landing moves it a band (CONFIG.FALL_LANDING_STEP: failures one band worse, Great or
// Critical Success one band better; each tier past Critical Success, CONFIG.FALL_LANDING_PAST_STEP, one more band better, so a supernatural dodger lands a tower jump unhurt; past Critical Failure reads Critical Failure's row). The move event carries fall {metres, damage, landing} and the Narrator tells the jump
// and landing from it. NPCs do not jump yet.
// Metres (CO1–CO19, Ali, 11/11/2026; step 1, no rule reads these yet): the range unit is the pace, CONFIG.PACE_M (1 m,
// about one stride; 1.5 m until 11/11/2026). Nothing is measured in zone links (ZR8: LINK_PACES and its conversion are gone). A zone's area is m²: the Weaver
// gives a size word (ZONE_SIZES cramped 9, small 25, room 60, hall 225, yard 400, open 1,600, vast 10,000; CONFIG.ZONE_SIZE_M2,
// placeholders) or area in m², else the kind's default size. Each library zoneSizes row names a real or fantasy reference.
// Each side (n, e, s, w by map compass) has an edge kind from the library's edgeKinds table (EK1 wall, EK2 open, EK3 low:
// counter or fence, EK4 window; a new kind is a new row with walk/see/reach/climb flags); indoor kinds default to wall,
// outdoor kinds to open, and the Weaver gives edges only for a side that differs. An unknown size or edge kind is a bad_zone.
// Placement (CO1 B, src/scenario/geometry.ts placeZones): code, never Claude, gives each new zone a rectangle (Zone.rect, metres:
// centre east/north, w east-west, d north-south) of its area at its kind's aspect (corridor and street 4:1, stairs and gate
// 2:1, else square), touching the zone it links to on the side its exit's compass word names (else the first free side),
// lined up with that zone's centre or either end, sliding outward only when all are taken. Zones up or down stairs sit
// straight above or below. doorway() is the middle of a shared wall (CO3). The Map's "Draw to scale" button draws the
// rectangles at 12 px a metre, each side by its edge kind, with a 10 m bar in metres and paces.
// Layouts (CO7, CO17; src/library/layouts.ts, typed ids LY): a common building's zones, sizes, sides and exits, prebuilt.
// A plan place may name one ("layout": LY id from catalogs.layouts, optional "names"/"looks" by the layout's zone keys);
// code adds its zones as placeKey.zoneKey (not counted against the zone cap) and places them like any others. The Weaver
// links its own zones to placeKey.<entry>. An unknown layout is a bad_place. LY1 village tavern (common room with a counter
// on its north side, behind the counter, kitchen, back yard, upstairs landing, cellar), LY2 cottage, LY3 shop, LY4 smithy
// (open to the street on its south side), LY5 gatehouse. A building type may have several; a new layout is a new row.
// Positions (step 2, ZP1–ZP6, src/engine/position.ts): everyone stands at a point (Actor.pos, metres in the rectangles'
// frame). Code places them (placeAll, at scenario setup and each turn's start): anyone whose point is missing or outside its
// zone gets the zone's centre, else the nearest free point on a CONFIG.PLACE_SPACING_M (1 m, arm's length) grid around it.
// Distance (distM): a straight line when there is sight between the zones (the same zone, or a path of sight links;
// sightDistM, which attacks use: a blow or shot never goes round a wall), else the walking path through the doorways,
// adding any climb. A move walks one leg at a time (walkLeg): to the next zone's doorway, then on; a move to a zone ends
// CONFIG.ENTER_M (1 m) past its doorway. Every zone has a rectangle, the test village's too (ZR8); measuring in a zone
// without one throws, as it is a world-building bug. The narration and views carry metres (a move's `metres`, a seen
// actor's distanceM). PR 2 moves sight and sound to metres; PR 3 the hostile-within-2 m shot rule and the aura. Step 3
// is travel along a road that is one long zone (CO18, its own deep dive).
// Roads (TV3, TV4): a road between places is ONE zone of the road kind (ZK11), however long. The Weaver gives it a length
// (a ROAD_LENGTHS word: a short walk 0.5 km, half an hour 2, an hour's walk 4.5, half a day 15, a day's walk 30, two days
// 60, CONFIG.ROAD_LENGTH_KM; or km) and a danger (ROAD_DANGERS: safe, quiet, wild, dangerous; the chance per hour that
// something meets you is CONFIG.ROAD_ENCOUNTER_PCT 2/4/8/15 %, placeholders). Code makes its rectangle that long and
// ROAD_WIDTH_M (4 m) wide. Left out: an hour's walk, quiet.
type Zone = { id: string; placeId: PlaceId; name: string; look: string;   // look: public text
              links: ZoneLink[]; cover: 'none' | 'some' | 'heavy'; light: 'bright' | 'dim' | 'dark';      // zone ids unique within a story
              height?: number;                                           // metres above the ground outside
              rect?: Rect;                                               // set by code (placeZones): metres on the map
              area?: number; edges?: Partial<Record<'n' | 'e' | 's' | 'w', string>>; // CO16 m², CO15 edge kind ids by side
              danger?: 'safe' | 'quiet' | 'wild' | 'dangerous';          // TV4 roads only (ROAD_DANGERS)
              groundItems: ItemInstance[] };                             // Part 5 §4
type ZoneLink = { to: string; label: string; sight: boolean; frontier?: true; drop?: true };
                                                   // label: how the linked zone reads from here, e.g. 'left of the gate'
                                                   // sight: true = actors here can see into `to` [Proposed]
                                                   // frontier: `to` names an area not yet built
                                                   // drop: a way down only by jumping (a window, a ledge); see Drops

type Belief = {
  status: 'unaware' | 'suspicious' | 'aware' | 'tracking' | 'hunting' | 'engaged';   // AWARENESS keys (src/scales.ts)
  lastKnownZone?: string;
  lastSeenTurn?: number;
  source: 'seen' | 'heard' | 'told';
  closedTurn?: number;          // YD6: last turn either side attacked or closed in, which keeps hunting and engaged alive
  revealed: string[];           // facts learned about the other actor, e.g. 'has_crossbow'
};

type Fact = { id: string; text: string };

type GameEvent = {
  id: string; turn: number; actorId: string;
  kind: string;                 // 'attack', 'move', 'vanish', 'speak', 'call_help', 'alarm', 'arrival', 'presence', ...
  zone: string; target?: string;
  tier?: Tier;                  // absent for actions with no check, such as a plain move or speech
  effects: Effect[];            // structured; text is built per view, never stored
  noise: NoiseKey; visibility: VisibilityKey;   // NOISE / VISIBILITY keys (src/scales.ts); hearing: perception/noise.ts hearingOf (CONFIG.NOISE_DB, real metres, losses, background), CONFIG.SIGHT_M metres from `pos`
  pos?: { x: number; y: number };               // where it happened (the actor's point, step 2); absent = the zone's centre
  perception: Record<string, { level: Perception; cue: Cue }>;   // absent = not perceived
  bark?: boolean;               // words beside a deed: copy no belief, but are real speech (BF67): listeners who hear them get the words, and the speaker's said list has them
};

type Effect = { kind: string; actorId: string; data: Record<string, unknown> };
```

The actor fields Part 2 relies on live in Part 4's sheet: control, morale, knowledge, knows, overrides, routine, routines and alarm in Mind; bandId, posture and label on the Actor itself; secretsKnown and namesKnown in Journal. hasAgenda is derived, never stored: an actor has an agenda when it holds a secret, has a plan or has a memory note. It is computed when a dialogue call is routed, from turn-start state.

Routines, routine sets, activities and durations are library content, not saved state **\[Proposed\]**. The routine bank is the only source of routines. Claude drafts entries offline from a brief the designer sets; the designer edits and approves them; the import validator checks them; only the designer promotes them to the library. Agent Weaver picks one routine set per actor by id and binds its slots to zones; it never writes routines. An actor's saved state holds its set's routine ids, its slot bindings, and its current routine with its anchor (Part 4 §9).

```ts
type RoutineSet = {                       // the bank entry Agent Weaver picks
  id: string;
  brief: string;                          // one line for the Weaver
  tags: { place: string[]; role: string[]; time: ('day' | 'night' | 'any')[]; event: string[] };
  slots: Record<string, { kind: 'zone' | 'route'; hint: string }>;   // hint: one line for the Weaver
  routines: string[];                     // [default, ...others]; becomes Mind.routines
};
type Routine = {
  id: string;
  brief: string;                          // one line for the Planner, Responder and Voice
  kind: 'schedule' | 'follow' | 'wait';
  loop: boolean; steps: RoutineStep[];    // schedule only
  activity?: string;                      // follow and wait only
  alert?: string;                         // this routine's alert variant; listed in the same set
  onAlert: OrderTemplate | null;          // one order, fired once on alarm
};
type RoutineStep = { slot: string; duration: DurationId; activity: string };
                                          // a route slot expands to one stop per bound zone
type DurationId = 'brief' | 'short' | 'medium' | 'long';   // game minutes per id: DURATIONS (config, placeholders 5 / 30 / 120 / 360; NW10)
type ActivityDef = {
  id: string;
  look: string;                           // public text, e.g. 'dozing on a stool'
  attention: 'alert' | 'normal' | 'distracted' | 'asleep';   // a situational tag; the ruleset owns its value
  noise: NoiseKey; visibility: VisibilityKey;
};
// Tag values come from the library tag catalog. Slot names: see Slots, below.
```

A routine is a desired-state function: from the turn number and the actor's own ActorView it gives a desired zone and activity.

- **Schedule** (NW10, on the clock). A routine's steps may differ by time of day: `times` is a list of entries, each naming one or more times of day (`at: ['evening', 'night']`) and the steps for them, so one entry can cover several times (NW13); no time of day is named twice. `steps` serves every time no entry names (the barkeep tends the bar from morning to evening and sleeps at night). Code takes the steps for the time of day now and expands them: a step that reads a route slot becomes one stop per zone bound to that slot, in order, each with the step's duration and activity. The desired step is found from the game minutes since the routine started (its `at`) through the expanded steps' lengths in minutes (DURATIONS), looping if loop is true, else holding the last step. A loop starts at a random point along it; a startAt step starts there. The desired zone is the actor's binding for the step's slot. A routine runs at the same speed however long a turn is.
- **Routines through the day** (RT1–RT9, NW17). Every routine set says what its people do at each time of day, and none is for one half of the day only. Gate and patrol guards are on duty from dawn to dusk, drink in the evening and sleep at night; night gate and night patrol guards are the mirror (on duty from dusk to dawn, asleep in the morning and afternoon). The barkeep tends the bar from morning to evening and sleeps at night and dawn (the tavern closes when the night starts). A patron sleeps at home from night to morning, is out in the afternoon and drinks from dusk. A guest sleeps at night and dawn, eats in the morning, goes out in the afternoon and drinks from dusk. A street worker walks to work at dawn, works through the day, packs up at dusk, rests at home in the evening and sleeps at night. A thief sleeps at its refuge at dawn and in the morning and prowls from the afternoon on. A field slime settles for the evening and the night. A companion follows at all hours. Beds, seats and ways out (bed, seat, exit) are optional slots (`optional` on the set's slot): the Weaver may leave them unbound, and the actor then stays where it is at the times that read them. A bed is never left blank (BF68): placed with a set that has a bed slot and none given, an NPC gets a real bed within BED_NEAR_HOPS links of its own spots, else its refuge, else its work spot (`withBed` in src/scenario/apply.ts). One still without a bed in play sleeps where it stands when its routine says sleep, and the gap is filed once as a bug (bed_missing).
- **Time moves everyone** (NW11). At the end of every turn, each NPC not in a fight, held, broken or surrendered, and not moved by its own agent or a trigger this turn (BG-151), goes toward where its routine puts it at the new time, as far as it could walk in the time the turn took at the travel speed (travelSpeed, the road travel rule), less what its own move already walked. It is one jump, with no Claude calls; it stops at a way that is not simply open and at an unbuilt frontier, and a road is walked by its length. A way it can open itself (its key turns the lock, or it is on the bolt's side) it opens and walks through when the player is in neither zone, and leaves it as it was where it can: the key locks it again from either side, but a bar slid back from its own side can't be drawn again from the far side, so it stays open (CS11, Ali 13/11/2026). Those travelling with the player spent the turn on the road. On a turn that took one action's time, each leg of the catch-up is a move event like any walk, perceived by the normal rules, so whoever sees or hears it (the player or an NPC) sees them go, and where to while in sight; a leg nobody perceives stays silent (BF2a-d). Every jump is logged caught_up. On a longer turn (a trade, a rest, a wait), every NPC's move, its own and the catch-up's, is perceived by the same rules and summed up for the Narrator as cameAndWent: one line per person the player noticed, legs in order, at most LONG_TURN_LINES lines and the rest "others came and went"; asleep, the player notices none (BF2e1 a, BF2e2 a, BF2e3 a, BF2e4 c). A doorway right underfoot is crossed in 0 m and is still a leg (BG-151). An NPC its own agent moved somewhere stays there for AGENT_STAY_S (config, placeholder 180 game seconds) before its routine or the catch-up moves it, unless its agent decides again (BF11). A head_to walk that gets there goes back to its routine. Healing already counts minutes. After an 8-hour sleep, everyone is where their day has taken them. A sleep is stepped (see Sleeping and keeping watch), so it stops when someone hostile arrives.
- **Follow.** The zone where the actor believes the player is (its lastKnownZone, never the truth), with the routine's activity.
- **Wait.** The zone bound to the actor's here slot, with the routine's activity. Code writes here when the actor switches to a wait routine.

Nothing is resumed: when control returns, the actor reads its desired state for the current turn.

**Slots \[Proposed\].** Slot names belong to the actor, not to a routine: one binding per name, shared by every routine in the actor's routines list. A zone slot holds one zone id; a route slot holds 1 to MAX\_ROUTE\_ZONES (config, placeholder) zone ids; the here slot is written only by code. Shared routines, used by more than one set, read only the standard slots post, route, refuge, exit and here. A set may add its own slot names for its own routines.

**Anchors \[Proposed\].** Code sets every anchorTurn; no agent sets one.

```
At install, with the Weaver's startAt:
  anchorTurn = currentTurn − (turns of the expanded steps before the first step that reads the startAt slot)
At install, without startAt:
  looping schedule: anchorTurn = currentTurn − rng.int(0, loopLength − 1)   // seeded; replay is exact
  otherwise:        anchorTurn = currentTurn
On a switch to another routine: anchorTurn = currentTurn
On a calm return:               the anchor saved in returnTo (Explore mode: Alarm)
```

**Companions \[Proposed\].** Every player-band member uses the library set companion: follow (the default) and wait, each with an alert variant. All four have onAlert null, so an alert never makes a companion shout during stealth play. A player-band cast entry names this set and binds no slots.

Tier comes from the ruleset. Health and body parts are Part 3's health track and per-body-type parts (SavedCondition, Part 4).

```ts
type Tier = 'critical_failure' | 'failure' | 'near_miss' | 'success' | 'great_success' | 'critical_success';   // keys of TIERS (src/scales.ts), worst first; names only via tierName()
```

Six views are built from state:

| View | Sent to | Contains | Never contains |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| PlayerView | Agent Parser | Player sheet, current zone, zones and actors the player perceives | Hidden store, NPC beliefs, unperceived actors |
| ActorView | Code minds: routines, combat lists | The same fields as OppositionView, as structured data | The same as OppositionView |
| OppositionView | Agent Planner and Agent Responder, one per actor | That actor's condition and position, its beliefs, events it perceived (including the last few exchanges it heard), what it was told, its own last few spoken lines, passing remarks beside a deed too (SAID_LINES, so it does not repeat itself; BF67), its plan and memory note, its own secrets, its band's goals and relations; the brief of each routine in its routines list | This turn's player input (a Responder gets only the dialogue input), other actors' private knowledge, the player's true zone unless perceived, the player sheet beyond revealed, place facts and headlines, barks |
| DialogueView | Agent Voice | ActorView minus plan, memory list and agreements, plus its knows facts and the dialogue input | Hidden store, other actors' knowledge, place facts and headlines, barks |
| NarrationView | Agent Narrator | This turn's events the player perceived, with level and cue; player condition; visible surroundings; NPC lines the player heard; public stage text only; a label for each actor whose name the player does not know, and where each seen actor stands (zone, side, distance), half-seen ones too; the activity look of each actor in a presence event | Hidden store, NPC plans and beliefs, unperceived events, any number from a roll, damage, a pool or HP (code shows them in the mechanics panel); the tier, grades and hidden stage-plan fields; any tone hint from the tier |
| WeaverView | Agent Weaver | Tier and stakes group; the place or frontier being planned and its ancestors' headlines; the stage's zones; actors on the stage with their plans and secrets; quests, flags, band goals; player level, current class, health-track level; the previous Weaver note; catalog ids, including routine sets with their brief, tags and slot hints | Skill growth, Burden, Potential; this turn's player text |

OppositionView is ActorView plus prompt formatting. It serves allies and companions as well as enemies; the name is kept for continuity. Place facts and headlines (Part 4 §5) go only to PlayerView, NarrationView and WeaverView. NPCs learn only through perception, communication events and their knows list. Build every view deny-by-default: copy whitelisted fields into a new object. Never copy full state and delete fields from it, because a field added later would leak silently.

Gear in views follows Part 5 §8: each actor sees its own inventory by name, kind, slot and mode ids; on actors it perceives fully it sees held items, shield and armor by public look, accessories only when tagged visible, concealed items only once revealed, and never stowed items; it sees ground items in perceived zones. No view holds a gear number.

### Information model

Each actor knows only what it perceived itself, plus what reached it through a communication event the engine resolved. There is no band-wide knowledge. A bark is not a communication event: it copies nothing.

**Communication events.** Speech (whisper, normal, shout), call\_help, alarm and a face-to-face report are ordinary events. They are perceived by volume and zone links like any other event. A whisper to someone it does not reach, even after stepping up, is said low instead: the quietest sound up to normal speech that the listener hears clearly (BF3, logged whisper_raised). A dialogue call's line (Responder, Voice) is for the player whatever action carries it, and one order's words are said once a turn (BG-132). When an actor perceives one, code copies the sender's belief to the listener:

- source becomes 'told'.
- status is capped at 'suspicious'; being told never makes an actor 'aware' or 'tracking'.
- lastKnownZone and lastSeenTurn are the sender's values at the moment of sending, so passed-on information is dated and can be stale.
- No model is involved in passing information on.

**Belief decay.** Code lowers a belief one step when lastSeenTurn is older than a config value: tracking → aware → suspicious → unaware. The step lengths are engine config (DECAY\_TURNS), not ruleset numbers. Without decay, actors never calm down and the game never leaves Tactical.

**Hunting and engaged** (YD6 B, Ali, 11/11/2026; BG-4, BG-5). Two awareness states sit above tracking. **Engaged**: the two traded blows. A real (not sparring) blow engages the attacker with its target, and the target with the attacker once it knows who struck (at least aware; a shot from hiding leaves it suspicious). The target's bandmates who perceived the blow and know who struck become engaged with the attacker too. **Hunting**: a hostile that knows where someone is (aware or better) and moves closer to them (into a zone nearer them, or a step inside one zone that closes in on them), before any blow. Both stay up while either of the two keeps attacking or closing in on the other (Belief.closedTurn). They drop to tracking after CONFIG.ENGAGEMENT\_END\_TURNS (3) turns in which neither did, and ordinary decay takes over from there (src/engine/engagement.ts noteFighting, run each turn after the blows). Seeing someone never lowers hunting or engaged. **Being in a fight** means hostile and engaged, either way round (fightingWith). The player holds no beliefs, so a fight with the player is read from the NPC's belief about the player. A fight engagement counts on the turns either side attacks or closes in, so the quiet turns that drop engaged also end the engagement: a fight you walk away from ends ENGAGEMENT\_END\_TURNS turns later, even if the foe stays hostile and aware of you (BG-5). Tactical mode still starts as soon as a hostile is at least suspicious of someone (isEngaged), as before.

**Bandmates together, the band apart** (YD7, Ali, 11/11/2026). A band's members who each perceive the other right now (in sight, or close enough to hear a faint sound) are together. Members apart act as the band's faction: same friends and enemies, but a deal one of them makes on the spot binds only those together with it. Band membership (bandId) is permanent; only code changes it. Affiliations and larger groups are parked (D-196).

**Stance.** stance(a, b) = a's override for b if it has one, else the relation of a's band to b's band; same band is friendly.
