The stance scale, coldest first: hostile, opposed, neutral, friendly (Ali, 07/11/2026). **Hostile** attacks on sight: it engages, attacks, chases, blocks rest and counts for the crowded-zone modifier. **Opposed** is at odds but never strikes first: an opposed actor is wary, so its onAlert fires when it notices the other, but it does not engage, attack or chase. Code sets a's override to hostile toward an attacker when a perceives an attack on itself or a bandmate. The Planner, Responder or Voice may request a one-step change of its override toward an actor whose speech it perceived since its last call; the engine applies it (friendlier or colder, one step on the stance scale).

### Who thinks for each actor

Every actor gets exactly one order per turn, from the first controller in this order that has one: fired trigger > broken (Part 3, Morale) > agent (Planner, Responder or Voice) > onAlert > routine (desired state) for an actor not engaged, or its type's combat list for an engaged one (Fallback behavior). An inactive (incapacitated) actor gets none. Significance (control: agent or code) only decides who thinks when the actor is engaged.

| Actor this turn | Controlled by | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Inactive (incapacitated) | Nothing | No order, trigger, routine or reply; a revived actor is active again next turn |
| A trigger it owns fired | The trigger's order | Overrides everything below; when several fire, the first in plan order wins |
| Addressed spokesperson | Its dialogue call: Responder if it has an agenda, else Voice | Its Planner or combat-list order is discarded this turn |
| Significant and engaged | One Planner call | Engaged = active, and believes (suspicious or higher) in an active actor it is hostile to |
| Significant and in a fight, between turning points (NV1 b) | Its type's combat list (Fallback behavior) | A Planner call comes only on its first fight turn and at a turning point: someone on either side goes down, breaks or yields, it is asked to yield or offered terms, or a newcomer joins (src/agents/game.ts fightKey). Schemers and goal holders, deal partners, errand runners and the startled keep a call every turn (plansEveryTurn); schemer = holds a secret, a unique, or the sneak role \[Proposed\] |
| Minor and engaged | Its type's combat list (Fallback behavior) | \[Proposed\] |
| Over the call cap | Its type's combat list | \[Proposed\] |
| First leaving unaware of a wary actor (opposed or hostile toward it), or first perceiving an attack or alarm. Knowing one from a scenario's opening is not leaving unaware (BF1): the cast starts aware of everyone it can see except those set up hiding, armed with no alarm | Its routine's onAlert order, once, on the next turn only: a higher order that turn (trigger, flight, its agent) spends it (BG-94) | Then the alert variant of the routine it was following, until DECAY\_TURNS pass with no attack or alarm perceived; then it returns to the routine saved in returnTo. |
| Not engaged | Its routine's desired state | Even during a Tactical turn; companions default to follow |
| No valid order | Engaged: its type's combat list. Not engaged: its routine's desired state | Failed or late calls, dropped orders |

Every code mind, combat lists included, reads only its actor's ActorView, never GameState.

**Creatures that can't speak (NV5).** An actor whose race is below `intelligent` (slimes, beasts) is never a spokesperson and gets no Responder or Voice call; code runs it. Words the player says to it give a `not_understood` effect: the story says it does not understand words.

**Call cap.** At most MAX\_PLANNER\_CALLS Planner calls run per turn (config). Engaged actors beyond the cap run their type's combat list (Fallback behavior), the same one minor actors use \[Proposed\]; actors nearest the player get calls first. Dialogue calls are outside this cap; spokespeople are capped by MAX\_SPOKESPEOPLE. The values and the ordering rule are still to be reviewed (D-18).

**Control by actor and state.** Who controls each kind of actor in each state:

| Actor | Calm | Alarmed (not engaged) | Engaged | Addressed | Incapacitated |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Player | Parser, after submit | Parser | Parser | Not applicable: NPC replies reach the player as perceived events | Flagged: a story branch (deferred) |
| Significant actor (control: agent) | Routine, at resolution | onAlert once, then its alert routine | Planner, in the background before submit; over the cap or late: its type's combat list | Responder if it has an agenda, else Voice, after the Parser; returns its one action; its Planner is cancelled | Inert |
| Minor actor (control: code) | Routine | onAlert once, then its alert routine | Combat list, at resolution (Fallback behavior) | Responder or Voice, as above; its combat-list order is discarded | Inert |
| Companion (player-band member) | Follow or wait routine | onAlert once, then its alert routine | Planner or combat list, by significance | Dialogue call, as above: you command a companion by talking to it | Inert |
| Guarded NPC (agenda, not engaged) | Routine plus its own triggers | onAlert once, then its alert routine | As significant or minor | Responder | Inert |
| Ordinary NPC or bystander | Routine | onAlert once, then its alert routine | Only once hostile (attacked, or persuaded into it): Planner or combat list | Voice | Inert |

In both tables, "its alert routine" means the alert variant of the routine the actor was following (Routine.alert). An actor whose routine has none keeps that routine.

In every row a fired trigger replaces the actor's order at turn start, or mid-turn if the actor hasn't acted; for an addressed actor, its dialogue call still runs and its line is kept only as a bark. Every controller reads only its own actor's view: OppositionView for the Planner and Responder, DialogueView for the Voice, ActorView for code minds and defaults.

When each controller runs in a turn: turn start (triggers fire, engaged set computed) → background (Planners) → after the Parser (dialogue calls, in parallel; replies in the exchange slot) → resolution (code minds, deeds, mid-turn triggers, onAlert) → Narrator.

#### Fallback behavior \[Proposed\]

An engaged actor with no agent order this turn gets one order from its type's combat list. That covers minor actors, actors over the call cap, actors whose Planner call is late or failed, and actors whose order was dropped. An actor that is not engaged and has no valid order follows its routine's desired state. There is no separate default behavior.

Code checks the combat list top to bottom; the first rule that matches gives the order:

1. **Cower:** the actor is broken (its morale bar reached Broken, Part 3, Morale). For now a broken nerve blocks only walking (fleeing too, since flight bugged too much) and attacking (Ali, BF37): any walk or run, any attack, grab (restrain) or filch, and any perk move except Battle Cry (a broken one can still roar; Ali, 14/11/2026, minds NERVE_ALLOWED_PERK_MOVES). Talking, begging, persuading, lying, yielding, giving, using an item, picking up, hiding, and unlocking or opening a door or container within reach still work. Its Planner call is skipped; whatever order it does get (a dialogue reply, a trigger, an alert) loses only the blocked parts (minds nerveHolds), and with nothing allowed left it cowers where it stands (activity cowering). A surrendered actor (Part 3, Morale) holds.
2. **Attack:** an active hostile actor it believes in (aware or tracking) is in its zone. It attacks with its type's attackSkill, targetPart null, aimed false. Target: the actor that last hit it, else the player, else an rng pick.
3. **Close in:** it moves one link, by the shortest path, toward the lastKnownZone of its most recent belief about a hostile actor. Never into a frontier.
4. **Search:** it is already in that zone and no hostile actor is there. It searches by the player's rule (Explore mode, Searching).
5. **Hold.**

Like every code mind, it reads only its ActorView. The rng pick uses the turn's seeded rng, so replay stays exact. Its orders carry source 'fallback'.

**Per-type data.** Each body type, or the NPC template when it sets one, names fallback.attackSkill. Slime: unarmed\_natural; slimes have only the general area, so targetPart is always null.

The rule order is fixed in code for the prototype. Each rule is its own small check, so a per-type, data-driven rule order can replace the fixed one later (D-17). tactics does not change the list yet (D-17).

### Agent Parser

Agent Parser turns the player's free text into a structured request. It runs on the smallest fast model, sees only PlayerView, and never narrates or decides outcomes.

Output shape (notation, not literal JSON):

```
{
  move:    { toZone: zone_id, after?: true } | null,
  action:  {
    type:     attack | hide | sneak | talk | persuade | deceive | barter | intimidate | use_item | equip | give | observe | interact | other | rest,
    skill:    skill_id,
    target:   actor_id | zone_id | item_id | null,
    targetPart: part_id | null,     // a body part of the target the player named; never implies aiming
    item:     item_instance_id | null,     // weapon, consumable, tool or item given
    mode:     mode_id | null,              // attack mode; code default if null (Part 5 §5)
    approach: short phrase in the player's style,   // flavor only
    stance:   careful | normal | reckless,         // flavor only
    improvised: { base: improvised_light | improvised_medium | improvised_heavy, name: short phrase } | null   // a scenario object used as a weapon (Part 5 §7)
  } | null,
  perkMove:   { perk: perk_id, target: actor_id | null } | null,   // an owned perk move (AE8); a free one rides beside the action
  gear:       [ { op: draw | stow | swap | wear_acc | remove_acc | pick_up,
                  item: item_instance_id, from?: actor_id } ] | null,   // free; before the action
  spoken:     words said aloud | null,   // the speech if the main action is a
                                         // speech action; otherwise a bark
  addressees: [actor_id | band_id | "everyone"] | null,
  deferred:   [actions that didn't fit this turn],
  clarify:    one short question | null
}
```

- approach and stance are flavor: the engine ignores them unless an active skill or perk names them as a trigger. They are passed to Agent Narrator so the prose keeps the player's style.
- deferred is logged and otherwise ignored for now. How extra actions are handled is part of the action-economy discussion (D-19).
- A clarify answer stops the turn: no resolution, turn unchanged. Planner outputs are kept and apply when the turn resolves.
- Code adds aimed: true | false to the player's order from the Aim toggle or /aim (Part 1 §7), or when the Parser says the player's words aim at the part they name (BF26: "aimed": true with a targetPart).

Fixed instructions: the exact text is PARSER_INSTRUCTIONS in src/agents/prompts.ts, the one copy (CS6; the spec no longer copies it, a test keeps it so). In short, it turns the player's words into structured actions from PLAYER_VIEW only; at most one move and one main action a turn, the player's words in spoken, gear ops free; asks one short clarify question only when the intent is truly unclear. Never narrates, never decides outcomes, never assumes success; sets aimed only when the player's words aim at the part they name (BF26).

Rules 1 and 5 are provisional: how to handle a player naming something that doesn't exist is still open, because a clarify question can reveal what exists in the world (D-21).

### Agent Planner

Agent Planner plays one engaged significant actor, on any side: an enemy, an ally or a companion. Each such actor gets its own call, made before this turn's player action exists, so it acts only on what that one actor perceived or was told.

Code builds the OppositionView from that actor's own knowledge. If the player hid from this guard, the Planner sees status: 'suspicious' with the last known zone, never the true zone. The engine rejects any order targeting something this actor has no belief about.

Output shape:

```
{
  action:   { type: attack | move | search | hide | hold | call_help | flee |
              talk | persuade | deceive | intimidate | barter |
              use_item | equip | give | other,
              target: actor_id | zone_id | null, skill: skill_id | null,
              targetPart: part_id | null,      // preferred part; takes effect only through the gap
              aimed: boolean,                  // pays the aim penalty for a sure location
              defense: dodge | block | null,   // sets the standing defense; null keeps it
              item: item_instance_id | null, mode: mode_id | null,
              intent: short phrase },
  dialogue: { line, volume: whisper | normal | shout } | null,
            // the words if action.type is talk; otherwise a bark
  gear:     [ { op, item, from? } ] | null,   // as the Parser: free gear ops, before the action
  stance:   { toward: actor_id, step: friendlier | colder } | null,
  planUpdate: { goal, steps, triggers,
                routine: routine_id | null } | null,  // routine: next turn; null = keep the current plan
  memory:   { add: [string], drop: [number] } | null,  // PM1: edit the memory list; drop by its number in the view
  agreement: { with: actor_id, text, job? } | null,   // PM3/PM4: a deal said aloud this turn; job: a quest (QT1)
  endAgreements: [ { id, outcome: done | broken } ],   // a party ends its own deal (never one holding a quest)
  questEnd?: { id: quest_id, outcome: done | abandoned | called_off } | null   // QT6/QT12/QT22
}
// job = { line, goal: { kind: defeat | deliver | reach | find | other, target?, count?, item?, qty?, to? },
//         pay?: { coin?, item?, qty? } | null, due?: { days, by?: time of day } | null, after?: quest_id | null }
```

Standing defense is saved on the actor (Part 4 §4 Core gains standingDefense: dodge | block). Default: whichever of dodge and block has the higher effective skill **\[Proposed\]**.

Fixed instructions: the exact text is PLANNER_INSTRUCTIONS in src/agents/prompts.ts, the one copy (CS6; the spec no longer copies it, a test keeps it so). In short, it plays one engaged character from CHARACTER and its OPPOSITION_VIEW: picks its action, move, gear and stance for the turn. Never narrates outcomes, never rolls dice, never targets what the character has no belief about.

Shared plans between actors (a coordinated ambush across several guards) are an open design question tied to how actors are grouped. For now each actor holds its own plan and triggers.

### Goal-driven uniques

Decided on the "Goal-driven uniques" questions page (UG1–UG17, https://claude.ai/artifact/1yeiAAh9PLmVJiALgnYBuk). Code: `src/engine/goals.ts`, `goalThinkers` in `src/agents/game.ts`.

- **Who (UG1).** Any actor that holds goals follows these rules; only uniques are given goals. A goal holder has an agenda, so the Responder voices it.
- **What (UG2).** Drives (its ranked values), 1 to GOALS_HELD goals, and a queue of steps. A goal is text plus `about`: the ids it names (people, places, zones, items).
- **Where goals come from (UG3, UG4).** The Weaver writes a unique's goals when it casts it (`cast[].goals`), from its values, archetype and past; an echo's first goal is its unfinished business, fitted to the world. A template's `standingGoal` is always held first.
- **Over time (UG5).** Goals live in the world's save (`hidden.drives`). The Planner or Responder marks goals met or failed by number and adds new ones, up to GOALS_HELD. A standing goal is dropped only when met.
- **Who decides (UG6).** The Planner, with `goals`, `steps` and `thinkWhy` in its view; it returns `goals` (met, failed, add) and `steps`.
- **When it thinks (UG7, Ali: moments 1, 3, 4).** Its queue runs out or a step can't begin; it perceives something a goal names (a person in sight, the zone or place it stands in, an item it holds or that lies there; once, until it loses it); or as before (engaged, talked to, a deal, an errand). No time-of-day think.
- **Budget (UG8, UG9).** At most UNIQUE_THINKS_PER_TURN thinks a turn, after the fight calls, nearest the player first, near or far. Asleep or broken, it waits. A late or missing call leaves its think pending for the next turn.
- **Routines (UG10).** It keeps its routine set and runs its routine whenever it has no step; after a walk or a wait it goes back to its own routine. Sleep and watches stay routine rules.
- **Steps (UG11, UG16, UG17).** Up to STEPS_QUEUED in order: go (a zone or frontier, lead to take someone), routine (one of its own) or wait (stay here), each until a time of day or someone's arrival. A step with no end stops after STEP_MAX_DAYS. A step that can't begin is dropped. No call runs inside a long turn (a night, a rest, a wait): queued steps run on through it by code, and it thinks at the start of the next turn.
- **Limits (UG12, UG13).** Anything the rules allow, against anyone (Ali: "I will see how it goes"). A goal may name the player; it finds the player only through its own beliefs.
- **Seen by (UG14).** The GM view's hidden store shows goals and steps; the player sees only what it does and says.
- **Personality (UG15).** Goals move no personality yet; goal outcomes are noted as a likely input for VT-OI1.
- **Scaling.** Goals are text, so nothing runs out at 50, 75, 100 or past it; the Weaver sizes a goal to the unique's power. The one think a turn is shared by every goal holder, so more uniques means each thinks less often (UNIQUE_THINKS_PER_TURN is the lever).

### Agent Responder

Agent Responder plays one addressed spokesperson that has an agenda (it holds a secret, has a plan, has memory entries or is in an agreement). It runs after the Parser, on the same model as the Planner, and decides that actor's one action for the turn: a reply or something else.

Input: CHARACTER (name, role, band, band goals, temperament); OPPOSITION\_VIEW; DIALOGUE\_INPUT (the spoken words and the impression); COMMITTED (a turn-start trigger's order, or none); SLIPS (a secret that comes out this turn whatever it intends, or none).

Output shape:

```
{
  action:     { ...as the Planner... },   // talk to reply, or any other action
  dialogue:   { line, volume } | null,   // the reply if action.type is talk;
                                                      // otherwise a bark
  gear:       [ { op, item, from? } ] | null,   // as the Parser
  stance:     { toward, step } | null,
  reveals:    [secret_id],                // its own secrets told by choice [Proposed]
  planUpdate: { ...as the Planner... } | null
}
```

Fixed instructions: the exact text is RESPONDER_INSTRUCTIONS in src/agents/prompts.ts, the one copy (CS6; the spec no longer copies it, a test keeps it so). In short, it plays one character speaking with the player: answers, or does something else instead, and may tell its own secrets by choice. Never narrates outcomes, never rolls dice, never reveals a secret it doesn't hold.

### Agent Voice

Agent Voice plays one addressed spokesperson with no agenda, such as a shopkeeper or a ferryman. It runs after the Parser, on the same model as the Responder, and decides that actor's one action for the turn. It exists so that talking to an ordinary NPC and talking to a guarded one look the same from outside: same pipeline, same number of calls, same order, same timing.

- Receives DialogueView: its own view plus only this turn's spoken words and its impression (Dialogue input).
- Shares only listed facts. It may share facts from the NPC's knows list and nothing else. This closes the hole where the Narrator, voicing NPCs itself, could invent directions, prices or clues.
- Never sees the hidden store. An NPC that gains a secret, a plan, a memory entry or an agreement has an agenda, and from then on Agent Responder voices it.

Output shape:

```
{
  action:     { ...as the Planner... },   // talk to reply, or any other action
  line:       what the NPC says,          // the reply if action.type is talk;
  volume:     whisper | normal | shout,   // otherwise a bark
  shares:     [fact_id],                  // facts from KNOWS this line reveals; may be empty
  gear:       [ { op, item, from? } ] | null,   // as the Parser
  stance:     { toward, step } | null,
  routine:    routine_id | null           // from its routines list; next turn
}
```

Fixed instructions: the exact text is VOICE_INSTRUCTIONS in src/agents/prompts.ts, the one copy (CS6; the spec no longer copies it, a test keeps it so). In short, it voices a calm character's reply to the player (a line, an action, a stance step, a routine switch). Never narrates, never decides whether the player's attempts succeed, never states a fact outside its view.

The engine checks every shares id against knows and scans line for names and facts that are not in the NPC's view.

**Failure handling (Responder and Voice) \[Proposed; see D-43\].** One repair retry, then a neutral in-character line from the NPC's sheet as a talk action. The legality check on targets applies to both, as it does to the Planner.

### Resolution engine

The engine is pure code: the same state, the same logged agent outputs and the same RNG state always give the same events, so any turn can be replayed. Player and NPC actions go through the same ruleset calls.

```ts
interface Ruleset {
  readonly TIERS: readonly Tier[];                                      // ordered best to worst
  initiative(actor: Actor, state: GameState, rng: Rng): number;
  check(req: CheckRequest, state: GameState, rng: Rng): CheckResult;   // Part 1 §4 Layer 2: no dice; skill + modifiers vs the scenario's target, pass or fail
  contested(a: RollRequest, b: RollRequest, state: GameState, rng: Rng): ContestResult;
  roll(req: RollRequest, state: GameState, rng: Rng): RollResult;          // Part 1 §4 Layer 2b
  attack(req: AttackRequest, state: GameState, rng: Rng): AttackResult;    // Part 3, Damage flow
  eventRoll(actor: Actor, state: GameState, rng: Rng): EventRollResult;
  // Layer 1 (Part 1 §4): d100 + EVENT_BASE + modifiers from perks, gear and
  // statuses whose library entry names the event roll. No skill. Six tiers and
  // natural edges. Called once per scenario, at scenario setup step 2, always for the player.
  applyEffects(result: CheckResult | ContestResult | AttackResult, state: GameState): Effect[];
  recordUse(actor: Actor, skill: string, result: CheckResult | ContestResult | AttackResult, state: GameState): void;
  // improvement-roll timing is open; recordUse only records a use for now
}

type CheckRequest = { actorId: string; skill: string; target?: string; modifiers: { source: string; value: number }[] };
type CheckResult  = { actorId: string; skill: string; target: number; passed: boolean; modifiers: CheckRequest['modifiers'] };
type ContestResult = { a: RollResult; b: RollResult; gap: number; winner: 'a' | 'b' | 'tie' };   // a = the acting side; gap = b.tier − a.tier (Part 1 §4, Contests)
type EventRollResult = {
  actorId: string;                    // always the player
  roll: number;                       // natural d100
  tier: Tier;                         // one of the six TIERS
  modifiers: { source: string; type: 'perk' | 'gear' | 'status'; value: number }[];
};

type RollRequest = {
  actorId: string; skill: string;
  modifiers: { source: string; type: 'roll_perk' | 'wounds' | 'situational' | 'aim' | 'gear'; value: number }[];
  tierShifts: { source: string; value: number }[];      // tier perks; the natural edge is added by roll()
};
type RollResult = {
  actorId: string; skill: string; d100: number; margin: number;
  tier: Tier;                       // clamped to the table
  beyond?: number;                  // steps a shifted roll went past the end tier (+ above, − below); tierLevel() adds it
  natural: -1 | 0 | 1;
  modifiers: RollRequest['modifiers']; tierShifts: RollRequest['tierShifts'];
};
type AttackRequest = { attackerId: string; targetId: string; weaponId: string;
                       targetPart: string | null; aimed: boolean };
type AttackResult = {
  attack: RollResult; defense: RollResult | null;       // null when the attack floor missed
  defenseType: 'dodge' | 'block' | null;
  gap: number | null; gapShifts: { source: string; value: number }[];
  outcome: 'missed' | 'dodged' | 'grazed' | 'blocked' | 'full_hit' | 'heavy_hit' | 'crushing_hit';
  location: string | null;
  locationHow: 'rolled' | 'aimed' | 'redirected' | 'restricted' | 'shield' | null;
  multiplier: number; damage: number;                   // code only; never reaches an agent
  deciding: { attack: string[]; defense: string[]; gap: string[] };
};

type Order = {
  actorId: string;
  type: string; target: string | null; skill: string | null; intent: string;
  source: 'player' | 'agent' | 'trigger' | 'onAlert' | 'routine' | 'fallback';
};

function resolveTurn(state: GameState, playerIntent: Intent, dialogueOrders: Order[],
                     agentOrders: Order[], fired: Order[], rng: Rng) {
  const views = buildActorViews(state);            // active actors only; code minds never see GameState
  const actions = mergeOrders(fromIntent(playerIntent), fired, brokenOrders(views), dialogueOrders, agentOrders,
                              alertOrders(views), routineOrders(views), fallbackOrders(views));
  // one order per active actor: trigger > broken > agent (dialogue call, else Planner) > onAlert
  //                             > routine (desired state) (not engaged) or combat list (engaged)
  resolveExchange(actions, state, rng);            // dialogue turns: the player's speech, then spoken replies
  const ordered = orderActions(actions, state, rng);          // ruleset.initiative
  const events: GameEvent[] = [];
  for (const step of walk(ordered, state)) {                   // pairs hide/search, attack/defend, deceive/insight as it goes
    if (!isActive(step.actor)) continue;                       // downed: order dropped
    applyGearOps(step, state);                                  // free gear ops at this actor's initiative, before its action (Part 5 §4)
    if (isAttackOnUnaware(step, state)) resolveAmbush(step, state, rng);   // before the blow; see Explore mode
    const result = step.attack
      ? ruleset.attack(step.attackReq, state, rng)        // Part 3, Damage flow
      : step.contested
      ? ruleset.contested(step.a, step.b, state, rng)
      : ruleset.check(step.req, state, rng);
    const effects = ruleset.applyEffects(result, state);
    ruleset.recordUse(step.actor, step.skill, result, state);
    const event = toEvent(step, result, effects);
    event.perception = computePerception(event, state, rng);   // at the moment it happens
    updateBeliefs(state, event, result);                        // still the ONLY writer of Belief
    if (event.actorId === 'player' && isMajorAction(event)) detectPlayer(state, event, rng);   // see Explore mode
    events.push(event);
    fireMidTurnTriggers(state, event, ordered);                 // see Hidden state persistence
  }
  applyBeliefDecay(state);
  checkSecrets(state, events);
  state.events.push(...events);
  return events;
}
```

A defense is a free reaction: it costs the defender no action and uses its standing defense. Each defense after the first in a turn takes the outnumbered situational modifier (value *placeholder*).

check() reads its target from the scenario's challenge grade (Part 1 §4, Layer 2). Ambush, detection and stealth rolls run as before. A event roll's result is saved in the turn record; replay feeds it back with the logged Weaver output, so a replayed scenario start makes no Claude call.

Perception rules:

- **Perception per event.** Computing perception after the loop gave a guard killed in step 2 knowledge of step 3, and gave a moved actor sight of events from its new zone.
- **One belief writer.** Stealth contests return results; updateBeliefs alone writes Belief, so the stealth result and the perception result can't overwrite each other.
- **Passive perception.** Every event is checked against every actor in range, whether or not that actor ordered a search. The player's own events are the exception: an observer unaware of the player perceives them only through detection (Explore mode).

**Active actors.** isActive(actor) is false while the actor is incapacitated. An inactive actor gets no order of any kind and an order it hasn't resolved is dropped. It skips desired-state evaluation, its triggers stay armed but don't fire, it counts on neither side of engagement, and it gets no dialogue call when addressed. A revived actor is active from the next turn.

Rules for simultaneous orders, because the Parser and the Planner worked without seeing each other:

- **Target moved first.** An attack resolves against where the target stands now if it is still within the weapon's reach; otherwise it becomes a wasted-action event ("swings at empty air"). **A stale NPC blow (TP2, Ali 14/11/2026):** turns stay simultaneous (TP1 a); an engaged NPC whose agent-given blow can no longer happen when its turn comes (no target, target dead, or out of reach or no room after closing in) does its combat-list action from what it perceives then instead, closing in on its new target with the move time left, as an engaged NPC's unwalkable walk already does (order\_dropped). A free follow-up strike, a fallback order, a yield, a truce or a held spar blow is still wasted, and so is the player's (TP3 open). Reach comes from the attack mode, in metres with sight (Part 5 §5).
- **Closing in (ZP3).** A melee attack on someone out of reach, with no other move planned before it (none, or a move to the target's zone), walks at the target on the move slot first and stops just inside the weapon's reach (a move event with `target` and no fromZone). Still out of reach, the striker runs on at the target on its main time, exactly as a dash (AE2: same pools, same clock), and strikes next turn; no blow is swung this turn (BG-255, Ali NV9 b: the player, an agent's blow, a code-run fighter's combat-list blow, and an agent blow gone stale that code takes over, src/engine/resolve.ts runOn, logged `ran_on`). Only a run that gets no nearer (no way there) still loses the blow (a wasted action). A ranged attack never closes in.
- **Spots in a zone (BG-12).** A player's move can name a spot in its zone, the player's own zone included (Parser `move.spot`): `near` someone there (stops PLACE\_GAP\_M from them) or a way out of it (its doorway, or a frontier's side); `awayFrom` someone or a way out (the far edge straight across from it through the zone's middle; a way out at the middle, such as stairs straight down, keeps to the side the walker is on); or `side`, a compass word (the middle of that side). It walks there on move slots like any move, stopping ENTER\_M (a step) inside the edge (position.ts spotPoint). A spot that names nothing there makes the Parser's turn ask where. A move with no spot to the player's own zone is still no move.
- **Far moves (BG-15).** A player's move names where the player means to end up, not the first step: any zone the player knows (PlayerView `known`: zones stood in, seen across a sight link or left, and the ways out of them it has seen, named by the exit's words for a zone never entered). The walk finds the way through the zones between, slot by slot, as any move. A move-only turn is just the move: no hold event follows it unless words go with it (BG-16).
- **Ways out, the back, left and right (BG-18 to BG-20).** Walking *to* a way out ("to the cellar steps") is a spot near it in the zone it leaves from; walking *through* it is a move to the zone beyond. "The back" of a zone with no way named is away from the way the player came in. Everyone walking faces the way it last walked (Actor.facing); the player's view gives each person in sight a `side` from that (ahead, left, right, behind, each a quarter turn), and when words fit several people equally (two at the same distance), the Parser picks one and never asks.
- **Moves in the narration (BG-22).** Each move event the player sees says how it went: a leg that leaves a zone gives the zone it left (`from`) and the one it entered (`where`); a leg inside a zone is `within` it, and when it is on the way to another zone, names that zone (`heading`). The Narrator tells a turn's legs in order as one walk.
- **Moves.** A move resolves at its actor's initiative like any other action. Provisional: how many actions a turn holds is part of the action-economy discussion.
- **Same target or object.** Earlier initiative acts first; the later actor gets a contested check or a wasted action.
- **Stealth.** A hide or sneak is contested once per observer in perception range. Each result sets that observer's belief separately. A hide or sneak also sets the player's posture to stealthy.
- **Speech.** Talking is an action. A talk action resolves at the speaker's initiative and is perceived by volume and zone links, except addressed speech in a dialogue turn, which resolves in the exchange slot at the top of the turn, followed by the spokespeople's spoken replies. A bark rides on its action's event: perceived and narrated, but it copies no belief. An NPC's bark is the exception (BG-253): its words were picked at the turn's start, so they are said as its turn starts, before anyone's step, on an event of their own; words it keeps for after a walk (speakAt end, or a walk up to its target) and a late starter's (AT58) still come at its step.
- **Numbers.** Tier tables, caps, perk effects and skill gains come only from the ruleset module. Do not invent or tune them here.

**First-build ruleset.** Build TIERS, eventRoll(), roll() and attack() as Parts 1 and 3 define them, and check() and contested() as Part 1 §4 sets out. The rest works like this:

- initiative(): d100 + SKILL\_WEIGHT × the best skill with the Initiative function (full value, like a function roll; 0 when none) + roll modifiers naming initiative (rollMod roll `initiative`). No skill has the function yet, so it is a plain d100 for now (Dodge joins the Speed group directly, without the function). Higher acts first. An actor unaware of anyone attacking it this turn takes INIT\_UNAWARE (placeholder), so it acts last. Ties go to the seeded RNG.
- applyEffects(): attacks as Part 3 sets out. A check or a contest only changes beliefs (through updateBeliefs) and reveals secrets; it never changes an object or a place (OQ-22).
- recordUse(): writes the use to the agent log and changes nothing, so no skill changes in the first build.
- The morale roll (Part 3, Morale) is moraleRoll(): roll() on Resolve with renown and rally; morale lost is saved in Condition.moraleLost and a break in Mind.moraleState until the scenario ends. No new interface method.

**Replay.** Each turn record (Part 4 §6) holds the RNG state before the turn, each agent's exact output and any event roll. Replay feeds the logged outputs, not the player's text, back through the engine, and is exact only under the library version that record names. Merge Planner outputs in actor-id order, never in the order calls returned.

### Agent Narrator

Agent Narrator writes the prose the player reads, on the strongest model available. It receives only NarrationView, so the perception rule is enforced twice: structurally (it never gets hidden data) and in the prompt (it must not invent any). Its full text is checked before display; there is no streaming for now.

**Seen actors in the dark (P3, 07/11/2026).** An actor in a dark zone is perceived partially, unless it shares the viewer's zone and the viewer fully perceived one of its events since the scenario began (for example it spoke face to face): then it stays fully seen.

Partial events reach the Narrator without actor names or intent, with the zone link's label and the cue, e.g. `{ kind: 'move', where: 'left of the gate', perception: 'partial', cue: 'sound' }`, so it can write "boots scrape somewhere to your left" and nothing more. Actors whose names the player doesn't know reach it by their public label.

Output shape:

```
{
  text:     the narration,
  narrated: [event_id]        // every event the text describes
}
```

Fixed instructions: the exact text is NARRATOR_INSTRUCTIONS in src/agents/prompts.ts, the one copy (CS6; the spec no longer copies it, a test keeps it so). In short, it turns the resolved, perceived events into prose in STYLE and lists every event it describes in narrated. Never decides outcomes, never names what the player has not perceived or learned.

For attacks, Agent Narrator receives the combat packet (Part 3, The packet), which holds no numbers. When SHOW\_MECHANICS is on, code prints the dice and modifiers in a mechanics panel under the narration; the Narrator never sees them.

### Hidden state persistence

Plans and secrets live in state.hidden, so they survive between turns even when no agent is called. Triggers use a small, machine-checkable vocabulary; the Planner and the Weaver may only write triggers from this list, and the engine drops any it can't parse.

```ts
type HiddenStore = {
  plans: Record<string, Plan>;       // actorId -> plan
  memory: Record<string, MemoryEntry[]>; // actorId -> memory list { turn, text }, at most MEMORY_ENTRIES (PM1)
  agreements?: Agreement[];          // PM3: { id, parties: [a, b], text, madeTurn, madeBy }, kept until done or broken
  secrets: Secret[];
  firedLog: { triggerId: string; turn: number }[];
  weaverNote: string;                // Agent Weaver's rolling note; replaced each call
  stage: { eventSummary: string; challenge: Grade; gain: Grade; hiddenTerms: string[] };
};

type Plan = { goal: string; steps: string[]; triggers: Trigger[]; createdTurn: number };

type Trigger = {
  id: string;
  ownerId: string;                   // the actor whose beliefs the condition reads
  when:
    | { type: 'player_seen_in_zone'; zone: string }   // the owner's own perception, not truth
    | { type: 'turn_reached'; turn: number }
    | { type: 'actor_down'; actorId: string }
    | { type: 'alarm_heard' }                          // the owner perceived an alarm event
    | { type: 'quiet_turns'; turns: number };          // a beat: the scenario has been quiet this many turns in a row (Scenarios)
  then: OrderTemplate;               // exactly one order; the validator rejects lists
  once: boolean;
  endsScenario?: true;                  // a beat that ends the scenario when it fires (Scenarios)
};

type OrderTemplate = Omit<Order, 'source' | 'target'> & {
  target: string | '$player' | '$lastKnownZone' | '$triggerZone' | `$slot:${string}` | null;
                       // bound when the trigger or onAlert fires; $slot reads the owner's binding
                       // (a route slot gives its first zone)
};

type Secret = {
  id: string;
  holderId: string;                  // the actor who keeps it
  fact: string;                      // e.g. 'the captain is the informant'
  leakTerms: string[];               // words and phrases the leak scan looks for, e.g. ['informant', 'sold you out']
  revealWhen:
    | { type: 'event_in_zone'; kind: string; zone: string }
    | { type: 'check_passed'; skill: string; minTier: Tier; againstActorId: string };
};
```

Per turn:

1. **Turn start.** The engine checks every trigger once, after the previous turn is saved (Architecture step 1). Fired triggers become orders this turn and are written to firedLog; a once trigger is removed from its plan at that moment, so it can never fire twice. Triggers of inactive actors stay armed but don't fire.
2. **Mid-turn.** After each event, player\_seen\_in\_zone and actor\_down triggers are checked again. One that fires while its owner hasn't acted yet replaces that owner's order this turn; otherwise it fires next turn. An ambush springs while the player is still on the bridge.
3. **One order per actor.** A fired trigger's order replaces the agent's order for the same actor. When several of an owner's triggers fire in one turn, the first in its plan's order wins; the others stay armed unless they are once triggers.
4. **Next view.** The owner's next OppositionView lists its fired triggers under "already happened", so the Planner doesn't order the same thing twice.
5. **Plan updates.** A non-null planUpdate replaces that actor's plan. memory edits the actor's memory list on its own, with no plan update (PM1): drops by number first, then adds; past CONFIG.MEMORY\_ENTRIES (8, a placeholder) the oldest entries go (PM2). An older output's memoryNote is read as one added entry. Updates from a turn that stopped on a clarify question are kept and apply when that turn resolves. A late Planner output is discarded whole, planUpdate included (interim; D-43).
5a. **Memory and agreements** (PM1–PM8, Ali 08/11/2026, schema v6).
   - An agreement is recorded only when the other party (the player or an NPC) perceived a line spoken by its maker this turn; otherwise it is logged invalid\_agreement. The Planner and the Responder make deals with anyone (PM4); the Voice only with the player. Only a party can end a deal (done or broken).
   - Both parties' views list the deal (AgreementView: with, withId, text, madeTurn). The PlayerView lists the player's deals for the screen (PM5; the panel is the designer's).
5b. **Quests** (OQ-28 and D-105, settled by Ali 09/11/2026, QT1–QT28; src/engine/quests.ts).
   - A quest is a deal with a job in it; the giver is always an NPC and only the player takes quests (QT1, QT16). The giver's Planner, Responder or Voice offers the job in talk; the Weaver seeds givers only through an actor's plan (QT2). The deal's agreement carries the job; it becomes a quest only when the giver heard the player speak this turn (QT3). Unaccepted offers are not stored.
   - The job's line is one short line of what was asked. It goes through the narration leak scan; a refused line keeps the deal but makes no quest (QT4). One open quest per giver: a second is refused and logged (QT5).
   - Goals (QT6/QT7): defeat (N of a body type or race, or one named actor), deliver (an item, or one item instance, handed to someone), reach (a zone or place), find (see a named person clearly). Givers offer only these four (BF61: the job schema has no "other", and the giver's rule says never a chore code can't check, and never to say a tool or thing is somewhere its view doesn't show). A job code still can't read (a target not in the world) is refused (quest\_refused, unchecked\_goal), logged quest\_goal\_gap and filed in the play page's bugs/ list (QT27). A job of kind "other" made before BF61 stays so, and its giver's agent judges it done at report-back. Doable chores (mend, chop, carry at furniture with placed tools) are a feature for later. Goal kinds and quest states are unordered: their names are their IDs (QT21).
   - States (QT11, QT22): active, ready (goal met), done, failed, abandoned, called\_off. A ready quest is done when the player next talks to the giver in person, if the giver perceived the deed that met it (BF6: the counted foes' fall, the hand-over, the player getting there); otherwise the giver learns it only by judging the player's report, and its agent marks it done in questEnd (code ends it only if the goal is really met; a believed false report is logged quest_report_believed); handing a delivery to the giver finishes it at once (QT8). Code fails a quest when the giver dies, a named target dies, a delivery item no longer exists, or the goal is not met by the deadline.
   - The deal is locked: code refuses (quest\_deal\_locked) any endAgreements on it, and ends it with the quest. The giver may call the job off only in words the player hears (called\_off, no stance change). The player abandons on the Quests page or by telling the giver (QT12).
   - Payment (QT10/QT23): the promise is data (coin and/or a library item and count). Anything the giver gives the player afterwards counts toward it. Still owed when the player's engagement ends after hand-in: the promise is broken, logged quest\_unpaid, the giver remembers it, and the quest shows Unpaid.
   - Deadlines (QT25): in N days, optionally by a time of day, as a clock time; without a time of day, the end of that day. Only the goal must be met by then.
   - Stance (QT13): done, one step friendlier; abandoned or failed by a missed deadline, one step colder.
   - Linked quests (QT24): a quest may name the quest it follows (after). A job from the same giver after a hand-in in the same engagement links on its own. Each quest keeps one goal and pays its own XP.
   - Views: an NPC's view lists in jobs the player's quests it gave or that send the player to it (a ready quest shows as active until hand-in). The Quests page shows giver, goal and progress, reward level, payment, due time and state, linked quests folded under one head row; the Play screen's quest line shows the pinned or newest open quest.
   - PM8: an agent-controlled NPC in an open deal gets a Planner call each turn even when not engaged, so a companion can act on the deal instead of only following.
   - PM6: when events an NPC perceived fall out of its RECENT\_EVENTS window, code writes one short line for each into Mind.earlier (no Claude call): "turn 12: player attack you (hit)". A line that repeats the one before it becomes a count (turn 12–14: … ×3). Only the last CONFIG.EARLIER\_LINES (6, a placeholder) stay. Every NPC view shows them as earlier.
   - Deferred (PM7): the Voice writing memory entries.
6. **Secrets.** A secret becomes player knowledge only when a resolved event meets revealWhen: minTier is a tier key and the check must reach at least that tier (the Weaver writes the tier's name; code keeps the key), and a check\_passed reveal needs the check aimed at againstActorId. A secret is also revealed when its holder's Responder lists it in reveals; code accepts only secrets that actor holds **\[Proposed\]**. When the player wins a speech contest against a spokesperson with one of its check\_passed reveals' skills, at that reveal's minTier or better, its Responder is told so (SLIPS) before it answers. A search aimed at the holder reveals check\_passed secrets of a sight or hearing skill when its roll reaches minTier (SE17, Searching). A revealed secret is added to the player's Journal (secretsKnown); only then can it appear in NarrationView.
7. **Save.** After resolution, the story is saved as described in Part 4 §10. Hidden fields are saved but shown only in the GM debug view.

**onAlert** is not a trigger. Code fires a routine's onAlert order once, when its owner first leaves unaware of a wary actor or first perceives an attack or alarm: this turn if the owner hasn't acted and has no agent order, otherwise next turn unless an agent order exists by then. An armed flag resets when the owner is calm again: unaware of every wary actor, and DECAY\_TURNS turns with no attack or alarm perceived.

### Encounter modes

Code picks the mode each turn; agents run only when someone needs a mind. Ordinary and guarded dialogue now share one pipeline, so the mode no longer shows whether an NPC has a secret.

**Engagement** (Ali, 07/11/2026, E1). An engagement is one encounter from an actor's point of view: a fight, a negotiation, or both. It starts when any actor becomes engaged with that actor, either in a fight (active, hostile to it, and the two engaged, on a turn either attacks or closes in; YD6, see Hunting and engaged) or in talk (the two speak to each other in a dialogue exchange). It ends once no actor has been engaged with it, in either way, for ENGAGEMENT\_END\_TURNS turns in a row (config, 3; Ali, 11/11/2026), or at once when everyone who heard the player's yield has taken it (YD3). A negotiation that turns into a fight, or a fight that turns into talk, stays one engagement (Ali, 07/11/2026): once-per-engagement effects and the improvement rolls at its end happen once. A scenario can hold several engagements. Effects that work once per engagement reset when a new one starts, and other rules may count engagements too. Code keeps the current engagement's start turn per actor, and which kinds it has held so far (talk, fight, spar).

**Sparring** (Ali, 09/11/2026, SP1–SP12; src/engine/spar.ts). A spar is its own kind of engagement: a pact between sides that lets fighters hit each other while nobody's stance changes. The fighter who is hit does not turn hostile, nor do its bandmates or onlookers; no alarm goes up (SP11), and NPC views and the Narrator see each sparring blow marked spar. Sides: one against one is two sides of one fighter; groups and free-for-alls (every fighter on its own side) work the same way (SP3); fighters on one side are not opponents.
- **Start (SP2).** Agreed aloud in talk: an NPC's agent output gives spar {sides, cruel?}, with itself on a side, and every other fighter must have heard its line this turn (like a deal). Or the Weaver's stage plan lists spars by cast key or actor id; the validator checks the sides (bad\_spar, unresolved\_actor). A fighter is in one spar at a time.
- **Fighting.** Spar opponents count as foes for fighting rules (engaged, tactical mode, morale, crowding, rest and heal blocks, fallback attacks, Volley), but stance, hostility and XP rules see no change. Blows do their normal damage, with no exceptions (SP6).
- **Out (SP4, SP5).** A fighter is out once it reaches SPAR\_LIMIT on the health track (config, Critical) or yields. A cruel spar, which every fighter must accept, runs to Incapacitated instead. The player yields by saying so (the Parser's yields: true), applied after the turn's blows; an NPC yields in its output (yields: true), or when its morale bar reaches Broken while it has no real foe: it just leaves the spar, with no yielded stance and no flight.
- **End (SP7).** The last side with a fighter still in wins. A spar also ends on a scenario break, after ENGAGEMENT\_END\_TURNS turns with no blow between sides, or when a fighter and someone outside the spar attack each other (that attack is real and follows the normal rules). Fighters may be in different zones (a ranged fighter against a melee one). A blow on or by a fighter who went out earlier in the same turn is held back (the order was made before; spar\_blow\_held); from the next turn on such a blow is a real attack.
- **Rewards (SP8, SP9).** A spar pays no defeat XP (no one is really defeated) and counts toward no defeat achievements; skills still learn from the rolls, and the engagement's improvement rolls happen as usual. Each spar the player's side wins adds to the spar\_wins tally for spar achievements (D-144).
- **After (SP10).** A spar changes no stance; NPCs may still step friendlier or colder in talk.

| Mode | When code enters it | Pipeline | Claude calls per turn |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Explore | No fight concerns the player, and no one is being spoken to | Parser → engine → Narrator, plus background Planners for engaged actors | 2 |
| Dialogue | The Parser's main action is speech addressed to NPCs | Parser → speech check → one Responder or Voice call per spokesperson, in parallel → engine → Narrator | 2 plus one per spokesperson (at most MAX\_SPOKESPEOPLE), plus background Planners for engaged actors |
| Tactical | A fight concerns the player (BF14): someone fighting them knows they are there, or they can perceive a fight (blows traded, or a spar) | Background Planner per engaged significant actor; Parser → engine → Narrator | 2 plus one per engaged significant actor, capped by MAX\_PLANNER\_CALLS |

Engaged is side-neutral: an actor is engaged when it believes in an active actor it is hostile to, so allies facing bandits get Planner calls too. Whether the turn is Tactical follows the player only (BF14, Ali, 13/11/2026): a standoff or a fight the player cannot perceive keeps normal turns.

Modes are chosen per turn inside a scenario (Scenarios); a scenario can open in any mode.

Mode is computed after the previous turn is saved, from saved state; Dialogue is still decided after the Parser returns. There is one resolveTurn: mode only decides which agents ran.

An attack from Explore resolves in the same turn (Explore mode, Ambush). Tactical no longer has an "or the player attacks" condition **\[Proposed\]**: observers of the attack become engaged anyway, and a kill nobody perceives leaves the game in Explore.

Belief decay still returns actors to unaware. Planner calls follow engagement per actor, so encounter mode doesn't change which calls run. Triggers are checked in every mode, so an ambush can start a tactical turn by itself.

Every mode takes the same time after the player submits, except turns that start a scenario or cross a frontier (Latency and cost).

### Game clock

Settled by Ali 07/11/2026 ("Sense of time: options", T1–T11; settles D-101).

- **One clock.** Code keeps game minutes since midnight before day 1 in `clocks.time`. Claude never sets it. Every turn costs its action's time from the library durations table (*placeholders*): a fight turn 0.1 min (6 s), any other action 1 min, a trade (barter) 10 min, rest 60 min, sleep 480 min. A save from before the clock starts on day 1 at the start of day.
- **Time of day** is an ordered scale (TIME\_OF\_DAY: dawn, morning, afternoon, dusk, evening, night; six since NW13, so routines can tell the evening and the late night apart); each starts at a minute of the day in CONFIG.TIME\_OF\_DAY\_FROM (*placeholders*: 5:00, 7:00, 12:00, 18:00, 20:00, 23:00). Adding a time of day is an edit to that table, its start, its sky light, its routine-set tags and its icon. There is a day counter and no weekday or season names (T4).
- **What it changes (T3):** an outdoor zone (its zone kind says so, Z2) is lit by the brighter of its own lamps or fires and the sky (CONFIG.SKY\_LIGHT: morning and afternoon bright, dawn and dusk dim, evening and night dark); indoor zones keep their own light. A routine set's time tags must fit the time of day (CONFIG.TIME\_TAGS: a day set fits morning and afternoon, a night set evening and night, and dawn and dusk take either), otherwise it is a routine error. Shop hours come with shops (Stage 6). Routines that change mid-scenario wait (D-49).
- **Who sees it (T5):** NPC views and the Narrator get `time: {day, timeOfDay}`, never minutes. No new Claude call.
- **Waiting (T6):** the Parser reads "wait until dawn" as action wait with the time of day in target (none: the next time of day; the time of day it already is: nothing happens and the line says so, Q2 08/11/2026), and "sleep" as action sleep (the sleep row). Sleep also gives the rest heal (or the place's row, e.g. a clinic) once per REST\_COOLDOWN\_MIN of sleep, twice for 8 hours, and counts as the last rest (Ali, 07/11/2026). Code moves the clock; the scenario goes on (rest, sleep and waiting never end a scenario, SC4). A hostile in the zone or tactical mode blocks it, like rest. The turn shows "Time passes: 11 h (Day 2 · Dawn)".
- **Sleeping and keeping watch (NW12, NW14, NW15, NW18–NW22, src/engine/sleep.ts).** Asleep (Actor.asleep), an actor perceives nothing but a blow on it, hears no words, does nothing on its turn (its agent is not asked) and is struck as an unaware target (Never Surprised doesn't help it). It wakes on a blow aimed at it (hit or miss), on hearing a sound as loud as a shout or louder (CONFIG.WAKE_NOISE), when its sleep runs out, and, if it has its own hearing (NW14: every sleeper sleeps light, Ali 14/11/2026; this was the Light Sleeper perk, Earshot's 60 perk, now removed and PK127 retired), on a passed hearing check against someone hostile near it. Near means their steps reach it (CS20): their movement sound, a sneaker's at its sneaking noise, carries to the sleeper as it would to a listener awake (metres, walls and doors; a zone is never the distance), and a foe whose steps don't reach it gets no roll. The check is against their stealth roll if they sneak, else a hearing check whose grade comes from their noise (CONFIG.WAKE_GRADE, louder is easier, placeholders), rolled when they arrive and at the start of each turn while they are near (NW18, NW19). Woken by the check, it is aware of them.
  - **The player's sleep** is a night (GameState.night). Alone it lasts the sleep row. With companions in the zone (those following the player), the night is shared out in equal watches, one each, and everyone else sleeps: everyone gets the sleep row's hours, so the night lasts that × n ÷ (n − 1), capped at CONFIG.NIGHT\_MAX\_MIN (12 hours, NW22 b: a pair shares 12 hours, 6 of them asleep; three sleep 8 hours with 4-hour watches; four 10 h 40). The player takes the middle watch. The clock steps SLEEP\_STEP\_MIN at a time: everyone moves on toward their routines, sleeps or watches by the plan, and NPCs sleep by their routines. When someone hostile to the player comes near (their steps reach the player, as above; CS20), the night stops there (NW12 b): the clock has moved on only that far, they are there when the next turn starts, and each light sleeper they came near may hear them. Still asleep, the player's turns pass with nothing done (the game plays them by itself), and after each the rest of the night runs on with whoever is there accepted (NW18 a; AT42, stage 3d-2: a newcomer or a foe after the sleeper reaching them stops it again). Woken out of the plan (a blow, a noise, a hearing check), the player's night is over; "sleep" again during a stopped night carries it on. The player's sleep heals a rest per REST\_COOLDOWN\_MIN asleep (watch time doesn't count) and counts as the last rest. The player's wait or sleep resolves after everyone else has acted that turn. Sleep length is the night's, so fatigue can set it later (NW16 has its own thread).
  - **Sleeping NPCs** (NW20 a) follow the same rules. An NPC falls asleep when its routine has it asleep where it stands (not while engaged, alarmed, broken or held, and no sooner than FALL\_ASLEEP\_MIN after it last woke), and wakes when its routine no longer does.
  - **The wake-up shout** (NW21 a): a band member who is awake and aware of someone hostile (to it or to a sleeping bandmate) shouts on its turn when a sleeping bandmate is within reach of its shout. Code does it with no Claude call; the shout is loud, so it also wakes others nearby who hear it.
  - Alarm systems (bells, tripwires, watchdogs) are parked (D-230).
- **Starting time (T8):** the Weaver's first plan names `timeOfDay`, and code sets the clock to its start on day 1. After that a plan may only name the current one (bad\_time otherwise).
- **Shown (T11):** the Play header reads "Turn 5 · Day 2 · Evening · …"; its look is the designer's.
- **Later:** NPC needs such as hunger and sleep (T10, not planned). Travel time for long journeys is the Travel section below.

### Travel

Settled by Ali 11/11/2026 ("Travel on the Road", TV1–TV9). Code: src/engine/travel.ts. Roads are one long zone (Zone type, Roads).

- **When (TV1):** a player move with no main action whose route uses a road, outside tactical mode, with nobody hostile engaged with the player or perceiving them (suspicious or more), is a trip walked in one turn. Otherwise it is a normal move, a slot at a time. A frontier off a road is at the road's far end (the side its label's compass names, else the end farthest from the player): the trip walks there, then crosses it.
- **Speed (TV2, SR5):** travel has one pace (Ali, 14/11/2026, SR5: the slow, normal and fast paces are gone). Travel speed is the walking speed (body type, armor, speed factor: moveSpeed) × CONFIG.TRAVEL\_SPEED\_SHARE (0.9), which gives a 1.4 m/s walker 4.5 km/h (US Army FM 21-18: about 4 km/h on a day march), and × TRAVEL\_DARK\_SPEED\_SHARE (0.6, 3 km/h) in the dark. NPCs moved on by the clock keep the same speed (NW11). Each hour of travel adds TRAVEL\_STRAIN\_PER\_HOUR (1) point of strain straight to the long-term stamina bar, whatever the armour, for everyone in the trip (payTravel in src/engine/fatigue.ts). Everyone travelling together goes at the slowest one's speed. Mounts and carts are parked (D-201).
- **Legs (TV4):** code walks the trip in legs of TRAVEL\_LEG\_MIN (60) game minutes; the clock moves and natural recovery runs for each. A leg that ends on a road meets something with a chance of ROAD\_ENCOUNTER\_PCT for its danger × the leg's share of an hour (× NIGHT\_ENCOUNTER\_MULT 2 in the dark). On a meeting, code makes the event roll (the encounter's tier) and the trip stops there: the scenario breaks (`encounter`), and the next scenario starts with that roll, its people placed ENCOUNTER\_M (20 m) on along the road the way the player was walking. The Weaver gets `target.encounter` (zone, danger, noticed). Like any event roll, an encounter can be good or bad: its tier sets which (Ali, 11/11/2026); the danger only sets how often one happens.
- **Night (TV5):** a trip that reaches dusk stops there (`nightfall`); the next order is the answer: travel again to press on, or camp (sleep, wait). A trip that sets off at dusk, evening or night (CONFIG.ROAD\_DARK) presses on: at the dark speed, the event roll gets NIGHT\_TRAVEL\_EVENT\_MOD (−15, type situational, source "night travel"), and encounters come twice as often. Hunger and fatigue are parked.
- **Who comes (TV6):** active bandmates together with the player (YD7) travel along and stand beside it at the end, and so does anyone following the player (the follow routine) who sees the player and is seen by them (BF9). Reviewing who travels together, and agreeing a time to set off, is parked (D-202).
- **What is shown (TV7):** one `travel` event with one `travelled` effect (metres, minutes, night, stop); the Narrator gets it as one passage ("you travel 9.2 km in 2 h 2 min"). The mechanics line reads "Travelled 9.2 km · stopped at nightfall" and "Time passes: …". On the Map, a road you are on shows your point along it: in the exit diagram its tile draws the road end to end (W to E, or S to N) with your pin at your place and "1.8 km of 4.5 km" under the name; drawn to scale, the World shows the road as a line with your pin at your own position (Actor.pos) at a fixed size, and its card says how far along you are (alongRoad in src/ui/mapData.ts; Roads under Map). Landmarks are parked (D-203).
- **Noticing (TV8):** when something meets the player, one noticing roll: the sight function, with the light. Success or better: the player saw it coming, and the Weaver is told that no ambush springs and anyone lying in wait has not seen the player. What else could be hidden by a road (side paths, lost things) comes with landmarks.
- **Off the road (TV9):** parked. NPCs travelling between places use these rules in the NPCs-stay-in-the-world work.

### Land and regions

Settled by Ali 14/11/2026 ("Zones for a real-feeling world", WZ1–WZ18). Code: src/land/ (generate.ts draws, brief.ts holds the Weaver's brief, sight.ts the land made out); tables regionKinds (RK), landCovers (LC) and landmarkKinds (LM) in the library; numbers in CONFIG (REGION\_\*, SITE\_\*, LAND\_\*, FORD\_MAX\_WIDTH\_M, STREAM\_WIDTH\_M).

- **Land first (WZ1 c, WZ3 a):** the world is cut into square regions of REGION\_SIZE\_M (10 km) on a grid of REGION\_CELL\_M (100 m) cells. Code draws a region whole before any zone stands on it: relief, a river with its name, streams that run downhill (priority flood, then steepest descent), settlement sites, a branching road network that follows the land (A\* over slope, wild cover and crossings, reusing roads already drawn), bridges and fords where roads cross water (a bridge when the water is wider than FORD\_MAX\_WIDTH\_M), and land cover (farmland and pasture round settlements, water meadow along the river, woods, heath, moor, marsh and rock by the kind's shares). The same world and square always give the same land (seed from the world id and the square), and a drawn region never moves (MAP-03).
- **The brief (WZ2 a, WZ14 a):** once per region, before the story's first plan, the Weaver writes a brief in words (WEAVER\_REGION\_INSTRUCTIONS in src/agents/prompts.ts): the region kind, its name and look, the river, every settlement (size, name, look, where-word), landmarks, the roads' danger, the exits and what lies beyond each side. The call is as long as the details need. Code tidies it (tidyBrief: unknown kinds, sizes and where-words dropped and logged) and draws the land to fit; with no brief, the kind's table alone draws it (region\_brief\_fallback).
- **Region kinds (WZ11 a):** three: river valley farmland (RK1), forest (RK2) and upland moor (RK3). Each row sets relief, roughness, river (width like a real river: the Ouse at York, the Cam at Cambridge), streams, wild cover shares, open cover, how many towns, villages and hamlets, and the default road danger. Settlement spacing and sizes are in CONFIG (SITE\_SPACING\_M, SITE\_BUILT\_M, SITE\_FIELDS\_M), from deserted medieval villages such as Wharram Percy (about 40 houses).
- **Where the story starts (WZ13 a):** the brief marks one settlement start; code makes it the region's start site, and the opening plan's first top-level place stands on it (Place.site, Site.placeId): its zones are laid round the site's point.
- **Roads as zones:** a road zone the Weaver plans along a road of the land (StagePlan zone `road`) takes the road's real length and danger (WZ16 a: the brief's danger word, else the kind's), and the road remembers its zone (RoadLine.zone). In step 1 the zone itself is still a straight bar of that length; the map draws the road's real line.
- **Junctions (WZ8 a):** roads branch at junctions. A road zone runs between two nodes of the network (a site, a junction, or an exit at the region's edge), so a trip along it stops at its end: at an unseen junction the next leg is a frontier the World builder builds when reached. Junctions are no longer part of D-203.
- **What the map shows (WZ4 a, WZ17 "follow the spec"):** only land the character has made out. From outdoors, each look reveals the cells in sight out to the sight table's farthest glimpse (CONFIG.SIGHT\_M, glaring's 2 km; landSightM in src/land/sight.ts), by day or night: the spec's one sight rule, where light only caps how clearly something is seen. The eye is LAND\_EYE\_M (1.6 m) above the ground, with hills, woods (about 20 m) and houses (about 6 m) in the way (LandCover.standsM). The cells are kept per region on the journal (landSeen). Settlements known only by word are drawn faint. The World view draws the land layer: cover, contours, rivers, roads as lines, bridges, fords and sites.
- **Wild areas, borders and NPCs (WZ6 a, WZ7 a, WZ9 a, WZ10 a):** wild land is cut along natural lines; each border stretch gets an edge kind (new rows hedge, river bank, cliff, thicket, palisade) and crossings (gate, gap, bridge, ford, stile), an open border is crossed anywhere; story spots sit in wild areas; NPCs cross unfilled land by code. These come with step 2.
- **Settlements (WZ5 a):** rooms mostly rectangles, with round, L-shaped, many-sided rooms and caves; buildings face the street. These come with steps 3 and 4.
- **Steps (WZ12 a, WZ15 a):** 1 region land and the region map (inside settlements nothing changes yet); 2 irregular areas and borders outdoors; 3 village and town plans with plots; 4 more kinds, landmarks and room shapes.
- **Next region (WZ18 a):** once the player outdoors can see past their region's edge (the land sight reach above), or stands on a road zone whose road leaves by it, the Weaver's brief for the square beyond is asked for in the background (ScenarioDirector.drawAhead; the call sees the drawn regions beside it and what they called this land). When it is back, the region is drawn before the next turn (applyRegionsDrawn, logged region\_drawn; a failed brief draws from the kind alone), joined to every drawn neighbour (joinsFor in src/land/edge.ts): roads leave the neighbour where they come in, rivers and streams run on with their names and widths, and the ground eases into the neighbour's heights along the edge over REGION\_EDGE\_BLEND\_M (2.5 km), while bare rock still follows the region's own slopes. The neighbour then knows it by name on that side. Only the first region holds the story's start. Sight reaches across the edge into every drawn region, with hills on either side in the way.
- **Old saves (WZ12):** saves from before the land (schema below 9, LAND\_SCHEMA) are refused with a reason, and worlds from before it are not offered to carry on. Hall of fame heroes stay.
- **Flagged, not decided:** the jump distance when a story leaves its region, and any rung of the world above a region.

### Scenarios

A scenario is the stretch of play between two scenario starts.

- **A scenario is a situation** (Scenes Deep Dive SC1–SC10, Ali, 11/11/2026): a stretch of play around one hidden question, such as "will the player get the debt back from the smugglers?". The Weaver's scenario plan gives the question, a scale and two or three endings (`scenario`, SCENARIO\_ENDINGS\_MIN 2 to SCENARIO\_ENDINGS\_MAX 3: always more than one way out). A plan without a scenario, with an unknown scale or with the wrong number of endings is a routine error.
  - **Scales** (names table SCENARIO\_SCALES, so a scale can be inserted between two others; levers in CONFIG.SCENARIO\_SCALE, all placeholders): Moment (12 turns, 1 place), Situation (40 turns, 2 places), Episode (120 turns, 4 places), Venture (3 days, any number of places). The Weaver picks the scale at any tier (SC3); code raises a scale too small for the places the plan builds and notes it (scenario\_scale\_raised). The turn budgets are not used yet (SC12, step 2).
  - **Endings** (SC7) reuse the quest goal checks (one shared function each): band\_out (every member of a band down, broken or gone from the stage), reach a place, leave a place (once the player has been in it), find someone, hold an item, deliver an item to someone, a time of day, or judged (only the Weaver can tell; code never meets it). Each ending says how the scenario ends, from the names table SCENARIO\_ENDINGS: resolved, lost, left, escaped, interrupted, worn out, broken off (the last two code-only, SC10), with a line of what happened. An ending that names nothing real is dropped after a failed repair. Every scenario with a judged ending files a bug, scenario\_ending\_judged, so the endings code could not check are reviewed (Ali: a bug whenever code can't judge).
  - **When a scenario ends.** Code checks the scenario's endings after every turn, and the bigger scenario's too (below). The first one met is kept (scenario\_ending\_met), but it is held while the player is fighting, being chased (a pursuer, CH4) or in a talk that turn (SC13, scenario\_ending\_held); the scenario ends on the first clear turn (breakCause scenario\_end). It also ends at a story beat with endsScenario or a stall (worn out: STALL\_TURNS, *placeholder*, 8 quiet turns in a row with no beat left whose owner is active), when the player is knocked out (broken off), and at a road encounter (interrupted). **Rest, sleep, waiting and time skips never end a scenario** (Ali, SC4/SC9).
  - **Inner scenarios** (SC4). An Episode or Venture (CONFIG.SCENARIO\_HOLDS\_FROM) holds smaller scenarios one level deep: a road encounter inside one starts an inner scenario and keeps the bigger one as `outer`. When the inner scenario ends, the bigger one carries on with no new event roll and no Weaver call (scenario\_resumed). An ending of the bigger scenario met during the inner one ends both; being knocked out ends both.
  - **Scenario records** (SC6). When a scenario ends, code keeps a record in hidden state (scenarios, the last SCENARIO\_RECORDS\_SAVED 30): its number, question, scale, tier, ending and line, its turns, who fell (anyone up at the start who is down now; the player when knocked out), and the items the player gained. The next Weaver sees the last SCENARIO\_RECORDS\_SHOWN (5) as lastScenarios and writes the aftermath from them.
  - **Weaver check-in** (SC8, SC14). A background Weaver call (its own instructions, WEAVER\_CHECKIN) about the running scenario, started after a turn is saved, so nobody waits for it. It is asked at set moments: the scenario reaches its budget (once), a beat fires, or the scenario has judged endings (every CHECKIN\_GAP\_TURNS, 5); at most CHECKIN\_MAX\_PER\_SCENARIO (6) per scenario and 5 turns apart (placeholders); the over-time check-in (budget reached) always happens, never stopped by that cap and not counted toward it (Ali, 12/11/2026, BG-42). It sees the scenario's question, endings, turns and budget used, what has happened in it (ScenarioFrame.happened: code writes one hidden line per spar result, yield, defeat, give, trade, job and deal, the newest CONFIG.SCENARIO\_HAPPENED\_MAX (30, placeholder) kept; BG-41) and the memory lines people on the stage kept since it began, and answers continue (with new beats, triggers owned by actors on the stage) or end (with the ending it reached and a hidden line). The answer applies at the next turn it is back for, to the scenario it was asked about (dropped if that scenario is over); an end waits for a clear turn like any ending (SC13). A failed or invalid answer changes nothing, files a bug (checkin\_failed) and the next moment asks again. **SC12 (Ali, 11/11/2026):** a continue may also change the scenario: a new question, scale or endings (`change`; endings replace the list, checked like a plan's, and the scenario keeps its number, start and records); send people in through ways that already exist (`arrivals`: bands, cast and seeds as in a stage plan, each starting on a stage zone the player cannot see now and walking in from there, never placed by their routine); and add frontiers on stage zones (`frontiers`: {zone, key, label}, a hidden door or a cart track, built later by the normal stage extension). It never adds zones or rooms to a place already seen: the world grows only through frontiers. One bad part drops the whole answer (checkin\_failed). Built in src/scenario/checkin.ts on applyStagePlan's checkin kind.
  - **Code endings** (SC12). The budget leaves out turns spent fighting. At SCENARIO\_OVERRUN\_MULT (2) times its budget, code ends the scenario as worn out, after any fight or chase; the stall floor stays for every scale. Both file a bug (scenario\_overrun, scenario\_stalled), since the check-in should have ended the scenario first.
  - **Stuck** (SC15). /stuck [what is wrong] files a bug (player\_stuck) and asks a check-in at once, past its gap and limit, telling the Weaver the player is stuck: it must give a way forward (a beat or an ending) and never removes a danger. The GM view has an End scenario button (asks to confirm): the scenario ends after the next turn, recorded as interrupted.
  - **The ending moment** (SC11, SD1). As soon as a turn's resolve ends a scenario, before the next scenario's Weaver and the Narrator run, the game says how it ended (GameApi scenarioEnding(): the SCENARIO\_ENDINGS key and its name), so the play page can start the ending moment while those calls run (no false delay). The Narrator is told the ending's name only (NarrationView scenarioEnded, never the hidden question or ending line) and gives one closing sentence for the scenario that ended in `closing` (rule 20), apart from `text`, which tells the rest of the turn and the next opening; a closing on any other turn is dropped, and it is leak-checked with the text. The turn's narration line carries scenarioEnd {as, name, closing}, so the moment shows again after a reload. The play page builds the motion: the closing line, then the scenario break carrying the ending's name in its colour, then the opening.
  A frontier crossing in the same turn goes first; the break waits for the next turn.
- **Barriers on ways** (Scenes Round Three, Ali 11/11/2026: SD3–SD5, BA2–BA8; src/engine/barriers.ts). A way between two zones may carry a barrier: a door, gate, grate, hatch or rubble (library barrierKinds, BK1–BK5: each says whether it takes a lock or a bar, whether it is a blockage, whether sight passes it). One record per barrier (GameState.barriers) serves both sides; each side's link names it by id. Its grade (none to extreme) is how hard it is to pick and how stout it is. No barrier is impassable (SD4): a key, a pick, a word with the guard, or breaking it.
  - **Shut and passing.** A barrier is shut while it is locked, barred or blocked, unless it is broken through: Destroyed is open to everyone, and Crippled lets an amorphous body through (BA7). Walking paths for a walker go round a way shut to it (shortestPath with a walker); distances for sound and sight ignore barriers. When no other way exists, the walker walks up to it and tries it: the move is spent, the event says it was found locked, barred or blocked, and the walker knows it from then on (Barrier.tried). An NPC tries a shut way once.
  - **Open doors** (BG-70, Ali 12/11/2026). A door, gate, grate or hatch can stand open: the Parser's way op "open" (in the move slot) opens it without going through, turning the player's key or sliding the bar on their side as it goes; then sight passes the link as if it were a sight link (relations seesAcross), and views mark it open. Op "shut" shuts it again. Opening one shut against you is trying it (found locked, barred or blocked). An open barrier is never shut; it must be shut before it is locked or barred. NPCs don't open or shut ways yet.
  - **Keys** (BA3). A key is an item (IT40) whose instance names the barriers it opens (ItemInstance.opens). Walking through with it turns the lock in the move slot, and the walk goes on next turn. Locking or unlocking on purpose is the Parser's way op (lock, unlock), which takes the move. The Weaver names who carries a barrier's key (keys); keys can be bought, stolen or looted like any item.
  - **Bars** (BA4). A bar is on one side (bar.side). From that side it slides back in the move slot on the way through, or across or back on purpose (way op bar, unbar). From the other side the way is shut: break it, or get someone inside to open it. Balance waits on the walls thread.
  - **Blocked** (BA5). Rubble is shut to all until it is cleared; clearing and breaking belong to the walls thread (material, thickness, wear: the record leaves room for state, material and thicknessCm).
  - **Guarded** (BA6) is a tag only: the Weaver places guards with orders on that way, and the tag lets the tier counts see it.
  - **Who passes** (BA7). Code paths (routines, follow, flee) treat a way shut to the actor as no way (ActorView map links carry shut); an NPC passes where it holds the key or stands on the bolt's side. Agent-run NPCs may pick like the player (action pick).
  - **What a viewer knows.** The player's and NPCs' zone links show a barrier's kind, that it is blocked or broken, a drawn bar from the bolt's side, and a lock or a bar from the far side only once they tried it or hold its key. The Weaver sees everything.
  - **Picking a lock** (BA2). Lockpicking (SK29) has the Lockpicking function (FN15, in the Sleight group with Filching; FL1). The action pick on the way's zone rolls the function (the best effective skill with it; lockpicks in use add +25) with the lock's grade (CONFIG.LOCK\_GRADE\_MOD: none 0, minor −10, moderate −20, major −35, extreme −50). Success opens it once the picking time is done: out of a fight the time passes at once; in a fight each turn at it adds a fight turn of work. Near Miss: not yet, keep going. Failure: the lock is beyond the picker until its effective skill is higher than at that try. Critical Failure: the pick snaps and the lockpicks used are lost. Picking is a whisper (hushed).
