Time: CONFIG.PICK\_SECONDS\_AT\_50 (60 s, about a minute for a common lock) at effective skill 50, halved per CONFIG.PICK\_HALVING\_PER (25) points above and doubled per 25 below. Scaling: 75 is 30 s and 100 is 15 s. Past 100 it keeps halving (Ali, 12/11/2026): 150 about 4 s, 200 about 1 s, so supernatural fingers open locks at a touch. CONFIG.PICK\_FAST\_CAP (null) would stop the halving at a skill if set. Master or magic locks would add grades past extreme (CR4 grades).
  - **Filching** (FL1–FL7, TN1, TN2, TN4, CT4, CT5; Ali 13/11/2026). Filching (SK36, function FN16) and Lockpicking make the Sleight group (GR11, "Clever fingers on locks and purses that belong to someone else"); each stands in for the other at 0.3 (CONFIG.SUBSTITUTES). The action filch (player and NPCs, grip reach) takes one item from someone or from an owned container: anything carried and not held or worn, a seller's ware, or a share of a purse (FILCH_PURSE_SHARE 0.25), no bigger than FILCH_MAX_BULK (arm-sized). Not guarded (no owner, the owner down or yielded): it is just taken. Guarded: one Filching roll F; everyone outside the filcher's band who sees the act (FILCH_VISIBILITY, Close for now, TN3 open) rolls Sight against F, everyone the sound reaches rolls Hearing; the sound is Hushed, one level louder per tier F fell below Near Miss (TN1). A watcher's roll adds their attention (their latest activity: alert +10, normal 0, distracted −10; talking with the filcher is alert) and the item's size (FILCH_SIZE by bulk: 0, +5, +15, +30). Won and made out clearly: caught; won but only glimpsed or heard partly, or a tie: suspicious (TN2). The worst result decides (FL4): caught, nothing taken, the owner FILCH_CAUGHT_OWNER_STEPS (2) colder and every other catcher FILCH_CAUGHT_WITNESS_STEPS (1), all remember; suspicious, a fumble, nothing taken, the owner suspicious; else taken unseen. A taken item keeps stolenFrom (FL6): its owner sees it marked on the thief and won't buy it back; handing it back clears it. Opening or picking an owned container under watch runs the same contest at size 0 (CT4); a worn pack its wearer guards is only filched from, never opened (CT5). This is the first reader of an activity's attention (D-200 still parks it for sight in general). src/engine/filch.ts.
  - **The tier's lean** (BA8, SD3). The tier leans the ways and never limits them. The Weaver's view carries the words (ways): Critical Failure and Failure lean toward locked, barred or guarded ways at major or extreme grades, with pursuers chasing far; Near Miss a way or two shut, around moderate; Success mostly open, minor at most; Great and Critical Success open ways and a guard who looks away. A tier past Critical reads its end row for now (D-216). Each ended scenario records the barriers its plans made (ScenarioRecord.ways), and the GM view sums them per tier.
  - **Weaver plans.** A plan's link may carry barrier {kind, grade, locked, barredFrom, guarded, keys}. Code checks the kind, that a lock or a bar fits it, that barredFrom is one of the two zones, that it is not on a drop or a frontier, and that each key holder is in the cast or on the stage.
- **Ground, free roam and chases** (Chases and Free Roam, Ali 12/11/2026: CH1–CH7, building SC16–SC19, SC23 and SD2 a; src/engine/chase.ts).
  - **Ground** (CH1, to be reviewed later: D-217). A scenario's ground is the zone the player stood in when it began, every zone its plans built (the opening plan, frontier extensions built while it runs, and frontiers its check-ins added once built), and the stage zones those plans name (links and cast slots); ScenarioFrame.ground. **Whole places (Ali, 12/11/2026, BG-43):** every zone of each place the ground touches is on the ground too (a tavern scenario's ground is the whole tavern: back yard, cellar, upstairs), for leaving and for chase ranges (onGround in src/engine/chase.ts). **Leave endings (Ali, 12/11/2026, BG-44):** a Weaver ending "the player leaves this place" counts only once the player is also off the ground. A wake-up scenario's ground leaves out where the player fell. A fallback stage's ground is its place's zones.
  - **Leaving it** (SC16). Standing off the ground with no one chasing the player ends the scenario as left, after any fight or talk (SC13); if the player is back on the ground by then, the ending lapses. Off the bigger scenario's ground too, both end.
  - **Free roam** (SD2 a). Every scenario ending starts free roam for now, so players can explore the world (Ali, 12/11/2026; CONFIG.ROAM\_AFTER lists the endings that do, every one but broken off): no scenario runs and the Weaver is not called. The world goes on (routines, travel and its encounters). Quiet-turn beats, the stall and check-ins don't run. Frontier extensions are fetched ahead as in a scenario (WB5). A road encounter starts its scenario as always, and a knock-out (broken off) still wakes the player in a new scenario; an inner scenario's end still resumes the bigger one.
  - **What starts a scenario in free roam** (WB5 and WB6, Ali 14/11/2026, on the "World builder and Weaver" page; replaces CH2 and the FR1 b gap). Checked at the end of each turn after the one free roam began (checkRoam, src/engine/chase.ts). **Danger starts one at once:** the player attacks someone or is attacked, or someone hostile to the player spots them (aware or more, seen or heard that turn); the turn plays out, then a new scenario starts with its own event roll and Weaver plan (breakCause roam). **The turn does not wait for the Weaver (BG-238, Ali BF63):** the event roll is made at once and saved (stage.starting, scenario\_held in the log), the turn is shown, and the Weaver plans in the background after the save, on a copy of the state as the start leaves it (ScenarioDirector.holdStart, startPlanning; the call is logged under the next turn, whose record holds the event roll). Free roam goes on for one more turn, people there acting as usual; nothing else opens a window or starts a scenario meanwhile. The scenario starts after that turn, with its opening, from that plan checked again against the stage (awaited if not back yet; planned then, with the saved roll, after a reload or when the player has gone to another place). A knock-out or road encounter that turn starts its own scenario instead and the held one is dropped. A window the Weaver takes starts one the same way. A frontier crossing still waits for its ground. A road encounter starts one as always. Talking and trading start nothing by themselves. **Anything else that matters opens a scenario window** (roamWindow): the player's own acts at any time (taking a job, quest\_made, or making a deal they are party to, agreement\_made: why `job` (SW6 a); reaching a job's goal: `quest`), and past the rest (WB7) an active job taken THREAD\_STALE\_MIN game minutes ago (240, placeholder: `stale`) or a quiet stretch of ROAM\_QUIET\_MIN (120, placeholder: `quiet`). After the save the Weaver is asked in the background (WEAVER_WINDOW_INSTRUCTIONS in src/agents/prompts.ts; answer {start, note}); its answer is applied the next turn (applyWindow): start, and the scenario starts after that turn as above; not now, and no quiet or stale window opens for WINDOW\_DECLINED\_MIN (60, placeholder). A failed call starts the scenario (bug code window\_failed). One window is open at a time (stage.window due, then asked; a reload asks again); danger closes it. **The rest (WB7):** a scenario that ends into free roam sets roam.rest from its challenge grade (ROAM\_REST\_MIN: none 0, minor 30, moderate 60, major 120, extreme 240 game minutes, placeholders); during it only danger and the player's own acts open a window. Storyteller styles are for later. **Why it started (SW3 a, Ali, 14/11/2026):** free roam keeps what happened in it (roam.happened: the scenario's happened lines plus the player's attacks, newest SCENARIO\_HAPPENED\_MAX). A start or a window copies the reason, those lines and who saw the player that turn into stage.cause, and the Weaver's view shows them as `target.cause` {why, happened, seenBy} (for the window call and the plan). The Weaver builds the scenario around it; the tier sets only the luck around it: who saw or heard, who comes, and facts the player does not know yet (who the person they attacked is tied to: a retired master, or nobody). Facts already known stay true. The cause is cleared once the plan is applied, or when a window is declined.
  - **Unbuilt frontiers in free roam** (WB5, reverses CH3 a). Crossing an unbuilt frontier in free roam only builds the ground beyond it, as a frontier extension, with no event roll; extensions are fetched ahead in free roam as in a scenario. A saved zone re-entered through a frontier just loads, and free roam goes on.
  - **Pursuers** (CH4). A pursuer is an active NPC hunting the player (or engaged with them) that has not broken, surrendered or yielded, and whose chase is not lost. While anyone chases the player, leaving the ground does not end the scenario: the scenario follows the chase. This is also the chase that holds an ending (SC13).
  - **Chase range** (CH5, SC19: code-run NPCs only, temporary). Names table CHASE_RANGES, least first: post (never leaves its own place: the place of its routine's first bound zone), ground (the scenario's ground and its own place), near (those and every zone that starts within CONFIG.CHASE\_NEAR\_M, 200 m placeholder, past the ground's edge by the walking path; a pursuer standing that far past it turns back, even partway along one long zone such as a road: ZR12, ActorView self.atChaseLine), far (anywhere, through frontiers after the player). The Weaver may give a band one (bands[].chase); otherwise the template's (slime far, guard ground, barkeep, patron and thief post), else CONFIG.CHASE\_RANGE\_DEFAULT (ground, placeholder). A code-run NPC closing in stops at the edge of its range (ActorView self.chaseZones). With no ground kept for the scenario, ground and near do not limit. Agent-run NPCs decide for themselves. A new range is a new row.
  - **Through a frontier** (CH6, SC17). When the player crosses a frontier while chased, a pursuer that saw the crossing learns where it leads (its last known zone becomes the new zone), and one still hunting that reaches the zone the player crossed from follows too; code-run pursuers only at range far. One that did not see it go and is no longer hunting stops there.
  - **The chase ends** (CH7, SC18, SC23). When no pursuer has seen or heard the player for CONFIG.CHASE\_LOST\_TURNS (3, placeholder) turns, the chase is lost: each pursuer drops to tracking, keeps the memory line "The player fled from me and got away." (the grudge is the price; no roll) and goes back to its routine until it sees or hears the player again (Belief.lostTurn; a lost chase does not count as a fight). Off the ground, the scenario ends as escaped and free roam starts; on it, the scenario carries on. A pursuer that catches the player starts a fight, and the scenario carries on.
  - **Scaling.** No numbers here grow with skill: who catches whom comes from speeds and senses, which already grow with skill and perks, so a supernatural runner outpaces mortal pursuers and supernatural senses keep a chase alive longer, with no special rule. A range past far (hunting the player across the world for days) can be a new row when long hunts come.
- **Quiet turns and beats.** A quiet turn is one where nobody on the stage attacks, the player learns nothing new (no secret, witnessed fact or name), and no beat fires. The stage counts quiet turns in a row and resets after any other turn and at scenario start. Agent Weaver seeds at least one beat per scenario plan: a trigger with when quiet\_turns N, which fires at turn start once the count reaches N.
- **Stage.** A scenario is played on a stage: the set of zones currently loaded. A stage can span several neighbouring places; each zone belongs to exactly one place, and an actor's place is always the place of its zone. Moving between zones or places never starts a scenario; leaving the scenario's ground ends it (SC16, above).
- **Frontiers.** A stage grows through frontiers: labelled exits to areas not yet built. When the player crosses a frontier, code extends the stage (Stage extension) without a new event roll, using the scenario's tier; in free roam too, where it starts no scenario (WB5). A place already set up in this scenario is loaded, not planned again.
- **Loading** a save resumes the current scenario and stage. Encounter modes are chosen per turn inside a scenario. Per-scenario caps (fact promotions, minted items) reset at scenario start.

### Scenario setup

Runs inside the turn that calls scenarioBreak, after resolveTurn and before the save:

1. Resolve the player's place node.
2. Event roll: ruleset.eventRoll(player).
3. Agent Weaver returns a stage plan with the tier in its view: the event first, then the zones (each with its place, creating place nodes as needed) and frontiers, any new bands, the cast (each actor's band, routine set id and slot bindings, with an optional startAt, opening belief about the player, beliefs about other bands and personal stances), hidden seeds and reward refs. The tier shapes the event and cast; opening attention and beliefs are the Weaver's story choice.
4. Code validates the plan and instantiates the cast: templates, unique or copy, body types from the database.
5. Code checks the plan's challenge and gain grades against the tier, and nothing else, then applies it. Code computes each actor's anchorTurn (Anchors) and places the actor in its default routine's desired zone for the current turn, whose activity sets its attention. On open entry only, each opening belief about the player is written through updateBeliefs. New bands and personal stances (Mind.overrides) are installed; each belief about another band becomes one belief per member on the stage (lastKnownZone = that member's zone), written through updateBeliefs whatever the entry. A plan that fails gets one repair retry. After that, routine-only errors degrade the affected actors, and any other error gives the fallback stage (Agent Weaver: Failed stage plan).
6. Code installs each actor's routines list (its set's routines), slot bindings, current routine and anchor, then its knows facts (Mind.knows), plans, triggers and secrets. Each routine carries its one onAlert order; there are no onAlert triggers.
7. Arrival detection: if the player is stealthy, each observer in perception range runs the existing detection test against the player's last move; the plan sets no belief about the player for these observers.
8. Code builds the opening perception events (Opening narration), saves the turn (Part 4 §10) and computes the first turn (Architecture step 1).
9. The turn's Agent Narrator call narrates the arrival with the turn's other events.

Setup details (CS2):

- At story start the player has no zone on the stage yet and starts in the plan's first zone. Their place has no node yet either: code makes a bare one so the Weaver's plan and the fallback can use its id.
- Someone the Weaver brings back (a cast entry naming an actor already in the world) keeps their names; a name in the plan is ignored.
- A seeded trigger whose owner has no plan gets an empty plan (no goal, no steps) to hold it.
- A stage extension keeps the scenario's challenge and gain; its event summary is added after the scenario's, and its hidden terms join the stage's.
- A scenario that starts at a place with no saved zones and no frontier gets one empty zone named after the place.

Story start has no triggering turn: setup runs alone, is saved as turn 0, and one opening Agent Narrator call narrates it. Until turn 0 is saved, the build is kept at pending/start; reopening the game with it there starts the opening again (Ali, 08/11/2026). Turn 0's premise and starting place come from the story's setting, chosen per story (D-1).

**First build:** the premise is the config value STORY\_PREMISE, "A swordsman arrives at a village troubled by slimes", and the story starts in the village tavern.

The player sees the event roll only on the Rolls line, with Show rolls on (ER5): the scenario's first line reads "Event roll d100 63 → Success" with its modifiers. With Show rolls off it stays hidden, and Agent Narrator never gets it.

### Stage extension

- **Background call.** After a turn is saved, if the player's zone links to a frontier that has no held extension, code starts a background Agent World builder call for that frontier (WB1). It gets no tier and no story: it builds the ground beyond the frontier, honouring the frontier's brief (target.brief) when the Weaver gave one, with the same stage plan contract. Without a World builder wired (tests), the Weaver call builds it under the same world rules.
- **Held, not saved \[Proposed\].** The result is held in memory, keyed by frontier, for the rest of the scenario; it is not saved.
- **Crossing.** In the turn the player moves into that frontier, scenario setup steps 4–7 run on the held plan in that turn's setup slot, after resolveTurn and before the save. There is no new event roll and no grade check: frontier ground is world, not story. If the call is not back yet, the turn waits for it.
- **Failure.** A failed background extension is not retried; the fallback stage applies when the player crosses that frontier.
- **NPCs at frontiers** (NW3 A, NW8). An NPC whose move reaches an unbuilt frontier waits at its edge with its move spent (frontier_wait). If the player crosses that frontier the same turn, the NPC follows them through into the new zone, after them. Otherwise its wait starts the same background World builder build the player's frontiers get (target.ahead set, planned for the edge zone's place), at most NPC_BUILDS_PER_TURN (1) a turn and none for a frontier already held; a frontier to a saved zone just loads. When the build is back it is applied before the next turn without moving the player, anyone heading for the frontier now heads for the zone beyond, and the NPC steps in on its next move. If the player reaches that frontier first, the held build is theirs to cross into. Built is not seen: the Map shows the new ground only once the player sees it (S6), which is also how a secret area behind a hidden frontier can exist. In free roam an NPC's build is an extension, never a scenario of its own.
- **Saved zones.** A zone that is saved on its place page but not on the stage is entered again through a frontier link with no Weaver call. Stage trim (dropping far zones once the stage grows past a cap) was never called and is parked as D-225.

### Agent World builder

The Weaver's work is split between two roles (WB1). The World builder makes the world: places, everyday people and the situations that stand in it, with no event roll. The Weaver runs scenarios on top of that world. The story role keeps the name Weaver; the world role is the World builder (WB4).

- **What it writes** (WB2). The World builder writes places, everyday people, traders and the situations that stand in the world (wants, tensions, secrets, goals), in hidden form. It writes no scenario question, endings, beats or rewards, and uses no event roll. Everyday people are as strong as the region's people really are; they don't scale with the hero.
- **When it runs** (WB8). It writes the region brief (Land and regions) and builds the ground beyond frontiers (Stage extension). The scenario start changes are built with SW10 on the scenario event rolls page.
- **During a scenario** (WB3). The Weaver adds people, items, beats and situations. New ground comes only from story spots and frontiers, drawn by the World builder from the Weaver's brief. A frontier in a Weaver plan or check-in may carry a brief (one short line, kept hidden in hidden.frontierBriefs by frontier id); the World builder's view gets it as target.brief and builds to it.
- **WorldView** is the WeaverView without the tier, stakes, ways, recent scenarios or outer scenario. Its previousNote is the World builder's own note (hidden.worldNote), kept apart from the Weaver's.
- **Plan checks.** Its plan goes through the same validator and apply as a Weaver plan, with no grade check. Code drops any story part it returns (scenario, rewards, triggers; the event is set to none) and logs world_story_dropped.

Fixed instructions **\[Proposed\]**: the exact text is WORLD_BUILDER_INSTRUCTIONS in src/agents/prompts.ts; the region brief is WORLD_BUILDER_REGION_INSTRUCTIONS in src/agents/prompts.ts. Rules 3 to 6 are shared with the Weaver's text (PLAN_RULES_SHARED).

### Agent Weaver

Agent Weaver plans what a scenario is about and who is in it. It runs at scenario setup (inside the turn) and for stage extensions (in the background). It is hidden-side, like Agent Planner: it may see the hidden store, and none of its hidden output ever reaches Agent Parser, Agent Narrator or Agent Voice.

**WeaverView** (built deny-by-default, like every view):

- the tier and its stakes group;
- the place or frontier being planned, by id and name, and its ancestors' headlines;
- where the player stands (player.standsIn: place id, place name, zone);
- the stage sorted by place (NW6): each place by name, kind and parent, with its zones and the actors in them, each actor with its plans and secrets. Nobody on the stage is hidden, so far-off people can turn up when the story fits; sorting by place is what keeps the Weaver planning in the right town (the flat zone and actor lists made it lose the thread in tests);
- quests and flags; the bands on the stage, with their goals and relations;
- the player's level, current class and health-track level;
- the previous Weaver note;
- catalogs: the library and blueprint ids it may use (templates, body types, routine sets with their brief, tags and slot hints, items). Catalogs change only with libraryVersion and sit right after the fixed instructions as a stable prefix (OQ-11).

Never in WeaverView: skill growth, Burden or Potential (Part 1 §2); this turn's player text.

Output shape (notation, not literal JSON):

```
{
  event:       { summary: hidden text, challenge: grade, gain: grade },
  places:      [ { key, name, kind, parent: placeKey | placeId } ],   // new place nodes only
  zones:       [ { key, place: placeKey | placeId, kind: zone kind id, name, look: public text,
                   cover?: none | some | heavy, light?: bright | dim | dark,   // keys of COVER and LIGHT; default from the kind
                   links: [ { to: zoneKey | zoneId | frontierKey, label, sight } ] } ],
  frontiers:   [ { key, label } ],
  bands:       [ { key, name: public text, goals: hidden text, temperament: hidden text,
                   tactics: 'simple' | 'competent' | 'cunning', lethal: true | false,   // keys of TACTICS (src/scales.ts)
                   relations: { [bandKey | bandId]: hostile | opposed | neutral | friendly } } ],
                                    // new bands only; may also set relations toward existing bands
  cast:        [ { template: templateId | actor: actorId | compose: { race, role, theme, rank, class? },   // NG15: catalog IDs; code builds the sheet
                   templateGap?: hidden text,        // NG15: who it wanted when no template or composition fit; flagged in the GM view
                   label: public text, name, appearance: public text, pronouns: he | she | they | it,   // public; stored on the actor and shown to every agent that sees them (run 14 Q3)
                   knownOnSight?: true, knows?: [ { id, text: public text } ],   // facts this actor may share (Agent Voice); capped
                    key, band: bandKey | bandId, routineSetId,
                   slots: { <slot>: zoneKey | zoneId | [zoneKey | zoneId, ...] },   // every slot the set lists
                   startAt?: <slot>,                 // where in the loop to start; code computes the anchor
                   routineGap?: hidden text,         // what it wanted when no set fit; capped; GM view only
                   awarenessOfPlayer: unaware | suspicious | aware,      // what this actor knows of the player
                   awarenessOfBands?: [ { band: bandKey | bandId, awareness: unaware | suspicious | aware } ],
                                                                         // about every member of that band
                   overrides?: [ { actor: castKey | actorId, stance: hostile | opposed | neutral | friendly } ],
                   motive: hidden text } ],   // agenda is derived from seeded plans and secrets
  seeds:       { plans: [Plan], triggers: [Trigger], secrets: [Secret] },
  rewards:     [ { item: itemId | { base: libraryItemId, quality: 1 | 2 | 3 | 4, boosts: string,
                                    name: public text, look: public text },   // minted variant
                 qty?: number,
                 place: { carriedBy: castKey | actorId } | { groundIn: zoneKey | zoneId } } ],
  hiddenTerms: [ string ],     // words that must not appear in public text
  weaverNote:  hidden text     // replaces the previous note; keep it short
}
grade = none | minor | moderate | major | extreme
```

**Lethal.** Each entry in bands carries lethal: true | false, default true. It is saved on the band, and an existing band keeps its value. Agent Weaver sets false only for a conflict nobody means to end in death, such as a hostage-taking; monsters such as slimes are lethal. A spar is not a band setting: it has its own engagement kind (Sparring). Code reads lethal when the player goes down (D-54, built 09/11/2026): if any active actor hostile to the player belongs to a lethal band and perceives the player by any sense (Ali, 13/11/2026: a foe that can't sense you can't finish you), the story ends on the "You fell" screen, unless a foe is asked for mercy (D-239, below). Otherwise (bands marked not lethal, a spar, or nobody left standing to finish the job) the player makes one survival roll, roll() with no skill and no modifiers: Near Miss or better wakes at Critical and the scenario breaks (cause knocked\_out); the Weaver gets target.wake and plans a new place whose first zone is where the player wakes, somewhere safe. Failure or worse ends the story the same way. A wake plan with no new zone fails validation (wake\_needs\_zone).

**Mercy once down** (D-239, Ali 13/11/2026, OE5 b and OE7; built). When the player goes down in a lethal fight, each of those lethal foes (all of them perceive the player) that is run by Claude, of an Intelligent race, is asked instead: its next output answers spare (true spares, false finishes; no answer finishes), shown to it as self.playerAtMercy. The others finish. Sparing covers the sparer's bandmates together with it that were asked too (YD2). The game plays that next turn by itself while the player lies there, as a sleeper's turn. The player is spared only when someone spared and no one is left to finish (a finisher downed meanwhile no longer counts): spared works like a won survival roll, waking at Critical somewhere safe (cause knocked\_out). The fight counts as lost, and for the stronger-foe survive chains, never the win chains. Otherwise the story ends on the "You fell" screen. Codes: mercy\_asked, mercy\_spared, mercy\_finish, player\_spared, player\_fell.

**Challenge and gain grades.** A stage plan declares a challenge grade (how hard the scenario is for this player: level, class, health) and a gain grade (what it can pay out: loot, XP, information, allies, quest progress). Code accepts the pair only if it fits the tier:

| Tier | Challenge | Gain |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Critical Failure | Major or extreme | None or minor |
| Failure | Moderate or higher | None or minor |
| Near Miss | Minor to major | One step below challenge, none to moderate |
| Success | Minor to major | Within one step of challenge |
| Great Success | None to moderate | Major |
| Critical Success | Major or extreme, the same as Critical Failure | Extreme |

A Critical Success is as dangerous as a Critical Failure (D-247): what sets it apart is the opportunity inside the danger, not a milder danger.

Grades are hidden-side and never reach Agent Narrator. A computed cross-check (cast and rewards against the declared grades) is a stub that passes for the cast; rewards are held to the gain's Boon budget (Part 5 §7, loot V8).

**Validation.** Code checks every stage plan before it touches state:

- every id resolves (story blueprints first, then the library); an unknown body type fails (Part 3);
- caps on cast size, zones and frontiers (placeholders); every cast entry names a band, every band reference resolves (the plan's new bands, then the story), and relations and stances use only hostile, opposed, neutral or friendly;
- every zone link points to a zone in the plan, on the stage, or a frontier;
- triggers use the trigger vocabulary and hold one order each, of a type the engine knows (ORDER_TYPES); every secret has leakTerms; a check\_passed secret names a library skill;
- the grades fit the tier;
- no public field contains a hiddenTerm or a secret's leakTerm, matched as whole words (as the Narrator's leak scan matches);
- public text respects TEXT\_CAP (placeholder). Hidden-only notes (event summary, band goals and temperament, motive, routineGap, plan goal, secret fact, Weaver note) are not rejected for length: code trims them to NOTE\_CAP at a word boundary.
- rewards never fail a plan; code repairs them and logs each repair (reward\_repaired): a reward with an unknown item, base or place, or one that would take the plan over its Boon budget (Part 5 §7), is dropped; a bad qty becomes 1; a minted variant past the per-scenario cap, at a gain that allows none, or whose name or look is too long or uses a hidden term is placed as its plain base item; a quality above what the gain allows (Part 5 §7) is lowered to it; a weapon's boosts other than hitAdj or damageAdj becomes hitAdj (loot L1, V7);
- every routineSetId resolves; a player-band entry names the companion set and binds no slots;
- every slot the set lists is bound: a zone slot to one zone, a route slot to 1 to MAX\_ROUTE\_ZONES zones (one zone: the actor stays there); bound keys are zones in the plan or on the stage, never frontiers; startAt names one of the set's slots;
- every bound zone is reachable from the actor's other bound zones through zone links;
- routineGap respects its length cap (placeholder).
- every knows fact is public text: it respects its length and count caps (placeholders) and is scanned like other public fields;

**Defaults filled before validation.** A trigger inside an NPC plan with no ownerId belongs to the plan's owner; a trigger with no once fires once; a place with no parent (missing or null) is top-level, and a new place may name another new place as its parent; a plan with no steps gets an empty list. Validator errors are listed once each, with a hint where the Weaver mixes fields up (a stance in an awareness field; a secret in knows). The repair retry sends the rejected plan back with its errors and asks for only those fixes.

**Failed stage plan.** A plan that fails validation gets one repair retry with the validator's errors. If it fails again, or the call fails, or (scenario start only) it passes WEAVER\_TIMEOUT (config), code builds a fallback stage: the place's saved zones, or one empty zone per frontier label; no new cast; grades none/none. The fallback is flagged so it cannot be missed:

1. The stage record carries fallback: { reason, turn } until the next scenario start.
2. The turn record logs weaver\_fallback with the validator's errors.
3. The GM debug view shows a red banner while any fallback area is on the stage.
4. The play screen shows a small out-of-character marker ("Scenario planning failed: placeholder area"), with no details, until the next scenario start.

**Routine-only failures \[Proposed\].** After the repair retry, a plan whose only errors are routine errors still applies, minus those errors. Routine errors are an unresolved set, a missing or invalid slot binding, an unreachable bound zone or a bad startAt. Each actor with an error gets the library routine idle\_here: a wait routine with activity rest, here bound to its first valid bound zone, else the plan's first zone. Code logs routine\_fallback with the validator's errors, and sets the same four flags as a fallback stage.

**Model.** Agent Weaver runs on Claude Opus 5.5 for both scenario setup and stage extensions: model "claude-opus-5-5", with modelTier "complex" as the fallback, and effort always set explicitly (Opus 5.5 defaults to medium). The artifact runtime (contract 0.2.96) accepts only low, medium and high, so the build sets the Weaver's effort to "high" (config AGENT\_MODELS), never max (D-33 closed). The agent log records modelApplied for every Weaver call.

Fixed instructions **\[Proposed\]**: the exact text is WEAVER_INSTRUCTIONS in src/agents/prompts.ts, the one copy (CS6; the spec no longer copies it, a test keeps it so). In short, it decides what is happening at a place and who is there so the scenario fits the dealt tier, as a stage plan within the plan caps. Never narrates, never decides outcomes, never rolls dice. The check-in has its own text, WEAVER_CHECKIN_INSTRUCTIONS. The region brief (Land and regions) belongs to the World builder (WORLD_BUILDER_REGION_INSTRUCTIONS): it describes a region in words for code to draw, and never gives coordinates. In plans the Weaver sees the land in words (WEAVER_VIEW.land: sites, roads, landmarks, what lies beyond) and may stand a top-level place on a free site (`site`) or a road zone along a road (`road`).

### Opening narration

At scenario setup step 8, code adds to the turn's events an arrival event for the player and a presence event for each actor the player perceives on arrival, computed by computePerception (level full or partial, with a cue). Agent Narrator also receives the player's zone and the zones linked to it with sight (frontiers by label), and visible state per Part 3. Every full presence event must be listed in "narrated" (fact check); partial ones may be left out. Each presence event carries the actor's current activity look, which is public text. A look of keeping out of sight (an activity seen only from close) is not shown for someone in a talk who is not stealthy: talking openly, they are plainly not hiding (BG-133).

Never in NarrationView: the tier, grades or event roll; the event summary, Weaver note, plans, triggers or secrets; opening beliefs or attention; or any tone or mood hint derived from the tier.

**Public and hidden text.** Every free-text field in a stage plan is either public (what anyone present could see or hear: labels, appearance, how a zone looks) or hidden (event summary, motives, notes). Only public fields reach Agent Narrator. The plan lists hiddenTerms: words and phrases that must not appear in public text. At validation, code scans public fields for hiddenTerms and for every secret's leakTerms; a hit fails validation (repair retry, then fallback). While the stage is active, the Narrator's leak scan also checks its hiddenTerms.

**Names.** Each cast entry has a public label ("a scarred dockhand") and a name. Agent Narrator gets the label until the player knows the name. The Journal gains namesKnown, heardOf and aliases, and each NPC mind the same three. One rule for player and NPCs (BG-95, knowsName): everyone knows their own name, a bandmate's and a public figure's (the Weaver marks knownOnSight). Any other name is learned by hearing it in a spoken line, passing remarks included (NM6), whose speaker knows it, while seeing clearly whom it belongs to (the speaker, or someone in view): learnNames. NM2: a name heard of someone out of sight is kept as heard of (PLAYER_VIEW.heardOf lists them); it attaches when its owner answers a line that called them by it, or is named in view. NM3: a name the speaker gives for themselves that is not theirs ("Name's Tam") is a lie: an NPC's line that does so is deceive even if its agent called it talk, so its listener answers it with Insight, the player or (D-155, NL1–NL4) an NPC it was aimed at. Each listener who sees the speaker and did not see through it calls them by the false name until it learns the real one. A line holds a name when it has it whole or its first word that is not a title (CONFIG.NAME_TITLE_WORDS), with capitals kept. An NPC's view of the player carries the name it has for them. The existing leak scan already rejects names outside the view.

### Explore mode

In Explore no Planner runs: unaware actors follow routines, the player is detected only on major actions, and an attack needs an ambush roll to surprise.

#### Routines

- Each actor follows one routine at a time from its routines list, the routines of one library routine set. A routine is a desired-state function (Game state model). At scenario setup Agent Weaver picks the set and binds its slots; code sets the anchor.
- Each turn, every active actor with no higher-priority order reads its desired zone and activity from its ActorView. Elsewhere: it moves one zone link along the shortest path. There: it performs the activity. Actors are processed in actor-id order; ties use the seeded RNG. If no path to the desired zone exists on the stage, the actor stays in its zone, performing the step's activity, and code logs routine\_unreachable once, with the zone it stands in and the zone it can't reach (CS22; src/engine/resolve.ts noteUnreachable). It logs again only after its routine walks on or it gets there. Whether a stage trim may cut off a bound zone stays deferred (D-46).
- **Clear sight (Ali, 07/11/2026, Q17).** The player's Journal keeps clearSight: each actor the player has seen fully by sight (or made out with a successful look), with the zone it was in. While that actor stays in that zone, and the zone is the player's own or in sight of it, its events reach the player as full sight, dark zones included. A move or flee the player does not see fully removes it; one the player does see records the new zone.
- **Seen leaving (BG-23).** A move that leaves a zone is perceived where it set off as well as where it ended, and the better sight counts: someone walking out through a door in plain sight is seen going, even into a room out of sight. A full sight of it records the zone they went into in clearSight, so lastSeen names it ("the barkeep, last seen in the kitchen").
- **Last known place (BG-28).** The Journal also keeps lastKnown: the zone of every actor the player has seen or heard (any perception, a sound included), never forgotten by an unseen move. lastSeen reads it, so someone heard running off is still named where they were heard, and the Parser never asks who the player means when one of them fits.
- Moves and activities emit ordinary move and idle events with the activity's noise and visibility, and go through computePerception, so the player perceives patrols (a partial event, sound only).
- An activity's attention level (the ATTENTION scale) is not read yet: sight assumes everyone is looking (parked as D-200).
- Actors that aren't engaged keep following their routines during Tactical turns too.

#### Stealthy posture

Ali, 08/11/2026, ST1–ST10.

- A sneak action sets player.posture = 'stealthy' (display name "Stealthy", POSTURES scale). It stays over moves until the player acts openly.
- Sneak costs nothing: it keeps the turn's move and main action. It is offered in the action deck's Stealth group (there is no Hide action, D-327); code picks the skill (Sneaking, or Combat Stealth while engaged or attacking).
- What ends it: speaking above a whisper, any speech action, and a melee attack (the blow shows you). A ranged attack keeps it; the shot is noticed only through the stealth contest (Combat Stealth) per observer.
- While stealthy, the player's events reach only observers already aware of them; everyone else is reached only through the contest.
- **Situation tags** (ST21–ST22, Ali 08/11/2026). Situational modifiers apply by tag: each skill lists its situation tags (docs/skill-tags.md), and each situational table names the tags it reaches and the sign (CONFIG.SITUATION\_TAGS). Light (LIGHT\_MODS, read where the action is) hurts sight (every attack, Block, Dodge, Sight) and helps stealth (the three Stealth skills). Cover (COVER\_GAP, in tiers) helps hide (all three Stealth skills: HA1) and hurts a ranged attack against a target in cover (a gap shift, as before). An action can add a tag (a stash adds hide). Staying put (a Vanish in place, or hiding from a search) ignores the move role, so leg wounds don't count (ST10). In a stealth contest both rolls get their own tags' tables, so darkness also hurts the observer's Sight. Darkness never hurts hearing, so against an observer who only hears the player, light gives the stealth roll no help either (Ali, 08/11/2026, P14). Leg wounds stay on body-part roles. Hearing and Tremor carry a hearing tag (Ali, 08/11/2026); no table reads it yet (the noise work will).
- **Unblinking** (ST23, the sight 60 perk): an observer with it who loses a Vanish must also lose a Sneaking or Combat Stealth contest before losing the player.
- Sneak never shakes off someone who already sees you (ST12): those observers get no roll.
- **Vanish** (ST11–ST19) is active hiding: a move that breaks established sight, so it needs a destination one link away (with none, the Parser asks where). Which part of the turn it uses is a balance lever for later (ST13). Every observer who could perceive the move contests it against the player's Vanishing. One who saw the player and loses it drops to suspicious at the zone the player left (test 3); a tie or a win changes nothing for them. For everyone else a Vanish counts as a passed Sneaking or Combat Stealth check (ST16). Light and cover help it, and leg wounds count.
- For a foe who still sees you, being Stealthy changes nothing: they keep seeing you and the engagement goes on (ST15).
- A shot's target that loses the notice contest still turns suspicious, at the shooter's zone (ST19).
- Each observer gets at most one stealth contest per turn.
- **NPC stealth** (NS1–NS10, Ali 08/11/2026, settles OQ-14). Every actor follows the same rules: posture, the three Stealth skills, the contests, Vanish and Unblinking. An NPC's sneak or vanish names the zone it goes to (Planner actions sneak and vanish); its hide is the main action, as for the player. A stealthy NPC's events reach only its own band and observers who see it (aware). Observers are everyone outside its band, the player included, so NPCs contest each other too (NS5). The Planner decides when an NPC goes stealthy (NS8).
- **Your belief about a stealthy NPC** (NS2–NS4). You roll for yourself like any observer (NS3). You simply see open actors. For each stealthy one you keep a belief (journal.stealthSeen): unaware hides it, suspicious shows "someone", aware shows it as usual. When an NPC goes stealthy you keep it as far as you see it then (ST12); a stealthy NPC's move you don't perceive at all drops aware to suspicious. When it turns open the belief is cleared.
- **Ambush on the player** (NS6). A stealthy NPC that attacks you while you are unaware of it makes the ambush roll against your perception, as you do against NPCs. A melee blow still shows it.
- **Companions** (NS9). A member of your band on a follow routine copies your posture each turn. A routine can be stealthy (the thief's prowl and lie-low), which keeps the actor stealthy while it follows it.
- **Follow order** (BG-34, Ali 11/11/2026). Any NPC's agent (Planner, Responder or Voice) may switch it to the follow routine when it agrees to come along with the player, whatever its routine set; its deal says why. From then on it walks after the player's last known zone every turn: on code-run turns by the routine, on agent-run turns as a move beside whatever action the agent gave (unless that action is itself a move, or it is in a fight). It ends when the agent switches back to one of the NPC's own routines. A fight, morale break or no path pauses it. Bandmates never take follow from a spokesperson. NPCs never path through a drop (SD7).
- **Head to** (NW2). When an NPC's agent (Planner, Responder or Voice) sends it somewhere further than one move, its output names headTo: a zone or a frontier on the stage. Code switches the NPC to the shared head_to routine (in no set), bound to that place in slot dest, and keeps the routine it had. It walks there one way at a time, by the routine on code-run turns and as a move beside the agent's action on agent-run turns, as follow does. Bound for a frontier, it walks to the nearest zone with a way into it and steps in from there (NPCs at frontiers below). Once it stands in the destination it goes back to the routine it had, anchor kept, and walks on from there. A second headTo on the way changes only where it is going. A place not on the stage is refused (invalid_head_to). The same routine carries a yielded foe to its exit at the scenario's end (NW1), and a reused actor to its new start (NW7). When the agent takes someone along (lead: their id), the NPC waits at the end until they arrive or LEAD_WAIT_S (config, placeholder 90 game seconds) pass, then goes back to its routine (BF4).
- **No jumps** (NW1, NW7). An NPC the player has met always stands in a real zone and only ever walks. When a Weaver plan brings back an existing actor, it stays where it is: if the player can't perceive it, it heads to its new start by head_to and then takes up its new routine; if the player perceives it, it is not sent anywhere and its new routine walks it on from where it stands. A Weaver plan may still place a new actor anywhere in it.
- **The village thief** (NS7): template thief (Sneaking 40, Combat Stealth 30, Vanishing 30, Light Melee 35, Light Armor 25, Dodge 30, Sight 30, Hearing 30; dagger and gambeson) with the routine set thief (prowl, lie\_low\_alert, wait, wait\_alert).

#### Detecting the player

- Detection happens only on the player's major actions. isMajorAction() is a stub that counts every player action as major until the term is defined (D-34).
- Each observer who could perceive the action (the existing perception range) and is unaware or suspicious of the player gets one test:
  - Sneaking player: the observer's perception roll against the player's stealth roll (ruleset.contested).
  - Open player: no detection roll. The observer's perception level of the event (computePerception, after any make-out roll below) decides: full makes it aware, partial makes it suspicious, none leaves it unaware.
- One test per observer per action: for a hide or sneak, the stealth contest in the simultaneous-order rules (Resolution engine) is the detection test, and detectPlayer skips observers already contested on that event. To be revisited (D-40).
- The result goes to updateBeliefs, which stays the only writer of Belief. A won contest makes the observer aware and a tie makes it suspicious; against an open player, full perception makes it aware and partial makes it suspicious (settles OQ-5, Ali, 07/11/2026).
- Observers unaware of the player don't perceive the player's minor actions at all.

#### Making out what is half seen or half heard

Ali, 14/11/2026, PG3–PG9 all a (Questions hub page "Passive Eyesight and Earshot growth"); src/engine/makeOut.ts, perception faintness.

- **When** (PG3 a). Whenever an observer, the player or an NPC, perceives an event of someone not hiding only in part (glimpsed past the clear range or in the dark, or heard below the clear margin), it rolls that sense automatically, with no command: Sight when glimpsed, Hearing when only heard. Someone stealthy is left to the stealth contests; touch is never rolled; a sleeper perceives nothing.
- **How hard** (PG4 a). A sight or hearing function roll against a task grade (gradeMod). Sight: how far past the visibility's clear range it is, as a share of the way to its glimpse range (CONFIG.SIGHT\_M): up to 25% Minor, 50% Moderate, 75% Major, else Extreme (MAKE\_OUT\_SIGHT\_GRADES); darkness where it happens is MAKE\_OUT\_DARK\_STEPS (1) harder, which Night Eyes cancels; the easier of where it happened and where a leg set off. Sound: the dB it reaches above HEARING\_FLOOR\_DB: 11 or more Minor, 7 Moderate, 3 Major, else Extreme (MAKE\_OUT\_SOUND\_GRADES). No light modifier: darkness is in the grade. All placeholders.
- **What it gives** (PG5 a). Success or better turns that perception full: who it is, what they do, and their words when heard. The Q17 rule then keeps a still person seen clearly.
- **Named everywhere** (BG-252, PG11 a). Someone made out by sight counts as seen clearly that turn wherever the game shows who is seen (seenActors: Who's here, the Narrator's people, the actor views, name learning by seesClearlyNow), for every observer (src/perception/madeOut.ts keeps the turn's results). The player keeps anyone seen clearly (by distance, a look or a make-out roll) named while they stay put and can still be glimpsed: the glimpse range of what they did, never less than a still figure's (Low), in the dark too (stillGlimpsed). A half-seen move forgets it, and a new make-out roll decides.
- **How often** (PG6 a). At most once a turn for each observer, person and sense; that result holds for the person's other events that turn.
- **Words** (PG8 a). Someone seen clearly whose words reach the observer only as a voice: the observer rolls Hearing (the sound grade) and a success catches the words.
- **Learning** (PG7 a). It learns like any check against its grade (with PG2 a, any pass teaches).
- **Shown** (PG9 a). Only the player's roll is kept (GameEvent.madeOut), and only the Rolls panel shows it (Show rolls on); the narration tells what was made out.
- **Scaling.** Chance of Success or better: Minor 45 / 70 / 82 / 95% at skill 0 / 50 / 75 / 100, Extreme 22 / 47 / 60 / 72%; at 150 every grade is 95%. Past about 150 it stops mattering, because the clear and glimpse ranges and the hearing floor are human numbers: senses past 100 widening those ranges is parked as D-321 (PG10 a).

#### Searching

Ali, 11–13/11/2026, SE1–SE25 (questions page "Search redesign"); fight reach SA8 b (page "Search achievements"). A search may be repeated every turn with a fresh roll (SE12 a); its event is quiet and normally seen (SE13). Use, interact and other keep the scene-number check until their own deep dive (SE18 a; the numbers are D-7). Look around has no scene number (SE15).

- **The Search action** (SE1, SE19). A search is the main action: 6 s in a fight turn, 1 minute outside one; the move slot stays free. The Parser's action is "search" with target null (around the player) or a person in view; code picks the sense, so its skill is left empty. The action deck offers Search next to Look around.
- **What it covers** (SE20, SE21, SE24, SA8). The searcher's own area: all of it when the area is no bigger than the search area, else the search area around the searcher (a circle of that many m²). Search area = SEARCH\_AREA\_M2 (60 m², a tavern common room) × the rolling skill ÷ SEARCH\_AREA\_SKILL\_BASE (50), × (1 + the owner's searchArea % ÷ 100), + SEARCH\_M2\_PER\_FIGHT\_M (120) m² per metre of searchReach. A fight turn reaches only search area ÷ SEARCH\_M2\_PER\_FIGHT\_M metres around the searcher. PerkDef and AchievementDef carry searchArea (%) and searchReach (m); every source adds up (achievements along their chain, plus active perks). All placeholders.
- **Against a hiding person** (SE2 b, SE3, SE4 c). Everyone stealthy in reach whom the searcher doesn't already see (aware) is contested: the searcher's sight or hearing function, whichever is better after light, against the hider's Sneaking (Combat Stealth in an engagement) with light, cover and the hide tag, as in the passive stealth contest, with no search bonus. A win makes the searcher aware (the player keeps them seen while they stay put), a tie suspicious ("someone"), a loss changes nothing. A search aimed at one person contests only them.
- **Nothing to find** (SE6). With nobody hidden in reach nothing is rolled. A lost roll reads the same as an empty spot; the search's dice show only with a find.
- **One contest per turn** (SE5). A search always rolls, even after a passive contest that turn, and counts as that pair's contest.
- **Hidden items** (SE8, SE10). An item on the ground can lie hidden (ItemInstance.hiddenAt, an outcome tier) at its point (or its area's centre). Only those who know where it lies (knownTo) see it or can pick it up. A search makes one sight function roll, in the area's light, against every hidden item in reach it doesn't know of: a result above an item's tier finds it, the same tier gives a hint ("something here has been moved") and finds nothing. Nothing hidden: no roll. The Weaver may hide a ground reward (place.hiddenAt), story items included for now (SE10, flagged for later review).
- **Stashing** (SE9). The Parser's action "stash" with a carried item is the main action: a stealth function roll with light, cover and the hide tag, as for a hide. Its tier becomes the item's hidden-at tier, and the item lies where the stasher stands. The stasher, and anyone who sees the stash clearly, know where it is. A stash is a hushed sound and a low-visibility sight.
- **Searching a person** (SE11). Search aimed at someone in view. Hands-on (within grip reach of someone down, asleep, surrendered or held by the searcher) finds all their gear, concealed and stowed, with no roll. Anyone else is only looked over: one sight roll against each concealed item they wear (ItemDef.hiddenAt, hidden blade Great Success, placeholder), found when it beats that tier. What is found is revealed to the searcher (the player's journal.revealed, an NPC's belief) and shows in its view of them, stowed items included. Consent (a willing person) is not built: there is no way yet to tell that someone agrees.
- **Look around** (SE14). Look around is also a search against everyone hiding within its range (normal's clear reach, 25 m, in sight), with the sight roll it already uses, LOOK\_SEARCH\_SHIFT (−1) tiers down.
- **Marks** (SE7, SE25 a). Marks are invisible. Both sides of a contest against a hider mark as in the passive contest. An item search learns like a check against the best-hidden item it searched for: a stashed item keeps its stasher's true stealth skill (ItemInstance.hiderSkill) and that is the number; an item no one stashed (Weaver-hidden, concealed gear) counts its hidden-at tier as HIDDEN\_TIER\_SUCCESS\_SKILL (50) at Success and HIDDEN\_TIER\_SKILL\_STEP (25) more or less per tier step (Great Success 75, Critical Success 100, +25 per step beyond; never under 0). A stash learns against the best true sight skill of those who see it clearly, and learns nothing unwatched.
- **Secrets** (SE17). A player's search aimed at someone also rolls, once per sense, for that person's unrevealed check\_passed secrets whose skill is a sight or hearing skill: a roll at the secret's minTier or better reveals it (journal.secretsKnown; effect found\_secret) and shows the dice. It learns against the secret's minTier on the same table. None to find: no roll.
- **NPCs** (SE16). NPCs search by the same rule, the player included as a hider. The combat fallback's search and the alert sweep (search\_route\_alert, which searches at each stop) use it.
- **Scaling.** The area grows in a straight line with the rolling skill and never caps: 60 m² at 50, 90 at 75, 120 at 100, 180 at 150, 240 at 200; fight reach 0.5, 0.75, 1, 1.5, 2 m. The contest itself has the shared ceiling past about 200 (D-211, D-213; SE22). At 50 a fight-turn search reaches 0.5 m, inside the 1 m people stand apart (PLACE\_GAP\_M), so it finds someone only once it reaches past 1 m (skill 100, or a reward).

#### Ambush: attacking from Explore

When the player attacks an actor that is unaware of them, code resolves the ambush before the blow:

1. The player makes one ambush roll, contested separately against the perception roll of the target and of every other observer who could perceive the attack. Which skill makes the ambush roll is a ruleset matter (stub). The perception is the attack event's own if it was already worked out, otherwise it is worked out then, before the blow (CS2).
2. **Player beats the target: surprise.** The target keeps its routine order this turn. The target's defense roll takes the unaware situational modifier (value placeholder). The ruleset may also apply an unaware tag to its initiative.
3. **Target wins: failed ambush.** The target becomes aware before the blow and defends normally. Its onAlert fires: this turn if it hasn't acted, otherwise next turn unless it then has an agent order. It is engaged from the next turn, and a significant target's Planner order outranks onAlert. Running a Planner for failed ambushes in the same turn is deferred (D-36).
4. **A witness wins.** It becomes aware and its onAlert fires the same way. If it is hostile to the player it is engaged from the next turn; otherwise it is alarmed.
5. **Nobody wins, or nobody perceives the attack.** Observers stay unaware. A kill nobody perceives leaves the game in Explore.

#### Alarm

- A non-engaged actor that perceives an attack or an alarm becomes alarmed. The onAlert order of the routine it is following fires once (Hidden state persistence). Code saves that routine and its anchor in Mind.alarm.returnTo, and the actor switches to that routine's alert variant, anchored at the current turn.
- It calms after DECAY\_AWARE\_S (120 game seconds) with no attack or alarm perceived and returns to the routine in returnTo with its saved anchor, so a schedule continues in phase. An actor whose routine has no alert variant keeps that routine throughout. If an agent switches the actor's routine while it is alarmed, the switch stands and returnTo is cleared. The return to routine uses the timer only, but the alarm stays armed (its onAlert does not fire again) while the actor still knows of someone it is wary of (CS2).

### Latency and cost

Without streaming, the player waits for the whole turn, so the parallel parts matter more.

| Mode | Wait after submit |
| --- | --- |
| Explore | Parser + Narrator |
| Dialogue | Parser + the spokespeople's calls (in parallel) + Narrator, shown no earlier than DIALOGUE\_WINDOW after submit |
| Tactical | Parser + Narrator (the Planner already ran in the background) |
| Turn that starts a scenario | Parser + Weaver + Narrator |
| Turn that crosses a frontier | Parser + Narrator (the Weaver ran in the background), plus the Weaver's remainder if it is late |

- **Expected waits.** From the runtime docs: on the quick tier a short prompt answers in 1–2 s; on the default and complex tiers Claude thinks first, so first text usually comes after 5–60 s, and up to 2 minutes for a long structured prompt. The Narrator and the Weaver on thinking tiers set the turn's wait. The first Claude call of a session also waits on the viewer's consent dialog. Set BACKGROUND\_WINDOW and DIALOGUE\_WINDOW from times measured in playtests, not from these ranges.
- **Background Planner.** The Planner never needs this turn's input, so engaged actors' Planner calls run while the player reads the last narration. Background Weaver calls for frontiers run the same way.
- **Model per job.** The Parser on Claude Sonnet 5.5, model "claude-sonnet-5-5" at the platform's usual effort with modelTier "quick" as the fallback (it ranked above the quick tier in blind judging and answered faster); the Planner, Responder and Voice on Claude Opus 5.5 at low effort, model "claude-opus-5-5" with modelTier "default" as the fallback (it beat the default tier in blind judging); the Narrator on Claude Fable 5.2 at low effort, model "claude-fable-5-2" with modelTier "complex" as the fallback (it beat the complex tier in blind judging and playtests); the Weaver on Claude Opus 5.5 (Agent Weaver: Model). Named models and efforts live in config AGENT\_MODELS.
- **Runtime check.** The artifact's Claude-call capability (sample, runtime 0.2.96) accepts a model tier (quick, default, complex) or a named model, plus effort (low, medium, high), so agents can run on different models. It has no system prompt, no max-tokens setting and reports no token counts. Every agent call is built the same way: input = \[{role: "user", content: FIXED\_INSTRUCTIONS}, {role: "user", content: PER\_TURN\_DATA}\], plus modelTier or model and effort; agents that return JSON call sample() and parse the reply with sample.json's own tolerant rule (whole reply; else one code fence; else first { or [ to last } or ]), since sample.json drops modelTierApplied, modelApplied and truncated. Answer length is set only by the prompt. The agent log records modelTierApplied for every call, and modelApplied when a model was named \[Proposed\].
- **Call cap.** MAX\_PLANNER\_CALLS bounds cost in large fights; overflow actors run their type's combat list.
- **Call slots.** Only a couple of Claude calls run at once per viewer; a few more wait their turn and a flood is rejected (rate\_limited). Background Planner and Weaver calls share these slots with the turn's own calls, so parallel calls can queue and a queued Planner call is more likely to be late (planner\_late). Size MAX\_PLANNER\_CALLS and BACKGROUND\_WINDOW with that in mind. **Turn calls first (Ali, 10/11/2026, Motion Rules Review Q4):** the turn's own calls (Parser, dialogue calls, Narrator, a scenario-start Weaver) always go ahead of background Planner and Weaver calls. Background calls start only while no turn call is waiting or running, and together never take more than CALL\_SLOTS − 1 slots (config, 2 until the slot count is confirmed), so a turn call never queues behind them and a fight with more significant NPCs answers no slower; background Planners therefore run one after another, nearest first. A call already running is never cut off (src/agents/call.ts, callGate). Abort a call (its signal) as soon as its output can no longer be used, so the viewer stops paying for it. The exact slot count is unconfirmed.
- **Compact views.** Ids, short descriptions and the last few relevant events, not the full log.
- **Prompt order.** Each agent's input starts with its fixed instructions (a leading user turn, since the capability has no system prompt) and ends with the per-turn data; nothing that changes per turn or per actor goes in the fixed part. If the platform caches prompt prefixes, this makes repeat calls faster; the runtime docs don't say it does (OQ-11), so estimates assume it doesn't. Pass cache:false on calls whose repeat must give a new answer.
- **Waiting state.** Show the same waiting line in every mode until the Narrator's checked text is ready (Play screen motion, M-01).
- **Streaming** is off for now; revisit after playtests (D-24).
- **Timing rule.** Resolution never starts before the previous display plus BACKGROUND\_WINDOW (config), in every mode. In dialogue turns, results are also shown no earlier than DIALOGUE\_WINDOW (config) after submit, set at or above the Responder's usual latency, so guarded and ordinary NPCs answer in the same time. Known limit: retries still change timing (D-45). The call count and the running agents show only in the GM view (play screen review UI1, UI3).

### Leak prevention, validation and failure handling

Every model output is checked before it touches state or the screen, and every failure has a fallback so the game never stalls. All checks are code; none adds a Claude call except the single regeneration or repair retry.

| Check | What it catches | On failure |
| --- | --- | --- |
| JSON schema on Parser, Planner, Responder, Voice, Narrator, Weaver | Malformed JSON or a missing required field (a missing optional field reads as empty and the answer stands, CS9) | One repair retry with the error; then the fallback for that agent |
| Legality on the Parser | Targets, items or skills not in PlayerView (a speech action's skill is set by code from its type, so the Parser's is dropped) | Turn stops; ask the Parser's clarify question, else a question about the failed part ("Who do you mean? I can see …", "Which item do you mean?", "Where do you want to go?"), else a generic "What do you do?" (provisional, see D-21) Exception (Q16, Ali 07/11/2026): when the intent has a legal move and only its addressees or action target are out of sight, the move resolves this turn, the action and words are dropped into deferred (logged deferred\_to\_arrival), and the Play screen offers them in the empty action box after the turn |
| Legality on Planner, Responder, Voice | An order targeting something the actor has no belief about | Drop the order: the Planner's actor uses its combat list; a Responder or Voice uses the fallback reply. Log view\_leak if the id is real but outside the view; log invalid\_target if it doesn't exist |
| Gear legality on Parser, Planner, Responder, Voice | An item the actor doesn't hold or can't see, a mode the item lacks, a gear op on an item out of reach | Drop that op or field; the action stands if still legal; log invalid\_gear |
| Planner timing | A Planner call not back by BACKGROUND\_WINDOW | Combat list this turn; discard the whole output; log planner\_late |
| Reveals on the Responder \[Proposed\] | A secret id the actor doesn't hold | Drop that reveal; log invalid\_reveal |
| Barks in NPC views | A bark's words in the view of an NPC that did not hear them | Build-time error: a bark's words reach only those who heard them and the speaker's said list (BF67, unit test) |
| Facts on the Voice | A shares id not in knows, or a name or fact in line outside the NPC's view | Regenerate once; then a neutral line from the NPC's sheet |
| Leak scan on the Narrator | Names, ids, leakTerms of unrevealed secrets, or facts from unperceived events that are not in NarrationView; the active stage's hiddenTerms; names of actors not in namesKnown | Regenerate once with a note; then the fallback narration |
| Fact check on the Narrator | An id in narrated that isn't in NarrationView; a full event that changes the player's condition, inventory or position missing from narrated; a full presence event missing from narrated; names of characters or items outside the view | Same as the leak scan |
| Stage-plan validation on the Weaver | Unresolved ids, caps, broken links, dead ends (NW4: a street, square, gate, field, woods or road must link somewhere besides the way in, to a zone or a frontier; the zone kinds table marks these onward; yards and indoor zones may be dead ends), unparseable triggers, grades that don't fit the tier, hiddenTerms or leakTerms in public text, over-long text, unresolved routine sets, missing or invalid slot bindings, unreachable bound zones, a bad startAt | One repair retry with the errors; then the flagged fallback stage; after it, routine-only errors degrade those actors to idle\_here |
| Routine switch (Planner, Responder, Voice, calm bandmates) | An id not in the actor's routines list, or a routine reading a slot the actor has not bound | Drop the switch; keep the current routine; log invalid\_routine (bandmates: routine\_skip) |
| Routine bank import (designer tool, not play) | Unresolved ids; a schedule with no steps, or steps on follow or wait; a slot read but not declared in every set that lists the routine; a shared routine reading a non-standard slot; an alert id not in the same set; a $slot target not declared | Entry not promoted; errors shown to the designer. The library never holds a failing entry |
| Call failed: upstream\_error \[Proposed\] | A transient service or connection failure | That agent's fallback this turn; no automatic retry; log call\_failed |
| Call failed: rate\_limited \[Proposed\] | Too many calls, or the viewer's usage limit | Fallbacks this turn; skip background Planner and Weaver calls until the next player action; show a short notice; log rate\_limited |
| Call failed: refused \[Proposed\] | Claude declined the input | The agent's fallback; never resend unchanged; log refused |
| truncated: true, or no parseable JSON in the reply \[Proposed\] | An answer cut by the length or time limit, or no parseable JSON | Same as malformed JSON: one repair retry, then the fallback |
| Call failed: not\_granted, sampling\_disabled, session\_expired \[Proposed\] | The viewer can't use Claude in this view | Stop play with a message to allow Claude or sign in again; no fallback play |
| Call ended: cancelled | The page aborted the call itself, such as a Planner call cancelled on a dialogue intent | Not an error; log as unused |

Every retry is single and changes its input. The runtime docs forbid retrying from a loop and treat only upstream\_error as transient. Error codes are the runtime's own.

Limit: code can't catch an invented unnamed figure ("another guard"). The prompt rule and playtests cover that.

The fallback narration's form is still open (D-23). Until then, build it from the events with plain templates.

Each actor type's combat list (Fallback behavior) keeps the game playable even if every Planner call fails, and covers actors beyond the call cap.

**GM debug view.** A toggle that shows full state, hidden plans, rolls, each actor's beliefs, and each agent's exact input and output for the turn. It's the fastest way to spot a leak. Its **People** table lists everyone with their exact age, who runs them (Player, Agent for mind.control agent, or Code) and the agent calls made for them this turn. Clicking a name opens a movable pop-up with that person's last GM\_AGENT\_CALLS\_SHOWN (5) agent calls, newest first: turn, agent, status, model, errors, the exact input, and the output folded under it. Older calls come from the kept turn records (TURN\_RECORDS\_KEPT), whose large inputs are trimmed (GameApi.agentHistory).

### Play screen

One self-contained page for PC only, built with the HISAB design system (https://claude.ai/artifact/PsxYHump4Sjy9iz9GvXkfx): its tokens and HISABRound fonts (story text in Literata, SIL OFL, embedded; Ali, 07/11/2026), one theme only (Sky diary Night; no picture credit, as this is a personal-use project, Ali 07/11/2026), no network calls except the game's own Claude and database capabilities. No max width, 32px gutters, 1.3× zoom at 2400px and wider, 64px rail items, dates DD/MM/YYYY.

Read HISAB's README, tokens.json, components/bundle.css, navigation.md, patterns.md, data-display.md and checklist.md before building, and invent no colours, radii or spacing. These design rules are binding for every play screen:

- **Navigation:** a side rail per navigation.md. Items are 64px tall with no gap, and the active item is a pill filling the whole item. Nothing shrinks on short windows; the rail scrolls on its own. Its pages \[Proposed\]: Play, Character (/stats), Inventory (/inv), Quests (/quests), Journal and, when on, GM debug.
- **Visibility (Shown / Left out):** clickable summary tiles all behave the same. Clicking the big box cycles Shown → Left out → off (several Shown = either). Clicking the small heading ("INVENTORY ›") opens that group. Use the words "Shown" and "Left out", never "Only". A figure that can't be clicked this way goes in a text line under the row, not in the tile row. The Inventory page has no tiles: its boxes only repeated the group counts, so it shows the equipment panel, the Coin line and the table.
- **Matching tiles:** tiles in one row share the same sub-line kind, units and ⓘ placement. When one page gets a feature, matching pages get it too.
- **Tables and grouping:** related entries fold under one head row with combined values (sum, earliest start, latest finish, average, "Mixed" when values differ, latest status) and a ↻N chip that opens the sub-rows. Sub-rows are numbered by start date. Order by start date, then finish date, then title in natural number order. Clicking a field's text opens the details panel with that field in edit; clicking empty space folds or unfolds. Charts go under the table they explain.
- **Estimates:** every estimated number shows its source line and a link, and stays editable.

* **Creation screen:** the approved layout at https://claude.ai/artifact/HW45ivigqwZ5VDo4PWPv9u (Part 4 §4, Player creation). The story starts from it.
* **Loot:** when the character can see things to take where they stand, a Loot chip shows by the action box with how many ("Loot ×7"). It opens a large partial pop-up (src/ui/loot.ts) listing them by where they are: each body that is down (what it wears and what a search turned up, plus an open pack's contents), each open container on the ground (a chest's contents; a chest itself stays where it stands, ContainerView.fixed), and the ground. Each thing is a tick chip; Pick all picks everything; Take types one order for the picks, one phrase per place ("Take the bandage and the key from the halt-keeper, and pick up the sack") in the action box, unsent, like Move to here, so the turn still decides what can be taken. Built from PlayerView only. Back, ✕ or Esc closes it (a partial pop-up opened with `back` adds a step to the browser's history, as the full-screen one does, BG-220).
* **Item value:** an item's hover card and stats list its Value in coin words (itemValue, the price a trader starts from), each and for the whole stack when there are several, or Worthless.
* **Header:** where you are: the character's zone as the heading (Ali: the story's premise as a title told him nothing); under it the turn, then the place and parent place the zone sits in. Pointing at the sun icon shows the day, the time of day and the clock to the second ("Day 2 · Morning · 07:30:15"; Ali). The day and time of day follow the turn (T11). No mode word, Claude-call count or background-call note: each changes with the encounter mode, which is hidden. The call count and the running agents show in the GM view (play screen review UI1, UI3, 09/11/2026).
* **Your deals** (Ali, 08/11/2026, PM5 and Deals display K1 c): an "N deals" chip beside the scenario title opens the player's deals with NPCs (PlayerView.agreements) in a gold-edged, gold-tinted list: who each deal is with, what each side will do, and the turn it was made. A click outside or Escape closes it. There is no chip while there are no deals. A deal leaves when a party ends it as done or broken; deals between NPCs never show.
* **Layout A, "Book with a status strip"** (play screen review UI8): fixed rows (header, who's here when it drops below the header, story, input) so nothing jumps; one reading column of about 70 characters a line (Literata, sized by the fit-the-screen rule, line height 1.6) on the left, over a stronger veil; the free space goes to the right margin. The side sheet is a narrow status strip by default (health level and bar, damaged-part chips, what is held, Stealthy); a click opens the full sheet as the 420px side panel, which pushes the story narrower. Open or closed is remembered per PC.
* **Who's here:** sits in the header line, between "Turn N · place" and the switches, while everyone fits; when too many people are around or the screen is narrow, it drops to its own fixed-height row under the header. It lists the actors the character fully perceives (PlayerView), by label until the name is known; "Nobody in view" when empty. Others' health shows only from Wounded down (Part 3), never as a bar (Voyage C2, U6, U7).
* **Narration log:** checked narration, newest last. While a turn runs, one neutral line for every turn, "The story is moving… 12 s", with "Taking longer than usual" after 90 s; it never names the agent (play screen review UI2). Story text is set in Literata.
* **Turn results** (UI4): under each turn's prose, in one place: the check line, every roll made against the player that they perceived (LogLine.incomingChecks), the turn's changes row, gear changes (LogLine.gear, ready-to-show rows the game writes when the turn ends; with the place, scenario and the player line's aimed flag they survive a reload), skill gains, XP, and gold cards for level-ups, achievements and perk milestones, which show even with mechanics off. The check line never sits above the prose it describes.
* **Check line:** the player's own roll this turn with no numbers: skill, tier, the other side's tier, how far apart they are ("1 tier ahead") and the result, e.g. "Medium weapons Great Success vs the slime's dodge Success · 1 tier ahead · full hit, body" (Voyage U10). It opens with an icon for the kind of roll (attack or talk), and each tier is a chip tinted green (Great Success and up), blue (Success), amber (Near Miss) or red (Failure and worse) (Ali, 07/11/2026).
* **Speaker lines:** each line the character heard, word for word under the narration: the speaker's name in a column on the left, sized to the longest name, with how it was said (whispering, shouting) under it, the line on the right, vertically centred (Narrator rule 5; Voyage U9, U12, style A picked by Ali 07/11/2026). *Stage directions* show in italics without the asterisks, outside the quote marks; the player speaks as "You".
* **Turn changes:** one folded row per turn ("Turn 14 ↻3 You are Bruised", or "3 changes") that opens to one typed row per change: Combat, Condition, Health. Built by code from the combat packets the character perceived (Voyage U14 to U16).
* **Commands and Ideas:** two buttons in the tool row under the input open a panel above it, at most 40% of the story's height; Escape, a pick or a send closes it. Commands lists the / commands (D-100). Ideas lists everyone in view for Fight and Talk and up to three actions each for Move, Stealth and Use, built by code from PlayerView (D-107); the same name twice reads "the other …". Picking one fills the box; nothing is sent until Send. The character's personality orders what is shown (src/ui/ideaLeans.ts IDEA_PULLS): each group and idea has pulls from Nerve, the personality axes and held values (a value pulls IDEA\_VALUE\_PULL, *placeholder* 50, points), groups and the ideas in each sort by total pull (under PERSONALITY\_LEAN either way counts as 0, ties keep their place), and an idea pulled away from by IDEA\_AGAINST\_NATURE (*placeholder* 50) or more adds "against your nature" to its note. Nothing is added or hidden; the three-a-group cut is made before ordering, and a broken nerve's Get away stays first. A cautious, quiet character sees Stealth first and Talk last, marked; a daring, quick-tempered one sees Fight first.
* **Input:** one free-text box across the reading column with Send beside it; a line starting with / is a command (Part 1 §7). The tools (Commands, Ideas, Same again, Aim, Sneak, Vanish, the quick-heal slot) sit in one row under it. Pointing at (or tabbing to) someone on Who's here shows a tip with how far they are, their zone when it isn't yours, and whether your longest weapon in hand reaches them: "34 m away · in the yard · in reach of your shortbow (50 m)" or "beyond your arming sword's 1.1 m" (src/ui/play.ts whoDistance; PlayerView.player.reaches, each weapon in hand at its longest reach). Who's here lists everyone you perceive clearly in any zone, so a far foe in sight is there to aim at. Same again (BG-190) fills the box with the last order the game played out (a clarify and / commands are skipped; src/ui/play.ts lastOrder); nothing is sent until Send. What the player put off last turn shows as a dimmed suggestion in the empty box ("From last turn · click it or Tab to use, Esc to clear", with Use it and Clear buttons); clicking or tapping the empty box, Tab, or Use it puts it in the box, selected, so typing over it starts afresh; nothing is sent until Send. PageUp and PageDown scroll the story while the box has focus.
