When the game asks a clarifying question with no answers of its own, the people, places and things it names that the player's view knows (people seen now or earlier, zones, items in hand or on the ground; matched with or without articles, longest first) can be tapped: a tap fills the box with the order it asks about, with that phrase in place of the name the order used ("I punch the man" and a tap on Osric Fell give "I punch Osric Fell"; with no single known name in the order, "…, I mean Osric Fell"), built from the first order of that run of questions so taps never pile up (BF72), and nothing is sent until Send. A question that came with its own answers has no tappable names: the answer buttons are the one way to answer it. The words sent after a question reach the Parser beside it (ANSWERING: the question and the first order it was about; src/agents/answering.ts), and Parser rule 5 reads them as one order (src/ui/play.ts clarifyPicks). The fake page's "punch the man" asks one.
* **Input keys:** the action box keeps focus; what it sends is trimmed, and an empty line sends nothing. Narration is paced (Play screen motion, M-10); skipping uses a click on the story or Esc, never a key that types (Voyage A7, decided by Ali 07/11/2026; was OQ-24; M-12).
* **Aim toggle:** marks this turn's attack as aimed (Part 1 §7); an aimed turn's player line ends with "· aimed" (from the line's saved `aimed`).
* **Rolls:** while SHOW\_MECHANICS is on, each turn's dice, modifiers and damage fold under "Rolls" in that turn's results, wrapping. A "Show rolls" switch in the header sets the story's showMechanics (UI6). Every turn keeps its rolls on its story line whether the switch is on or off, and the switch only shows or hides them, so turning it on shows past turns' rolls too. A roll whose outcome moved by whole steps (gear beyond the holder's skill, cover, tier perks, the natural edge) shows the outcome before them, the steps and their sources, and the outcome after (BF70, `tiered` in src/agents/game.ts): "d100 49 → Near Miss [weapon +9], 8 steps down [Short of an old sword's requirement -8] → Critical Failure−−−−−−"; the same for attacks, defenses, contests and checks. An NPC's own gear in its view says when the gear is beyond its skill and by how many outcome steps (OwnGearView.beyondYou), so it can choose its fists or another weapon.
* **Strike card** (Ali, 13/11/2026: "hitting someone is not satisfying"). When the player's own blow lands (grazed or better), a card rises over the story as the turn lands, before the prose (BF66; after the gains moment when one opens): the outcome in large letters, "Your arming sword · the slime's body", and what the player saw it change ("The slime is Wounded"), from LineCheck.strike (the combat packet's target, weapon and visible changes). Tiers by outcome: a graze only arrives; a full hit draws a slash across the card and pulses the word; a heavy hit (warning colour) or crushing hit (danger colour) adds the hit-stop on the word and a short shake of the card only. It lives a fixed --m-strike (1600 ms) whatever the turn held, takes no pointer events, is hidden from screen readers (the results row carries the same words). It runs on its own clock: a skip (M-12) only lands its motion and never removes it, (BF66; src/ui/play.ts strikeCard). The fake page's "attack", "attack light" and "attack hard" show the heavy, full and crushing cards.
* **Undo:** after /undo the undone turn's lines fold under one "Undone · turn N" row (struck through when opened), and the note reads "Turn N undone. Dice are not rolled back."
* **Story list:** the story is an append-only list (role=log) that announces each new turn once; the waiting line is a separate status. It draws the last 50 turns with a "Show earlier turns" button, and a redraw never closes an opened fold.
* **Out-of-character notices:** the scenario-planning failure marker (Agent Weaver), the rate-limit notice and the stop-play message (Leak prevention, validation and failure handling).
* **Character page as one screen** (Ali, 14/11/2026, CP1–CP5): the page fits the window with no page scroll: the character card on the left (who, health, level and XP, capstone choice, then the body figure beside power, standing attack and defense), the skill trees on the right, and a strip of four tiles along the bottom, Essences, Perks, Gear and Personality, each with a one-line summary, each opening its section in a pop-up. Two kinds of pop-up (CP2, src/ui/pop.ts): partial ones float, drag by their head and leave the page usable (Essences, Gear, Personality, a skill, the body, power); the full-screen one covers the page for a board too big for a panel (Perks, CP3), and partial pop-ups and the perk card open on top of it. Both close with ✕ or Esc (the topmost first) and open and close with one short fade (exit faster). Clicking a skill opens what makes up its number (CP5): true and raw, each part that adds to it (perks, achievements, each essence, Hollowed), its rolls' augments and trades, what it adds to, its learning range and its next perk milestone, read from trueSkill itself (it lists its parts) so the lines always add up. Clicking the body figure opens each part with its state and pool; the Gear tile lists what is held and worn, and a piece opens its stats (the Inventory's rows) with a link to it in the Inventory; the Personality tile opens the Personality block. Links from Play (the Essences row, the level-up line's perk link) open the page with that pop-up up (charFocus).
* **Perks (Character page)** (Ali, 07/11/2026, K1–K4, S1, S7): a perk point is spent any time on the Character page, from the Perks tile, which opens the board full screen; the rail shows a dot while one is unspent, and the level-up line on Play links there (K2). A taken perk can be undone until the next turn is played, then it is permanent (K3). Taking or undoing a perk, and abandoning or pinning a quest, is saved at once (the saved turn is amended, no new turn), so a reload keeps it (CS12, Ali 13/11/2026). So is a command that changes the character: /essence take, pass, give, sell and remove, and /title (BF40, BG-208). Every skill tree shows at once, each in its own compact box (the boxes side by side, wrapping into rows; a box as wide as its skills need), with no tabs and no By effect view (Ali, page comment, 14/11/2026). In a box each skill is a ladder of its seven milestones on a gold line showing true skill, the rail at the left of its column with the perk names beside it (cut short with the full name on hover in a narrow box), the All-50/100/150 steps as dotted lines across the box; on narrow screens the ladders become horizontal tracks. The Story / Effect toggle for the perk write-ups sits where they are written: in the perk card's head and beside the Innate and Linked headings, kept per browser. Milestones not yet reached are sealed and show only their number; the page never hints at what comes next (K4). Tapping a taken or open milestone shows a small card beside it with the perk's text and, when open, "Take · 1 point". Mock-ups: https://claude.ai/artifact/TVfF7qs3WnDdDZzzM6Yjf5
* **Journal page:** People (actors seen clearly now, or whose name is known; a label until the name is known; health only from Wounded down; then a Heard of group: names heard of people never met, from PlayerView.heardOf, leaving it once the person is listed above; src/ui/pages.ts heardOfNames), Places (the current place and those above it, plus places of facts the character witnessed, folded under their parent place; the map draws their zones) and a Timeline (turns played since the page was opened). Built from PlayerView, the Journal and the story log only, never the hidden store (Voyage U26, U28; Ali, 07/11/2026).
* **Power (Character page)** (Ali, 13/11/2026, D-273, FS17): the character card shows power() (rating plus gear rating, the measure defeat XP and the stronger-foe achievements use) as one number above Standing attack and defense. Clicking it opens the details pop-up listing its parts from the same function (powerParts in src/engine/xp.ts, src/ui/power.ts), never a second formula: Level N (LEVEL\_RATING\_SHARE of the XP to reach it), each skill group that adds something, largest first, with its XP factor (open a group for its skills: value, worth, counted in full, at 1/2, 1/4…), and gear held and worn (GEAR\_RATING\_FACTOR of its coin value; open for each item), then the total. The lines always add up to the number.
* **Skill rows (Character page):** each skill takes two lines so the bar always spans its tree's width: the name with its true value at the right, then the bar with its learning window. When the true value differs from raw, the value gets a dotted underline and the raw value shows in a small card beside it only on hover or keyboard focus.
* **Inventory equipment (Ali):** the Inventory page opens with what the character has equipped, the way MMOs show it: one slot per Grip part (a two-grip item sits in its first grip; the other reads "(both grips)"), Body for armor, then each accessory slot, with empty slots dashed. Beside it, "In your pack" shows every unequipped item as an icon tile, with plain stacks of the same item merged and a ×N count. Each item has a line icon drawn for its kind of thing, tinted by item kind. Hovering an item, or focusing it from the keyboard, shows a card with its exact stats (the same rows as the side panel). Equipped items offer Unequip; weapons, shields, armor and accessories in the pack offer Equip. Each button sends the words a player would type ("I draw my…", "I put away my…", "I put on my…", "I take off my…") as a turn, so drawing and putting away stay free and armor on or off is the action. The buttons wait while a turn runs or after the player falls.
* **Icons** (Ali, 14/11/2026, IC1–IC5): things in the world are filled silhouettes; screen controls stay line drawings. Sources are mixed: game-icons.net (CC BY 3.0) first, Fluent Emoji High Contrast (MIT) where it draws a subject better, and more filled sets may join. `scripts/icons.mjs` (npm run icons) copies only the picked icons into `src/ui/iconArt.ts` as SVG text: no network calls, no base64. Every library item, container and loot thing has an icon (items sharing a type share one), tinted by item kind; the loot pop-up shows each thing's icon; each class has one, shown on the class tree, the Character page's Class line and the creation screen's Starts (a Start shows its class's; The Blessed shows a holy symbol). The map keeps its own glyphs (silhouettes blur at 12 px). The credit line sits at the foot of the GM page and the licences in `src/ui/assets/icons-LICENSE.txt`. Scales past 100: game-icons.net has supernatural subjects (rune blades, auras, creatures) for gear and beings above the human range.
* **Map:** a big full-screen pop-up over the page, not a tab of its own: the Map button in the Play header opens it, and ✕, Esc or leaving the page closes it (an old #map link opens Play with the map up). A zone's card (any zone but the one you stand in) has **Move to here**: it closes the map and types "I go to <the zone>" in the action box, unsent, for the player to send or change (moveOrder, src/ui/map.ts); a zone in another town or building than the one you are in names it, "I go to the village square in Thornbury" (moveHere; Ali: the bare name read as a spot nearby). Unexplored exits are clickable too (an exit's arrow or its "· unexplored" note, or unexplored stairs): the card names the way and the zone it leaves, with **Move to here**, which types "I go <the way>" ("I go back out to the road", "I go to the hut door"; exitOrder). One map per top-level place, with each inner place outlined as an area; zones are tiles joined by their exits, placed by a force layout that follows the exits' compass words (north is up; up/stairs above, down/cellar below). Shows every zone the character stood in or saw across a sight link (journal.zonesSeen, noted at the start and end of each turn), plus exits not taken as stubs. Each top-level place (a village, say) is its own map; a road to a known zone on another map is a stub naming that place, and clicking it opens that map. A World crumb, first in the crumbs, shows every top-level place you know as one tile, joined by the roads you know between them (laid out by their compass words); clicking a place opens its map. Light, cover, people and ground items show only for zones in view now; remembered zones keep name, look and exits; people out of sight show where last seen. Pan, zoom, find me, show the whole map, place crumbs; clicking a zone opens its card (look, exits with a go button, people, items) beside the map with the places list and key. Places never overlap: after the layout, place outlines (and zones in no inner place) are pushed apart, with the player's group kept still (BG-37). A barrier the character knows stops them sits as a badge on its exit: a padlock for locked (green when they hold its key), a door with its bolt across for barred, a cross for blocked; locked or barred shows only once they have tried it, hold its key or see the bolt on their side (barrierView). The zone card's exits name each barrier ("door, locked", "gate, standing open"), and the key lists the badges once one shows. Furniture (FR5): on the to-scale plan each piece is a small line glyph for its kind (src/ui/furnitureIcons.ts, one per FU kind) on a disc at its point, kept inside its walls, with a piece in the middle stepped below the zone's name; hover names it, the zone card lists it under Furniture, and the key shows the mark once the plan draws any. People on the plan (Ali, 13/11/2026): everyone in full sight stands at their own point (Actor.pos, MapPerson.at), as you do; someone only heard or glimpsed, or last seen, has no point, so they sit in a row under the zone's name ("somewhere in here" on hover). Each person's disc shows the initial of what you know them by, a learned name or a description ("a lean man" shows L), and pointing at it shows that full name or description beside it; the discs sit above every tile so a neighbouring zone never hides one (whoInitial, src/ui/map.ts). The exits view keeps the row, since its tiles are not to scale. Built from PlayerView and the Journal only, never the hidden store. **Real coordinates** (Ali, 14/11/2026, CO9): whenever code has placed every zone of the map (each has a rect), the map is drawn to scale by default: each zone at its real position and size in metres, so zones and places that adjoin touch along x and y and their shared side is the exit; each inner place's outline is exactly the box around its zones (square corners, its name above its north side), and the World view draws each top-level place as the box around all its known zones (extentOf, src/ui/mapData.ts), still joined by the roads you know, with place names on a fixed-size layer so they stay readable when a road's kilometres zoom the World far out (it zooms out as far as the whole World needs, with room kept for the names), and a scale bar of 10 m, 100 m or 1 km to suit. **Roads (RD1-RD3):** a road that is a place of its own (kind road) has no map of its own: it is one dashed line in the World along its length, whatever the zoom, with its name and length centred above its middle, and the World card lists it once as that road between the places at its ends. A place along a road (a waystation inside the road place) is a World place and a map of its own, a mark beside the road that opens its map (rootPlace, src/ui/mapData.ts). On a road outside any town the map opens on the World zoomed to that road with the places at its ends, your pin at your own point on it, and the road's card: its length, "You are here · 1 km from its west end", its look, where it goes and the places along it, each with a button that opens its map. Clicking the road in the World shows that card; a town's arrow onto the road, a place's road crumb or the road in the places list opens the World on that road. To scale, the World shows your pin wherever you are, and a place too small on screen to hold its name has it just under its south side. The fake page's Ashford sits at the far end of a 30 km west road (a day's walk) from Millbrook's square; the slider shows heights below ground too (a cellar or dungeon, "3 m down") once one is known. ▦ switches to the exit diagram, which a map with any unplaced zone always uses. **Height slider:** the map shows one height at a time; a vertical slider at the bottom left has one stop per height the map has (lowest at the bottom, mapHeights), each named ("Ground level", "3 m up"), with "· you" at yours; hidden when the map has one height. **Stage map (rpg-map MAP-01..53):** code places every zone on the 1 m grid (whole-metre sides and corners, the area's best whole-metre fit; MAP-10), beside what it links to, never partly over another zone, and never inside another building's footprint (the box around a place's indoor zones), so a street runs beside a building and never through it (MAP-11, MAP-12); among the free spots it takes the one that leaves room for the sides its exits name ("behind the counter, north" keeps the common room's north side clear). Positions are stored with the zone and never moved once placed (MAP-03), except once (MR2): a story whose stored layout breaks MAP-10, 11 or 12 (saved before these rules) has the broken places' zones placed again when it loads, everyone and everything in a moved zone keeping its spot in it (the same share of the way across and up), and the next save keeps the new layout (relayBroken, src/scenario/geometry.ts). Zone states (MAP-33): where you are, explored (stood in: journal.zonesVisited, each zone once as you stand in it; a save without the list starts it from its trail), seen but never stood in (hatched), and exits to somewhere unknown. To scale, a place's map is laid out by code at the zoom shown (src/ui/mapPlan.ts) and drawn on one screen-pixel layer: doors as gaps in the wall two zones share, at its middle (a barrier's padlock, bolt or cross in its place; more than three on one wall become one badge with the count); zones apart joined by a line 6 px off each; each zone's name in bold letters inside it, centred, on up to three lines and as large as fit its box (4 to 32 px), on a layer behind every mark so it takes no room (MZ2-MZ4; a zone too small for 4 px letters shows its name in its tooltip only); your pin first; place titles above their outline's top-left corner, the outline padded only where it stays off other places' zones; people in the bottom band or at their own point; every exit to somewhere not on the map an arrow out of the wall its compass word names, with a note "→ its name · unexplored" (or "· to Ashford", ", locked"); stairs inside their zone clear of its corners; furniture last, left off (and still listed in the zone's card) when its zone has no free spot. Marks inside a zone (BG-216) are placed once at the closest zoom and every zoom keeps each at the same share of its zone (inside the room its size leaves): furniture and stairs never move, and zoomed out where one would cover another mark it hides in its own spot (listed in the zone's card) and comes back there; you and people are never hidden and step aside only then. No mark covers another at any zoom; the first view fits the whole map with everything inside it. The key lists exactly what the map shows. In dev views (?fake=, ?mapcheck) the rpg-map checker (checkMap, src/ui/mapCheck.ts), checkReach, and the MAP-10/MAP-12 stage checks run after every render and log each finding to the console ("[map-check] …", window.__mapFindings). Test stages: ?fake=1&stage=bent-nail|bent-nail-saved|one-room|long-names (src/ui/fakeStages.ts).
* **Way rows:** a turn where the player tried, opened, locked, barred or picked a barrier adds Way rows under the story (LogLine.ways, "Door to kitchen: locked"); breaking one waits on the walls thread.
* **Coin row:** a turn that changed the player's coin adds a Coin row under the story with the difference in coin words, gold when gained (+1 silver and 5 copper) and red when lost (−12 copper), so a theft or a price never passes unseen (LogLine.coin, in copper; coin is left out of the gear rows). Live, its amount flares in its colour and pulses once (M-20 strength), and the sheet's coin value swaps with a floating difference and then pulses.
* **Side sheet health:** the health bar marks the health-track thresholds and names the current level; clicking it opens each body part with its pool and state (Ali, 07/11/2026). Any part that is not Healthy shows as a chip under the bar ("Left leg crippled") in its state's colour, and the body figure opens by itself on a turn that changes a part. A "Held / worn" line names the gear in use.
* **Colours and depth:** every colour comes from the HISAB tokens in `:root` (health levels run ok → warn → danger → grey; time of day uses c4, scenario-mark, c7 and accent; perk gold is scenario-mark). No glows and no drop shadows except the toast shadow; tests/unit/styles-tokens.test.ts enforces both.
* **Place and scenario breaks:** when a turn takes the player to a new place, a quiet rule named for it ("River path · the Riverside") sits between the player's line and the prose; a new scenario in the same place gets the rule without a name. It reads the place and scenario saved on each narration line (`place`, `scenario`), so breaks survive a reload; undone turns are skipped when comparing.
* **Scenario ending moment (SD1):** when a turn ends a scenario, the rule between scenarios carries the ending's name in its middle, in that ending's colour (resolved ok, escaped accent, left muted, lost danger, interrupted scenario-mark, worn out faint, broken off warn), with the place name after it when the place changed. While the turn runs nothing on the page says that a scenario ended (BF64, Ali): no rule draws above the waiting line, and the rail's perk-point badge keeps the count it had until the turn lands, so neither gives the ending or a level-up away. When the line arrives, a scenario that gave something opens its gains moment, which names the ending (that turn's results in the story leave out the Skills and XP rows, which the moment shows in full: Ali); otherwise the turn's own rule draws with the ending's name, the closing sentence fades in above it in muted italic story type, and the opening follows below. Esc or a click on the story skips the motion (M-12). It reads scenarioEnd from the narration line, so it shows the same after a reload.
* **Morale bar (MB11, MB16):** the status strip and the full sheet show the player's morale under health, in its own colour (c5): the level word in the strip; the level, morale left of max and the track's thresholds as ticks in the full sheet. Who's here shows another actor's morale level only from Afraid down, as PlayerView gives it.
* **Morale stakes and a broken nerve (MB12 on screen):** from CONFIG.MORALE\_HINT\_FROM (Terrified) down, the sheet's morale level word carries a tip (hover or keyboard focus, dotted underline as the cue; NB2: never a line of its own) with the level's roll penalty and what Broken would stop ("−15 on every roll. One more fright and your nerve breaks: you won't be able to attack."). At Broken it reads "You can't walk, run or attack until the fight is behind you: with nothing else to do you cower where you stand", how that comes (no one fighting you for ENGAGEMENT\_END\_TURNS turns, your yield taken, the scenario's end, or a rest: the composure rows that lift the bar off 100% lost) and what still works (talk, beg, yield, give, hide, use an item such as a bandage, pick something up, open a door or container within reach: BF37). The stamina level word carries its roll penalty the same way (Tired and worse). The turn the nerve breaks adds a violet Nerve broken card under the story with the same words (LogLine.nerveBroke), An order of only what Broken blocks (a walk, an attack, a grab, a filch, a perk move, with no words) is turned away before the turn (NB5, BF37): the game answers like a clarify (agents/game.ts NERVE\_REFUSED), nothing happens and no time passes. With words or an allowed action beside it, the turn goes ahead and the engine takes the blocked part away (log broken\_cowers); that, or an attack the engine refuses because the nerve broke during that same turn, adds a Nerve row (LogLine.nerveRefused). While Broken, the action deck's Fight and Move groups give way to "Your nerve is broken · you cower" (Yield, Sneak), and the suggestion is Yield; there is no Flee for now (BF37; M-13).

* **The action deck and minimap (AD1–AD16):** the input is a list of every action the code allows right now (src/ui/deck.ts, built from PlayerView only), in groups (Fight, Talk, Move, Stealth, Use, Perks), always open, one entry per action. Clicking one asks only what it still needs in a small pop-up (whom, from the Who's here list; with Aim on, which body part; with what), then plays the turn at once: code builds the Intent and GameApi.act sends it through the same legality checks as a Parser order (acceptIntent), with no Parser call (AD7). Persuade, Lie to, Threaten, Haggle and Demand surrender wait for your words in the Say box. Above the list, a suggested action scored by code (src/ui/suggest.ts, CONFIG.SUGGEST levers) with its reason and Another. No keys pick actions (AD10). The Say box keeps free text through the Parser and / commands. Aim is on/off; Stance is careful, normal or reckless. The right column holds the folded health strip (a click opens the full condition), the minimap (src/ui/minimap.ts: a click plans a move to an exact point, MoveSpot.at; Enter, Just go or a double-click walks there; the ring shows one move slot's reach with carried-over time; only what the character knows), Who's here as rows (foes first, pins, scrolling; a click lists what you can do to or with them) and the short sheet (held, coin, level; skills on the Character page). A move planned on the minimap rides along with the next clicked action.
* **After /yield (MB40–MB43):** while the player's yield stands, the status strip and the full sheet show a "You yielded" box under morale (warn tint): who took it, who is still deciding and who fights on (danger), by the names the player knows them by. It goes when the scenario ends.
* **Learning window (SP2):** every skill bar, on the Character page and in the full sheet, draws the skill's learning window (raw − MARK_WINDOW to max(raw, true) + MARK_WINDOW) as an outlined range on the same 0–100 scale; the Character page also prints it as numbers ("20–40") beside the true skill. An augmenting skill (Riposte, Blade Dance, Shrewd Dealing) learns only on the rolls it adds to, at the roll's level (AU1), so its bar shows that roll's window, dashed, and the numbers read "via Parry 50–70" (augmentWindow). The play page shows only the player's own window, never the foe's skill.
* **Agent pop-up look:** "Watch an agent play" is a switch in the header beside Show rolls (✓ when open). Open, it is a large floating pop-up over the play screen (Ali, 14/11/2026: "this whole thing should be a pop-up", then "make it a partial pop-up (large)"; up to 880 px wide, its settings in as many columns as fit), non-modal so the story stays in view, first at the top right under the header and dragged by its head to anywhere (kept there while the page lives); it stays open beside any details pop-up. Inside: which story; Mode, Playstyle, Pace, Style, Speaker and Speed two to a row, each list under its name; standing orders when guided; One turn and Play (main button); then the agent's spoken line in story type, labelled Agent, or the Your call card, and the status. ✕ in its head closes it and pauses the agent.
* **Fit the screen (Ali):** the Play screen always fills the window, with no empty spaces. The full sheet is open by default at every width (wide and narrow screens each remember a fold); the open sheet is a fixed 300 px (--sheet-w), as narrow as its skill rows, and every block on it is kept that compact: a small body figure with its key beside it, labels that don't wrap. The sheet shows no scrollbar: the mouse wheel scrolls it, and so does dragging it with the mouse (a drag doesn't count as a click). The story column fills all the space beside it, with no reading-width cap, and its type grows with the screen (clamp(16px, 0.45vw + 10px, 20px)) so wide lines stay readable; speech lines are 16px.
* **Divider lines:** the gap under the story and the sheet sits inside each column, so the sheet's divider runs to the foot of the screen.
* **Perk series colours (Perks):** the 20/60/100/140 milestones (perks that change what you can do) are gold and the 40/80/120 milestones (number boosts) are violet (tokens --perk-20 and --perk-40), the same wherever a perk shows: the ladder and track diamonds, perk names beside them, the perk card, and the "New perk unlocked" card on Play. A small key under the Perks heading names both: "Breakthrough" for 20, 60, 100, 140 and "Refinement" for 40, 80, 120. Sealed milestones stay neutral so nothing hints at what comes next (K4).
* **Linked perks (Perks):** a perk granted by a perk link (PERK_LINKS, OwnedPerk source 'link') shows in a Linked block under the Perks heading, beside Innate: its name, a Linked tag, the perks it came from with each one's skill and milestone ("From Dread Presence (Intimidation 100) + Overwhelm (Resolve 100)"), and its Story or Rules text. A merge (for later, LR5) shows the tag Merged and its set struck through as asleep while the merge holds. The card of an owned perk that is part of a link says so ("Linked with Overwhelm: together they grant Crushing Presence."). A link not yet earned stays unseen, so the page never hints at what comes next (K4).
* **All-XXX counts (Perks, by tree):** each All-50/100/150 line reads "n of need", where need is the tree's rating requirement (CONFIG.ALL_STEP_SKILLS via allStepNeed), the same rule the mastery achievements use; an inferior tree reads "never".
* **Keyboard order:** Tab runs input, tool row, side sheet, then the rail (the rail is last in the page and placed in the first column by CSS). Rail labels use tighter tracking so "Achievements" fits the 84px rail.
* **"You fell" screen:** shown when a lethal fight incapacitates the player and no foe spared them (D-54, D-239), offering /undo or reset. Undo goes back to the turn the foes decide, so the next turn asks them again.
* **GM debug view toggle** (Leak prevention, validation and failure handling).

### Play screen motion

Decided by Ali, 10/11/2026 (Motion Rules Review, answers Q1–Q4; it replaces the draft rpg-motion rules). Code resolves every number; the screen only animates what code already resolved. Built in src/ui/motion.ts (helpers), src/ui/play.ts (the turn's order) and the --m-\* tokens in styles.css. Q1: narration is paced sentence by sentence (this settles D-24's pacing; streaming stays off). Q2: the shield's state and an armor pool show on their gear lines. Q3: no minimum wait for scenario-start turns (no false delays). Q4: the turn's own calls go ahead of background calls in the call slots (Latency and cost).

**Timing leak**

- **M-01** One pending state for every turn: the line "The story is moving… N s", kept apart from the story as a status. It looks the same in every mode and for every turn, and adds "Taking longer than usual" after 90 s. It never names an agent, an actor or a step, and has no progress bar or step counter.
- **M-02** A turn shows only when the game hands over the whole finished turn: checked narration, outcome labels and state changes, after the BACKGROUND\_WINDOW and DIALOGUE\_WINDOW gates. Nothing of it shows while any of its calls is still out. Streaming stays off (D-24); if it is ever turned on, this rule is reviewed with it.
- **M-03** After a turn lands, every animation's length comes from the motion tokens and from what is on screen (how many sentences, how many changes), never from response time, which agents ran, how many calls ran, retries or fallbacks. Two turns that show the same things animate for the same time.
- **M-04** Background work is invisible. Background Planner calls, background Agent Weaver stage extensions, actors run by their type's combat list (minor, over MAX\_PLANNER\_CALLS, or after a late or failed call) and every fallback get no animation, indicator or layout shift of their own. A frontier turn that waits for a late Weaver shows only the normal pending line. The out-of-character notices (scenario-planning failure marker, rate-limit notice, stop-play message) appear only when the turn shows, or when play stops, never while calls run.
- **M-05** Nothing on screen shows who or what decided an NPC's words or deeds: Responder or Voice, Planner, combat list, routine, trigger, a reply or a bark, a fallback line. Their words reach the screen only inside the narration, so they share its container, reveal and timing.
- **M-06** While a turn runs, nothing on the play screen changes except the pending line's seconds: no sheet value, Who's here entry, header line or chip updates until the turn shows.

**Narration reveal**

- **M-10** Each turn's narration appears sentence by sentence: each sentence fades in and rises at most 4 px. Never character by character.
- **M-11** Paced narration uses one fixed gap per sentence (--m-sentence) and a cap on the whole reveal (--m-reveal-cap); past the cap the remaining sentences appear together.
- **M-12** A click on the story, or Esc, finishes every running animation at once. Keys that type never skip. While something animates, Esc only skips; otherwise it does its usual job. Sending the next action also finishes everything still running.
- **M-13** Input is never blocked or disabled by an animation.
- **M-15** Screen readers hear each new turn once, through the story list (role=log), when the turn lands, never sentence by sentence. A value that counts up carries its final value in text from the start, so nothing is read half-way.

**Outcome beats**

- **M-20** Each outcome label the player perceived (their own check line and the rolls made against them) gets one beat on its result word, growing with severity: missed and dodged appear with no motion; grazed and blocked fade in; full hit gets one short pulse; heavy hit a pulse plus a shift of at most 3 px; crushing hit the strongest, still under 300 ms. The word's colour says whose loss it is: danger for a hit on the player, accent for the player's hit.
- **M-21** A turn's results play after its narration, top to bottom as they sit under the prose: check lines in action order, the changes row, then sheet values in the order health, body parts, morale, skills, XP and level, coin, then gold cards. Each starts --m-stagger after the last, and past --m-results-cap the rest lands at once.
- **M-22** Outcome beats carry no numbers. Numbers about other actors never show at all. The player's own numbers that the sheet already shows (skills, XP, coin, quick-heal count) may count to their new value. The Rolls fold (Show rolls) never animates.

**Body parts, health and morale**

- **M-30** A body-part state change animates once on that part: its colour and pattern change, then one short pulse. Worsening and recovery use the same motion; colour carries direction. When one of the player's parts becomes Crippled or Destroyed, or recovers from it, its state chip lands on the sheet ("Right arm · Crippled") and the turn's results get a card naming it. Only states the player is shown animate.
- **M-31** Body-part changes play as one batch per turn, in their place in the M-21 order.
- **M-32** Destroyed is the only part state with a second beat: the item the part held, or a destroyed shield, leaves its gear line. A destroyed head or neck (essential) is Dead for the player, so the fall sequence replaces every other beat that turn.
- **M-33** Crossing a level on the health or morale track gets one beat on that track: the level word changes and the bar settles. Never a loop or a flash. Reaching Wounded or worse also gives one short red pulse at the screen edge; at Critical the edge keeps a steady tint. Reaching Incapacitated hands over to the fall sequence.
- **M-34** State must be readable without motion: colour plus a text label or icon on every state.
- **M-35** A held shield shows its state on its gear line (Healthy, Damaged, Crippled, Destroyed) and animates like a body part (M-30, M-32). An armor pool (a heavy helmet) has no states: its gear line shows how much is left, which drops with one short beat when it absorbs a blow and reads "empty" once spent.

**Sheet values**

- **M-36** Every other value that changes on the play screen gets one feedback beat on its own element. A number counts to its new value with a small "+n" or "−n" beside it; a bar moves to its new length with a fading trail of the part lost; a word swaps with a short cross-fade; a chip or line (Shaken, Swayed, Stealthy, the yield box, a quest step, the deals chip) fades in or out. This covers skills, XP and level, coin, the quick-heal count, morale, time of day, and arrivals and departures in Who's here.
- **M-37** Motion never shows more than the screen shows: no direction, size or timing that PlayerView doesn't already give. Another actor's level word only animates when PlayerView shows that word.
- **M-38** Gold cards land last in a turn's results, one at a time. A level-up also fills the XP bar, then starts it again from empty, as one short moment (the Thread weaves). The perk-point badge on the rail's Character item is held back until then: it lands with the level-up card that gave the point, never before it (src/ui/play.ts pointsBeat).
- **M-38a** An achievement is the turn's special moment. Its gold card shows the reward tier ("Tier II") and what it asks for (earnedAtText). It arrives with its own beat within --m-honor: it rises in, one band of light sweeps across it, and then its tier lands. A scar arrives heavier and in the danger colour, with no light. A click, Enter or Space on the card opens the movable, non-modal details pop-up (src/ui/pop.ts), which shows why it was earned, what it asks for, its XP and its rewards. Esc closes the pop-up, unless something is still moving, in which case Esc only skips (M-12).

**Feedback tiers** (game-feel GF-01 to GF-52 fitted inside the rules above; kept by Ali on the Motion Tuning Questions page, MT1: --m-stagger stays 350ms)

- **M-23** Every beat has a tier set by how rare its event is, and rarer events get more channels: T0 missed or dodged (no motion), T1 grazed, blocked, a small gain (one fade), T2 full hit, a level word crossed (one pulse), T3 a heavy or crushing hit on the player, a part Crippled or Destroyed (hold, shake, edge), T4 level-up and achievement (the milestone beat, M-38b). A pulse grows a word by up to 6% per strength but a wide card by only a few pixels, so card text never slides.
- **M-24** A crushing hit on the player: its result word snaps to a struck pose and holds for --m-hold (hit-stop), then the story and the sheet shake once and the screen edge flares red once. The shake is decaying trauma: offset = 3 px × trauma², gone within --m-shake; a heavy hit on the player adds a smaller shake (about 1 px) with no hold. Blows close together merge (trauma adds and clamps at 1), so the cap never grows. The input bar never moves. A part that becomes Crippled or Destroyed flares its red card the same way, holding for --m-hold, with no growth.
- **M-33a** Bars have two layers. A loss shows the new fill at once and leaves a pale ghost of the part lost, which holds for --m-ghost-hold and then drains toward the fill. A gain shows a faint ghost of the new length at once and the fill grows into it. The body figure keeps a part's old colour until that part's beat, then changes.
- **M-33b** The nerve's warning (MW1 to MW4, Ali's morale-break feedback). The morale track has its own screen edge in the morale violet. Reaching Terrified or worse (CONFIG.MOTION_NERVE_FROM) flares it once. From Panicking (CONFIG.MOTION_NERVE_STEADY), Broken included, it keeps a steady tint. When the red health edge shows too, the red one wins and the violet one stays out. Breaking adds one beat on the morale track and brings in the "until" words beside the level; the "Nerve broken" story card lands like a wound card, in violet. Climbing back above Panicking fades the tint over --m-state with no beat. The words, the card, the hint line and the input-area chips belong to the play-screen interface (U1 to U5).
- **M-38b** A level-up card gets the same milestone beat as an achievement: a --m-hold pause, the card rises in, its star holds its struck pose, and one band of light sweeps across it once. Milestones play one at a time after every other beat, each skippable.
- **M-39** The scenario-end gains moment (D-226), the game's second authored moment after the scene break (M-40). When a scenario that gave anything ends, a gains moment opens as a pop-up over the whole screen as soon as the turn lands (Ali: the grand moment covers everything; the veil is there at once, so nothing behind it shows the ending first). It carries the ending's rule, a plain line under it saying what that ending means (SCENARIO\_ENDING\_OUTCOMES, one fixed sentence per ending, e.g. worn out: "It dragged on and wound down with nothing settled either way."; it says what happened, never what the scenario was about, so the hidden question stays hidden; the Gains page shows it too), the old scenario's closing line and "What you gained". The new scenario's opening and the turn's results play only after it closes. It has no total cap: each gain gets a fixed step from the tokens (--m-gain-gap between gains), so only how many gains there are sets its length (M-03). The whole moment runs slower than a turn's beats: every duration inside it is stretched by --m-gains-pace (2; Ali found the first cut too fast), and further when the gains are few, so the whole moment lasts at least --m-gains-min (10 s, Ali) unless skipped: the timeline is first added up without moving anything (motion.ts measured()), then played at the pace that reaches the minimum. Ali kept M-39 to M-39b on the Scenario-End Gains Rules page (all option a). An essence offered at the ending (ScenarioGains.essences) gets its own row after achievements, in blue, landing like a perk; it is an offer, so the live panel says to take it or let it go when the moment closes (the essence pop-up follows).
- **M-39a** Gains come one kind at a time, from the commonest to the rarest and in the order they feed each other: skills (the value counts, its bar grows; T1), new perks (the star lands in its struck pose; T3), tree perks (All-XXX) and achievements or scars (the milestone beat, M-38b; T4), the Thread (first its sum: each source of the scenario's XP lands as its own row, foes defeated together, each achievement and quest alone, and the total counts on by that row's amount; then the rule under the sum draws, the total lands in its struck pose, and the bar fills, wrapping once for a level), and last the level-up finale (the number rises, holds its struck pose, and one ring leaves it once). A gold thread draws down a spine of knots, one per kind, while that kind's gains land. No shake and no bounce.
- **M-39b** The action box stays usable under the panel (M-13). While the panel moves, a click on it, Esc or Enter lands every gain at once (M-12). Once it is still, Continue, Esc or Enter in an empty box closes it (exit quicker than entry, M-51), and sending a turn closes it at once. The turn keeps one "What you gained" line in the story that reopens the panel, settled (M-60). Gains in the panel get no second beat in the turn's results.

**Scenarios**

- **M-40** A scenario break (story start or a scenario's end) is the one transition between scenarios, within --m-scenario: the quiet rule draws across (with the place name if the place changed), then the opening narration appears as normal (M-10). Earlier lines stay as they are. The only other authored moments are the fall (the player is incapacitated), the level-up (M-38) and an achievement (M-38a).
- **M-41** Moving between zones, crossing a frontier or reaching a new place in the same scenario gets no transition: the header's place line changes quietly, and a new place gets its named rule in the story, drawn with no motion of its own.
- **M-42** The opening narration at story start uses the scenario-break transition once.

**Motion tokens**

- **M-50** All durations and easings are named CSS variables in :root; no literal durations in components. Starting values: --m-feedback 120ms, --m-state 200ms, --m-beat 220ms, --m-sentence 180ms, --m-reveal-cap 1500ms, --m-stagger 350ms, --m-results-cap 3500ms, --m-count 600ms, --m-trail 700ms, --m-scenario 650ms, --m-fall 1400ms, --m-honor 900ms, --m-hold 80ms (hit-stop), --m-shake 280ms (a shake runs out within it), --m-pulse-step 20ms and --m-pulse-max 290ms (a pulse runs a step longer per strength, never past the max: M-20's under 300 ms), --m-ghost-hold 250ms (a bar's ghost waits this long before it drains), --m-gain-gap 300ms (between gains in the scenario-end panel, M-39), --m-gains-pace 2 (the gains moment's durations are this many times the tokens, M-39), --m-ease cubic-bezier(0.16, 1, 0.3, 1), --m-ease-exit cubic-bezier(0.4, 0, 1, 1).
- **M-51** Exits are faster than entrances.

**Replays, undo and loading**

- **M-60** Loading or reloading, "Show earlier turns", opening any fold (Undone, a turn's changes, Rolls), restoring a backup, /undo, the GM view and a read-only second window all draw the final state with no animation. Turns that reach a read-only window from the other PC land with no animation too.
- **M-61** Only the newest live turn ever animates.
- **M-62** Turns played by "Watch an agent play" are live turns and animate like the player's. The agent's send follows M-12: anything still running finishes at once.

**Always**

- **M-70** Animate only transform, opacity and colour (colour for state changes). Never animate width, height, margins, positions or other layout properties; a bar moves by scaleX on its fill. No glows and no shadows, moving or still, except the toast shadow.
- **M-71** No bounce or elastic easing.
- **M-72** Motion always plays (Ali, 14/11/2026, replacing "reduced motion, or the Motion switch, means nothing moves"). The game has no Motion switch, and it does not follow the system's reduced-motion setting: an old stored switch and prefers-reduced-motion change nothing, on the play page and the library viewer alike. A skip (M-12) still lands everything at once.
- **M-73** Every animation must be interruptible and must end in exactly the state a skip would produce.
- **M-74** Every element is set to its final state first, and the animation plays on top of it. A value animates only when it differs between the screen before and after the turn, never because the screen redrew, and a redraw never restarts or cuts short a running beat.

### Game sound

Decided by Ali, 14/11/2026 (Questions hub page "Audio for the game", AU1–AU12). Built in src/ui/sound.ts (playing, the switch, code-made sounds), src/ui/sfx.ts (the sound files) and CONFIG.SOUND (every level, pitch and length; placeholders).

- AU1: sound effects only for now: every clickable clicks, the player's own roll, blows on either side, the scenario end and the death scene. No background sound per place; music is parked as D-328.
- AU6: sound keeps the motion rules. Sounds come only from the newest live turn, once it has resolved, and start with its strike card; never while a turn runs, never on load, undo, earlier turns or a read-only window, so their timing can't tell which agents ran.
- AU7: a Sound switch beside Show rolls on the play header, with one volume slider while it is on; both kept per viewer. Browsers allow sound only after the first click on the page.
- AU2/AU8: the player's own roll is one code-made sound family: one note per tier row, a dull sagging thud at Critical Failure rising to a bright ring at Critical Success, the ends ringing longer. Each step past either end (Critical Success+, Critical Failure−) adds one voice an octave out, so it never runs out of tiers. An attack plays its blow instead.
- AU9/AU11: blows by outcome from Kenney's public-domain packs (fetched from GitHub copies, since Sonniss blocks cloud downloads): missed or dodged swishes (code-made), parried rings steel, blocked thuds wood, grazed slices, full and heavy hits punch, a crushing hit lays plate over a heavy hit; each blow that lands on the player thuds, a stagger apart. Sonniss picks replace them when Ali's PC sends them (AU11 b).
- AU12 a: BBC Sound Effects (personal use) and Sonniss (no redistribution) files never go in this public repository. They live in the project files (audio/licensed/) and are uploaded only to the private play page, at the paths SOUND_FILES names as `licensed`. The scenario end plays BBC's gong and the death scene BBC's groan and body fall; where a licensed file is missing, Kenney's heavy bell and a code-made falling tone play instead.
- AU5: the project skill rpg-audio holds these rules for every thread.
- Scaling: sound depends on outcome tiers and blow outcomes only, never on skill values, so nothing caps at 100; past the tier ends a voice is added per step.

### Turn checker

TQ1 (Ali, 13/11/2026): world rules checked after every turn, whatever feature ran (src/save/turnCheck.ts, one rule per line in RULES). In tests a broken rule fails the test (the w4-kit play() helper and every createGame turn); in a playtest it stops the run and writes turn-check.log; in the game it is filed as bug code turn\_check and play goes on. Rules: real\_zone, inside\_zone, move\_event (a zone change has a move, a ride on the player's travel, or a logged caught\_up), dead\_still, event\_refs, fight\_ends, invariants (the load checks: one player, one current class, slots, grips, worn covers, stacks), one\_item\_one\_place, condition\_range, links\_real, view\_pronouns, heard\_only. A rule an open bug breaks in named tests is listed under that BG in tests/harness/turnCheckSetup.ts until its fix lands. A new feature adds its own rule.

Soak run (TQ2 b): a seeded random player plays whole turns through createGame with no Claude calls (its Parser answers are random intents from its own PlayerView; every other call fails, so NPCs run on code routines and fallbacks), with the turn checker every turn (tests/soak). When the player falls the world starts again (the rng goes on) until the turn budget is spent, every 10 turns the game reopens from its save, the random player tries every Parser action and gear op, and the same world and seed must play the same turns. A throw the game catches so play can go on (a background Planner, the story text save) is filed as code\_error and fails a strict test. Every CI run plays a small one (with the director, so new ground is fallback ground) on the test village and on real worlds saved from playtests (tests/soak/worlds, added with scripts/soak-worlds.mjs); a large one runs nightly with new seeds (.github/workflows/nightly.yml). A failure names its world and seed, so it replays.

Replay set (TQ3 a): every playtest run's real agent answers replayed through today's code in CI (tests/replay). A run's fixture is its earliest backup the log covers (for a resumed run whose log no backup covers, the saved story as it resumed: resumed-from.json, written by playtest --resume, else the run folder one letter back) and every later step's player input with the Parser, Planner, Responder and Voice answers it got (scripts/replay-set.mjs, run on each new playtest folder and committed with the next PR). Each call gets the logged answer for its agent and actor from the step being replayed (or the next step, for a Planner that runs in the background), so a refused line doesn't push the rest of the run out of step. A throw or a broken world rule fails, and so does a line the run accepted that today's rules refuse (a regression, unless named in REFUSED\_ON\_PURPOSE with the reason), until the world parts from the run's (background Weaver ground). Weaver answers made inside a turn are in the step log; ones made in the background between turns (new ground ahead of a crossing) are not yet, so that ground is fallback ground in a replay and the run's later lines may not fit it.

Parser test set (TQ4, nightly only): about 40 player lines, many from past Parser bugs, parsed by today's Parser prompt on the real model against the test village (tests/parser-eval, PARSER_EVAL=1, through the signed-in `claude` CLI that playtests use, scripts/claude-cli.ts). It runs as a daily scheduled Claude task, not in GitHub CI, so it needs no API key there; a line that misses twice in a row fails and becomes a BG-n bug. A new Parser bug adds its line.

PR rule (TQ5 a), for every feature and fix PR: (1) a new action, actor kind, view field or shared rule adds its rows to the completeness tables, which fail CI when a row is missing (tests/unit/action-table.test.ts: every Parser and NPC action decides its reach need and who can take it); (2) a change of meaning (zone links to metres, numbers to keys) adds a guard test that bans the old pattern, with a short list of allowed places and why (tests/harness/guard.ts bannedLines); (3) a change that touches a view, an agent call or narration adds one whole-turn test through createGame (tests/harness/turn.ts wholeTurn: the Narrator narrates every event, other agents fail unless queued so NPCs use their code minds), and never sets by hand what the turn itself sets; (4) the PR description fills "Siblings checked" and "Tests" (.github/pull_request_template.md; the PR rule workflow fails without them).

Browser smoke tests (TQ7): five flows on the play page built as in production, on the real game with an in-memory save and a stand-in Claude (tests/smoke, `npm run smoke`, its own CI job with Chromium): load and create a character, play a turn, report a bug, switch to the agent's story and back, restore a backup from the GM view. The stand-in Parser reads every line as a look or, after "say ", as words; the Narrator narrates every event; other agents fail, so NPCs use their code minds. Time is virtual, so the turn windows pass at once.

### Case tests

One hundred and three tests: the original eight, updated, eight for the rev. 2 changes, ten for Explore mode, twenty for scenario setup, fourteen for the actor-control review, one for scenario-plan bands, thirteen for the routines review, ten for the hit roll, fourteen for gear and five for fallback behavior. Each runs against logged agent outputs, so it is repeatable.

| # | Test | Passes when |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Ambush stays hidden | A guard's ambush trigger is set on the bridge; over the turns before it springs, the Narrator's text contains none of its terms |
| 2 | Simultaneous turns | A Planner call's logged input has no trace of that turn's player text; a dialogue call's logged input has only that turn's spoken words and impressions |
| 3 | Stealth, per observer | The player hides from guard 1 (success) and guard 2 (failure); guard 1's next view shows suspicious with the old zone and its orders target that zone; guard 2's shows aware |
| 4 | Partial perception | An enemy moves out of sight noisily; the Narrator describes the sound only, with no name |
| 5 | Plan persistence | A trigger written on turn 3 fires on turn 7 without the Planner ordering it again |
| 6 | Malformed Planner output | The game continues with combat lists and routines and logs the failure |
| 7 | Mode switch | Explore turns make no Planner call; the first hostile actor who becomes suspicious switches the next turn to Tactical, and only that actor gets a Planner call |
| 8 | Replay | The same saved state, RNG state and logged agent outputs reproduce the same events, run under the library version the turn record names |
| 9 | Knowledge isolation | Guard 1 sees the player; guard 2, out of earshot, has no belief about the player until it perceives guard 1's shout, and then holds suspicious with source: 'told' |
| 10 | NPC hears the question | In a dialogue turn, each spokesperson's logged input contains that turn's spoken words and never the player's action type, move or approach |
| 11 | Same dialogue shape | An ordinary and a guarded conversation make the same number of calls in the same order, and show results at the same time |
| 12 | Voice can't invent facts | A Voice output sharing a fact outside knows is rejected and replaced |
| 13 | Clarify keeps Planners | After a clarify question: turn unchanged, no events; when the player answers, the turn resolves with the Planner outputs logged before it, within one session |
| 14 | Trigger fires once | A once trigger whose condition holds at turn start and mid-turn fires exactly once |
| 15 | Perception at the moment | A guard downed in step 2 perceives step 1's event and not step 3's |
| 16 | Same roll code | Player and NPC attacks pass through the same ruleset.roll and ruleset.attack path (unit test) |
| 17 | Patrol is heard | A patrol crosses a zone linked to the player's; NarrationView has a partial move event with cue sound and no name |
| 18 | Sneaking persists | The player sneaks on turn 1 and moves on turns 2 and 3 without saying "sneak"; detection on turn 3 uses the contested roll |
| 19 | Open player is seen | An open player's major action in view of an unaware guard makes the guard aware with no check or roll; at partial perception the guard becomes suspicious |
| 20 | Detection is individual | Guard 1 detects the player; guard 2 elsewhere stays unaware, keeps its routine and gets no Planner call until it perceives a communication event |
| 21 | Successful ambush | The target's order source is routine and no Planner call is made that turn; next turn only engaged actors get Planner calls |
| 22 | Failed ambush | The target is aware before the blow; its order that turn is its onAlert order; no Planner call that turn; next turn its Planner order is used |
| 23 | Silent kill | Nobody perceives the attack; the next turn is Explore |
| 24 | Same shape after submit | Explore and Tactical make the same sequence of calls after submit, and resolution never starts before the previous display plus BACKGROUND\_WINDOW |
| 25 | Late Planner | A Planner call not back by BACKGROUND\_WINDOW gives that actor its type's combat list, discards the whole output and logs planner\_late |
| 26 | Code minds read ActorView only | Routine and combat-list functions can't receive GameState (type-level test) |
| 27 | Story start | Turn 0 makes one event roll, one Weaver call and one Narrator call, and is saved as turn 0 |
| 28 | Place change is not a scenario | The player walks from the bar's zone to the street's zone (two places, one stage): no event roll, no Weaver call |
| 29 | The fight spills outside | An engaged NPC follows the player from the bar into the street; its placeId changes with its zone, and the turn stays Tactical |
| 30 | Background extension | With the player in a zone linked to a frontier, one Weaver call starts after the save, is logged, and is not applied before the player crosses |
| 31 | Crossing reuses the tier | Crossing applies the held extension with the scenario's tier; no event roll is made |
| 32 | Late extension | The player crosses before the extension returns; the turn waits for it, and no fallback is used |
| 33 | Grades must fit the tier | A Failure-tier plan with gain major fails validation, gets one repair retry with the errors, and the corrected plan applies |
| 34 | Fallback is flagged | A plan that fails twice yields the fallback stage, and all four flags appear: stage record, weaver\_fallback log, GM banner, play-screen marker |
| 35 | Stakes only | A Failure-tier plan whose threats are unaware and unalert passes validation |
| 36 | Event-roll modifiers | A perk naming the event roll changes its total; a Wounded health level and a terrain bonus do not |
| 37 | Natural edge | A natural 01 on the event roll lowers the tier by one, never two; only the lowest and highest NATURAL\_EDGE results shift a tier |
| 38 | Hidden terms | A public zone description containing one of the plan's hiddenTerms fails validation |
| 39 | Opening narration covers who is seen | Every full presence event appears in the Narrator's narrated list; a narration that omits one fails the fact check |
| 40 | Nothing hidden in the opening | The logged NarrationView at scenario start holds no tier, grade, event summary, Weaver note or opening belief |
| 41 | Labels until names are known | The opening uses the stranger label; after a line in which the speaker says their name, the next NarrationView uses the name. A bandmate named in front of the player is learned too; a false name, or someone named out of sight, is not. An NPC who hears the player give their name keeps it (BG-95). A name said of someone out of sight is heard of and attaches when they answer to it (NM2); a believed false name is what the player calls them, a caught one is not; a passing remark still teaches a name (NM3, NM6) |
| 42 | Sneaking arrival | A sneaking player crossing a frontier triggers detection tests; the plan's opening beliefs are not applied to those observers |
| 43 | One belief writer | Opening beliefs on open entry are written through updateBeliefs only (unit test) |
| 44 | Undo rerolls the hand | Undoing the scenario-start turn and redoing it makes a new event roll and a new Weaver call |
| 45 | Replay of a scenario start | Saved state, RNG state and the logged Weaver output reproduce the same stage, with no Weaver call |
| 46 | Saved zone re-entry | A zone saved on its place page but off the stage is linked by a frontier and loads again with no Weaver call |
| 47 | Words beside an attack | The player speaks while attacking: the words are a bark, no dialogue call runs, and only those who heard them have the words (BF67) |
| 48 | Barks copy nothing | An NPC shouts a warning while attacking; listeners' beliefs are unchanged |
| 49 | Deed instead of a reply | An addressed Responder attacks instead of answering: no line is spoken, and the attack resolves at its own initiative |
| 50 | Addressee's Planner | On a dialogue intent the addressee's background Planner call is cancelled; on a clarify question it is kept |
| 51 | Impression only | A deceive check reaches the dialogue call as an impression; the logged input contains no action type |
| 52 | Two bands addressed | Two spokesperson calls run in parallel, each with its own impression; replies resolve in initiative order |
| 53 | Calm bandmates \[Proposed\] | A Voice spokesperson switches to a leave routine; a calm bandmate who heard follows next turn; one who didn't keeps its routine |
| 54 | Allies engage | Guards hostile to bandits engage them without the player present; the guards get Planner calls, and with the player away the turn stays Explore (BF14) |
| 55 | Companion follows | The player moves two zones; a following companion walks one link per turn toward its believed zone, never jumping |
| 56 | Back to the post | A guard pulled off its post walks back and takes up the zone and activity its routine gives for the current turn (from its anchorTurn) |
| 57 | Inert when down | An incapacitated guard gets no order, its ambush trigger doesn't fire, it isn't engaged, and addressing it makes no dialogue call |
| 58 | Derived agenda | An NPC voiced by the Voice gains a plan; next turn the Responder voices it; no save field holds hasAgenda |
| 59 | Place facts stay player-side | No OppositionView, DialogueView or ActorView contains a place fact or headline |
| 60 | Secret slips | A persuade check that meets a check\_passed reveal sets SLIPS; the secret is revealed and the Narrator's leak scan passes |
| 61 | Bands from the plan | A plan creates a smugglers band hostile to the guards' band and sets the guards suspicious of it; each guard gets a suspicious belief about every smuggler on the stage, written through updateBeliefs, and one guard's personal stance overrides its band's relation |
| 62 | Slots per actor | A gate guard's default routine and its alert variant both read its one post binding; the plan binds post once |
| 63 | Route expands | A patrol bound to a three-zone route desires each zone in order, each for the step's duration, then loops |
| 64 | Anchor from startAt | A cast entry with startAt: route places the actor at the first route stop; the logged plan holds no anchorTurn |
| 65 | Phase from the RNG | Two gate guards on one set without startAt get anchors from the seeded RNG; replay reproduces them |
| 66 | Durations are ids | Changing DURATIONS.short changes every step that uses short, with no bank edit |
| 67 | Routine-only degrade | A plan with an unbound route slot fails, gets one repair retry, fails again: only that actor gets idle\_here, the rest applies, all four flags appear |
| 68 | No frontier bindings | A slot bound to a frontier key fails validation |
| 69 | Invalid switch | A Voice output naming a routine outside the actor's list is dropped; the current routine is kept; invalid\_routine is logged |
| 70 | Wait binds here | A companion told to wait stays in the zone it was in when it switched; told to follow, it follows again |
| 71 | Alert keeps the wait | A waiting companion that hears a fight switches to wait\_alert in the same zone, and returns to wait after DECAY\_TURNS |
| 72 | Calm return in phase | A worker alarmed on turn 10 and calm on turn 16 returns to its work routine with its original anchor |
| 73 | Bandmate skip | A calm bandmate whose list lacks the spokesperson's new routine keeps its own routine; routine\_skip is logged |
| 74 | Import rejects | A shared routine that reads slot counter is not promoted, and the error names the slot |
| 75 | Attack floor | An attack whose shifted tier is Failure or worse logs a miss and makes no defense roll |
| 76 | Gap ladder | With forced dice, gap +1 against dodge gives 0.8×, and gap 0 against block lands on a Non-essential part or the general area |
| 77 | No clamp | Critical Success plus a natural 00 gives Critical Success with beyond 1; the packet shows Critical Success+ |
| 78 | Ladder ends | A gap of +6 or −6 uses the ±5 entry |
| 79 | Named part | At gap −2 a hit rolled on the general area moves to the named left leg; one rolled on an arm stays |
| 80 | Aim is code-set | The Parser output never carries aimed; /aim sets it for one turn only |
| 81 | Forbidden part | An aimed head attack against a dodge tie lands in the general area |
| 82 | Shield routing | A block with a shield at gap 0 damages the shield's pool and not the health track |
| 83 | Cover | A ranged attack into a cover-2 zone adds +2 to the gap |
| 84 | No numbers to the Narrator | The combat packet in NarrationView holds no roll, margin, damage, pool or HP value (unit test) |
| 85 | Packed sword does nothing | A sword stowed in the inventory adds no skill bonus to a melee hit roll; drawn, it does |
| 86 | One copy counts | Two identical lucky charms give the same event-roll modifier as one |
| 87 | Carried charm | A charm whose entry names the event roll and is active when carried modifies the roll at scenario setup; one without the event roll never does |
| 88 | Tool auto-pick | A lockpick action with two lockpick kits carried uses the better one; with both hands full, the off-hand item is stowed and no action is spent on it |
| 89 | Crippled arm holds | A shield arm goes Crippled: the shield stays held, no block is offered, the hit table gains no change |
| 90 | Destroyed arm drops | A Destroyed sword arm puts the sword on the zone's ground; an observer perceives an item\_dropped event; pick up returns it |
| 91 | Free gear ops | A turn with draw, swap and pick up plus an attack resolves one main action; equip on armor takes the main action |
| 92 | Reach | A spear (2.8 m) can't reach the next zone; a shortbow (50 m) hits a target one linked zone away with sight; the same shot through a link without sight becomes a wasted action |
| 93 | Thrown | A thrown dagger ends on the ground of the target's zone, hit or miss |
| 94 | Uncovered part | A hauberk covering general area and arms: an aimed head hit takes no armor, a torso hit does |
| 95 | Stacked armor | An actor whose race has natural armor wearing armor takes damage reduced by worn, then natural, floor 0, not rounded |
| 96 | Armor pool | A head hit on a heavy helm drains the helm pool before the head pool; the health track ignores the helm pool |
| 97 | Concealed blade | An NPC's view omits a concealed blade until the NPC perceives an attack with it; after that its belief keeps it |
| 98 | Minted reward cap | A quality-3 variant on a moderate-gain scenario is lowered to quality 2, and a weapon boosts of "keen edge" becomes hitAdj; the plan applies with no repair call |
| 99 | Fallback attack | An engaged slime with the player in its zone attacks the player with unarmed\_natural, targetPart null; the order's source is fallback |
| 100 | Fallback target | An engaged minor actor that a companion hit last turn attacks the companion, even with the player in the same zone |
| 101 | Fallback close in | An engaged minor actor whose belief puts the player two links away moves one link along the shortest path, never into a frontier; in that zone with nobody there, it searches |
| 102 | Late Planner fights on | A significant engaged actor whose Planner call is late runs its type's combat list that turn instead of holding |
| 103 | Broken flees (retired, BF37) | Replaced by s302: a broken actor cowers, keeps an allowed action, and never walks or attacks |
| 104 | Improvised weapon | The Parser's improvised chair leg becomes a held improvised\_medium story item with hitAdj IMPROVISED\_HIT\_ADJ and no kit bonus, and the attack uses it |
| 106 | XP and levels | Rating: a slime's is the worth of its one offensive skill, a guard's is higher, misc skills add nothing; defeat XP applies the taper and DEFEAT\_XP\_MULT; a slime the player hit and downed pays its defeat XP once, plus the first-defeat achievement; ten defeats of slimes earn first\_defeat:slime, defeats\_10:slime and defeats\_10 once each, named for the slime; a foe with a race also earns its first race defeat; a foe downed by someone else or broken pays nothing, a surrender pays; LEVEL\_XP + 5 XP levels into the chosen unlocked class, which becomes current, with a perk point at level 2 |
| 105 | Story pacing | Quiet turns count up and an attack resets them; a quiet\_turns 2 beat fires on the third turn start and, with endsScenario, marks a break; rest never ends the scenario; a stall marks one only with no live beat left; the director then starts a new scenario with one Weaver call |
| 107 | Opposed standoff | An opposed guard who notices the player is alarmed (its onAlert fires) but not engaged, so the turn stays Explore; made hostile, it engages |
| 108 | Look around | Someone sitting still one zone away is a shape; a successful look shows them clearly and they stay seen while they stay put; a move the player can't see clearly forgets them; looking is a sight roll, not a check |
| 109 | Skill progression | A use marks a skill only when the opposition is within MARK\_WINDOW of raw or true skill, once, and only for the player; a hit costing ENDURANCE\_MARK\_PCT marks endurance; at the scenario's end a marked skill's paid roll gains by its tier (SG1 C) and shows "Eyesight 38 → 40" plus "New perk unlocked" past a milestone; a failed roll shows nothing; raw never passes its cap (100, 125 tagged, 75 flawed); marks clear |
| 110 | Healing | Rest heals HP and working parts but not a Crippled part; a clinic lifts it; a bandage heals its row in the turn it is used, is used up, and shows "Bandage: Hurt → Bruised"; with nothing to heal it stays in the pack and says so (Q3, 08/11/2026) |
| 111 | Game clock | Each action costs its durations row; waiting until dawn moves the clock to dawn, keeps the scenario and shows "Time passes: 11 h (Day 2 · Dawn)"; a hostile in the zone blocks it; outdoor zones are dark at night unless lit; the Narrator sees the day and time of day; the Weaver picks the hour only at story start; a night-only routine set by day is a routine error; rest cools down in game minutes; natural recovery heals 0.5% an hour at endurance 50, never a Crippled part, fights included |
| 113 | Action economy | A ranged attack in reach drops the move; out of reach the move stands and the attack is dropped; a move two links away with no action is a dash, stopped by a Crippled leg; "after" resolves the action before the move |
| 117 | Closing during the opening | The player's build is kept until turn 0 is saved; a game closed during the opening starts that opening again when reopened, instead of returning to character creation |

### Deliverables and acceptance criteria

Done means every case test passes except those waiting on other work: 46 and 53 test rules still \[Proposed\]. Also, every non-dialogue turn that doesn't start a scenario shows checked narration after one Parser call plus one full Narrator call.

**First build scope.** A fantasy village with a guard, a trader and a tavern patron to talk to. The player is a swordsman, and slimes living around the village are hunted. Hunting gives XP (Part 1 §5) but no skill changes (recordUse() only logs). The slimes' routine set, slime\_wander, is the test of the Agent Weaver integration (Appendix: prototype routine bank).

Build in this order, so the game is playable with stubs before any agent is wired in:

- [ ] State types, seeded RNG with saved state, agent log
- [ ] Ruleset: TIERS, eventRoll, roll, attack, check and contested built (Part 2, First-build ruleset); applyEffects and recordUse stubbed
- [ ] Perception per event, the single belief writer, communication events and belief decay
- [ ] The six deny-by-default view builders (DialogueView built from ActorView; a bark's words only to those who heard them)
- [ ] isActive; bands, relations and overrides; engagement
- [ ] Resolution engine with the simultaneous-order rules, order merging and the exchange slot
- [ ] Hidden store, trigger checker (turn start and mid-turn), fired log and secret reveal
- [ ] Routine bank types, the import validator and the prototype bank (Appendix)
- [ ] Desired-state routines (schedule, follow, wait) with slot bindings, route expansion and code-set anchors, alarm state, onAlert and returnTo; detection on major actions and ambush resolution (Explore mode)
- [ ] Scenarios, the stage, frontiers and the event roll (ruleset.eventRoll)
- [ ] Item library and blueprints, the active-effect reader, slots and gear ops, ground items, the damage step with armor (Part 5)
- [ ] Fallback combat list per actor type (Fallback behavior)
- [ ] Agent Parser, Planner, Responder, Voice and Narrator wrappers, prompts as constants, schemas validated
- [ ] Agent Weaver wrapper, stage-plan validator and the flagged fallback stage
- [ ] Mode switcher, the call cap, spokesperson selection and MAX\_SPOKESPEOPLE
- [ ] Planner scheduler, and the BACKGROUND\_WINDOW and DIALOGUE\_WINDOW gates
- [ ] Leak scan, fact checks, the Voice fact check and the templated fallback narration
- [ ] Save, load, undo and automatic backups (Part 4 §10–11)
- [ ] Creation screen and play screen (Part 2, Play screen)
- [ ] GM debug view
- [ ] The case tests passing, as Done above sets out

Ask before you:

- change any ruleset number;
- add a Claude call to any turn type;
- send anything from state.hidden to Agent Parser, Agent Narrator or Agent Voice;
- settle anything listed under Deferred items.

### Engine details (CS5, CS7)

Choices the engine made where the spec said nothing, settled by Ali (leftover scan CS5 and CS7, 14/11/2026):

- **Event noise and sight.** Each event the engine makes outside an activity has a noise and a visibility (the SENSE table in src/engine/resolve.ts; placeholders to tune). A kind not listed is quiet and normal. Worn armor makes the body-moving kinds louder (AN2).

| Event | Noise | Visibility |
| --- | --- | --- |
| travel, move, item dropped, set down, put in, pick up, way (a key, a bar, a door tried) | quiet | normal |
| attack, wasted | normal | high |
| speak | normal | normal |
| hold, rest | silent | low |
| call for help | loud | normal |
| battle cry | loud | high |
| alarm | deafening | high |
| pick (a lock), stash | hushed | low |
| filch (its own contest sets both) | hushed | close |

- **Bandmates turn on an attacker** only when they perceive the attack fully; a half-seen attack names no attacker.
- **Crossing a frontier.** The player who walks into a frontier stays in their zone until scenario setup or the stage extension places them.
- **Heard speech.** An NPC's "speech heard since its last call" is the previous turn's speech and this turn's exchange.
- **Agent output shapes.** Each agent's output is given here in the spec's own notation; the exact JSON shape the model is told to return is that agent's \*\_SHAPE text in src/agents/prompts.ts, written from that notation and sent with the fixed instructions.
- **Half-seen people (CS7).** Someone the player only half perceives keeps their id, so the player can still target them, but shows only as "someone", with no name, label or gear.

## Part 3 — HP system with partial-damage penalties

### Chosen design: health track + body parts

Every successful hit damages the body part it lands on and, depending on that part's impact, the health track. Total HP comes from endurance and the body type (Total HP); part pools are shares of total HP set by size (Body parts). Health-track levels and part states are % of those pools. Code owns all of it, including a database of body types: Agent Weaver names the body type, and code knows its parts.

- **Health track (World of Darkness):** levels by % of HP lost; one wounds modifier on every roll (Part 1 §4, Layer 2b; rolls only, not checks); each level is a trigger for perks, tactics and story.
- **Body parts:** every body type has a general area plus a list of parts. Each part has a size (hit chance and aim penalty), an impact (how its damage counts on the health track) and a role (what its states do); a part can have several roles. Parts are Healthy, Damaged, Crippled or Destroyed.
- **General area:** always present, stateless, normal impact, and always at least 30% of the hit table. Any damage that isn't aimed at, or doesn't land on, a specific part counts here.
- Attack and defense are two rolls; damage is not rolled. The attack roll is contested by the defender's dodge or block roll, and the gap between their tiers sets the multiplier and can move or restrict the location. Damage is fixed by weapon and armor. Perks can act on the attack roll, the defense roll, the gap or the damage values, each on its own.
- **One damage type** for now.
- WoD's sixth level "Crippled" is renamed **Critical**, so "Crippled" only ever means a body part.

### Total HP

```
HP = (HP_BASE + HP_PER_END × endurance) × race HP multiplier
```

HP\_BASE 50, HP\_PER\_END 1 (*placeholders*); race HP multiplier Human and Elf ×1, Slime ×0.6, Dragon ×3; every race has one (BR2, Ali 08/11/2026). Endurance 0 → 50 HP, 50 → 100 HP, 100 → 150 HP.

### Health track

Total HP is divided into levels by the share of HP lost, following World of Darkness.

| Level | HP lost | Wounds modifier (roll points) | Example hooks |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Unhurt | 0% | 0 | — |
| Bruised | 1–15% | 0 | Free buffer; a toughness perk can widen this band |
| Hurt | 16–30% | −5 | Perk: ignore the Hurt penalty |
| Injured | 31–45% | −10 | Fear: reaching Injured, Wounded or Mauled costs morale (rolled) |
