| Wounded | 46–60% | −15 | Perk: +10 to melee hit rolls while Wounded or worse. Story flag "visibly hurt": NPCs react |
| Mauled | 61–75% | −20 | +20 to intimidate a Mauled foe; Mauled enemies may surrender |
| Critical | 76–99% | −30 | One action per turn; last-stand perks switch on; fear: reaching Critical costs more morale (rolled) |
| Incapacitated | 100–149% | Out of the fight | Lethal fight: the story ends; otherwise a survival roll (D-54) |
| Dead | 150% | Out for good | The player's own Dead is never shown (it shows as Incapacitated). Others show "Down" once they fall, and "Dead" when the player sees them clearly (full sight); the Narrator does see it. No healing row heals it (`revivesDead` is no on every row, a lever for resurrection later) |

HP lost keeps counting past 100% while an actor is down, up to 150%, where it is Dead (Ali, 08/11/2026; first set at 200%). Someone down can still be struck (BG-25): the blow lands as on someone unaware (no defense), and a blow on someone already down does not count as downing them again. Only the dead can't be struck, and an NPC's blow on the downed player is wasted, since the survival roll (D-54) decides the player's fate. A wasted action carries its reason (already dead, out of reach, nerve broken, yielded to, spar held) for the Narrator, which tells that and never makes up a miss (Narrator rule 18). A destroyed essential part (head, neck) is Dead.

The penalty is a roll modifier of type wounds (Part 1 §4, Layer 2b), diminishing together with body-part role penalties. It does not apply to no-dice checks (settles OQ-15).

Bands are 15% wide; Critical takes the remaining 24% so a last stand lasts a little longer. Penalty values are placeholders; they stack by Part 1 §4, Stacking within a type. The health track never modifies the event roll (Part 1 §4).

**Morale** (Ali, 10/11/2026, morale bar MB1–MB43). Morale is a second, mental health bar that every actor has, the player included (MB11). Max morale = MORALE\_BASE (50) + MORALE\_PER\_RESOLVE (1) × Resolve (raw), as HP comes from Endurance, plus the flat max morale the actor's current achievements give (the maxMorale reward, lower levels of a chain included; D-278); an NPC template sets its starting Resolve (MB4; a high Resolve is not immunity), and raw Resolve also sets the Nerve tier (Part 4, Personality). Fears cost flat points (MB5), one row each in the library fears table: reaching Injured to Mauled 15 and Critical 50 (rolled, only while engaged, the player while someone is fighting them; the health range can move by steps with a perk's or achievement's fearFrom, MB21), a bandmate going Down 10 or breaking 10 when the actor perceives it (MB23), a hit that leaves the actor's own parts Crippled or worse (rolled once per hit, paying for each part by its impact: non-essential 5, normal 15, essential 30 for a 6-part body, × 6 ÷ the body's part count; a part Destroyed rolls again at 1.5 × those costs, read from the Crippled row; Ali, 11/11/2026), losing to an intimidation 10 per tier of the gap, and downing a foe gives back 10. A rolled fear makes the morale roll (Resolve, with renown on every morale roll of a foe fighting the player, MB24, and rally) and pays the cost × MORALE\_TIER\_MULT for its tier: Critical Failure 2 (+0.5 for each step beyond), Failure 1.5, Near Miss 1, Success 0.5, Great Success 0.25, Critical Success 0. The level is the share of max morale lost (MORALE\_TRACK): Calm, Uneasy 15%, Tense 30%, Rattled 45% (−5), Afraid 60% (−5), Terrified 75% (−10), Panicking (−15), Broken at 100% (Shattered banked, MB2). The penalty is type wounds, so it stacks with the health penalty and hits the morale roll too: fear feeds on itself (MB3). Reaching Broken replaces the old instant break (MB1): an NPC is broken for the scenario and, for now, can neither walk nor attack: it cowers unless it chose something still allowed (Fallback behavior, rule 1; BF37); if the roll that broke it was Critical Failure or worse it surrenders instead; its perceiving bandmates pay the bandmate-broken fear. The player at Broken keeps control but cannot walk, run or attack (MB12, BF37), and a break counts as defeated (achievements). **The player's yield** (MB40–MB43): saying "I yield" in a real fight (the Parser's yields, as in a spar) or typing /yield asks the foes who hear it (state.yielding, src/engine/yield.ts): every foe fighting the player (hostile and engaged), and hostile foes who perceive the player by any sense (the yield said at normal volume) and are fighting someone on the player's side (YD1, Ali, 11/11/2026). A hostile who is only tracking or hunting the player, such as one listening from another room, does not hear it (BG-4). A taken yield also covers the taker's hostile bandmates who are together with it (YD2, YD7) and did not refuse it themselves; bandmates apart keep fighting. Once everyone who heard the yield has taken it, the player's fight ends that turn (YD3); a hostile who never heard it starts a new fight. Who counts as a foe in the fight log stays parked (YD4). A foe run by Claude, of an Intelligent race, answers in its next output (acceptYield; no answer is a refusal); code-run foes and lesser races fight on. A taken yield is a truce for the rest of the scenario: neither is hostile to the other, the fight ends, and it counts as defeated. Once yielded, the player cannot attack a foe that took the yield or has yet to answer, and a foe that took it cannot attack the player (MB43). What happens after a yield is OQ-30. Broken is a win condition: a broken actor counts on neither side of engagement. Surrender and a break also turn its stance toward everyone it was hostile to into **yielded** (Q1, Ali 08/11/2026); a yielded actor never attacks and is not a threat, so it blocks no rest or waiting; it still pays defeat XP; attacking it again turns it hostile, and talk steps it from opposed. Recovery comes from the library **composure** table, apart from healing so morale tunes on its own (Ali, 11/11/2026, D-183): natural composure is its hourly row (0.1% of max morale per Resolve point an hour, 5% at 50, MB8); Danger passed, when an engagement ends, gives back MORALE\_ENGAGEMENT\_END\_PCT (25%, MB9); Rest gives REST\_MORALE\_PCT (50%) and Clinic CLINIC\_MORALE\_PCT (100%) (MB34). A healing row names the composure row its action also restores (`composure`), so a rest restores health from the healing table and morale from the composure table in one action. One engine call (calm) applies a row; a perk that restores morale directly calls calmFromPerk and counts under the Perks row (CP5, count only); a unit test (morale-writes) fails if code restores morale anywhere else. Downing a foe and being rallied still restore through the fears table (negative cost). When the scenario ends, yielded becomes opposed (a grudge), broken and surrendered end (Q4), a bar still at Broken moves up to Panicking (one point short), and the actor goes to its exit slot (with no off-stage yet, D-46/D-67, that is how it leaves). Others see an actor's morale only from Afraid down (MORALE\_SHOWN\_FROM, MB16); the actor itself always sees its own. Yielded is not on the STANCES scale: agents cannot choose it. **Dreaded on Sight** (D-236): a quirk whose owner frightens and repels at first sight. Whoever outside the owner's band sees the owner for the first time pays the fears-table row "You set eyes on someone dreaded" (15, rolled, the one rolled fear paid outside a fight too), and from then on reads its band's stance toward the owner one step colder until it has a stance of its own. Once per owner, for good; no race, class or template has it yet. What else the player can do with it is deferred (D-98).

**Fatigue and stamina** (Ali, 13/11/2026, Fatigue and Stamina page FT1–FT28; src/ruleset/fatigue.ts, src/engine/fatigue.ts). Every actor has a **stamina bar** in two layers, both sized STAMINA\_BASE (50) + STAMINA\_PER\_POINT (1) × Stamina (raw), as HP comes from Endurance (FT2). The **long term** (its name in game text: **sleep debt**) fills with hours awake, spilled strain, a share of all strain and travel, and only sleep clears it for now (FT18). The **fatigue level** is the share of the long term used (FATIGUE\_TRACK, names in the FATIGUE\_LEVELS table): Rested 0%, Fine 15%, Tired 30% (−5), Weary 45% (−10), Exhausted 60% (−15), Drained 75% (−20), Dead on your feet 90% (−30), Collapsed 100%. The penalty is type wounds, so it stacks with health and morale wounds (FT3). The **short line** (the thin line under the bar) holds the level's band of the bar: 100, 90, 80, 65, 50, 35, 20% (FT19, FT22). **Strain** has no roll and no multiplier (FT24): every row of the Action times table names an **effort tier** (EFFORT, an ordered table; a new tier is a new row), and the action pays that tier's strain (STRAIN\_COST: Base 0, Light 0.5, Moderate 1, Hard 2, All-out 4; FT25) when it is made: once for a row of STRAIN\_LONG\_S (10 s) or less, per 10 s it runs for a longer one (a long action pays for the share it ran). Movement pays per STRAIN\_MOVE\_S (3 s) moved: a walk (move time) at WALK\_EFFORT Base, a run (a dash, main time) at RUN\_EFFORT Hard. A dodge, block or parry, which has no row, pays DEFENSE\_EFFORT Light. Placement (FB1–FB3): Base free actions, words, drawing, stowing, swapping and picking up, a door, bar or key, a drink, rest, sleep, waiting, trading; Light a look round, handing over, a crossbow's shot itself; Moderate bandaging, picking a lock, hiding (armour on or off is Base and instant since 14/11/2026); Hard a blow, a throw, a bow shot, spanning a light or stirrup crossbow; All-out spanning a heavy crossbow, Battle Cry. An action with no row of its own (a grab, a filch, steadying yourself, a shout) takes the default row, Base; picking a lock pays the Pick a lock row. Worn armour no longer multiplies strain (TP6 d, less realism; it was medium ×1.5, heavy ×2). Strain drains the line; what an empty line can't hold spills into the long term at SPILL\_SHARE (half, FT23). Every strain point paid also adds STRAIN\_RESIDUE (10%) of itself to the long term (SR4), so fights carry into the night: at Stamina 50 four 5-turn fights add about 9% of the bar. Travel pays by the hour straight into the long term (SR5, see Travel on the road). The line itself gives no roll penalty (SR1 d). Check: a blow and a defence every 3 s (2.5 a round) empties the line in 1 min at Stamina 0, 2 at 50, 3 at 100, 5 at 200. **Sleep debt** (FT4, FT12): each hour awake adds SLEEP\_DEBT\_FLAT 0.5 points + SLEEP\_DEBT\_PCT 1.5% of the bar, × the body type's sleepNeed (left out = 1, every body type today; 0 = never needs sleep, still feels strain). **Recovery** is its own library table beside healing and composure (recovery, RV; FT7), and every refill goes through one engine call per layer (recoverLine, recoverDebt): **Sleep** clears SLEEP\_CLEAR\_PCT 4% of the bar an hour from the long term × the spot (SLEEP\_SPOT\_MULT: in a zone with a bed 1, elsewhere 0.75) × worn armour (SLEEP\_ARMOR\_MULT: all 1 since Ali 14/11/2026, sleeping in armour makes no difference; was light 1, medium 0.75, heavy 0.5), and fills the line (FT8). **Rest** (the one-hour long action) fills the line by the share of its hour that ran. **Catching your breath**: while awake, once BREATH\_CALM\_S (10 s) have passed since the last effort above Light, the line refills BREATH\_BASE\_PCT 15% + Stamina × BREATH\_PER\_POINT\_PCT 0.05% of its size a minute (FT28; to balance later). **Perks** is a counting row for later stamina perks. The clock runs this for every active actor, on stage or off: an NPC's routine sleep is sleep, and off-stage time adds hours awake and asleep (FT11). **Sleep length** (FT9): "sleep" lasts until the long term is clear at this spot's rate, within SLEEP\_MIN\_HOURS 1 to SLEEP\_MAX\_HOURS 24, or until the time of day it names; waking (a blow, a noise, Light Sleeper) or a watch ends it early, and the watches of a shared night still split it (NW15). The Sleep row's own seconds are 0 for that reason. **Limits** (FT10, for now): sleep debt that fills the bar (an hour awake taking it to 100%) puts the actor to sleep where it stands until rested (the player's sleep is a night of its own); spilled strain that fills it leaves the actor **Spent**: no action above Light (it comes to nothing, wasted with reason spent; a dash and Battle Cry are refused) until the bar is below full, which only sleep does. A Spent actor who stays awake still falls asleep once its hours awake alone would have filled the bar (FB4). Falling asleep from exhaustion keeps the guards of falling asleep by routine (BF69, `mayDropOff` in src/engine/sleep.ts): not within FALL_ASLEEP_MIN of waking, nor while alarmed, broken, engaged or held; once those lift, the next minute takes it. Fatigue changes no healing, composure or fear cost, and sleeping gives no bonus (FT13, FT14). **Who sees it** (FT17, FT20): the player sees their own bar (PlayerView.player.stamina: level, points left of the bar and of the line, spent); others' level shows from FATIGUE\_SHOWN\_FROM (Exhausted) down; an NPC's own view and the Narrator get the level word. **On screen** (FT20): the sheet shows Stamina under Morale, in its own colour: the level word (and on the full sheet the points left of the bar), one bar of what is left of the long term with ticks at the level marks, and under it the thin breath line, as wide as the level's band of the bar and filled by what is left of it (hover: what breath is). Tired and worse add "Tired: −5 to rolls"; Spent shows as a chip beside the label (src/ui/charsheet.ts staminaBar). The fake page's "tire" adds sleep debt and spends breath; "breathe" fills the line. Who's here shows someone else's fatigue as a chip with the level word in the stamina colour, only from Exhausted down and never on someone Down; no points or breath line (FT17; src/ui/play.ts). The fake page's guard Bren starts Exhausted. **The Stamina skill** (FT15, FT27): SK37, the third Fortitude skill, so Fortitude's All-50 (Hard to Kill) is earnable; function Stamina (FN17) in its own group Stamina (GR12) at quarter XP. It is buyable at creation like any skill; NPC templates copy their Endurance value (FB7). The player's Stamina is marked once per STAMINA\_MARK\_EVERY (50) points of strain paid, any effort, armour included (FB6); no rolls. **Scaling:** the bar and line grow in a straight line with Stamina, with no cap; strain is paid in full at every level, and supernatural efforts get new tiers above All-out (8, 16 …). Tired comes after 12 h awake at Stamina 0, 15 h at 50, 16 h at 100, 18 h at 200, never later than 20 h: staying up for days is perk territory. A full breath back takes about 6½ min at 0, 5¾ at 50, 5 at 100, 4 at 200. Status effects such as bleeding stay separate (D-198).

**Healing** (Ali, 07/11/2026, "Healing: options" H1–H15). Every source is a row of one library table (healing): HP %, part-pool %, its reach (the worst part state it heals, a key of the PART\_STATES scale, so a perk can shift it by steps), whether it works in a fight, and the place kinds it needs; one engine rule applies a row, so amounts stay consistent (B5) and stronger tiers are new rows. Rows now (placeholders): rest 25/25 (its morale from the composure Rest row), clinic 100/100 (morale from the composure Clinic row) and lifts Crippled parts (resting in a place of kind clinic or infirmary), bandage 10/10, usable in a fight as the main action and used up (an item names its row with `heal`; the player starts with STARTING\_BANDAGES 2). A part worse than the row's reach is skipped; a Crippled or worse part a row reaches starts again from 0 (H4). Rest and bandage reach Damaged; the clinic reaches Crippled. A rest action (the turn's main action) needs no active hostile in the zone and waits REST\_COOLDOWN\_MIN (*placeholder*, 240) game minutes (H5, T7). **Natural recovery** (H12–H15) is the hourly row passive: every active actor on the stage heals endurance (true skill) × 0.01 % an hour (0.5% at 50) of HP and of each part it reaches (never Crippled or worse), all the time, fights included, as the clock moves; a line shows only when a health level or part state changes. The composure table's natural composure row does the same for morale from Resolve (Part 3, Morale). NPCs use the same table when they rest; healing off stage waits for D-67 (H8). A Destroyed part stays gone unless a special perk, item or story event brings it back (settles OQ-6). The turn shows a plain line such as "Bandage: Hurt → Bruised"; its look is the designer's (H11). Later: a first-aid skill (H9, D-14), bleeding and other statuses (H10, D-15). **Healing by source** (Ali, 10/11/2026, D-172): achievements count the HP the player actually gets back, as a flat number (the health % healed × their total HP; body parts not counted), kept apart by where it came from: the healing row's id (rule `healed`, by `heal_source`, name placeholder {source}). Rest, natural recovery, clinic and bandage each keep their own count. Healing from perks counts under the Perks row (HL6, `countOnly`: never applied itself), and only for perks that heal directly (Second Wind's jump). A perk that boosts another source counts as that source: Fast Healer's doubled natural recovery is all natural recovery (Ali). Every perk that heals directly goes through one engine call (healFromPerk), so a new healing perk counts here with no extra code; a unit test (hp-writes) fails if code lowers health lost anywhere else. A rest bonus from an achievement (`restPct`) still counts as rest.

**Visibility:** for other characters, Claude is told the level only from Wounded down; Bruised to Injured stay hidden, though code still applies their penalties.

**Thresholds as triggers**

- Perks read the level: they can ignore a level's penalty, widen a band, or switch on a bonus at a level.
- Tactics read the level: enemy AI retreats, surrenders or turns desperate at set levels, and players get options against hurt foes.
- Story reads the level: crossing into Wounded or worse sets flags that scenarios, companions and NPCs can check.

Code fires one event per change, `threshold_crossed {who, from, to}`; perks, AI and story scripts listen for it.

### Body parts

Every part carries a size, an impact and one or more roles, all stored in code. Each part's pool is a share of total HP set by its size: Extra small 15%, Small 20%, Normal 30%, Big 50% (placeholders; Tiny is not in use). Its state is measured as % of its own pool. The general area has no pool and no state.

Role penalties (Damaged and Crippled columns of the role table) are roll modifiers of type wounds.

An essential part (head, neck; the impact column) that is Crippled or worse adds a further wounds modifier of −5 (CONFIG.ESSENTIAL_CRIPPLED_PENALTY) to every roll the character makes, once however many essential parts are crippled. Tough It Out counts the part one step milder, as for role penalties. The D-54 survival roll stays modifier-free.

At reference HP 100: head 20, each arm 30, each leg 30, neck 15. A medium blow (25) Cripples a head and a second Destroys it; a limb goes Damaged, Crippled, Destroyed over three blows.

**States**

| State | Part pool | What Claude sees |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Healthy | Above 50% | Healthy |
| Damaged | 50% to 1% | Healthy: Claude is never told; code still applies it |
| Crippled | 0% to −99% | Crippled |
| Destroyed | −100% or lower | Destroyed |

A Destroyed part no longer exists: code removes it from the hit table and rebuilds the table without it. Its roles stay lost for good.

**Size: hit chance and aim penalty**

| Size | Hit weight | Aim penalty (hit roll) | Example |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Tiny | 0.25 | −40 | An eye, a finger (not in use yet) |
| Extra small | 0.5 | −35 (placeholder) | Human neck |
| Small | 1 | −30 | Human head, a dragon's tail |
| Normal | 2 | −20 | Arms, legs, wings |
| Big | 4 | −10 | A giant's oversized arm |
| General area | 30% minimum, +4 weight if marked big | 0 | Human torso; a dragon's body |

The aim penalty is a roll modifier of its own type (aim).

Size is set per body type, not per part name: one body type can have big arms and small legs.

**Impact: link to the health track**

| Impact | Damage counted on the health track | When Destroyed |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Non-essential | None: the part absorbs it | Part is gone; total HP untouched |
| Normal | 1× | Part is gone |
| Essential | 1.5× | Total HP drops to 0 |

**Roles: what each state does**

| Role | Shared | Damaged | Crippled | Checks it applies to |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Sense (head) | Yes | −10 | −20 to all checks | Perception and mental checks when Damaged; every check when Crippled |
| Grip (arm) | No: per limb | −10 | Unusable: keeps holding its item but can't attack, block or use a tool with it; no two-handed actions. A Destroyed arm drops what it held to the ground (Part 5 §4). | Anything done with that limb: melee, shooting, climbing, fine work |
| Move (legs) | Yes | −10 | −20 to dodge (movement effects: D-99) | Dodge, athletics, stealth, fleeing |
| Fly (wing) | Yes | −10 to flying | Can't fly | Flight |
| Strike (tail, horns, bite) | No: per part | −10 to that attack | That attack is unavailable | Attacks made with the part |
| Speech (head) | Yes | −10 | −20 | Speech rolls: persuasion, intimidation, barter (Ali, 08/11/2026). With a Crippled head a speech roll takes both −20s, stacked within the wounds type (SR4) |

- **Several roles:** a part can carry more than one (a dragon's head is Sense and Strike, for its bite); each role's effects apply.
- **Shared roles:** the penalty is split between the parts that have the role. A dragon has two Move parts, so one Damaged pair of legs gives −5, not −10.
- **Per-limb roles** (Grip, Strike) are not split: each limb acts on its own. Two-handed actions take the worse arm's penalty.
- Code tags every skill with the roles it uses, so a Damaged left arm never touches a persuasion check.
- **Destroyed** (CS24): a Destroyed part's role penalty is DESTROYED\_PART\_WEIGHT (1.2) times the Crippled one (−24 where Crippled is −20), on top of what Destroyed already does (off the hit table, its roles lost for good, an arm drops its item).

On/off effects of shared roles (no running, can't fly) switch on when Crippled and Destroyed parts reach SHARED\_ROLE\_OFF\_PCT (60%) of the role's parts (ruleset roleOff), with a Destroyed part counting DESTROYED\_PART\_WEIGHT (1.2) Crippled ones, the same weight as its role penalty (CS24). A dragon with one Crippled wing still flies (50%); one Destroyed wing grounds it (60%). A spider needs 5 of 8 legs Crippled, or 4 Destroyed.

### Building the hit table

1. The general area takes its 30% minimum.
2. Every other part still in existence gets its size weight; a big general area also gets weight 4 on top of its minimum.
3. The remaining 70% is split in proportion to weight and rounded to whole numbers that sum to 100: each takes its whole share, and the points left go one each by largest remainder; ties go to the bigger weight, then to the later entry (this reproduces the tables below).
4. Parts are laid out in database order, general area last; a separate d100 location roll picks the band, read as rolled (a 37 hits the part whose band holds 37; no digit reversal).
5. When a part is Destroyed, the table is rebuilt from step 1 without it.
6. A shield is never on the hit table; it takes only hits routed to it by a block.

**Humanoid**

| Part | Size | Impact | Roles | Location roll |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Head | Small | Essential | Sense, Speech | 01–07 |
| Left arm | Normal | Normal | Grip | 08–22 |
| Right arm | Normal | Normal | Grip | 23–37 |
| Left leg | Normal | Normal | Move | 38–52 |
| Right leg | Normal | Normal | Move | 53–67 |
| Neck | Extra small | Essential | — | 68–70 |
| General (torso) | — | Normal | — | 71–00 |

Humanoids have a left and a right leg, each with its own pool (Ali, 07/11/2026). Move is a shared role, so one Damaged leg gives half the Move penalty; one Crippled leg (50% of the role's parts) does not stop running, one Destroyed leg (counted twice) does. Saves from schema v3 are converted: both legs start from the old legs pool, and armor that covered legs covers both.

The neck is adjacent to the head and the general area. Head and neck are separate in every calculation.

**Dragon (example)**

| Part | Size | Impact | Roles | Location roll |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Head | Normal | Essential | Sense, Strike, Speech | 01–09 |
| Left wing | Normal | Non-essential | Fly | 10–18 |
| Right wing | Normal | Non-essential | Fly | 19–27 |
| Forelegs | Normal | Normal | Move | 28–36 |
| Hind legs | Normal | Normal | Move | 37–46 |
| Tail | Small | Non-essential | Strike | 47–51 |
| General (body) | Big | Normal | — | 52–00 |

The dragon's big body gives its general area 49% (30% minimum + 19%). Crippling a wing hurts its flight without costing it any HP; destroying the tail removes it from the table, and the other parts' bands grow to fill the gap.

### Body type database

Code keeps every body type, its parts and the role table. Agent Weaver names the body type by catalog id in the stage plan; code loads the entry, builds the hit table, and tracks every part from then on.

```json
{
  "roles": {
    "sense": {"shared": true},
    "grip": {"shared": false},
    "move": {"shared": true},
    "fly": {"shared": true},
    "strike": {"shared": false},
    "speech": {"shared": true}
  },
  "bodyTypes": {
    "humanoid": {
      "general": {"name": "torso", "size": "normal"},
      "naturalAttacks": [{"id": "punch", "tree": "unarmed", "class": "punch", "skill": "unarmed_strike", "reach": 0, "grips": 0},
                        {"id": "grapple", "tree": "unarmed", "class": "grapple", "skill": "unarmed_grapple", "reach": 0, "grips": 0}],
      "parts": [
        {"id": "head", "size": "small", "impact": "essential", "roles": ["sense", "speech"], "adjacent": ["general", "neck"]},
        {"id": "left_arm", "size": "normal", "impact": "normal", "roles": ["grip"]},
        {"id": "right_arm", "size": "normal", "impact": "normal", "roles": ["grip"]},
        {"id": "left_leg", "size": "normal", "impact": "normal", "roles": ["move"]},
        {"id": "right_leg", "size": "normal", "impact": "normal", "roles": ["move"]},
        {"id": "neck", "size": "extra_small", "impact": "essential", "roles": [], "adjacent": ["head", "general"]}
      ]
    },
    "amorphous": {
      "general": {"name": "body", "size": "normal"},
      "naturalAttacks": [{"id": "slam", "tree": "unarmed", "class": "slam", "skill": "unarmed_natural", "reach": 0, "grips": 0}],
      "parts": []
    },
    "winged_quadruped": {
      "general": {"name": "body", "size": "big"},
      "parts": [
        {"id": "head", "size": "normal", "impact": "essential", "roles": ["sense", "strike", "speech"]},
        {"id": "left_wing", "size": "normal", "impact": "non_essential", "roles": ["fly"]},
        {"id": "right_wing", "size": "normal", "impact": "non_essential", "roles": ["fly"]},
        {"id": "forelegs", "size": "normal", "impact": "normal", "roles": ["move"]},
        {"id": "hind_legs", "size": "normal", "impact": "normal", "roles": ["move"]},

```

adjacent lists the parts a hit can move to at gap −2. Default when absent: the general area only. The general area is adjacent to every part. A body type is the shape only (BR1, Ali 08/11/2026): parts, general area, silhouette and the natural attacks the shape always has. Shape names are generic so later races can reuse them (BR4): the Slime race is amorphous and the Dragon race is a winged quadruped. Toughness (hpMultiplier), natural armor and a race's own natural attacks are on the race (BR2, BR3, BR6).

**Animal body types (NR14 a).** Quadruped (BT4: head that bites and carries in the jaws, neck, forelegs, hind legs, tail), Serpent (BT5: head, coils, tail; no grip) and Eight-legged (BT6: head and eight legs; no grip). Speed is the race's, not the body type's (RaceDef.speed, AB1 c), so one Quadruped serves a dog and a tiger. Animal races, each with real speeds, life stages and its own natural attacks (bite, claw, kick, gore, tail lash, constrict): Dog RC5, Wolf RC6, Tiger RC7, Bear RC8, Wild boar RC9, Horse RC10, Monitor lizard RC11 (Komodo-sized), Python RC12, Giant spider RC13 (AB8 a: a wolf-sized monster on the Eight-legged shape), Lion RC14, Leopard RC15, Hyena RC16, Crocodile RC17 (AB14 a). HP multipliers are (mass ÷ 70 kg)^(2/3) (AB6 a). Wild boar, monitor lizard and crocodile carry natural armor (AB13 a). Snakes and spiders can learn Tremor (AB11; its tree is now Tremor senses); slimes smell instead (AB19 a: Scent replaces Tremor on the slime template and the Gel Patience essence). **Natural attack skills** (AB16 a): Natural weapons split into Bite, Claws, Hooves and Horns (kick, tusks, horns), Tail (tail lash) and Slam (the slime's body blow; it keeps the old Natural weapons ID SK10, so slimes keep their points). Each skill, and the tree, opens only to the bodies and races that have an attack rolling it, worked out from the attacks; a Python's constrict rolls Grapple. A beast's role lists all five and an NPC trains only those its body can use (role and theme bands are filtered by access). The dragon got a bite and a tail lash, and weighs about 1,000 kg, HP ×5.9 (AB17 a). **Scent** (AB18 a): a skill with its own sense function (smell, Notice group), open to the animals and slimes, anyone else only through an essence. Anyone within Scent-skill metres along the open ways (the straight line where they see each other, else the walking path through the doorways: walls stop a smell) is perceived partly by smell, seen or not, in any light (perception smells): something is there, not who, and a smell is never made out. Stealth contests against a smell roll Scent, and a search rolls Scent when it beats sight and hearing. Scaling: 50 m at Scent 50, 100 m at 100, growing in a straight line past it. **Hide** (AB20, "one equation, different multiplied depending on the race"): natural armor stops ARMOR\_FACTOR × the owner's Hide skill × the race's hide multiplier (ruleset naturalReduction, the worn-armor equation with Hide in place of the need), then the light armor multiplier. Race multipliers (estimates): wild boar 1 (a gristle shield), monitor lizard 0.8 (small bony scales), crocodile 1.5 (large bony plates); Hide 25 at 1 is Common light armor. Hide sits in Combat – Defense, opens only to races with natural armor, and has no function, so nothing stands in for it. Animals are not playable (AB4) and may wield what their jaws can hold (AB5 b). A race trains its rank's years × its Adult start ÷ 18 (AB7 a, CONFIG.TRAINING_ADULT_AGE). Venom waits on D-322 and flight on D-323.

**Body types not in the database**

1. Agent Weaver names a type that is not in the catalog.
2. Stage-plan validation fails; the one repair retry sends the list of existing types, and the Weaver picks the closest fit. No extra call.
3. Code runs the encounter on that type and flags the substitution to you (the name asked for and the type used), so you can enter its own data. Data entered mid-story goes to the story's blueprints (Part 4 §2); only you promote it to the library.

### Weapons, armor and shields

Weapons, armor and shields are items (Part 5): attack modes and reach in §5, armor, shields and the damage step in §6, item data in §7. This section keeps the weapon class table and the shield as a part.

| Class | Damage | Hit | Against block |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Light | 20 | +10 | — |
| Medium | 25 | 0 | — |
| Heavy | 30 | −10 | gap −1 |

Natural attacks have their own classes: punch (damage 15, hit +10), slam (damage 20, hit +10) and grapple (damage 5, hit 0; any humanoid can grapple, and the Hold perk, A8, locks the target in place). All values are *placeholders*. Reach is set per attack mode (Part 5 §5).

**Shield as a part:** Non-essential, pool from the item. When the defender blocks with a shield, a hit at gap 0 or better goes to the shield (**\[Proposed\]** for gaps +1 to +5). Its states: Damaged −10 to block (wounds type), Crippled: can't block with it, Destroyed: the item is lost. The arm holding it must be a working Grip part.

### Defense

- A defense is a free reaction with the actor's standing defense (dodge or block).
- Dodge takes Move-role penalties and armor weight.
- Block needs a working Grip arm holding a shield or weapon.
- Against missiles: dodge gets +10 situational; block works as against melee.
- Outnumbered: each defense after the first in a turn takes a situational penalty (*placeholder*).
- Unaware: the defense roll takes a situational penalty (*placeholder*).
- Heavy weapons against block: gap shift −1.

The gap ladder is binding (gap = defense tier − attack tier). A gap past +5 uses the +5 entry. Below −5 the rows run on to −10 in CONFIG.GAP\_LADDER (placeholders, +0.5 a step), and past −10 each further step adds GAP\_PAST\_STEP (0.5) with no top (CS10):

| Gap | Dodge | Block |
| --- | --- | --- |
| −5 | 2× | 2× |
| −4 | 1.5× | 1.5× |
| −3 | 1×; hit moves to the named part anywhere | 1×; hit moves to the named part anywhere |
| −2 | 1×; hit moves to the named part if adjacent | 1×; hit moves to the named part if adjacent |
| −1 | 1×, no location limit | 1×, no essential parts |
| 0 | 1×, no essential parts | 1×, Non-essential parts only, else general area |
| +1 | 0.8× (graze; never an essential part, like the tie: settles OQ-17) | 0.75× |
| +2 | 0× | 0.5× |
| +3 | 0× | 0.25× |
| +4 | 0× | 0.2× |
| +5 | 0× | 0.15× |

At −4 and −5 the named-part move also applies, anywhere **\[Proposed\]**. A part forbidden by the defense sends the hit to the general area. Off the shield, a block or parry from +1 up keeps the 0 row's limit: Non-essential parts only, else the general area (CS2).

The outcome and location thresholds live in CONFIG.HIT\_GAPS beside GAP\_LADDER (CS3): a dodge that wins by +1 is grazed; a block or parry from +1 is blocked (a shield from 0, and it takes the hit); a hit at −4 is a heavy hit and at −5 or worse a crushing hit; the named-part move works at −2 if adjacent and at −3 or worse anywhere; essential parts are off limits from 0 up for a dodge and from −1 up for a block or parry.

### Reach, range, cover and light

An attack reaches targets within its attack mode's reach (Part 5 §5), with sight on every link of the shortest path.

- **Situational roll modifiers (ranged):** a hostile actor within 2 m of the shooter, close enough to strike or grab, −10 (RD5: HOSTILE\_NEAR\_M, HOSTILE\_NEAR\_MOD; Point Blank removes it). Range (`rangeMod`, CONFIG.RANGE\_SHARE\_MODS): the distance as a share of the mode's reach, both from real metres: up to 25% 0, 50% −5, 75% −10, 100% −15. A longbow (90 m, 11 links) at 48 m is −5 where a shortbow (50 m, 6 links) is −15 \[Proposed\].
- **Light** (any roll that needs sight): dim −5; dark −10. The attacker uses the target's zone; a defender uses the attacker's zone.
- **Cover** (ranged attacks only): the target's zone cover 1 → gap +1, cover 2 → gap +2.

### Damage flow and the roll packet

Code runs the whole exchange; Claude gets one packet afterwards and narrates from it.

1. **Setup** (once per body type): Agent Weaver names the body type in the stage plan; code loads it from the database and builds its hit table.
2. Attack roll (Part 1 §4, Layer 2b) with the weapon's skill. Aimed: add the aim penalty for the part's size. Tier shifts. Failure or worse: miss, end here.
3. Defense roll with the standing defense, then its tier shifts.
4. Gap = defense tier − attack tier + gap shifts; read the gap ladder (past +5: the +5 entry; past −10: +GAP\_PAST\_STEP a step, CS10). A 0× result ends here as dodged.
5. Location: aimed → the aimed part; else the location roll on the hit table. At gap −2 move to the named part if adjacent; at −3 or lower, to the named part anywhere. A part the defense forbids → the general area. Blocking with a shield → the shield.
6. Damage: by the damage step in Part 5 §6 (class damage and bonuses, the tier-gap multiplier, worn then natural armor, floor 0 with no rounding, armor pools).
7. **Body part:** apply the damage to that part's pool (the general area skips this) and update its state; a Destroyed part is removed and the hit table rebuilt. Fire `part_state_changed` if the state changed.
8. **Health track:** add the damage × impact (non-essential 0, normal 1, essential 1.5); a Destroyed essential part sets HP to 0. Fire threshold\_crossed if the level changed.
9. **Listeners:** perks, enemy AI and story scripts react; each reaction becomes an event.
10. **Packet → Agent Narrator.**

A source is deciding if removing it alone would change the attack tier, the defense tier, or the gap's row on the ladder (miss, multiplier or location). Natural edges are listed as "lucky" or "fumble". Damage values never reach Claude.

Change lines carry the effect text, so the narration never contradicts what code enforces. Code only sends what Claude is allowed to see: Healthy → Damaged is never sent, though its penalties still apply in code, while Damaged → Crippled is sent because Crippled is visible.

Visibility rules apply only to other characters: the player's own character is always fully visible to Claude, Damaged parts and lower health levels included.

```
{
  "event": "evt_0412",
  "actor": "player",
  "target": "the scarred bandit",
  "action": "aimed melee attack",
  "weapon": "arming sword",
  "armor":  null,
  "shield": null,
  "attack":  { "tier": "Great Success",  "natural": null, "deciding": [] },
  "defense": { "type": "dodge", "tier": "Success", "natural": null, "deciding": [] },
  "outcome": "full hit",
  "location": { "part": "left_leg", "how": "aimed" },
  "changes": [],
  "events": []
}
```

Outcome labels: missed, dodged, grazed, blocked, full hit, heavy hit (gap −4), crushing hit (gap −5). Location how: rolled, aimed, redirected, restricted, shield. The packet carries no numbers: no d100, margin, skill, needed number, damage, multiplier, pool or HP. Here changes is empty because the bandit's left leg only went Healthy → Damaged and he is only Hurt; both are hidden for anyone but the player. The packet also has armor and shield beside weapon. All three hold public item names only: the item used (a natural attack gives its mode's name), the worn armor covering the struck part, and the shield when the hit went to it; null when there is none (Part 5 §8).

## Part 4 — Save data model

How the game saves and loads. Part 4 replaces the single GameState document as the saved shape. Items marked \[Proposed\] follow the convention above: built as written, easy to change. Values marked placeholder are config, not constants. This part changes no ruleset number. A scenario start saves inside its triggering turn's normal save: one commit, no new save path. Backups (section 11) are unchanged.

### 1. Principles

- **Code is the game's memory.** Every Claude call starts empty. Each turn, code builds every agent's view from the save.
- **Unsaved means forgotten.** Anything Claude says that code doesn't save is lost. What must stay true is saved as data; Claude may re-describe it freely.
- **Saving costs no tokens.** Only what a view sends costs tokens. A crippled wing on an absent dragon costs nothing; on a present one, one line.
- **No model writes the save.** Agents return requests and proposals. The validator and engine decide; only the engine writes (section 8).
- **Numbers become words at the view.** Views send states ("Crippled"), never pools, HP or Burden.

### 2. What is saved

The save has two layers. The library holds blueprints shared by every story. A story holds everything that happens in one playthrough. **The Universe** (Ali, 13/11/2026, HF6, HF17, HF22, HF25): a hidden layer over every world, kept apart from each story's save (db `universe/…`, src/save/universe.ts). It holds the shared calendar: the furthest day any world has reached (`universe/calendar`), raised after each saved turn. A new world starts on the day after it (`clocks.startDay`), so a story's day 1 is no longer always day 1, and the new character's birth day moves with it, keeping the age picked at creation. Universe › worlds › stories: a world is its places, people and everything that happened in it; a story is one character's life in a world. **Carrying a world on** (Ali, 13/11/2026, HF26, HF31 b, HF32 a, WP1–WP5; src/save/world.ts): a story's meta names its world (StoryMeta.worldId; absent = it began a world of its own, whose id is its storyId). A reset writes how the story ended (StoryMeta.end: the character's name, the day, the Threadbound items left in the world). A world is free when every story in it is archived (WP1 b: a character reset while standing left it). The creation screen lists every free world (World: New world, or "<title> · day <last day> · <name> fell, <name> left"), newest first, only when there is one. A world's title is its first character's ("Aldric’s world") until one is given in the game: Rename beside the picked world (db `worlds/<worldId>`; an empty title goes back to the default) (WP5). Picking one copies the world's last save into a new story (carryOn): places, people, their memories, deals and things lying about stay. A character who fell stays where they fell, Dead, as an NPC under their own id (hero_<story>), so everything people knew of them (beliefs, stances, deals, their band and its relations) stays theirs (WP3), except what their foes took: before the newcomer arrives, everyone standing, hostile to them and in the same place (those in their zone first) loots the body; the first takes all the coin, then each in turn takes the most valuable thing left, at most LOOT_TAKE_MAX (6, placeholder) each; worn containers stay, and with no foe standing everything stays (WP6 a; lootTheFallen). A looted Threadbound item keeps its dead owner's bond, so whoever takes it from the looter can wear it; a character reset while standing is gone with everything they carried, and what pointed at them is dropped as for anyone gone (the load's reference repair). The newcomer starts a stranger; NPC plans, the stage, quests and fights start fresh, and the world's turn count runs on (the opening's turn record is marked opening). The world's date jumps to the Universe date, with nothing else changed (D-251 parks a Weaver summary of the gap); the new character's age is still the one picked. The Weaver's opening gets target.arrive and must plan at least one new zone at the start place, where the newcomer arrives (WP4 a). HF28/WP2 b: a Threadbound start gets a Threadbound item not already in that world; when none is free the start is off for that world, with a line saying why. WP2: a Threadbound item's bond dies with its owner, so the player can wear a dead owner's and it binds to them; the Weaver is told the player's Threadbound items (WEAVER_VIEW.player.threadbound) and on Critical Failure or worse may bring a strong hostile foe who has come for one. The old story is never changed. Uniques live in the code library (HF34 a); the Universe will record only where each unique is (which worlds, alive or dead). One story runs at a time **per owner** (Ali, 10/11/2026, replacing "one story at a time"): the player's own story, and at most one story the autopilot agent plays on its own save (StoryMeta.owner 'agent'; absent = the player's). Opening the game always resumes the player's story; the agent's story opens only from the autopilot bar, and "On my story" returns. While the agent's own story is on screen, yellow marks it: the pressed "Its own story" choice and the header's "Watch an agent play" switch (no banner row). Each owner has its own pending start (pending/start, pending/agent), and a reset archives only the story on screen. **Watch an agent play (autopilot)** (Ali, 10/11/2026): a floating pop-up over the Play screen (Agent pop-up look). The agent (AgentName 'autopilot', src/agents/autopilot.ts) gets the last 14 story lines, its own last AUTOPILOT_SAID_KEPT (6) commentary lines with the rule never to reuse their exclamations, Japanese words, openings or phrases (Ali, 11/11/2026: they went stale) and a playstyle (the playtest personas: Curious, Eager, Bloodthirsty, Patient, Shopper) and answers JSON {command, say, direction}. The command is typed into the action box where the player can see it and sent like Send; say is the agent's commentary, 2 to 4 short sentences of at most 70 words, spoken as someone living the moment, never reporting it: it reacts first, and the outcome of its last move and what the people around did come out in that reaction, then what it does next and why (Ali, 11/11/2026: the watcher only hears it, and it must not sound like a statement of facts), shown under the bar and acted out by the RPG Voice or read aloud in one of Claude's own voices (Voice on/off). direction is a few English words telling a voice actor how to say the line, and say may carry up to two of the RPG Voice's sound tags where the sound really happens (VOICE_TAGS: <gasp>, <laugh>, <chuckle>, <giggle>, <sigh>, <breath>, <short pause>, <long pause>); readMove keeps those tags and drops any other markup, and the screen and Claude's voices get the line without them (plainLine). Controls: Player's / Agent's (which story), Mode, Playstyle, Pace (2/5/10 s between turns), Style, Speaker (Off mutes the commentary, which is still shown), Speed, One turn, Play/Pause, Close; there is no Stop voice button (Ali). The bar keeps updating while one of its lists is open or its boxes is being typed in: only that part waits, so the status, the agent's words and a guided question always show (Ali, 12/11/2026: the whole bar froze after a pick until a click elsewhere, so the agent looked idle). A Claude call ends when its time runs out even if the page never answers it (sampleClient races the abort signal). The status line counts the seconds of a wait of 5 s or more (Thinking about the next move… 12 s), so a slow call or a stuck turn shows. The agent's /inv, /stats or /quests is answered in the story and never takes the watcher to another page. **Style** (GA18, SP1–SP2): Plain (default) or In character (Ali, 14/11/2026, SP1 a, SP2 b): the agent is the player character. Its commentary and any words its command has the character say are worded from the character's personality line, signatures included, given every move, and that line also decides its moves: a playstyle is only a leaning, and where it clashes with who the character is, the character wins. AI companion and Speaks Japanese were removed (Ali SP1: they failed for now; parked D-315). VOICE_STYLES in src/agents/autopilot.ts. **Speaker** (GA20 a): the RPG Voice's 30 Gemini voices (default Leda, "Youthful"; the list is refreshed from its list_voices tool once it has spoken) and Claude's own voices, Buttery, Airy, Glassy and Rounded, through the page's tts capability; the PC's speech voices were removed (Ali, 11/11/2026: "they all suck"). **RPG Voice** (src/ui/rpgVoice.ts; PARKED by Ali 12/11/2026, "keep the Claude voices only for the time being": CONFIG.RPG_VOICE_ON false hides it and the page never calls the connector; Claude's voices are those tts.voices() reports, four today, since the Claude app's fifth voice, Mellow, isn't offered to pages): Ali's claude.ai connector "RPG Voice" (a relay to Gemini's free speech model, set up in the thread "Find best-in-class voice acting"), reached through the page's mcp capability (tools speak and list_voices). speak({text, direction, voice}) answers a base64 WAV, played in the page at the chosen Speed. Gemini makes a whole clip before any of it plays, so a long line was heard seconds late (Ali, 12/11/2026): each line is now asked for in two pieces side by side, its first sentence and the rest (splitLine), and the first starts as soon as it is made; the agent's command waits for the voice to start, at most AUTOPILOT_VOICE_WAIT_MS (6 s), so what you hear lines up with the move. Its voices show only where the page can reach the connector; when a call fails, Claude's voice reads that line and the bar says why and what fixes it (reconnect or add the connector, allow it on the page, its free allowance used up); a lasting failure stops further tries until another voice is picked or the page reloads (rpgFailure). A Stop voice button shows while one reads. Where Claude can't speak (not offered in this view, or over its allowance) the commentary is only shown, and the bar says why. **Samples** (Ali, 12/11/2026): picking a Speaker, Style or Speed reads a short line from that style's own bank (VOICE_SAMPLES in src/ui/voiceSamples.ts: Plain ten lines, In character eight lines from different people, never the same line twice in a row) in the chosen voice and speed, and is heard only, with no text in the bar (Ali, 12/11/2026: "remove the useless text"), so each voice and style can be told apart before play. **Speed**: 1×, 1.25× or 1.5× (default 1.5×, the fastest Claude's voices read). Play and One turn always start: while a turn is still running the bar says it is waiting, then goes on. A move the agent takes longer than AUTOPILOT_TIMEOUT_MS (60 s) to decide is given up and asked again (two failures pause the bar). Every move it is told how it is (health, wounded parts, morale, coin, perk points to spend; condition() in src/agents/autopilot.ts), and where it stands with the ways out it can see and the other places it knows (surroundings(); BG-30: it walked toward a cellar door that wasn't there, because it only had the story lines), so it never needs /stats or invents a way out: a /stats move is always asked again once with "act", and /inv or /quests within AUTOPILOT_INFO_EVERY (8) moves of another look likewise (BG-25, BG-26, Ali: "/stats is stupid"). It never asks for the same thing more than twice: when someone keeps putting it off or the same try fails twice, it tries another way, such as going to look for itself, asking someone else or moving on (BG-183: it asked the barkeep for a cellar key five turns running). **Guided mode, "Asks me at crossroads"** (Ali, 11/11/2026, GA1–GA17, every recommendation taken): a Mode picker in the bar (Plays alone / Asks me at crossroads), on either story and with every playstyle, remembered on this PC. The agent plays most turns and hands the big calls to the watcher. All kinds live in one table, CROSSROADS in src/agents/crossroads.ts (GA2). Code checks each finished turn (turnSnap before, spotCrossroads after) for: a fight starting that the agent didn't start, asked once per fight (GA3); health first reaching Wounded, Mauled or Critical, or a body part becoming Crippled or Destroyed (GA4); a foe yielding (GA5); and, on the watcher's story only, a perk point waiting, shown as a card that suggests the agent's pick with "Take <perk>" and "Not now", or another perk picked on the sheet (GA6). The agent itself stops before: attacking anyone not already attacking it, finishing off someone down, yielded or fleeing (GA7); agreeing to, turning down or giving up a deal or quest (GA8); selling or giving away gear, or spending more than GUIDED_COIN_SHARE (a quarter) of its coin in one go (GA9); leaving for a place it has never been, judged from the places in the story lines (GA10); and choices that shape who the character is (GA11). It never asks about scenario breaks, arrivals and departures, achievements, skill gains, level-ups, ordinary talk, small buys or a fight it is already in, and the same kind about the same thing asks at most once per scenario; a repeat is answered with its own pick (GA12). The question card sits where the spoken line is: the situation in the agent's voice (read aloud), 2 to 4 choices with its pick marked, a box for the watcher's own words and You decide; the tab title shows "Your call" (GA13). A choice is typed as the next move; own words become the next move and guidance until the scenario ends, shown in the bar (GA14). It waits with no time limit, the action box is the watcher's meanwhile, and a turn the watcher plays answers it; Pause or × drops the question (GA15). Standing orders (GA16) are gone: the player character's signatures replace them (Ali, 14/11/2026), and the guided agent is told them every turn, to keep to and never ask about. The bar shows only the last call and its answer; nothing is added to the story (GA17). It pauses when the character falls, when play stops, or after two failed calls. On its own save the agent spends its perk points itself (the open milestone of its highest skill, as the playtest does); on the player's story it never spends them. While it plays, the action box is the agent's. A reload or a new version of the page in the same tab carries on where the bar was: still open, still playing, on the same story (kept per tab, so opening the game afresh still opens the player's story). **Hall of fame** (Ali, 10/11/2026): resetting a story whose character is down writes a fame entry on its meta (StoryMeta.fame: name, title, classes and level, best true skills, perks, achievements, scars, defeats, day, turn, place and the blow that dropped them); the Hall of fame page lists every fallen character of either owner, newest first, and opens even with no story running. **Name and gender** (Ali, 10/11/2026): the creation screen asks for the character's name and gender (Man, Woman, Other = he, she, they); the build's pronouns become the player's Actor.pronouns, which others use for them.

| Data | Layer | Saved | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Ruleset numbers | Library | Yes | Retuning applies to the running story |
| Body types, races, classes, perks, skills, item and NPC templates | Library | Yes | Edited only by the designer; stories always use the latest version |
| Story blueprints | Story | Yes | Items Claude minted, body types filled in mid-story. Never leave the story unless the designer promotes them |
| Player | Story | Yes | Core, Progression, Journal (section 4) |
| Temporary NPCs | Story | Yes | Core, Mind (section 4) |
| Recurring NPCs | Story | Deferred | Every NPC gets a stable id so it can be promoted later |
| Places, state slots, facts | Story | Yes | Skeleton and canon, never descriptive prose (section 5) |
| Hidden store, quests, flags | Story | Yes | Plans, triggers, secrets, memory lists, agreements, fired log |
| Bands, clocks, settings | Story | Yes | Settings: SHOW\_MECHANICS, tone (no difficulty setting: difficulty comes from who is there and the event roll, CS10) |
| Engine state | Story | Yes | Turn, mode, RNG seed and state, pending, stage, scenario caps |
| Turn records | Story | Last N | N is a placeholder (section 6) |
| Story text | Story | Yes, all of it | The play screen's story column, in documents of HISTORY\_CHUNK\_TURNS turns (section 10). Ali, 07/11/2026, easy open questions Q18 |

- **Story isolation.** Every story-scoped read and write takes the story id. Each story lives under its own storage namespace. No function reads across stories. Views are built only from the loaded story.
- **Lookup order.** An id resolves in the story's blueprints first, then in the library.
- **Reference, don't copy.** A story stores library ids, never copies of entries, so library edits reach it at once. The one exception is a rolled NPC's values (section 4).

### 3. Raw and derived fields

The save stores only what can't be rebuilt. Everything recomputable is derived on every use and never written.

| Kind | Fields | Rule |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Derived | True skill, effective skill, Total, Burden, cap in effect, health-track level, part states, hit table, active item effects, gear bonuses, protection per part, grips and slots in use, race perk, class perks, character level, excluded skills | Never saved. Effective skill differs per check, so it can't be cached |
| History | Raw skills and their states, StartingTotal, all-XXX perks, level perks chosen, marks, classes and class levels, race, body type, part pools, minted item blueprints, item instances, shield and armor pool states | Saved as plain fields |
| Grey zone | XP, unspent perk points, Potential | Saved, with a source trail where the value can change |

- Potential is saved as a base plus a list of {source, delta, turn} adjustments, so a perk or event change can be traced or reversed.
- Character level is the sum of class levels unless a perk changes it, so it is derived.
- Values used at resolution (the Burden snapshot, the effective skill of a check, the event roll) go in the turn record, not on the sheet.
- Retuning never recalculates saved progress. Levels are stored and never lost. A retuned XP curve changes only the distance to the next level; if a character's XP already passes the new threshold, it levels up at its next XP award, not on load. A retuned perk-point rule applies only to future levels: points already earned and perks already bought stay.

### 4. Actor sheet

The player and every NPC share one sheet shape: a Core every actor has, plus optional modules. Roll code reads Core only, which guarantees one resolution path for everyone.

| Module | Who has it | Holds |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Core | Every actor | Race, body type, skills and states, classes, stored perks, condition, inventory, standing defense, public label |
| Progression | Player; NPCs only if NPCs improve (deferred) | StartingTotal, Potential, XP, unspent perk points, marks |
| Mind | NPCs | Control, morale, beliefs, overrides, knows facts, current routine and its anchor, routines list, slot bindings, alarm state. The agenda flag is derived, not stored; the band moved to the Actor |
| Journal | Player | Secrets learned, facts witnessed, last-seen state slots, names known |

Promoting an NPC to recurring adds a module; it never converts the sheet. A companion needs no module: it is any member of the player's band.

**Race and body type** (BR1–BR9, Ali 08/11/2026). The body type is the shape; the race is what the being is. Every race names exactly one body type (BR5), its HP multiplier (BR2), optional natural armor (BR6), its own natural attacks and fallback attack skill on top of the shape's (BR3), and innate perks and quirks. An NPC template and the player's build name a race, and the body type comes from it. Races today: Human and Elf (humanoid, ×1), Slime (amorphous, ×0.6), Dragon (winged quadruped, ×3). Defeat achievements count both the body type and the race (BR8). Tree access rules may name either (BR9); a tree the shape already blocks is not blocked again on the race.

**Skills.** General skills are open to every actor unless a perk or race excludes them. Each class adds its own tree of three skills. Exclusions are derived from the library and never stored.

**Skill tree access** (Ali, 08/11/2026, "Skill tree access" TA1–TA11). Every tree has its own access rule in the library (TreeDef.access), and each tree is coded on its own. A rule has up to three lists of conditions: **Must** (every one holds), **Either of** (at least one holds, when listed) and **Blocked by** (any one closes the tree). A block always beats an opening (TA2). A tree with no rule is open to everyone. A condition names one source: a race, a body type, a class with a minimum class level (default 1; the class counts while Current or Previous, never Unlocked, TA3), or a perk (quirks included); a new kind of source is one more case in one check. A skill in a tree the actor can't use counts as 0 (rolls, HP, the rating) and can't be raised; its points are kept and come back with access (TA4 a). Rules are per tree (TA8), with one exception: a skill can carry its own rule on top of its tree's (SkillDef.access, SR1 below). Rules hold for the player and every NPC: a test fails if a template gives a skill its actor can't use (TA9). Trees a character can't use are hidden on the creation screen and the character sheet unless the character has points in them; those show "Blocked by: …" or "Needs: …" (TA10). Today's rules: Swordsman must have class Swordsman; Tremor senses needs race Python or Giant spider (AB11, AB19); Scent needs an animal race or Slime (AB18); Natural weapons and each of its skills need a body or race with an attack rolling that skill (AB16, worked out from the attacks); Hide needs a race with natural armor (AB20); body type amorphous blocks Melee, Ranged, Unarmed, Perception, Speech and Tools (no hands, voice, eyes or ears, TA6). A block the shape gives is not repeated on the race (BR9). **Speaking races** (Ali, 08/11/2026, SR1): each race is tagged if it can speak (RaceDef.tags 'speaks': Human and Elf yes, Slime and Dragon no), and a condition can name a race tag. Intimidation is open to anyone with the Speech tree; persuasion and barter must have a speaking race. Banked: TA7 (Tools, lockpicking) and TA11 (nested groups of conditions), D-113.

**Classes.** Each class an actor knows is saved with its state and its class level:

| State | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Unlocked | Offered at level-up. Same as not having it: tree unusable, class level 0 |
| Current | Exactly one. Receives every new level for persistent characters |
| Previous | A former current class. Keeps its levels; its skills stay usable |

Putting a level into an unlocked class makes it current, and the old current class becomes previous. Class perks come at class levels set in the library (normally 10 and 20) and are derived. Previous-class skills keep improving like any other skill. Returning to a previous class is deferred.

**First-build classes.** Swordsman, the player's class: its tree holds only riposte (Ali, 10/11/2026; it augments Parry, D-170). Feint and disarm are removed for now and parked as skill ideas for future classes (D-157; ids SK22 and SK24 retired). **Sword-dancer tree** (Ali, 10/11/2026): mirrors the Swordsman with one skill, Blade Dance (formerly Slip Counter), Riposte's twin for dodging instead of parrying (it augments Dodge, D-170); its 60 perk Step and Cut mirrors Answer the Blow (strike back at once off a clean dodge with a light or medium weapon), and it sets class floors of melee\_medium 25 and block 15. Slime, every slime's class: its tree, Slime senses, holds tremor (tier 0; every slime has 50, Ali 08/11/2026). Taste was removed for now (Ali, 08/11/2026); the other skills are open.

**Player creation.** The player builds the sheet by point-buy on the creation screen (https://claude.ai/artifact/HW45ivigqwZ5VDo4PWPv9u): 100 points over the open skills (CREATION\_POINTS, every Start; Ali 14/11/2026), an untagged skill at most 30 (CREATION\_MAX\_UNTAGGED) and a tagged one at most 40 (CREATION\_MAX\_TAGGED) at creation (creationCap; untagging a skill above 30 lowers it to 30), the class floors as minimums, and exactly 3 Tagged skills. The screen opens on a recommended build and shows HP, attack, standing defense and the odds against one slime as points move. The story starts only when every point is spent and 3 skills are tagged. StartingTotal is the sum of the untagged skills at that moment. The player starts with the starter kit their skills meet (below) and 20 coin (placeholder), at their Start's level (1 for every Start today; START\_LEVEL 4 is only the default for a Start that names none). **Starts** (Ali, 09/11/2026; renamed from starting classes, DX1, 14/11/2026). A Start is one way to begin on the creation screen, a library table of its own (`starts`, IDs ST… in src/library/startIds.ts, StartDef), apart from classes, so a start need not have a specific class and character creation can grow. A start names its class (StartDef.classId), or takes it from what it gives (the blessed start: its god's class); its creation points and level (StartDef.points and level, defaults CREATION\_POINTS and START\_LEVEL; the level is the class's), the age picker's default (StartDef.age) and its story line. The screen lists the starts in table order, each with its class tier, points and level; a blessing's tier never shows on the creation screen (the blessed start reads "a random god's blessing" and its Blessed class's tier, Seasoned). **A Start with no class** (CC6 b, Ali 14/11/2026): a start with neither a class nor a blessing begins with no class, at level 0 whatever its row says (startLevel), with the default class (Commoner, ClassDef.fallback) open to pick (unlocked, level 0); XP banks, so levels wait until the player picks a class, and picking one takes every banked level at once (src/library/spawn.ts classlessStart, defaultClass). No start is classless, and none is planned (CC9: no). The Swordsman start (ST1) was removed (Ali, 14/11/2026; ST1 is retired); the Swordsman class stays and unlocks in play. Unwritten Legend (ST2, the late bloomer; the first Start, and the default): the unique class Unwritten Legend, level 1, no floors, and its level 1 perk Quick Study (a marked skill whose raw value is below 15 gets two improvement rolls, each with its own gain). The A Nobody start (ST3) was removed (CC3, Ali 14/11/2026; ST3 is retired); the A Nobody class stays, opened by Commoner's capstone. The Threadbound (ST4; TB7, Ali 12/11/2026): the class Threadbounded (tier 4, Heroic: TB11), level 1, no floors, and its level 1 perk What Was Always Yours, which gives a random Threadbound item of Rank IV or higher (Part 5 §9). **The Blessed** (ST5; DX1, Ali 14/11/2026): level 1 (CC3 a), named The Blessed (CC4 a); it begins with a random god's Tier V blessing: a god is picked at random among those who give a Tier V blessing, then one of that god's Tier V blessings, seeded by the story id (src/ruleset/blessings.ts rollBlessing), given with source 'start'. The god is rolled, and shown, when the story starts (CC5 a). It begins in that god's Blessed class (ClassDef.blessedBy), level 1. While no god gives a Tier V blessing the start can't begin (validateBuild, and the screen greys it out). **Blessed classes** (DX1): a variant per god, "Blessed by X", tier 2 (Seasoned); one opens (unlocked, level 0) for any character that god blesses. Today only Blessed by Imbra (CL10); its class perk and capstone, about earning a higher blessing from the god, are to come. A character's stranger label is its start class's ClassDef.label. **Class-level perks:** ClassDef.levelPerks lists perk ids by class level; a character gets them (source 'class') when the class reaches that level, at creation or on a level up. **Class IDs** (Ali, 09/11/2026): every class has a fixed ID (`CL` in src/library/classIds.ts: Swordsman CL1, Unwritten Legend CL2, Sword-dancer CL3, Commoner CL4, A Nobody CL5, Trader CL6, Threadbounded CL9, Blessed by Imbra CL10); code and saves use the ID, so a class can be renamed in the library alone. **Class tiers** (Ali, 09/11/2026): every class has a tier on the ordered scale CLASS\_TIERS (lowest to highest: Common, Trained, Seasoned, Renowned, Heroic, Legendary; ClassDef.tier stores the key tier\_0 to tier\_5), shown on the creation screen and the class tree, to compare classes and to place NPC templates later. Commoner Common, Trader Common, Blessed by Imbra Seasoned, Swordsman Trained, Sword-dancer Trained (mirrors the Swordsman), Merchant Trained, Guard Trained, A Nobody Seasoned, Threadbounded Heroic, Unwritten Legend Legendary. **Class unlocks:** ClassDef.unlock lists true skill per skill; a player without the class unlocks it (state unlocked, level 0) when every one is met, checked after improvement rolls. Swordsman unlocks at Parry 40 and Medium or Heavy Weapons 40. ClassDef.unlockAny is a list of either-of groups: in each group at least one skill must also be met. **Unlocks are floors** (Ali, 10/11/2026): a class's skill floors are its own floors plus its unlock conditions (`classFloors`), so every character of a class meets what unlocks it. An either-of group is met when one skill in it reaches its value. A template with a class has its minimums raised to them (`templateMinimums`; an unmet group raises the skill the template has most of), and the village guard template carries the Guard class. ClassDef.unlockPerkWith needs an active perk whose numbers reach the listed ones (PerkDef.hearingReach: zone links added to hearing; Keen Ear 1, and hearing reach sums it over perks). **Merchant** (Ali, 10/11/2026): a tier 1 class (top level 10) unlocked by the Trader capstone Known at Every Stall (PK99), which Trader (tier 0, top level 5) grants at level 5; Merchant has unlockPerk set to it, as A Nobody has with Nobody Special. ClassDef.unlockClass (class levels other classes must reach, a number or 'max' for that class's top level) is kept unused for D-159. Its tree (Merchant, TR15) holds one skill, Shrewd Dealing (SK33), Merchant's advanced barter. **Guard** (Ali, 10/11/2026): a tier 1 class (CL8) that unlocks with any weapon skill at 40 (Light, Medium or Heavy Weapons, or Light, Medium or Heavy Ranged), any armor skill at 40, Block or Dodge at 30, and a perk that sharpens hearing (`hearingDb`: Keen Ear, which patrolling templates have). No tree or floors yet. **Skills augmenting skills** (Ali, 10/11/2026; a frequent theme to come): a skill can list `augments` [{ skill, share, rollKinds }]: its true skill × share (default 1) × its tier is added to the effective skill of every roll or check that asks for the augmented skill, or only rolls of the listed roll kinds (RollKind) when some are listed. The augmenting skill's tree must be open. Shrewd Dealing augments Barter at share 1 on every roll. **Augment kinds** (D-276, Ali 14/11/2026, AK1 to AK4; src/ruleset/gear.ts augmentK, the one equation): an augment entry (SkillDef.augments or PerkDef.augments) may set `kind`. *plain* (the default) is the rule above. *trade*: the receiving skill gains the giving skill's true skill × share (default AUGMENT\_TRADE\_SHARE 0.5, placeholder) × its tier, and every roll of the giving skill loses the same amount (tradesOut), always while the perk or trait is owned; points are moved, never made, true skill is untouched. *percent*: the receiving skill gains the giving true skill ÷ 100 × share (the rate, default AUGMENT\_PERCENT\_RATE 0.2, placeholder) × its tier × the receiving skill's own true skill (both at 50 add 5, at 100 add 20, at 150 add 45); two percentage augments on one skill stack, the largest in full and the next at STACK\_RATIO, and plain or trade augments never halve them. Every kind learns as below, with its factor in place of share × tier. **First trade:** Fists Do the Talking (PK131), the trait of the Tavern Brawler essence (a placeholder until D-229): Strike trades into Intimidation. The essence pop-up and Essences section name a held or offered essence's trait and quirk beside its points. The percentage kind is for the 120 perks (D-258). **How augmenting skills learn** (Ali, 10/11/2026, AU1–AU5, option B): the roll gets one learning window from its sums, built like any skill's window: the low end is the rolled skill's raw plus each augment's raw × share × tier, minus MARK\_WINDOW; the high end is the rolled skill's max(raw, true) plus each augment's max(raw, true) × share × tier, plus MARK\_WINDOW. Every augment that added to the roll is marked when the opposition is inside that window; the opposition is a check's difficulty, or the other side's true skill plus what its own augments added (AU2). A tie (tier gap 0) marks every skill and augment that went into the roll (AU4). An augment not yet on the sheet counts as raw 0 and learns once its tree opens (AU3). An augment limited to roll kinds learns only from rolls of those kinds (AU5). The rolled skill keeps its own window and opposition (markAugments in src/ruleset/improve.ts). **Sword-dancer** (Ali, 09/11/2026): a class that unlocks at Dodge 40 and Light or Medium Weapons 40. It mirrors the Swordsman: its skills and perks are meant to be the Swordsman's, so the two are designed together. It has no tree, floors or Start yet. **Starter kit** (Ali, 09/11/2026; pairs rule 10/11/2026): items tagged 'starter'. **Rule for every gear class, including new ones: the starter kit holds one item needing 25 (Common) and one needing 50 (Fine) in that class, and nothing above 50.** Improvised weapons are exempt. Today: light melee dagger / short sword; medium melee hand axe / arming sword, spear; heavy melee woodsman's axe / greatsword; light ranged shortbow / recurve bow; medium ranged flatbow / longbow; heavy ranged light crossbow / stirrup crossbow; light armor gambeson / boiled leather (plus Crude rough clothes); medium armor brigandine / mail hauberk; heavy armor lamellar coat / half plate; shield round shield / kite shield (tests/cases/s143 checks it). After creation the player gets, per gear class, only the highest-level starter item whose equip requirement the build meets (`starterKit`): the weapon whose required skill is highest is held (then the higher level), a met shield goes in the other hand unless the weapon takes two hands, the highest-level armor met is worn, and the rest is carried. The recommended swordsman gets the arming sword, round shield and gambeson, and carries the spear. Crude rough clothes (need 0, stop nothing, no Dodge cost) are in the starter kit too, so the late bloomer starts in them with no weapon (Ali, 09/11/2026). Crude weapons (cudgel, rusty knife) are library items outside the starter kit. **Equipment balance lever** (Ali, 09/11/2026): a standard-kit piece's bonus is EQUIP\_FACTOR × its need (EQUIP\_FACTOR 0.4 for now; it replaces the flat KIT\_BONUS 25), so all equipment is rebalanced with one number: Crude 0, Common +10, Fine +20, Superior +30, Masterwork +40. Kit pieces still share one bonus per roll, the best of them. A minted item of quality 3 or 4 is a level up (EQ11), so its bonus rises one step too. **Quality lever** (QE1): a minted weapon's hit bonus is QUALITY\_HIT\_FACTOR (0.04) × its need × its quality (1–4), and quality 3–4 still moves it a level up (EQ11), so a fine sword gives +2/+4/+9/+12 hit. Damage boosts and armor and tool quality keep their tables for now (QE2 open). **Rule of thumb for all gear:** an item's stats scale with its need. Armor will get its own lever and shields a lever for their pool (QE3, under discussion). The recommended build (`recommendedBuild`, one source for the screen and tests), for every Start: Medium Weapons 40, Parry 25, Dodge 10, Endurance 15, Eyesight 5, Earshot 5; tags Medium Weapons, Parry, Endurance. It holds the Common hand axe and wears crude clothes; no Fine (50) starter piece can be met at creation now.

**Random identity** (Ali, 14/11/2026): the creation screen opens on a random name, gender, age and personality, all editable, and its Random button picks them again without touching the skill points (src/library/spawn.ts randomIdentity). Gender: Man, Woman or Other at even odds. Name: from a placeholder list per gender (src/library/names.ts; Other draws from both). Age: whole years from the race's youngest age (Youth start; no children, D-316) to just before its Old start (13 to 59 for a human), so no one starts in old-age decline \[Proposed\]; a Start's own age default (StartDef.age) is no longer the opening value. Personality: a random archetype's targets and values on a random Nerve line. Race: a random playable race (below), and the age is rolled in that race's range.

**Race choice** (CC7 a, Ali 14/11/2026): the creation screen's Race picker lists the playable races (RaceDef.playable): Human and Elf. Slime and Dragon were playable for a while (CC8 b) and were taken off again (Ali, 14/11/2026); they stay NPC races, and the body rules below stay ready for any race made playable later. The build stores it (CreationBuild.raceId, left out = Human); the race sets the body type, the innate perks and quirks and the age range (newPlayer, createPlayer), and validateBuild refuses a race that is not playable. **A start a race can't begin** (CC8): when the start's class floors need a skill the race's body can't learn (a Slime has no hands for Medium Weapons or Parry), the start is greyed out for that race and validateBuild refuses it (raceStartBlock); the screen opens on the first start the race can begin. The recommended build for such a race moves the closed skills' points to its open skills, then to other open skills, up to the creation cap, and tags the highest open skills (recommendedBuild(start, race)). **Starter gear by body** (CC8): the starter weapon and shield go in the body's own grip parts (a Human's right arm then left; a Dragon's jaws, one grip, so no shield; a Slime has none, so nothing is held), and only a humanoid body wears the starter armor \[Proposed\]; the rest is carried. Picking another race rolls an age in its range and builds the screen again, since trees and ages can differ, so the skill points go back to the recommended build (src/library/spawn.ts playableRaces).

**Gods and blessings** (DX1, Ali 14/11/2026, "Essences: bested foes, extraction, balance"; the blessing system beyond this is parked as D-311). Gods are a library table (`gods`, IDs GD…, src/library/godIds.ts), each with a narrow field and a standing (`GodDef.rank`, an ordered scale GOD\_RANKS, weakest first; today only `minor`, "Minor god"); beings above them govern wider concepts (to be built up). Every current god is a **minor god** (Ali, 14/11/2026): grand to the average person, but the minimum for the divine, and the Divine NPC rank's anchor (400) matches it. The standing is game-side only: mortals call every god a god, so in-world text (looks, stories, narration) never says "minor". Divine NPCs keep the player's skill cap like every other NPC (NG5 b); lifting it is parked as D-314. Varo (GD1), minor god of extreme fortunes, no blessings yet. Imbra (GD2), minor goddess of essence manipulation (named by Ali, 14/11/2026). A blessing is a perk with `PerkDef.blessing` = its god and its tier, an ordered scale BLESSING\_TIERS that counts up (CC1 b, Ali 14/11/2026): Tier I is the weakest, the blessed start's Tier V is mid-ladder, and higher gods' blessings go on to Tier VI, VII and up with no end (rows are generated to BLESSING\_TIER\_ROWS, 30, in src/scales.ts; raising it adds tiers). Tiers read as numerals only (CC2 a); names may come later. Lore around the gods belongs to the lore thread.

**NPC generation.** All generation runs in code. A template (a preset; NPC Generation NG1–NG21, Questions hub page npc-generation-rework) gives a body type and race, an optional class, a role, a theme, a rank, a minimum for each skill, a personality, a loadout and a control setting. Kinds of NPC:

- **Rolled** (a bandit): each NPC made from it rolls its own person once at spawn, then its values are saved and frozen. Called "unique" until HF15 (Ali, 13/11/2026).
- **Copy** (a wolf, a slime): saves only the template id and live state, and follows the latest template.
- **Unique** (HF15): one specific character, a whole saved sheet (NpcTemplate.unique, a UniqueSheet) spawned exactly as saved at full health, under its own name, pronouns, personality and age (the Weaver gives only its label, band and place). At most once per world (HF11): the Weaver's catalog lists only uniques not yet in the world (catalogs.uniques, with name, classes, level, power and the hidden past; player.power to set it against), and validation refuses a unique already in the world or cast twice (unique_in_world). Uniques live in the code library, in their own file (src/library/uniques.ts, HF34 a); items made in a hero's own story travel in its sheet (itemDefs) and join the library items. **Echoes** (HF18–HF24, HF29, D-243): a Hall of fame hero comes back as an echo (origin 'echo', src/library/echo.ts echoSheet): its sheet at death, achievements, scars, title and essences kept, its age at death. It never learns (no marks). Its gear is soulbound copies of what it had (weapons, shields, armor, accessories and tools, pools full); its coin and other goods stay behind (HF24 c).
