A soulbound item (ItemInstance.soulbound) is usable only by its owner (boundTo): it can't be picked up, looted, sold, given, dropped, stashed or knocked from a destroyed hand, it is not a Threadbound item's one of a kind (HF19: the hero's original is freed), and it fades when its owner dies. A Threadbound copy keeps only the abilities the hero had unlocked (its own achievements). A beaten echo always offers an essence (no appearance roll); its quality roll goes ECHO_ESSENCE_TIERS (2) tiers up, past Legendary too, and below Success still gives ECHO_ESSENCE_FLOOR (Common). The Weaver (catalogs.uniques past, rule 10) and the Narrator (actors[].echo, rule 32) get who an echo was and how it fell as a hidden note (HF2).

A template may carry a loadout: a list of library item ids given to every NPC made from it (Part 5 §8) \[Proposed\].&#32;

**Layered generation** (NG1–NG9, NG20, NG21; src/library/spawn.ts presetSkills, templateSkills). Every number is a placeholder lever in CONFIG.
- **Rank** (NG3): an ordered scale RANKS (rank\_0 Untrained, rank\_1 Novice, rank\_2 Trained, rank\_3 Veteran, rank\_4 Elite, rank\_5 Paragon, rank\_6 Beyond, rank\_7 Mythic, rank\_8 Divine). Each row of RANK\_ROWS gives a skill anchor and the NPC's level: 15/L1, 30/L2, 50/L4, 65/L8, 75/L12, 100/L20, 150/L30, 200/L40, 400/L60 (Divine raised from 300 to 400 by Ali, 14/11/2026: minor gods are the minimum for the divine). Past the last row each step adds RANK\_BEYOND\_STEP (anchor +100, level +20) and the name gains a + (Divine+, Divine++). The level comes only from the rank (`templateLevel`); an echo or other unique keeps its own level. A theme may rename ranks (ThemeDef.rankNames).
- **Role** (NG1, NG2; library table npcRoles, IDs RO…): which skills the NPC is built around, in three bands. Today: soldier, skirmisher, brute, shooter, sneak, watcher, talker, worker, beast.
- **Theme** (NG1; library table themes, IDs TH…): who the NPC belongs to, adding its own bands (village folk, town watch, road bandits, wild beasts).
- **Bands** (NG2): a skill's value is the rank's anchor × its band share (ROLE\_BAND\_SHARE: High 1, Moderate 0.8, Low 0.6) × NPC\_BAND\_MULT (1; one lever over every NPC's skills at once, which took over from the old points per level). A skill in more than one band takes the highest. High-band skills are tagged.
- **Cap** (NG5): the player's cap, SKILL\_CAP (100), and SKILL\_CAP + TAG\_CAP\_BONUS (125) for a tagged skill. Rows past Paragon are about perks, tiers and gear, not raw skill.
- **Spread** (NG9): each skill moves by up to ±COPY\_SPREAD (10%), seeded by the template and the actor's ID, so two copies differ a little and the same NPC always rebuilds the same. With no actor (the essence lists) the centre values are used.
- **Floors** (NG8): the template's minimums, its class floors (`templateMinimums`), and NPC\_COMMON\_SKILLS (Sight and Hearing at Low) when the NPC's body can use them (a slime has no Sight or Hearing). Stamina copies Endurance.

A copy does not roll: its skills are rebuilt from these layers on load (`refreshCopies`), so changing a lever or a role reaches existing NPCs.

**Gear, compositions and the cast check** (NG10, NG15, NG16; src/library/spawn.ts, src/scenario/apply.ts). Every number is a placeholder lever in CONFIG.
- **Gear** (NG10): a preset's pinned items (NpcTemplate.loadout, kept where the story needs one, like the guard's round shield), then for each gear class its role uses (NpcRoleDef.gear), the highest-level library item its skills meet, up to its theme's wealth (ThemeDef.wealth, an EQUIP\_LEVELS key: village folk and road bandits Common, town watch Fine); a starter item wins a tie (`templateLoadout`). It is the starter kit's rule without the starter tag. A slot a pin fills gets no pick, nor a shield beside a two-handed weapon. Equip levels end at Masterwork and the library has few items above Fine, so the items deep dive thread reviews this further.
- **Compositions** (NG15): a cast entry may name `compose` (race, role, theme, rank by key or name, optional class: catalogs.compose) instead of a template; validation refuses unknown parts (bad\_compose). Code builds a copy preset (`composeTemplate`; Resolve, control and chase from COMPOSED\_NPC by the role's kind) and keeps it as a story blueprint, one per composition (ID NC\_race\_role\_theme\_rank\[\_class\]), so it shows in the GM view under "Made this story" with Send to library. When no template or composition gives who it wants, the Weaver uses the closest and says who in `templateGap`; every one is kept in hidden.templateWants and listed in the GM view (Templates wanted), so a template can be made. This settles the NPC half of D-11.
- **Cast check** (NG16 b): at each scenario's opening, the power of the cast hostile or opposed to the player over the player's power is set against the challenge grade's band (CAST\_POWER\_BANDS: minor 0.25–0.75, moderate 0.5–1.25, major 1–2, extreme 1.75–4). It is kept in hidden.castPower, shown in the GM view, and an off-band cast logs cast\_power\_off\_band. Nothing is refused and no NPC changes; a reject waits until playtests show the bands hold.

**Perks, traits, rules and personality** (NG11–NG14; src/library/spawn.ts). Every number is a placeholder lever in CONFIG.
- **Milestone perks** (NG11): an NPC spends its level's perk points (one per PERK\_POINT\_EVERY levels, as the player) on ladder milestone perks by the player's rules: the milestone reached by true skill, a perk written there and in the game, and enough perks below it in that skill. Its role's High skills come first, then Moderate, Low and the rest, highest first, each from its lowest milestone up (`npcMilestonePerks`). They are 'level' perks. A copy's are re-picked on load from its rebuilt skills; a rolled NPC's points left over stay unspent. Milestones read true skill, so a tagged 125 reaches 131 with its 40 perk and stops short of a 140 perk.
- **Rank traits** (NG12): a rank draws RANK\_TRAITS (0, 0, 0, 1, 1, 2, 2, 3, 3 from Untrained; + RANK\_TRAITS\_BEYOND per step past the last row) from its theme's and role's trait pools (ThemeDef.traits, NpcRoleDef.traits), perks and quirks alike, seeded by the actor's ID, as 'template' perks. The pools are thin placeholders until the perk thread adds rows; no preset below Veteran draws any today.
- **Generator rules** (NG13): a role's kind sets its rank floor (RANK\_FLOOR: combat Trained, civilian Novice; a beast none; `templateRank`), and a template a routine set with the patrol routine is for (by its TP key in RoutineSetDef.tags.role) gets Keen Ear (`patrols`, `templatePerks`). Neither is typed into a preset any more.
- **Personality** (NG14): the theme's lean, then the role's, then the preset's own pins (NpcTemplate.personality, only what differs), a later layer winning per axis; values strongest first: the preset's, the role's, the theme's, up to DRIVES\_SHOWN. A copy's every trait (not Nerve) then moves by up to ± PERSONALITY\_SPREAD (20), seeded by its ID; a beast gets no spread. The Weaver's Nerve line, types and values go on top (PL12, `presetPersonality` then personalityFrom).

**Personality** (Ali, 11/11/2026, questions PL1–PL19, page "Personality Lines"). Every actor, the player included, holds points in two layers (Core.personality), and everything shown is read from them (src/ruleset/personality.ts):
- **Nerve** (how a character meets risk; PL1–PL4): one axis from Cautious (−100) to Daring (+100). It leans at PERSONALITY\_LEAN (20) or more from 0, which gives three lines: Cautious, Steady (no lean) and Daring. Raw Resolve picks the tier on the line (NERVE\_TIER\_FROM: 0, 20, 40, 60, 80, 100; one ordered scale per line in NERVE\_LINES): Cautious Skittish, Timid, Careful, Prudent, Cool-headed, Unflappable; Steady Jittery, Settled, Steady, Stalwart, Unbreakable, Immovable; Daring Blustering, Brash, Spirited, Bold, Fearless, Death-defying. Resolve 100 and up is superhuman (Ali, PL3); past it, every NERVE\_BEYOND\_STEP (50) adds a + (PL20). The morale level is the mood and never moves the tier (PL4). Names are set by the lore thread (PL18).
- **Personality** (how a character behaves; PL6, PL7): seven axes from −100 to +100, a library table (PA…) with a behaviour hint on each end: Honesty (deceitful ↔ honest), Pride (humble ↔ proud), Temper (patient ↔ quick to anger), Sociability (quiet ↔ talkative), Discipline (impulsive ↔ disciplined), Curiosity (set in their ways ↔ curious), Levity (earnest ↔ playful; from the personality voice tests). Plus up to DRIVES\_SHOWN (3) **values**, ranked strongest first (PL19, VL…), each with a hint: duty, wealth, family, faith, power, freedom, safety, pleasure, knowledge, kindness (PL28). Ali asked for more (PL28), so the list now covers all 19 values of Schwartz's refined theory (Schwartz et al. 2012) plus family: glory (achievement), adventure (stimulation), reputation (face), humility, nature, justice (universal concern), tolerance, loyalty (dependability), courtesy (interpersonal conformity), order (societal security) (PL29). The voice tests added propriety, gratitude and desire.
- **Words** (PL9): a trait under 20 from 0 is left out; 20–39 "a bit", 40–69 the plain word, 70 and up "very" (TRAIT\_WORDS). **Archetypes** (PL10, PY…; the personality line prototype, voice-tested in the "Personality Voice Tests" doc) are library rows with a target on the axes and Nerve (left out = 0), the values the type lives by, and a behaviour hint: Ordinary, Prideful, Mature, Childish, Honourable, Schemer, Bully, Loner, Busybody, Free spirit (targets are placeholders). A character's archetype is the nearest row: the gap averages |points − target| over every axis and Nerve, plus the drive fit (0 when the type's values are the character's top values, by DRIVE\_RANK\_WEIGHT; 100 when none are held or the type has none, ARCHETYPE\_NO\_VALUES\_GAP) counted ARCHETYPE\_VALUE\_WEIGHT (2) times; a tie goes to the earlier row. A **"but"** is a trait the archetype doesn't explain: BUT\_MIN (20) points or more, BUT\_GAP (40) or more from the target, and not just a weaker version of it; widest gap first, at most BUTS\_SHOWN (3). **Tensions** (TN…) are library rows of two parts that pull against each other (conditions on axes, raw Resolve, the best raw skill of any kind / of a fighting group / of an attacking group, the Nerve line, the life stage and the values); the agent gets the first TENSIONS\_SHOWN (2) met, in table order. Scaling: axes stop at ±100, so archetypes, buts and tensions are a human-range description; past 100 the Nerve tier keeps growing (+), and a tension's skill bar (40, 60) is simply met by every supernatural fighter.
- **What it does** (PL13): words only. The Planner and Responder get one line in CHARACTER: "Nerve: Timid (keeps out of danger when they can; Calm right now). Mature at heart (thinks before acting, …), but very curious (wants to know how things work, …). Drives: knowledge first (…), then loyalty (…). Tensions: wary but dangerous (…)." A unique NPC's template may add hand-written signatures (if-then lines, right after Nerve) and a title (given with its name in CHARACTER); copies get neither. Any character may also hold its own signatures (Core.signatures, after the template's): the player sets theirs in play, one box each in the Character page's Personality pop-up (Ali, 14/11/2026), at most SIGNATURES_MAX (5) of SIGNATURE_MAX_CHARS (120) characters each, saved at once (Ali, 14/11/2026, SP3 a, SP4 a: "we will see as we go"). The player's line goes to the Narrator (player.personality, SP5 a) and to the agent bar's In character style. Both rules tell the agent which line wins when they disagree: a line written for it alone, then a tension, then the "at heart" line. The Voice gets none of it (Q1). Code-run NPCs are unaffected; numeric effects are D-205.
- **Where points come from**: an NPC template gives its points and its starting Resolve (PL11). The Weaver may name a Nerve line (±NERVE\_LINE\_POINTS, 40), up to two archetypes (each sets the traits it names to its target unless already past it) and up to three ranked values for a new actor (PL12). The player sets them at creation (CreationBuild.personality; left out = no lean) (PL14). Perks, the current class and achievements add points while held (PerkDef, ClassDef, AchievementDef `personality`; achievement chains include lower levels by reference) (PL15, PL16). Acting in or against character moves nothing (PL16; personalities that shift with actions are open, VT-OI1).
- **The player's personality in play** (Ali, after playtests runs 34 and 35): narration flavour only. The Narrator gets player.personality (the sheet's words) and may colour how the player's actions are told, e.g. a cautious character who lashes out does it with a shaking hand. It never changes, softens, refuses or undoes an action, and the Parser never sees it.
- **Who sees it** (PL17): the player sees their own on the sheet, never an NPC's. The Character page's Personality tile (archetype, Nerve tier and line) opens a Personality block: the Nerve tier and line ("Steady · Daring nerve"), the trait words, the values, and the archetype with its hint (src/ui/personality.ts).
- **Creation picker** (PL14): below the skill groups, a Personality section: a Nerve line picker (Cautious, Steady, Daring; ±NERVE\_LINE\_POINTS) with the tier the current Resolve gives, up to three values (strongest first), and a slider per axis (−100 to +100, step 5) showing its word live. A line under it reads the result back as the sheet will ("Honest, proud. Values duty, freedom. Honourable at heart."). Clear resets to no lean. The picks carry over when the Start changes; validateBuild refuses points outside −100 to +100, unknown traits and more than three values.

**Age** (Ali, 12/11/2026, Character Age AG1–AG22, page https://claude.ai/artifact/Nrx5ZkmVhdb7txGcZT4GNh; src/ruleset/age.ts). Every actor of a race that ages has an age; it is descriptive, plus the old-age decline.
- **Kept** (AG2): one saved number, Core.bornDay, the game day it was born (day 1 is the story's first). Age = (today − birth day) ÷ YEAR\_DAYS (365), so it grows with the clock. No calendar or birthdays.
- **Life stages** (AG3, AG7): ordered scale LIFE\_STAGES (stage\_0 Child, stage\_1 Youth, stage\_2 Adult, stage\_3 Middle-aged, stage\_4 Old, stage\_5 Venerable, stage\_6 Ancient). Child describes the race's life only: no one in the game is in it (no children, D-316). Past Ancient each further step, the gap between the race's Venerable and Ancient starts, adds a + (Ancient+, Ancient++); from AGE\_PLUS\_NUMBER\_FROM (4) steps it reads "Ancient +4".
- **Lifespan on the race** (AG4–AG6): RaceDef.ageStages lists the start age of each stage. Human 0/13/18/40/60/80/100 from real references (adolescence, age of majority, middle age, the UN's older persons at 60, the oldest old at 80, centenarians); Elf 0/13/20/150/250/350/450 and Dragon 0/5/100/200/300/400/500 (Ali set Ancient at 450 and 500; the stages between are placeholders). A race with none is ageless (Slime): no age, no decline.
- **Old-age decline** (AG9, AG17–AG20): from the start of Old (AGE\_DECLINE\_FROM), a skill in the Melee, Ranged, Defense, Health, Stealth or Notice group (AGE\_DECLINE\_GROUPS) keeps 0.5 ^ (years past the start of Old ÷ (the race's Ancient start − its Old start)) of its effective skill: a human with 100 fights like an 84 at 70, a 50 at 100, and never reaches zero. It multiplies effective skill at the tier step (ageMult), so raw and true skill, perks, milestones, All-50s and learning are untouched ("an old retired hero who still has something in him"); augments to a declining skill decline with it. HP reads raw Endurance and does not drop. A flat penalty would stop mattering past 100; a share keeps biting at any level.
- **NPCs** (AG13, AG14): at spawn code picks a stage from the template's ageStages (none: NPC\_AGE\_STAGES, Adult and Middle-aged) and a year inside it, seeded by the actor's ID, and saves the birth day once; copies keep it when refreshCopies rebuilds their skills. The Weaver may name a new actor's life stage in its cast entry (`age`); code picks the years, and a stage that isn't a life stage, or one for an ageless race, is bad\_age. A save from before ages gets them on load.
- **Player** (AG15): the creation screen's Age field, whole years from the race's youngest age (Youth start; no children, D-316) to its Venerable start (13 to 80 for a human), default CREATION\_AGE\_DEFAULT (20) or the Start's StartDef.age.
- **Who sees what** (AG16): your Character card reads "Age 20 · Adult". Others see only the stage they look (the who's-here row's hover, the Narrator's actors and an NPC's view of who it sees), never the number. Each NPC's own agent gets "Age 31 · Adult" in self.age; the Narrator gets the player's. The GM view lists exact ages. When your stage changes as time passes, the turn says "You are now Middle-aged (age 40)".
- **The youngest age per rank** (AG12 b, AG21–AG27). Training years are the same for every race, so long-lived races have an edge: each rank row (CONFIG.RANK_ROWS) carries `training`: Untrained 0, Novice 1, Trained 4, Veteran 8, Elite 12, Paragon 20, then +10 per rank step (Beyond 30, Mythic 40, Divine 50; RANK_BEYOND_STEP, AG22). Youngest age = the race's Youth start + years × the NPC's talent (ruleset/age.ts ageFloor), never under the race's youngest age (below); Untrained is the youngest age itself. **Talent** is an ordered scale TALENTS (talent\_0 Common ×3, talent\_1 Steady ×2, talent\_2 Able ×1, talent\_3 Talented ×0.75, talent\_4 Prodigy ×0.5; multipliers in CONFIG.TALENT_MULT; past the last row each step multiplies by TALENT_BEYOND_MULT 0.5, so it never reaches zero, and the name gains a +). Able ×1 is a real professional's pace (AG24). An NPC's talent is the Weaver's cast `talent`, else its preset's (NpcTemplate.talent), else its theme's (ThemeDef.talent), else CONFIG.NPC_TALENT_DEFAULT (Able) (AG25); it is saved once at spawn (Core.talent) and the GM view shows it beside the age. The age roll never goes under the floor: a stage wholly under it rolls inside the stage that holds the floor instead. A Weaver stage wholly under the floor is refused (`bad_age`, with the youngest age in the message) and a talent off the scale is `bad_talent`; the Weaver's template catalog lists each preset's `youngestStage`. Talent only sets the age floor (AG26); the player has none and is never held to the floor (AG27). A Common human reaches Elite at 49 and Paragon at 73; an Able human Trained guard is at least 17. **No children** (D-316): no one in the game, player or NPC, is younger than the start of CONFIG.YOUNGEST\_STAGE (Youth: 13 for a human or an elf, 5 for a dragon; ruleset/age.ts youngestAge). Teenagers stay (Ali chose "Allow teens from 13 again"). Every age floor, NPC age roll, creation range and random identity starts there; a Weaver stage wholly under it is bad\_age ("there are no children"), and its age field leaves out Child. Every agent's fixed prompt says the world has no children. On load (save/checks.ts dropChildren), an NPC under the youngest age leaves the world (links to it are dropped like any missing actor's) and a player under it is aged up to it. The Child role (RO10), the village child preset (TP6) and the "old head on young shoulders" tension (TN3) are retired. Turned down: faster learning for the young (AG10), dying of old age (AG11). Parked: slower ageing from power (D-222), revisiting training years past Paragon (D-223), talent and learning speed (D-224).

**Slime template** (first build): a copy of race Slime (body type amorphous) with no class (BR7: the Slime class is gone; the Slime senses tree needs race Slime), role beast, theme wild beasts, rank\_0 (level 1), Resolve 50 and no personality points (Steady; slimes are no longer fearless). Skills: unarmed\_natural 30 (minimum), dodge about 9 (beast Low band), tremor 50, everything else 0. HP multiplier 0.6 (30 HP). Its slam uses the slam class: damage 20, hit +10, reach 0.

**Village templates** (first build; every number a placeholder). All three are copies of race Human (body type humanoid) with no class:

**Template rules** (Ali, 08/11/2026). Anyone with fighting experience (guard, thief) is at least level 4, and a villager at least level 2. A template can list perks (`perks`) that every NPC made from it has. Any template that patrols (its role has a routine set with the patrol routine), now and later, has Keen Ear, given by the generator (NG13), and a test enforces it. Copies follow their template live: on load, each copy's skills are rebuilt from the layers above, and missing template perks are added (`refreshCopies`). Rolled NPCs keep what they rolled. A guard (soldier, town watch, Trained) has about melee\_medium 50, block 50, medium\_armor 40, endurance 40, sight 40. Every template carries the skill its loadout asks for (AR9), and a test checks it (EQ5).


- **guard:** soldier, town watch, rank\_2 (level 4), Keen Ear (it patrols); minimums melee\_medium 50, medium\_armor 50, block 30, dodge 20, sight 30, hearing 30, intimidation 30, persuasion 20; Resolve 50, Discipline 40 (soldier), value duty (town watch) \[Proposed\]; loadout arming\_sword, mail\_hauberk, round\_shield (Ali, 13/11/2026: the guard blocks with a shield again, not Parry); control agent.
- **trader** (the barkeep): talker, village folk, rank\_1 (level 2); minimums sight 25, hearing 25, barter 40, persuasion 30; Resolve 30, Nerve −40 (village folk), Sociability 20 (talker), values wealth, family \[Proposed\]; no loadout; control code.
- **patron:** worker, village folk, rank\_1 (level 2); minimums unarmed\_strike 20, sight 20, persuasion 30; Resolve 30, Nerve −40 (village folk), Sociability 20 (worker), values pleasure (its own pin), family \[Proposed\]; no loadout; control code.

### 5. Locations and story memory

Every turn happens at one node of a location tree (the place of the player's zone), so a castle in one city is never confused with a castle in another. A scenario can cross several nodes.

**Place tree.**

- Nodes have opaque ids and a parent link. Depth is unlimited; each node has a kind (country, settlement, place, and any added later), and the library says which kinds may nest under which.
- Each of Part 2's zones belongs to one place. The stage (the zones in play) may hold zones from several neighbouring places; a fact is saved at the place of the zone where it happened.
- Actors are not in the tree. Each actor holds a placeId pointer, which always equals the place of its zone; every move updates it, so people and creatures move freely between cities.
- When Claude names a place ("the castle"), code resolves it in the current settlement first, then upward. Claude never handles ids.

**Renames.** Places and people keep a current name plus past names. Old names still resolve. Fact text stores references, not names (met {npc:leona} at {place:gilded\_cup}), filled in with current names when a view is built, so a rename never needs a rewrite.

**State versus history.** Two records are kept apart so old details can't contradict new ones:

| Record | Holds | Rule |
| --- | --- | --- |
| State slots | What is true now (gilded\_cup.north\_wall: repaired) | One value per slot; a new value overwrites. Written by code from events, or by a validated set\_place\_state proposal naming subject and attribute |
| History facts | What happened ("the brawl broke the north wall") | Append-only, past tense, never edited |
| Player memory | The last value the player saw per slot, and when | Lets narration avoid "knowing" about a change the character never saw |

Whether every slot tracks player memory, or only narrated slots, is deferred.

**Facts and promotion.** A fact is saved once, at the node where it happened, linked to the entities it involves. Location facts are things the player witnessed or was told; world truth the player doesn't know stays in the hidden store. Promotion copies a short headline to an ancestor node:

- Code rules promote the important events: first meeting with a named NPC, a named death, a quest step, a revealed secret.
- Claude may propose promote\_fact; the validator accepts it within a per-scenario cap (placeholder).

**What a view receives.** PlayerView, NarrationView and WeaverView receive all facts for the current place, the top HEADLINES\_PER\_PLACE (3) headlines of each ancestor (CS7), and any fact linked to an entity present. Leona's "met at the Gilded Cup" arrives with her in another country. NPC views (OppositionView, DialogueView, ActorView) never receive facts or headlines: facts record what the player witnessed or was told.

### 6. Persistence

The save is many small versioned pages in the artifact's database, committed by flipping one bookmark. State is never rebuilt by re-running old turns, because the library always moves to its latest version and replay under a newer library would change the past.

**Three records.**

| Record | Holds | Kept |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Snapshot (head bookmark) | The page versions that make up the story at turn start | Current and one turn back |
| Turn record | Player text, agent outputs, RNG state before, events with effects, facts added, library version | Last N (placeholder) |
| Story history | Facts and promoted headlines | Never pruned |

**Where it lives.** The artifact's db capability, not browser storage and not a self-republishing page. Database paths (even segment counts, as the platform requires):

```
library/meta                          libraryVersion, alias table
library/<kind>.<id>                   one library entry, e.g. library/body_type.dragon
stories/<storyId>                     story meta
stories/<storyId>/control/head        the bookmark
stories/<storyId>/control/lock        turn lock (lease)
stories/<storyId>/pages/<key>@<turn>  one page version, e.g. actor.rook@41
stories/<storyId>/turns/<turn>        one turn record
stories/<storyId>/backups/<turn>      one in-database backup (section 11)
stories/<storyId>/history/<n>         story text for turns n×HISTORY_CHUNK_TURNS onward
```

Page keys: engine, hidden, actor.\<id>, place.\<id>, facts.\<placeId>.\<n>, blueprint.\<id>.

**Platform limits** (artifact runtime contract 0.2.96):

- 256 KiB per document, 32 levels deep; paths up to 16 segments of 200 bytes.
- No transactions; last writer wins. Hence the bookmark commit.
- An artifact's database holds at most 25,000 documents in total, shared by the library and every story. Documents per collection, total size and write rate are also capped, but those numbers are not published.
- Leases give a single-writer lock.

**Undo.** One turn. It points head back at the previous bookmark and drops that turn's record. The RNG does not roll back: the engine page keeps the undone turn's RNG state, so a retried action rolls new dice. Undo after a library edit is allowed; the restored state uses the new library from the next turn. Undoing the turn that started a scenario rerolls the event roll and runs Agent Weaver again, like any other dice. Undoing a turn that crossed a frontier reuses the held extension, if this session still holds it.

**Removed.** No named save slots and no /save export. Backups are automatic (section 11).

**Two windows.** The turn lock makes a second window read-only while the first plays.

### 7. Versioning and migration

Three things can change under a running story, and each has its own rule.

| What changes | Example | Rule |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Save shape | Code adds or moves a field | Numbered migration steps, run in order on load (v3 → v4 → v5). Defaults for missing fields are allowed only inside a step |
| Library entries | A dragon gains a tail; a perk is revised | Reconcile on load (table below) |
| Ruleset numbers | K, tier tables, penalties | Derived values follow automatically. Saved levels, XP and perk points keep their values; a new XP curve or perk-point rule applies from the next level (section 3) |

**Library reconcile rules.** The library keeps a status per entry, so saves always know where an id went.

| Entry status | Effect on a save |
| --- | --- |
| Added | New body part starts Healthy; new skill starts at its default |
| Revised | New effect applies at once (effects are derived) |
| Renamed | Library keeps old id → new id; saves follow the alias and keep raw values |
| Replaced | Old id points to its successor; saves move to the new entry |
| Deleted | Perk, skill or class stays dormant on the sheet: kept, not applied, shown in the GM view. A removed body part's pool is dropped |

**Land (WZ12).** Schema 9 (LAND\_SCHEMA) added the land; a save from before it is refused with a reason ("made before the land"), and is left as it was. Regions are saved as pages `region.<id>`.

**Resets.** A change too big to migrate may reset the story, but only after the designer confirms it. Code never resets on its own. If a migration can't run, the game refuses to load, leaves the save untouched, and asks. A confirmed reset archives the old story: its pages and backups stay under its own id, and play starts a new story id (D-73). The GM view has a **Start a new save** button (Ali, 08/11/2026): it warns that the current story ends and play can't return to it, offers Download backup, and only a second click archives the story (its meta gets `archivedAt`, so the newest story list skips it) and goes to Create. The "You fell" screen's Reset does the same.

**Safety bookmark.** Before migrating, load keeps the pre-migration bookmark. It is dropped after the next good turn.

### 8. Integrity

Only the engine writes the save, every load is checked, and the save stays inside platform limits.

**One write door.**

- The store accepts only typed engine operations. Agent outputs are a different type, so the compiler rejects passing one to the store.
- Agents receive copies built by the view builders, never live state.
- Claude in chat may read saves to debug and may write library entries when asked. It never writes story pages.
- The head lists a checksum for every page. A page changed from outside (by hand, or by Claude in chat) or torn by a half-finished save fails the check on load.

**Checks on every load.**

| Check | Examples | On failure |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Checksums | Every page matches its hash in the head | Refuse to load, ask the designer |
| Shape | Every page matches its schema version | Refuse to load, ask the designer |
| Invariants | Exactly one current class; character level = sum of class levels unless a perk says otherwise; every page carries the loaded story id; every actor's placeId equals its zone's placeId; every stage zone id resolves to a zone on its place page; no slot used twice per actor; every slot names a part the actor still has; no part covered by two worn items; stackables hold qty ≥ 1 | Refuse to load, ask the designer |
| References | Every id resolves: place, actor, fact, library entry (through alias or dormant) | Repair and report: drop the dangling link, list it in the GM view |

Raw skill above 100 is logged as a warning, not refused, because workaround mechanics for the mortal limit are coming. When a load is refused, the designer chooses: the undo bookmark, the safety bookmark, or a confirmed reset.

**Size budgets.**

| Item | Budget | When reached |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Any page | 256 KiB (platform) | Busy places split facts into more facts.\<placeId>.\<n> pages |
| Turn records | Last N (placeholder) | Oldest records are deleted; story history stays |
| Story text | HISTORY\_CHUNK\_TURNS turns per document (20) | A new document starts; nothing is pruned. If one document passes 256 KiB its lines keep their text and lose the code-built extras (mechanics, heard lines, changes) |
| Claude proposals | Fact text and name length; promotions and minted items per scenario (placeholders) | The validator rejects the proposal |
| Whole story | 25,000 documents per database, shared by the library and every story; total size and per-collection caps unpublished | Saving stops and the game says so. Data is never dropped silently |

### 9. Types

These types replace Part 2's GameState and Actor as the saved shape. Belief, Fact, Zone, ZoneLink, Band, HiddenStore, GameEvent, AgentTurnLog, Tier and EventRollResult keep their Part 2 definitions. Fields marked reserved hold a place for an open question and are not used yet.

```ts
// ---------- ids ----------
type StoryId = string;
type ActorId = string;
type PlaceId = string;
type FactId = string;
type LibraryRef = string;   // id in the story's blueprints or the library (story first)
type SlotKey = string;      // '<subject>.<attribute>', e.g. 'gilded_cup.north_wall'
type Turn = number;
type Grade = 'none' | 'minor' | 'moderate' | 'major' | 'extreme';   // [Proposed] challenge and gain scale

// ---------- library (shared by every story) ----------
type LibraryMeta = {
  libraryVersion: number;   // bumps on every library edit
  status: Record<LibraryRef, EntryStatus>;
};
type EntryStatus =
  | { state: 'active' }
  | { state: 'renamed'; to: LibraryRef }
  | { state: 'replaced'; by: LibraryRef }
  | { state: 'deleted' };   // saves keep the id dormant

type ItemDef = {                       // library entry or story blueprint (kind 'item'); Part 5
  id: LibraryRef;
  kind: 'weapon' | 'armor' | 'shield' | 'accessory' | 'tool' | 'consumable' | 'misc';
  name: string; look: string;          // public text
  bulk: 0 | 1 | 2 | 3;                 // no carry limit yet
  value: number;                       // base price in coins; placeholder, unused until trade
  stackable?: true;
  consumable?: true;                   // removed on use
  concealed?: true;                    // hidden from others until used
  hiddenAt?: Tier;                     // SE9: a look over the wearer must beat this tier to spot it
  visible?: true;                      // accessories: shown to others
  slot?: 'armor' | 'accessory';
  grips?: 1 | 2;
  effects?: ItemEffect[];
  weapon?: { modes: AttackMode[] };
  armor?: { covers: string[]; reduction: number; multiplier: number;
            weight: ArmorWeightKey;   // ARMOR_WEIGHTS: light, medium, heavy
            pools?: Record<string, number> };   // part id -> pool in points, the same for every wearer
  shield?: { pool: number };                     // pool in points, the same in any hand
  base?: { itemId: LibraryRef; quality: 1 | 2 | 3 | 4; boosts: string };   // minted variants
  tags?: string[];
};

type ItemEffect = {
  kind: 'skill' | 'rollMod';           // rollMod only when the entry notes it
  skill?: LibraryRef;                  // kind 'skill'
  roll?: 'hit' | 'dodge' | 'block' | 'perception' | 'stealth' | 'event';   // kind 'rollMod'
  value: number;
  active: 'worn' | 'held' | 'inUse' | 'carried';
  scope: 'withItem' | 'any';
};

type AttackMode = {
  id: string;
  tree: 'melee' | 'ranged' | 'unarmed';
  class: 'light' | 'medium' | 'heavy' | 'punch' | 'slam' | 'grapple';   // natural attacks use their own class; unarmed with no class: the unarmed profile
  skill?: LibraryRef;                    // unarmed only: which placeholder skill
  reachM: number;                        // metres, from real weapons
  reach: number;                         // metres in use: reachM + a thrown weapon's perk metres (modeReach)
  grips: 0 | 1 | 2;                      // 0 for natural attacks
  thrown?: true;
  damageAdj?: number;
  hitAdj?: number;
  partId?: string;                       // natural attacks: the Strike part used
};

// ---------- story control ----------
type StoryMeta = {
  storyId: StoryId;
  title: string;
  schemaVersion: number;
  createdAt: string;        // ISO date
  settings: { showMechanics: boolean; tone: string };
};

type PageRef = { version: Turn; hash: string; bytes: number };
type Bookmark = { turn: Turn; pages: Record<string, PageRef> };   // page key -> version

type Head = Bookmark & {    // writing this is the commit
  storyId: StoryId;
  schemaVersion: number;
  libraryVersion: number;
  undo: Bookmark | null;    // one turn back
  safety?: Bookmark;        // pre-migration, dropped after the next good turn
};

// ---------- pages ----------
type EnginePage = {
  storyId: StoryId;
  turn: Turn;
  mode: 'explore' | 'dialogue' | 'tactical';
  rngSeed: number;
  rngState: number;         // NOT restored by undo
  pending: { turn: number; mode: 'explore' | 'dialogue' | 'tactical'; engaged: string[]; fired: string[] };
  stage: {
    zoneIds: string[];      // zones in play; may span several places
    tier: Tier;             // this scenario's event-roll tier
    scenarioNumber: number;
    scenarioStartTurn: Turn;
    fallback?: { reason: string; turn: Turn };   // set when the fallback stage was used
  };
  scenarioCaps: { promotions: number; minted: number };   // reset at scenario start
  clocks: Record<string, number>;
  flags: Record<string, string | number | boolean>;
  bands: Band[];
  quests: Quest[];   // src/types/state.ts: id, state, text, giver, dealId, goal, progress, level, startTurn, after?, pay?, due?, pinned?
};

type HiddenPage = { storyId: StoryId } & HiddenStore;

type BlueprintPage = {
  storyId: StoryId;
  id: LibraryRef;
  kind: 'item' | 'body_type' | 'npcTemplate';
  data: Record<string, unknown>;   // same shape as the library entry of that kind
};

// ---------- actor ----------
type NameHistory = { current: string; past: { name: string; untilTurn: Turn }[] };

type Actor = {
  id: ActorId;
  storyId: StoryId;
  kind: 'player' | 'npc';
  names: NameHistory;
  label: string;                     // public stranger label, e.g. 'a scarred dockhand'
  knownOnSight?: boolean;            // name known without an introduction
  placeId: PlaceId;                  // always the place of `zone`
  zone: string;                      // zone id (Part 2)
  posture?: 'open' | 'stealthy';     // player only (Part 2)
  bandId: string;                    // moved from Mind; the player belongs to the player band
  core: Core;
  progression?: Progression;         // player; NPCs only if NPCs improve (deferred)
  mind?: Mind;                       // NPCs
  journal?: Journal;                 // player
};

type SkillState = 'tagged' | 'normal' | 'flawed';
type SkillEntry = { raw: number; state: SkillState; dormant?: boolean };

type Core = {
  raceId?: LibraryRef;               // absent for actors with no race
  bodyTypeId: LibraryRef;        // body plan; set by the race when there is one
  templateId?: LibraryRef;           // NPCs: the template it came from
  generation?: 'rolled' | 'copy';    // rolled: values below are frozen; copy: skills read from the template
  skills: Record<LibraryRef, SkillEntry>;
  classes: ClassEntry[];
  perks: OwnedPerk[];                // stored perks only; race and class perks are derived
  condition: SavedCondition;
  inventory: ItemInstance[];
  standingDefense: 'dodge' | 'block';   // Part 2, Agent Planner; default [Proposed]: the higher effective skill
};

type ClassEntry = {
  classId: LibraryRef;
  state: 'unlocked' | 'current' | 'previous';   // exactly one 'current'
  level: number;                                // class level; 0 while unlocked
  dormant?: boolean;
};

type OwnedPerk = {
  perkId: LibraryRef;
  source: 'level' | 'mastery';       // 'mastery' = all-XXX perks, never removed
  gainedTurn: Turn;
  treeId?: LibraryRef;               // mastery only
  tier?: 50 | 100 | 150;             // mastery only
  dormant?: boolean;                 // set on load when the library entry is deleted
};

type SavedCondition = {
  hpLostPct: number;                 // health-track level is derived from this
  parts: Record<string, number>;     // body-part id -> pool %, state derived
  destroyed?: string[];              // reserved: Destroyed latch, pending Healing
  status: string[];
};

type ItemInstance = {
  id: string;
  itemId: LibraryRef;
  qty?: number;                      // stackables only
  equippedIn?: string[];             // 'grip:<grip part id>' | 'armor' | 'acc:<n>'; absent = carried
  pools?: Record<string, number>;    // % left: 'shield', or a covered part id
};

type Progression = {
  startingTotal: number;             // record only; never changes
  potential: { base: number; adjustments: { source: string; delta: number; turn: Turn }[] };   // base: POTENTIAL, or this character's own value
  xp: number;                        // toward the next level
  unspentPerkPoints: number;
  marks: LibraryRef[];
  xpLog?: { source: string; amount: number; turn: Turn; levelUp?: { classId: LibraryRef; level: number } }[];   // source trail (B8)
  achievements?: { id: string; key: string; of?: string; turn: Turn }[];   // each once (B4); name and level read from the library row `key`
  defeats?: Record<string, number>;   // 'all', 'body_type:<type>', 'race:<race>'
  nextClass?: LibraryRef;            // the class the next level goes to (X9)
};

type Mind = {
  control: 'agent' | 'code';
  moraleState?: 'broken' | 'surrendered';   // set when the morale bar reaches Broken (Part 3, Morale); cleared when the scenario ends
  knowledge: Record<ActorId, Belief>;
  overrides?: Record<ActorId, 'hostile' | 'opposed' | 'neutral' | 'friendly'>;  // sparse; else band relations
  knows: Fact[];
  routine?: { id: string; anchorTurn: Turn };            // anchor set by code (Part 2, Anchors); zone removed
  routines?: string[];                                   // the routine set's routines; player band: the companion set
  routineSetId?: LibraryRef;                             // the set those routines came from
  slots?: Record<string, string | string[]>;             // slot -> zone id, or zone ids for a route;
                                                         // shared by every routine in `routines`;
                                                         // 'here' is written by code on a switch to a wait routine
  alarm?: { armed: boolean; lastAlarmTurn?: Turn;
            returnTo?: { id: string; anchorTurn: Turn } };   // set on alarm, cleared on calm or an agent switch
};
// Removed from Mind: the old group id (now Actor.bandId) and hasAgenda (derived).

type NpcTemplate = {                 // library entry or story blueprint (kind 'npcTemplate'); Part 4 §4
  id: LibraryRef;
  bodyTypeId: LibraryRef;
  raceId?: LibraryRef;
  classId?: LibraryRef;              // its class tree, e.g. 'slime'
  generation: 'rolled' | 'copy';
  minimums: Record<LibraryRef, number>;   // skill -> starting raw; absent = 0
  roleId?: LibraryRef;               // NG1: role (RO), its skills in High / Moderate / Low bands
  themeId?: LibraryRef;              // NG1: theme (TH), extra bands and rank names
  rank?: RankKey;                    // NG3: rank row (RANK_ROWS) sets the skill anchor, the level and the training years (AG12)
  talent?: TalentKey;                // AG25: sets the youngest age for the rank; left out = the theme's, then NPC_TALENT_DEFAULT
  level?: number;                    // uniques (echoes) only; presets take level from the rank row
  resolve: number;                 // starting Resolve (Part 3, Morale)
  personality: Personality;        // Nerve, trait and value points (Part 4, Personality)
  loadout?: LibraryRef[];            // [Proposed] items given at spawn (Part 5 §8)
  potential?: number;                // overrides POTENTIAL for NPCs made from it
  control: 'agent' | 'code';
  label: string;                     // public stranger label
};

type Journal = {
  secretsKnown: string[];
  factsWitnessed: FactId[];
  remembered: Record<SlotKey, { value: string; seenTurn: Turn }>;
  namesKnown: ActorId[];             // actors the player knows by name
  clearSight?: Record<ActorId, ZoneId>; // actors seen clearly and where; kept until they move unseen (Q17)
  lastKnown?: Record<ActorId, ZoneId>; // where each actor was last seen or heard; never forgotten (BG-28)
};

// ---------- places and memory ----------
type Place = {
  id: PlaceId;
  storyId: StoryId;
  kind: string;                      // 'country' | 'settlement' | 'place' | ...
  parentId: PlaceId | null;
  names: NameHistory;
  zones: Zone[];                     // Zone and ZoneLink from Part 2
  state: Record<SlotKey, { value: string; setTurn: Turn; setBy: string }>;
  headlines: FactId[];               // facts promoted to this node
};

type StoryFact = {
  id: FactId;
  storyId: StoryId;
  placeId: PlaceId;                  // where it happened
  turn: Turn;
  text: string;                      // past tense; '{npc:leona}' refs, never names
  refs: string[];                    // linked entity ids
  source: 'rule' | 'proposal';
  promotedTo: PlaceId[];
};

type FactsPage = { storyId: StoryId; placeId: PlaceId; facts: StoryFact[] };

// ---------- turn record ----------
type TurnRecord = {
  storyId: StoryId;
  turn: Turn;
  libraryVersion: number;            // exact replay only under this version
  rngBefore: number;
  playerInput: string;
  agentLog: AgentTurnLog[];
  events: GameEvent[];
  factsAdded: FactId[];
  eventRoll?: EventRollResult;       // set on the turn that started a scenario
};
```

### 10. Save and load flow

A save counts only once the head is written, so a crash at any earlier step leaves the last good save intact.

**Save, once per turn:**

1. Hold the turn lock: acquire control/lock at turn start and renew it while the turn runs. If it is busy, this window is read-only.
2. Resolve the turn in memory (Part 2), including scenario setup or stage extension when the turn starts a scenario or crosses a frontier. A turn stopped by a clarify question saves nothing.
3. For each page whose content changed: serialize it, check it is under 256 KiB (split facts pages if not), hash it, write pages/\<key>@\<turn>.
4. Write turns/\<turn>.
5. Write control/head: the new turn, the full page map, undo = the previous head's bookmark. This is the commit.
6. Clean up: delete page versions that no bookmark (head, undo, safety) points to, and turn records older than the last N.
7. After the narration is shown, write the story text: every history/\<n> document the turn's new lines fall in, rewritten whole. It is not part of the commit, so a failure costs only text. Loading a story reads every history document, so a reload shows the whole story column (Q18).

On unavailable, retry the failed write once. On quota\_exceeded, stop, keep the last good head, and tell the player.

**Undo:**

1. Hold the turn lock.
2. Read the undone turn's final RNG state from the current engine page.
3. Point head at the undo bookmark, with undo set to null.
4. Write a new engine page version carrying that RNG state forward, and add it to the head.
5. Delete the undone turn's record.

**Load:**

1. Read control/head, then every page it lists. Verify each checksum.
2. If the save's schema version is older: keep a safety bookmark, run the numbered migration steps in order.
3. If the library version changed: reconcile every library reference (section 7).
4. Validate shape, invariants and references (section 8). A structural failure stops here with the save untouched, and the designer chooses: undo bookmark, safety bookmark, or a confirmed reset.
5. If steps 2 to 4 changed anything, write the changed pages and a new head, as in save steps 3 and 5.
6. Build in-memory state from the pages. The game is ready.

### 11. Backups and playing from several PCs

One player, signed in to the same Claude account on every PC. Backups run on their own; the player never has to remember them.

**Several PCs.**

- The story lives in the artifact's database, not on the PC, so opening the game on any PC signed in to the same Claude account continues the same story.
- Nothing needed to resume a story is kept in browser storage. Browser storage holds only per-PC conveniences, such as panel layout.
- The turn lock lets one PC play at a time. A second PC that opens the game mid-turn is read-only until the lock's lease ends. A PC closed mid-turn loses nothing: that turn never committed, and its lease expires.

**Automatic backups, two layers.**

| Layer | What is copied | When | Kept | Protects against |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| In-database backup | Every page the head lists, packed into as few documents as fit under 256 KiB, at stories/\<storyId>/backups/\<turn> | Every BACKUP\_EVERY turns (placeholder), and on load before any migration | Last K per story (placeholder) | A bad migration, corrupted pages, mistakes beyond one-turn undo |
| Off-artifact backup \[Proposed\] | A scheduled task copies every story's current pages and the library into a separate backup artifact's database | Daily (placeholder) | Last K per story (placeholder) | Deleting or losing the game artifact |

- Backups are written only by engine code or the scheduled task, never by an agent.
- A restore runs the same checks as a load (section 8). It writes a new head pointing at the backup's pages, and keeps the current head as the safety bookmark.
- The GM view lists backups and restores one on request. Backups are never shown to the player during play, so they can't spoil hidden plans or secrets.
- In-database backups count toward the 25,000-document cap; size BACKUP\_EVERY and K together with N (OQ-9).
- Optional manual copy: an owner-only "Download backup" button in the GM view. It asks to confirm each time, so it supplements the automatic layers rather than replacing them.
- Before relying on the off-artifact layer, the build confirms that a scheduled task can read the game's database and write the backup artifact's database.

## Part 5 — Gear

### 1. Principles

- Gear is data in the library (or a story blueprint) plus a small instance in the story. Code reads it; no model decides a gear number.
- One gear path for everyone: the player and every NPC use the same effect reader, slots, gear ops, damage step and views.
- Numbers become words at the view. Agents see item names, public looks, kinds, class and weight labels and mode ids, never values.

### 2. When an effect is active

Every item effect declares one activation state. Code collects an actor's active effects from its inventory before each check or roll.

| State | Active while | Examples |
| --- | --- | --- |
| worn | the item is in the armor slot or an accessory slot | armor, rings, cloak |
| held | the item is in a hand | weapon, shield, torch |
| inUse | only for the action that uses the item | lockpicks, rope, potion |
| carried | anywhere in the inventory, only if the entry says so | charm, holy symbol, curse |

- **Tools.** For an action whose skill an inUse effect names, code picks the actor's best carried tool for that skill unless the action names one. The tool needs a free usable hand; if both are full, code stows the off-hand item as part of the action (free). \[Proposed\]
- **One copy counts.** A second instance of the same item id adds nothing. \[Proposed\]
- **Event roll.** An effect may modify the event roll only when its entry names it. Such an effect may be carried. Code reads the player's inventory once, at scenario setup step 2. Only the player makes this roll.

### 3. What an effect can do

Three kinds, all subject to §2's activation and to a scope (withItem: only actions made with this item; any: every roll or check of that kind while active).

1. **Skill bonus (default).** A flat bonus or penalty to named skills, entering effective skill (Part 1 §3). It counts in full in checks and at the roll's skill weight in rolls. The standard kit bonus is one +25 per roll, shared by all kit pieces, so two kit pieces that both apply still give +25 (Part 1 §3). Armor weight is a skill penalty on Dodge: light −5, medium −10, heavy −20 (*placeholders*).
2. **Item stats.** Read by the combat flow, never stacked as modifiers: weapon modes (§5), armor protection and pools, shield part (§6).
3. **Noted roll modifier (rare).** A flat modifier on one roll kind (hit, dodge, block, perception, stealth, event), of type gear (Part 1 §4, Layer 2b), only when the entry says so. The weapon hit adjustment is one: it applies at full value on the hit roll.

Every gear modifier enters the check or roll request as its own entry, naming its item and state, e.g. "Gear (held): arming sword". How gear modifiers combine with perks and statuses is decided by the modifier-stacking rules, not here. Initiative is not a gear target yet (D-85).

### 4. Slots, grips and gear ops

- **Grips.** One grip per Grip part of the actor's body type (humanoid 2; wolf, dragon 0). An item or attack mode says grips: 1 or 2; a two-grip item needs two usable Grip parts.
- **Worn slots.** One armor slot, plus ACCESSORY\_SLOTS accessory slots (5, Ali 14/11/2026) not tied to body parts.
- **Accessories (Ali, 14/11/2026, hub page accessories AX1-AX7).** Every body has 5 accessory slots (AX6 a). An accessory gives a flat skill bonus while worn, sized by its equipment level: ACCESSORY\_FACTOR (0.2) × EQUIP\_NEED, so Common 5, Fine 10, Superior 15, Masterwork 20, and on (AX1 d, AX2 c; ItemEffect.byLevel, ruleset/gear effectValue, which also prices it). Every accessory worn adds in full, copies included: two spectacles or two rings on the same skill both count (AX5 c; other gear still counts one copy). Rows (all Common, placeholders): spectacles (IT48, Eyesight, fragile), charmed ring (IT49), ear cup (IT50, Earshot), amulet (IT51, Resolve), dark hooded cloak (IT52, Sneaking) (AX7). A charmed ring's skill (ROLLED\_SKILL `$rolled`) is rolled once for each ring, from the ring's id and the story, so it keeps its skill for good (AX3); its name shows it, "charmed ring (Dodge)" (pieceName, in views and the Inventory). The pool is every tier 2 skill, weighted by tree (AX4 c, ItemEffect.weights): fighting and senses (melee, ranged, unarmed, defense, perception) 3, fortitude, armor, stealth, speech, trickery and tools 2, the rest 1 (placeholders).
- **Crippled Grip arm.** It keeps holding its item but can't use it: no attack, block or tool use with that arm, and no two-handed actions. A Destroyed arm drops what it held to the ground of the actor's zone; an item\_dropped event is perceived like any other.
- **Gear ops (free).** draw, stow, swap, wear or remove an accessory, pick up (from the ground or from a downed actor in the zone). Code applies them at the actor's initiative, before its action. They cost no action.
- **equip (main action).** Putting armor on or taking it off. Its final cost belongs to the action-economy discussion.
- **give and take (main action; BF34, Ali 14/11/2026; src/engine/handover.ts).** Trades at no price, open with anyone, NPCs with each other too. A **give** holds the item out (from a grip, the pack or the giver's own unlocked bag; Actor.offer): it changes hands only when the other side takes it, and an offer not taken by the end of the next turn is withdrawn. An NPC the item is held out to takes it at the end of that turn when its stance toward the giver is at least TAKE\_FROM\_STANCE (neutral), it has not yielded to them and it has a free grip; otherwise its mind may take it next turn. The player takes by saying so (Ideas offers "Take the X from Y"; PlayerView shows the offer as holdsOut). A **take** is the taker's at once when the item is held out to them, or its owner yielded or surrendered to them, is down or asleep; anyone else refuses (take\_refused), and taking it unseen is filching. Both need the two to reach each other's grips; out of reach, the taker or giver steps up first.
- **Ground items.** Each zone holds a list of item instances lying there. They are saved with the zone on its place page.

### 5. Weapons

- **Class table.** In Part 3, Weapons, armor and shields (ruleset config): damage and hit by class, and block's tier gap counts one less against heavy.
- **Skills.** Combat-Melee, Combat-Ranged and Combat-Unarmed trees, each with three skills. The skill of an attack is the tree plus the class (melee\_heavy, ranged\_light). The three Combat-Unarmed skills are placeholders that behave the same for now, sharing one unarmed profile (*placeholder*: 15 damage, +10 hit). A natural attack uses its own class's damage and hit instead (Part 3, Weapons, armor and shields): the humanoid's punch is class punch, the slime's slam is class slam. Worn hand gear such as gauntlets may change unarmed damage later (D-97).
- **Attack modes.** A weapon lists one or more modes, each with its own tree, class, reach and grips, plus optional bonuses on top of the class table (damageAdj, hitAdj). The class hit and hitAdj are added together into one gear modifier at full value; the class hit has no modifier type of its own (CS2). Dagger: melee light, and Throw. **The Throw action** (Ali, 10/11/2026): the throw's rules live on the Throw row of the attack modes table (`attackModes`), so every weapon that lists Throw gets them and nothing is coded into the gear. Reach (RD6, Ali 11/11/2026): each throw mode's own throw range in metres (CONFIG.THROW\_M), aimed at a man-sized target: spear or javelin 17.5 (the Roman pilum's effective range, 15–20 m), knife 7 (the longest standard distance in European knife-throwing rules), improvised 5 (placeholder, no source); a throwing axe would be 12 (the francisca). Perks' `throwReach` adds metres, and gear `modeReach` turns it into links once: spear 2, knife and improvised 0 (their own zone until positions exist). Roll: the first **combined skills roll**: THROW\_BLEND weapon (0.75) × the weapon's own effective skill (its gear class skill: a dagger Light weapons) + THROW\_BLEND ranged (0.25) × the effective ranged skill of the same class (Light ranged). Each part takes its own stand-ins and only the gear that boosts its own skill, and both skills that rolled can learn. `AttackMode.blend` carries a combined roll, so later actions can use one too. A thrown item leaves the thrower's hands and lands on the ground of the target's zone, hit or miss (item\_landed), where anyone can pick it up. \[Proposed\]
- **Reach.** Each mode has a real reach in metres (`reachM`, from real weapons): fist 0.7, dagger 1.0, short sword 1.3, arming sword 1.6, greataxe 2.2, greatsword 2.3, spear 2.8; shortbow 50, flatbow and light crossbow 60, recurve and stirrup crossbow 70, crossbow 80, longbow 90 (effective ranges against a man, not the longest shot). Reach is compared in metres with the distance to the target with sight between them (sightDistM): a spear reaches 2.8 m into the next zone across a doorway, a sword only 1.6 m. Ranged modes take the range modifiers in Part 3 (Reach, range, cover and light). Item value counts reach per metre (ITEM_VALUE.reachM, ZR7). \[Proposed\]
- **Thrown.** A thrown mode moves the item to the ground of the target's zone. There is no other ammunition.
- **Natural attacks.** A body type lists the natural attack modes its shape always has, and a race may add its own (BR3). A mode tied to a Strike part follows Part 3's Strike role (Damaged −10, Crippled unavailable). A humanoid's unarmed mode has no part. \[Proposed\]
- **Blocking.** Any held melee mode can block, which meets the "shield or weapon" rule. Ranged, thrown and unarmed modes can't.
- **Default mode.** If an order names no mode, code uses the first held mode whose reach covers the target, else the unarmed mode.

### 6. Armor, shields and the damage step

- **Coverage.** An armor item lists the parts it covers ('general' for the general area). Hits on uncovered parts take no armor. Head cover comes only from the armor slot.
- **Protection.** One reduction and one multiplier per armor item. Placeholders: light 2 and ×0.8, medium 4 and ×0.65, heavy 6 and ×0.55.
- **Armor pools.** An armor item may add a pool to a covered part (a heavy helm on the head). The pool is a fixed number of points on the item entry, the same whoever wears it. After protection, damage drains that pool first; the rest reaches the part. The pool never counts on the health track. An empty pool absorbs nothing until repaired (D-89). \[Proposed\]
- **Shields.** A held shield adds its Non-essential part to the holder, off the hit table; a block at gap 0 or better sends the hit to it (Part 3, Weapons, armor and shields). Its pool is a fixed number of points on the item entry, the same in any hand. The pool's state is saved on the item instance, so damage survives swaps. A Destroyed shield is lost.
- **Natural armor.** A race may list natural armor with the same fields (BR6). It stacks with worn armor: worn first, then natural. A natural-armor pool has no saved state yet, so it absorbs nothing (parked: D-256).
- **Damage step** (replaces Part 3's damage roll):

```
base  = WEAPON_CLASSES[mode.class].damage + mode.damageAdj + damage perks
dmg   = base × gapMultiplier(defense outcome)                 // Part 3, Defense
if worn armor covers the struck part:    dmg = (dmg − worn.reduction) × worn.multiplier
if natural armor covers the struck part: dmg = (dmg − nat.reduction)  × nat.multiplier
dmg   = max(0, dmg)          # no rounding: fractions allowed (Ali, 07/11/2026)
if an armor pool covers the part: the pool absorbs first, the rest goes on
then Part 3: part pool, then the health track × impact (unchanged)
```

**Damage perks.** A perk can change damage at any step above: base, the gap multiplier, armor (reduction, multiplier or pool) or the final number. Each damage perk names its step, and code applies it there **\[Proposed\]**.

Reduction before the multiplier bites light weapons hardest, so heavy weapons earn their hit penalty against armor. With the placeholders, a medium weapon drops a 100-HP fighter in 4 full hits unarmored, 6 in light armor, 8 in medium and 10 in heavy.

### 7. Item data

- **ItemDef** (library entry or story blueprint) holds everything fixed about an item. **ItemInstance** (story) holds only what changes in play. Part 4 §9 has both types.
- **Stackable items** (coins, potions) are one instance with a quantity, merged by item id, never equipped as a stack. Everything else is one instance per object.
- **bulk** 0–3 on every item. No carry limit for now: capacity is infinite (D-84).
- **Durability** only for pooled items: shield parts and armor pools. Repair is deferred.
- **Consumables** are a flag: the item is removed on use. Their effects wait for Healing and Status effects.
- **Coins** are a stackable library item, bulk 0. **value** is a base price in coins on every item (*placeholder*); no code reads it until trade exists.
- **Concealed items** stay out of other actors' views while held or worn, until used in an event that actor perceives; that observer's Belief.revealed then keeps it. Accessories are hidden by default unless tagged visible.
- **Minting** (for now): a variant = a base library item + quality tier 1–4 + public name and look. Code reads the tier's bonuses and writes a story blueprint (gear stats scale with need: a weapon's hit QUALITY\_HIT\_FACTOR × need × quality; armor's reduction ARMOR\_FACTOR × need + ARMOR\_QUALITY\_FACTOR × need × quality; a shield's pool POOL\_BASE + POOL\_FACTOR × need + POOL\_QUALITY\_FACTOR × need × quality; a damage boost QUALITY\_DAMAGE\_FACTOR × need × quality × DAMAGE\_CLASS\_FACTOR[class] × DAMAGE\_TREE\_FACTOR[melee or ranged], fractions kept; tools still use QUALITY\_TABLE), within the per-scenario minted cap. A minted item with no close library match uses the nearest generic base: improvised\_light, improvised\_medium or improvised\_heavy for an odd weapon, curio (misc, no effects) for a thing with no game effect (Ali, 07/11/2026, loot L2).
- **Improvised items** (Ali, 07/11/2026, loot L3): when the player fights with a scenario object not in the view, the Parser names an improvised base by size and a short name. Code writes a story blueprint (that base, the given name as name and look), puts it in the player's inventory and swaps it into hand before the attack. Improvised bases roll the Improvisation skill (Combat – Unarmed), carry hitAdj IMPROVISED\_HIT\_ADJ (*placeholder*, −10) and no kit bonus.
- **Boon** (Ali, 07/11/2026, loot V8): a reward's worth to the player. An item's Boon is its hand-set boon when it has one, else its value ÷ BOON\_COIN (20); a reward's Boon is that × qty, × BOON\_QUALITY (2/3/5/8) for a minted item of quality 1/2/3/4. Fractions are allowed. A plan's rewards together stay within BOON\_BUDGET by gain: none 0, minor 2, moderate 5, major 10, extreme 20. Boon is the only limit on rewards (no count cap). Only Weaver rewards count: NPCs' own template gear does not. All numbers are *placeholders*, to tune after playtests. The Weaver's item catalog lists each item's boon.
- **Equip requirements** (Ali, 08/11/2026, EQ1–EQ13; `src/ruleset/requirements.ts`). Each weapon, armor and shield has an equipment level from EQUIP\_LEVELS (Crude, Common, Fine, Superior, Masterwork), which asks for EQUIP\_NEED true skill (0/25/50/75/100, *placeholder*). The skill comes from the item's gear class (`gearClass`, a GC id in the library `gearClasses` table): the class sets which skill an item asks for and the level sets how much, so a new item only names a class and a level. The classes are light, medium and heavy melee and ranged weapons, improvised weapons, light, medium and heavy armor, and shields; an item with no class asks for nothing. Each class is tagged by use (`use`: melee, ranged, improvised, armor or shield), so a melee weapon stays melee gear even when thrown (Ali, 10/11/2026); the QE2 melee/ranged damage factor reads this tag. True skill counts, and a stand-in counts at its usual share (EQ3). A minted item of quality 3 or 4 is one level above its base, up to Masterwork (EQ11). Common: dagger, shortbow, gambeson, round shield, improvised weapons; Fine: arming sword, spear, hidden blade, longbow, mail hauberk; Superior: greataxe, crossbow, plate harness. Anyone can equip anything (EQ2); every roll with an item you fall short on is one outcome step worse per EQUIP\_SHORT\_STEP (10) points short or part of it (EQ12 c), and worn armor you fall short on reaches Dodge and stealth rolls. Points above the requirement do nothing for now (AR4 d, Ali 09/11/2026). An armor skill grows from hits (AR5): a hit on a part your worn armor covers marks the armor skill for its weight when the attacker's true skill or the armor's requirement is within MARK\_WINDOW of your raw or true armor skill (either one, Ali 09/11/2026).
- **Containers** (Ali, 13/11/2026, Containers and Filching CT1–CT7; `src/engine/containers.ts`). A container is an item that holds items (ItemDef.container): a chest or crate (IT44, IT45, fixed: it stands in a zone and is never picked up), a sack (IT43, carried or set down) and a pack (IT42, worn). ItemInstance.contents holds what is inside, owner whose it is, open whether its lid stands open, and lock, grade, beyond and work a lock like a barrier's (BA2).
  - **Opening** (CT3 a). The action open, within grip reach (NEEDS open: grip), is the turn's action: the container stands open and its contents show in every view of it. Taking an item out is a pick-up (no from needed). A locked one opens with a key naming it (ItemInstance.opens) and stays shut without one; the action pick on the container's id picks its lock exactly as on a way (Lockpicking at its grade, default minor). One's own carried sack is always open to one.
  - **Theft** (CT4 a, CT5 a). Opening, picking or taking from a container whose owner (else its carrier) is someone else, up and not yielded to the taker, is theft. Theft waits for the Filching contest (FL3, and the noise and hearing contest asked on the page as TN1–TN4), so for now it is refused (wasted, reason theft). A downed owner's containers open freely, and so does the pack of one yielded to the taker (its stowed items only; held and worn gear stays theirs).
  - **Worn packs** (CT7 b; CT7b a recommended, awaiting Ali). A worn pack (equippedIn pack) holds its wearer's stowed items: they are its contents, with no list of its own. Every new character starts wearing a plain pack (starterKit). Taking a pack off its downed or yielded wearer moves their stowed items into it; one who wears no pack puts a picked-up pack on, and what it held joins their stowed items. Another pack is carried inside the one worn, its contents kept. Others see a worn pack (where pack); what it holds shows only once it is opened. No capacity limit (CT6 a, D-84).
  - **On screen** (Ali, 13/11/2026). The Inventory lists a carried container's own row with "Holds 3 items" or "Empty", and each one that holds something gets its own group, "In your sack", listing what is inside with nested containers' contents after them by path ("Sack › Healing draught"). Clicking one shows where it is and Take it out, which drafts "I take the healing draught out of my sack" in the Play box (a pick-up; nothing is sent until Send; src/ui/pages.ts insideOf, kit.ts playDraft). A worn pack's contents are the stowed items the table already lists. Clicking a loose item while a container is carried shows Put in, one button per carried container kind ("Put it in your sack"), which drafts "I put the bandage in my sack" in the Play box (a stow into it; nothing is sent until Send; src/ui/pages.ts putInto). A container never offers to go into another, and something already inside one offers Take it out instead. The same orders come by drag and drop on the Inventory table (src/ui/pages.ts dropOrder): drag a loose item onto a carried container's row, its "In your …" group or anything already inside it to put it in; drag something inside a container anywhere else in the pack to take it out. A drop fills the Play box and opens Play; nothing is sent until Send. Moving an item from one container straight to another is two orders (take it out first). Container capacity is parked as D-254.
  - **The Weaver** (CT2 a). A plan's containers list places each one (key, item, carriedBy or groundIn, owner, locked with grade and keys); a bad one is dropped with a note (container\_repaired), never failing the plan. Rewards fill it with place {in: key}; into a worn pack they go to its wearer's stowed items. The container itself costs no Boon. Its catalog items say container: worn, fixed or carried.
- **Needs data.** Every story item blueprint (minted or improvised) is listed on the GM page under Needs data, so the designer can give it its own data and promote it.

QUALITY\_TABLE (*placeholder*): per tier 1/2/3/4, weapon hitAdj +2/+4/+6/+8 or damageAdj +1/+2/+3/+4 (the variant names which); armor reduction +1/+1/+2/+2; tool skill bonus +5/+10/+15/+20. Highest tier allowed by the scenario's gain grade: minor 1, moderate 2, major 3, extreme 4.

### 8. Agents

- **Requests.** Agent Parser, Agent Planner, Agent Responder and Agent Voice share the same gear fields: action.item, action.mode, and a gear list of free ops. Code minds choose gear in code. The legality check rejects an item the actor doesn't hold or can't see and a mode the item lacks.
- **Own gear in views.** The full inventory by name and kind, where each item is equipped, mode ids, class and weight labels. No values.
- **Others' gear in views.** Only on actors perceived fully: held items, shield and armor by public look; accessories only when tagged visible; concealed items only once revealed; stowed items only once a search finds them (SE11). Items on the ground of perceived zones, by name, except hidden ones the viewer doesn't know of (SE8).
- **Agent Narrator.** Describes gear by public name and look only: the weapon, armor and shield named in the roll packet, item\_dropped and give events, visible gear of perceived actors. Its fact check already rejects item names outside the view.
- **Agent Weaver.** Rewards are typed (Part 2, Agent Weaver): an item id or a minted variant, a quantity, and a place (carried by a cast actor, or on the ground of a planned zone). NPC loadouts come from their templates' loadout field \[Proposed\]. Loot is gear in place: a downed actor keeps its inventory, and pick up takes from it.

### 9. Threadbound items (D-218)

Asked by Ali (12/11/2026): unique, soul-bound gear whose abilities open through achievements. The name stays **Threadbound** (TB8). **Relics** (Ali, TB1) are unique items that are not Threadbound. Threadbound items get grand, chuunibyou, anime, or cool and edgy names (Ali, TB2, TB10). Their Rank is a plain number because they can reach divine power (Ali).

- **Three names kept apart** (TB8, Ali 12/11/2026). *Threadbound item* is the kind of gear (`ItemDef.threadbound`, all its code in `src/ruleset/threadboundItems.ts`, functions named …ThreadboundItem…). *Threadbounded* is the class (`CL.threadbounded`). *The Threadbound* is the Start that uses that class (ST4, `StartDef.name`). Comments and text always say which one. Items and perks may give the same or similar abilities.
- **Rank** (TB11). Every Threadbound item has a Rank, I to VI for now (`THREADBOUND_RANKS` in scales; adding a rank means adding a row).

- **One of a kind.** An item with `threadbound` exists once in the world. The Weaver's reward of one is dropped while any actor carries it or it lies on any ground, and it is never minted (tidyRewards).
- **Binding** (TB6, Ali 12/11/2026). Threadbound items are the player's alone: nobody else can wear one (wear\_acc refuses), and one bound to the player stays bound (`boundTo`). The player can **summon** their own from wherever it is, at any time: a free gear op (`summon`; PlayerView lists `summonableThreadboundItems`), and the item lands worn if a slot for it is free, else stowed (TB12).
- **How one is had** (TB7). The Threadbound start (class Threadbounded, tier 4 \[TB11\], 100 points like every Start, level 1) has the level 1 perk What Was Always Yours. It gives, once, a random Threadbound item of Rank IV or higher not yet in the world, bound and worn (seeded by the story id). The Weaver placing one is parked (D-220): its rewards still drop any that is already in the world, and the hand-set Boon stays 20.
- **Abilities.** Each grant names a perk and an achievement. While the owner wears it, the perk is theirs (source item) once they have earned the achievement; a level a higher one replaced still counts. Code adds and removes these perks at the end of every turn (syncThreadboundItemPerks). An active ability is not a move (Ali, 13/11/2026): it fires by itself on the owner's next essence quality roll once its cooldown (game minutes) has passed, and then starts the cooldown again. Only one fires per roll, the longest cooldown first (`fireEssenceAuto`, `PerkDef.essenceAuto`).
- **On screen** (Ali, 13/11/2026: show what unlocks each power, hide the power itself). The item's stats card (hover on Equipped or the pack, and the Inventory panel; src/ui/pages.ts itemRows) adds: Threadbound, its Rank and whom it is bound to; Powers, "1 of 6 awake"; then one row per grant: a sealed one reads "A hidden power · wakes with “Fortune's Favourite Accident”: an event roll at Critical Success+" (the achievement's name and what it asks, earnedAtText), a woken one names its perk ("Loose Odds · woken by …"), since it is the owner's perk from then on. One bound to someone else shows only its Rank. An achievement that asks for a tier past Critical Success or Failure reads as that tier's name everywhere ("an event roll at Critical Failure−−"), not as a count of steps.
- **Varo's Loop** (earring, accessory, visible, Rank IV). Named for Varo, the god of extreme fortunes in this world (TB9; lore in the lore thread). It is called Varo's Loop once you know it, and "a small hoop earring of dark silver, worn smooth, stamped with the coin of Varo, god of extreme fortunes" (its look) by anyone who sees it. The player's own gear reaches the parser and narrator with each item's look, so a worn earring is never taken for a weapon or a ring (BG-157). Every ability widens the event roll both ways and never moves its middle. Numbers live on the perk rows (placeholders):

| Ability | Kind | Opens with | Effect |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Loose Odds | passive | Fate Meant It as a Curse | the 5 naturals just inside each critical range shift a tier outward (no tiers opened: 91–95 raise one, 06–10 drop one; each opened tier moves them 5 further in) |
| Twin Abyss | passive | Fate Has a Sense of Humour | one more tier past Critical Success and one below Critical Failure |
| Call the Toss | active, once per game day, fires by itself | Fortune's Favourite Accident | an essence quality roll uses two dice and keeps the one farther from 50.5 |
| Pull to the Edges | passive | Cursed by Coincidence | the die counts 1.5 × as far from 50.5 for the margin; modifiers unchanged |
| All or Nothing | active, once per 7 game days, fires by itself (before Call the Toss) | The Joke Is on Fate | an essence quality roll is Critical Success on 51–100 (a rare essence) and Critical Failure on 01–50 (it fades) |
| Further Still | passive | Fate's Favourite Punchline | one more tier past each Critical, on top of Twin Abyss |

- **Scaling.** The event roll has no skill, so none of this changes at skill 50, 75, 100 or 200; that is by design (the roll stays random). Opened tiers have no ceiling. Loose Odds follows the critical range as opened tiers widen it (EVENT\_CRIT\_NATURALS), so it never gets swallowed.

Every bug is numbered (see Bug log): the game files a new report (bugs/new-\<time>), and Claude numbers each real bug at triage, bugs/BG-\<n> from one counter (bugs\_meta/counter), status open until a build thread marks it fixed or wont\_fix (src/save/bugs.ts). Not lethal: one survival roll, roll() with no skill and no modifiers; Near Miss or better survives and wakes in a safe place at Critical, Failure or worse ends the story the same way. Built 09/11/2026 (SP12, Part 2 Lethal): the fight is lethal when an active actor hostile to the player belongs to a lethal band (and, since 13/11/2026, perceives the player); the safe place is the first zone of a new place the Weaver plans (target.wake). Capture and rescue stay deferred |

Levels are linked (AchievementDef.after): earning a higher level switches the lower ones off, so only the highest is checked. | D-135 | Achievement rewards round 2 | 1, 2 | Built | Decided by Ali (09/11/2026, reward variations page T13, T15, T16, T17, T19). T13 Master of the Moment ({group}): stand-ins on rolls of a skill in that group count +0.2 share (`standIn`). T15 Adept of {tree}: equip requirements on that tree's skills ask 5 less (`reqLower`); only the check moves, never the item's numbers (kit bonus still reads the item's own need). T17 new chain Few Against Many (1) / Hold the Line (10): fights won beside at least one ally while the foes outnumber your whole side; Hold the Line gives allies fighting beside you +10 on morale checks (`rally`). T19 Seasoned: +10% healing on each rest (`restPct`), carried up to Old Blood. Rule (Ali): rewards and perks are written as + amounts (+10%), never as "from x% to y%", so they never conflict. |
