| D-153 | Free levels | 1, 2 | Built | Asked by Ali (10/11/2026). A free level is a level-up that costs no XP. Achievements (AchievementDef.freeLevels, carried up a chain like every reward) and perks (PerkDef.freeLevels, while owned and active) give them. Owed = earned − Progression.freeLevelsTaken. They are taken like any level (into the class the next level goes to, with its perk points and class perks), before XP, and bank while no class has room. Ali chose (10/11/2026) that free levels never raise XP costs: the XP for the next level counts only levels not taken free (xpToNext). The tiers chain gives +1, +2, +3, +4, +5 by place (+15 in all at Every Height Climbed). |
| D-154 | NPC speech rolls at the player | 1, 2 | Partly built | Decided by Ali (10/11/2026, OQ-29 page https://claude.ai/artifact/KpDQrP5S86A2sQPRphL1vZ, NT1–NT20). An NPC run by Claude may speak with persuade, deceive, intimidate or barter aimed at the player (its plain talk never rolls; code-run NPCs never do it); when the player hears it, it rolls the type's skill and the player answers with the same skill, or Insight against a lie (pushContest, src/engine/speech.ts). One rule works both ways (NT4, applySpeech): a listener that loses to intimidation is Shaken toward the speaker, −SHAKEN_PER_TIER (5) × the tier gap on every roll against them; to persuasion Swayed, −SWAYED_PER_TIER (5) × the gap, also on every roll against them (NT15: a persuaded fighter can be talked into lowering the sword); both last until the listener's engagement ends, and the bigger one stands. A listener that beats a lie gets a tell (the Narrator's pressed.took for the player, the impression for an NPC); barter with a Trader sets prices as PY6. The Narrator gets how it struck the player in words (pressed); the speaker's view says how the player took it when it can see them (NT10). Easy Speaker covers the player as listener. NT3: each earlier try of the same kind (persuade, deceive, intimidate, barter; never plain talk) at the same listener this engagement gives the speaker −REPEAT_TRY (15) more, added up (Repeat try). NT16–NT18: a Trickery tree (TR14) with Lying (SK31, influence, speaking races) that deceive rolls; Insight (SK32, Perception's third skill, Notice group, no function so nothing stands in) answers lies; only the shady thief template has Lying. NT19: haggling stays one check both ways (no change). NT20: Shaken and Swayed show as chips on the status strip and sheet. LI4 (Ali, 10/11/2026): every lie can be caught however it is told: the Planner, Responder and Voice give any line they know is false as deceive, never talk (PUSH_RULE), and the Parser gives the player's lies as deceive (rule 19). Waiting: NT14, the intimidation aura (Design perks) and its morale side (morale thread, MB15) |

Persuading, intimidating and haggling between NPCs stay banked |

| D-163 | All-50 from any three skills of a tree | 1, 2 | Built | Decided by Ali: an All-50 is one part per skill plus one passive that fits the tree. Once any three skills of a tree reach 50 (CONFIG.ALL_STEP_SKILLS), the tree's passive and those skills' parts are granted; a fourth or later skill reaching 50 adds its own part. So a tree can have more than three skills, some behind unlock conditions. Each skill's part belongs to the skill (SKILL_PARTS in src/library/allPerks.ts), so a skill that moves to another tree takes its part along. The mastery achievements use the same three-skill rule. Trees with fewer than three skills (Defense, Fortitude and the one-skill trees) earn no All-50 for now. The part-to-skill mapping is Claude's first proposal, for Ali to adjust. |
| D-164 | Skill tree ratings | 1, 2 | Built | Decided by Ali: skill trees have a rating on the TREE_RATINGS scale (Inferior, Normal, Superior, Legendary; TreeDef.rating, default Normal). For now the rating only sets how many of a tree's skills at 50 earn its All-50 passive, its skills' parts and its mastery achievement (CONFIG.ALL_STEP_SKILLS): never for Inferior, 3 for Normal, 2 for Superior, 1 for Legendary. Every existing tree is Normal (Ali said "common"), except Slime senses, which is Inferior. |
| D-165 | Mastery achievement lines by tree rating | 1, 2 | Built | Asked by Ali (10/11/2026): each kind of All-50 (tree rating) has its own achievement line, grander the higher the rating. Legendary: Chosen of {tree}, {tree} Incarnate, The Heavens Kneel to {tree}. Same steps (50/100/150), reward levels (II/III/IV) and rewards on every line. Inferior trees earn none. |

Insight 100 Read Their Face (the stance toward the owner of anyone who spoke to it this scenario shows in its view). Not Born Yesterday parked (D-187). Insight 20 still open: Ali asked for more options. Perk moves now tell the Parser what they look like (PERK_MOVES.does), after a parser test showed "Behind you!" alone was not read as Bluff. |

| D-169 | Auras: Dread Presence | 1, 2 | Built | Ali's answers in the Intimidation aura doc (https://claude.ai/artifact/S5a9Zwiu7cu3pzpQ2gMoPi) and the morale page (MB19, MB36–MB39). New functions engagement_aura and offensive_aura in a new Aura group. Dread Presence is the Intimidation 100 perk and the first engagement aura (src/engine/auras.ts, AURAS table): at the start of each turn, each target opposed or hostile to the owner and aware of it, within CONFIG.AURA_REACH_M (8 m, today's one link) by distM (RD1: the aura skill will set the size once RD9's formula is chosen; D-193), checks once per engagement. The owner's Intimidation is rolled against the target's Resolve (its morale roll, renown included). A win leaves the target Shaken toward the owner (−5 × gap, the bigger Shaken stands) and costs it FR.dread_presence, 10 morale per tier. Both skills mark. NPCs can own it. Battle Cry moves to Intimidation 140 as the offensive aura; Ali wants it buffed (ideas in the doc). |
| D-170 | Parry and the Block rework | 1, 2, 3 | Built | Asked by Ali (10/11/2026), answered PA1–PA12 in the Parry doc. Parry (SK35) is a new Combat – Defense skill (the tree has three skills again): a held melee weapon parries a melee blow, and Block is shields only. Parry's gap ladder cuts less than Block at small gaps (+1 ×0.85, +2 ×0.65), stops the blow fully from +3 (outcome parried) and punishes failures harder (0 ×1, −1 ×1.1, −2 ×1.25, −3 ×1.5, −4 ×2, −5 ×2.5; placeholders). A parry at PARRY_STRIKE_GAP (+3) or better gives the defender one free melee attack back with the parrying weapon (a fist after a barehanded parry), once per turn; a free attack can earn one too, since once per turn ends any chain. Parry can't stop a ranged attack: the defense falls back to the usable one with the highest effective skill, as does a standing defense whose item is missing. Heavy weapons are −1 gap against Parry as against Block. Dodge, Block and Parry never stand in for each other. Riposte augments Parry at full share; Slip Counter is renamed Blade Dance and augments Dodge at full share. Answer the Blow (Riposte 60) brings the strike 2 gaps sooner with a medium weapon (ANSWER_THE_BLOW_DROP); Step and Cut is unchanged. Turn the Blade, Guard Drill and Steady Guard move from Block to Parry (Steady Guard's roll bonus needs a medium weapon); Blade Wall is the Parry 60, Shield Wall the Block 60 and Slip the Crowd the Dodge 60 (no outnumbered penalty). Open-Hand Guard parries barehanded. Cover Ally stays a Block perk (a shield). Swordsman unlocks at and floors Medium Weapons 40 + Parry 40 (story: blade against blade); Guard unlock takes Block, Dodge or Parry 30; the recommended build uses Parry, so the recommended swordsman starts without a shield; the village guard carries a round shield and blocks (Ali, 13/11/2026). Parry 20 is parked (PA13), and Parry's All-50 part is not designed yet. |

Insight's part is parked (PT3); Vanishing (PT4) and Resolve (PT5) went back to Ali with a coding note and new options. Battle Cry (Intimidation 140, offensive aura) is redesigned, not yet built: every foe that perceives the Loud roar, at any level and any distance, rolls Resolve against the owner's Intimidation and loses 10 morale per tier on a loss; every ally that perceives it gets 10 morale back and loses any Shaken. |

Scars push their own way, never just a weaker copy (AP3). Unaware defeats Honesty −; yielded foes Pride +; talked down Temper −; enemy to friend Temper − Sociability +; outnumbered, held the line, stronger foes won, won while panicking Nerve +; flawless Discipline +; speech won Sociability +, and Honesty − when earned with Lying (`personalityByKind`, AP12); deals Honesty +; quests and early quests Discipline +; unpaid quest Pride −; zones visited Curiosity +; cruel spars Temper +; abandoned quests scar Honesty −; morale breaks scars Nerve −. Losing scars (fights and spars lost) Pride − (AP18). None: fights won without a blow (AP8 turned down: even a wild person can win with a battle cry), survival chains (AP10), morale lost (AP20). |

| D-211 | Event-roll criticals open tiers past Critical | 1, 3 | Built | Asked by Ali (11/11/2026): achievements for Critical Success and scars for Critical Failure on the event roll, each opening the next tier past that Critical, with a rule that lets the opened tiers be reached; flavor on the irony of fate. Chains (Ali): each level is earned by reaching once the tier the level below opened. Successes: Fate Meant It as a Curse (Critical Success, I), Fortune's Favourite Accident (Critical Success+, II), The Joke Is on Fate (Critical Success++, III). Scars: Fate Has a Sense of Humour (Critical Failure, 0), Cursed by Coincidence (Critical Failure−, I), Fate's Favourite Punchline (Critical Failure−−, II). Each level opens one more tier on event rolls (summed along the chain). Reach (Ali picked A): each tier opened widens the natural critical range by CONFIG.EVENT_CRIT_NATURALS (5), the top block being the highest open tier: one opened, 96–100 Critical Success+ and 91–95 Critical Success; two, 96–100 ++, 91–95 +, 86–90 Critical Success. Each opened tier takes the original Critical's 5%; Critical or better grows 5% per tier. Failures mirror from 01. Without an unlock the roll is the normal table. Mechanism (general, Ali in the features thread): `tierUnlocks: [{ roll: RollKind or 'any', side: up or down, steps }]` on PerkDef and AchievementDef, read by `tierReach(actor, roll)`; only the event roll reads it for now. A doubled natural edge was built and taken back the same day (Ali had not asked for it). Levels are placeholders. Skill rolls (Ali, 11/11/2026, "keep the extra band"): an unlock scoped to a roll kind (hit, dodge, block, parry, perception, stealth, initiative) or to any roll adds, per tier opened, a margin band CONFIG.TIER\_EXTRA\_BAND (50) wider past the end of the table (Critical Success+ from margin 125, Critical Failure− below −125); skill rolls still go past the ends by natural edges and tier shifts as before (`extraBands`, src/ruleset/index.ts). |

Inworld takes free-form acting directions per line and costs about $25 per 1M characters (about 1 cent per agent line). Options: a keep parked; b buy credits and switch the RPG Voice relay back to Inworld (a code swap in the Worker only); c offer both behind the same connector. |
| D-213 | The roll ceiling past skill 100 | 1 | Parked | Parked by Ali (11/11/2026, CR1 and CR2): "extra tiers of success, perks, and others will change the landscape of the game as we move forwards." A roll is d100 + 0.5 × skill + modifiers − 63.5 on five margin bands, so past about 200 both sides of a contest land Critical Success: equal skills tie 24/31/33/36/44/81% at 50/75/100/150/200/300, and a 50-point lead wins 54/54/53/45/36/9%. Options: a shared frame, both sides past FRAME\_FLOOR (100, or 50: CR2) drop by the weaker side's points past it before rolling (ties stay 33% and a 50-point lead wins 53% at every level past 100; 300 vs 200 wins 68%); b on a shared top tier the higher margin wins (SE22 for every contest; 300 vs 200 wins 82%, by one tier only); c bands past Critical open for everyone (undoes D-211's earned tiers); d ratio frame, both divided by the weaker skill / 100 (a 50-point lead wins 40% at 300). Recommended: a. Noted from the D-238 study (OE4, Ali: ok): a skill-50 defender still matches a skill-300 blow about 6% of the time through its own natural 96 to 100, and that floor is kept; an opened tier on a skill roll pays only once the margin can reach its band (the best margin is 36.5 + half the skill), so none below about skill 177, two at 300, one more per 100 skill. |
| D-214 | Loudness levels past Deafening | 2 | Parked | Parked by Ali (11/11/2026, CR10). NOISE tops at Deafening, 120 dB at 1 m. Hearing is in metres now (BG-57), so no cap stands in the way. Visibility tops at Glaring the same way. Options: a add all five rows; b add up to Cataclysmic 194; c keep parked. |

| D-218 | Soul-bound unique gear ("artifacts") | 5 | Partly built | Parked by Ali (12/11/2026): "park artifacts as an idea (unique soul bound equipment)". Then built in part (Ali, 12/11/2026: one Threadbound item, an earring whose abilities open through achievements and widen event rolls): Part 5 §9. Built: 1 binding on first wear, 2a, 4 nothing in other hands; achievements as the deeds. Still open: 3 growth, 5 loss and death, 6 trade. TB6 (Ali): only the player owns them, and can summon them at any time. Weaver placement parked as D-220. **Name** [Proposed]: "artifact" already means the Claude pages this project publishes, so call them **Threadbound** in game and code (bound by Thread, the lore of XP); alternates Relic or Heirloom. **Options.** 1 Binding: a a deed binds it (an achievement-style trigger, e.g. first defeat with it or surviving a stronger foe with it); b the wielder pays Thread (XP) to bind it; c it is made for one person and born bound. 2 Uniqueness: a one of a kind in the world, never minted twice (its own item id, no quality roll); b a limit per person (e.g. one bound piece per gear class); c both. 3 Growth: a its need tracks the wielder's true skill in its gear class, so the existing quality levers grow with them (hit bonus = QUALITY\_HIT\_FACTOR × need × quality: at quality 4 that is +8 at 50, +12 at 75, +16 at 100, +24 at 150, +32 at 200, no cap); b it grows by deeds done with it (defeats, achievements), unlocking named parts like perks; c fixed when bound. Growing by quality alone runs out at quality 4 (QUALITY\_TABLE has four rows), so b or c needs new rows past it. 4 In other hands: a anyone else wields it as Crude (need 0, quality 1); b it refuses them (cannot be equipped); c it works fully for anyone and binding only adds growth. 5 Loss and death: a it stays bound and the owner can always find or call it back (a reach in real metres, per the distance rule); b on the owner's death the bond breaks and it goes dormant until someone binds it again (keeps or resets its growth); c it is buried with them. Permanent NPCs can own one too (same rule as achievements), which waits on D-148. 6 Trade: a cannot be sold; b sells at its summed-benefit value (SL12) but the bond breaks. Scaling: 3a has no ceiling and matters past 100 because it rides the wielder's skill; 3b needs an open-ended parts list (a part every N points, like perks every 20). Ties in with D-90 (crafting, a bound piece could be forged), the Weaver's Boon budget (a Threadbound reward would be a big boon) and gear classes (it names class + level like any gear). No banked library rows: the item library has no banked rows yet. Recommended: name Threadbound, 1a, 2a, 3a, 4a, 5b keeping growth, 6a. Ali (13/11/2026, FS8): "we will make threadbound items that level up"; no penalty for changing items is needed, "since getting threadbound items is hard as is". |

| D-221 | Refund when an All-X perk reuses a skill's milestone perk | 1, 3 | Parked | Raised by Ali (12/11/2026), no build now: an All-X perk (tree passive or skill part) might one day be the same perk as a skill's milestone perk. If so, a perk point spent on that milestone perk is refunded. The use case is a perk that unlocks an ability, for example parrying ranged projectiles: a skill's milestone perk and a tree's All-X could both grant it. One perk with several ways in already exists: Every Sense One is the All-150 part of both Eyesight and Earshot (D-197), granted once by whichever comes first; both ways are free, so nothing is refunded there. Today no All-X perk reuses a ladder perk. grantAllSteps skips a perk the actor already owns, so the spent point would stay spent, and canTake refuses a ladder perk already granted by an All-X. Options to settle when it is built: (a) when the refund applies: only when the All-X grant arrives after the point was spent (recommended), or also block taking it once the All-X is near; (b) what is refunded: the one perk point (recommended), plus any milestone gating it counted toward (a ladder perk counts toward 60/100/140 gating; the refunded perk should still count, since it is still owned); (c) the owned row: switch its source from 'level' to 'mastery' so undo and the refund cannot repeat (recommended); (d) the player is told with a log line and a screen note. |

| D-257 | Build design principles (Barbarian novel study) | 1, 4 | Agreed | Ali (13/11/2026, Barbarian novel study, https://claude.ai/artifact/97hDbTps7FetGoiEuvdoA1, round 7). **Adopted as standing rules for perks, classes, essences and gear.** A build is a chain: fuel, multiplier, threshold, payoff; name the four links when a tree or class is designed (FP2). A piece earns its place by its rule; points alone are the failed build (FP3). No new hard caps: if something can run away, give it a type so the halving series applies (FP5; review of today's caps is D-275). Room is the currency, and any new source of slots, perks or free picks says what it costs or pushes out; no free respec (FP7). One copy of each rule: a repeated trait counts once (FP8). Every build has a named counter that the world carries; "ok but mark this one for further review as we play" (FP9). Gear can be the core of a build (FP11). Past skill 100 a new piece brings a rule, since points alone stop mattering; "every essence is required to have a rule, it's just not built yet" (FP12). A piece is worth its power times how often it works, so narrow pieces get more power: "narrow perks should be equally powerful" (KB15, SY2). New perks key to a function or group by default (SY2). **Exceptions** (FS20): a rare, very limited piece may be a plain best choice: a Threadbound item, a 140 perk, an essence granted by a mythical or divine being. **Luck** (FS21): a build that chooses to be swingy may be; luck traits are allowed. **Achievements side:** growth comes from new firsts, not more rungs (DP7); hidden rewards and finds are claimed in the world (DP10); a first is recorded once and the world changes with it (DP11). **Not adopted:** a rule against counting perks in the power rating (FP1), thresholds-only small rewards (FP4), a stated price on every strong piece as a rule (FP6), "a build must stand with its tricks off" (FP10). **Turned down as mechanics:** XP only on the first kill of a kind (FS22), hard tops on stacking (FS23), a removal price that doubles per removal (FS24), essences as points only (FS25). **Side lanes** (DP9b b, Ali 14/11/2026): achievement rewards live in side lanes by default (max morale, death's door, rest, rally, renown, nerve); a row may pay true skill when the deed itself is about that skill, as Breaker of Wills does, and there is no limit. **Round 9** (Ali, 14/11/2026, after discussing his "tbd" items). **Counters** (FS2b): withdrawn by Ali (14/11/2026); no tree or perk carries a "beaten by" or "strong against" line, and the questionnaire for them is set aside. **Advice, not a rule** (FP13b b): a flat reward fades past 100; if it is meant to matter there, write it as a share. **Lane guide** (DP8b a): an ordeal pays what would have carried you through it (max morale, death's door, rest), a feat done with a skill may pay that skill, a named foe pays an answer to what that foe did, and a place pays its own theme; it guides whoever proposes rows and bans nothing. **No principle** on rewards that multiply a whole build (DP12b c): the parked blessing D-289 is enough. **Still open with Ali:** nothing. |
| D-258 | High perks that read a skill instead of adding a flat number | 1 | Built | **Built** (SH1-SH7, page https://claude.ai/artifact/M7LAeim1yQEV2nMNvRjEH9). The 40 and 80 perks keep +5 in their own skill (SH3 a). A 120 perk adds HIGH\_PERK\_SHARE (0.05) × its own skill's true skill as true skill instead of +5, so it still counts for 140, All-X steps and power; its group spread is +1 and HIGH\_PERK\_SPREAD (0.01) × its own skill's true skill (SH1, SH4; read as 1% of the perk's own skill). Shares read true skill without shares, so they never feed each other, and several shares on one skill stack by the halving rule (SH7 b, to be confirmed in play). The one extra number: an 80 perk's is stronger than a 40's, a 120's stronger than an 80's (SH5). All-50 pieces stay flat (SH6, D-302). Earlier: Ali (13/11/2026, FS1): "40 perk stays as is, to be considered for 80 perk, certain for 120 perk, an option for other perks." Today the 40/80/120 perks add +5 to their own skill, which is 10% of the skill at 50 and 3% at 150. **Suggested method.** The 120 perk's own-skill part becomes a share of true skill through the augment form that exists (PerkDef.augments), sized so it equals +5 near skill 100 and grows past it; the +1 to same-function skills and the pick stay. Ask the 80 perk on the same page. Belongs to the perk thread, on its own questions page. Scaling: a 0.05 share is +2.5 at 50, +3.75 at 75, +5 at 100, +7.5 at 150 [Proposed placeholder]. |
| D-259 | Link rows for loops the perk list already holds | 1 | Built | **Built** (LR1-LR5 a, perk link rows page https://claude.ai/artifact/RUiBRRvQxCc7nPWTbUJjtJ): four grants, LK2-LK5. Ghost Blade (Hidden Strike + Strike and Fade): Hidden Strike's +1 also on a target suspicious of the owner, once per target per engagement. Combination (Set Them Up + One-Two): the mark One-Two's free Strike leaves gives +2 instead of +1 (PerkMark.value). Blind Spot (Dirt in the Eyes + Melt Away): while a foe's sight is spoiled by Dirt in the Eyes, Melt Away needs no cover or shade; observers still contest. Bulwark (Shield Wall + Cover Ally): Cover Ally covers melee attacks too. Merges stay for later (LR5). Marks: the same perk's mark on one target never stacks (a new one replaces it); different perks' tier shifts add in full. Showing linked perks on the play character sheet is asked of the interface thread (LR5). Earlier: Ali (13/11/2026, FS3 "ok"): four candidate pairs for PERK_LINKS, beside the one row built (Dread Presence + Overwhelm): Hidden Strike + Strike and Fade; Set Them Up + One-Two; Dirt in the Eyes + Melt Away; Shield Wall + Cover Ally. **Suggested method.** One questions page from the perk thread with two or three options for what each link grants; a granted effect shifts an outcome or takes a share of true skill so it still counts past 150. |
| D-260 | Payoff perks that count matching pieces | 1 | Built | Ali's CN1-CN8 answers ("Payoff perks that count pieces" page, 14/11/2026). **Themes** (CN1 b, CN2): perks carry theme tags, as many as needed, kept in one table keyed by theme (src/library/perkThemes.ts): ambush, counter, guard, shout, trick, shadow. **Pieces** (CN3 a): every owned perk with the tag, whatever its source, counted once; quirks are counted the same way (PerkDef.quirk). Dormant perks count for now (D-310). **Share** (CN4 a, CN9 d = RN4 a): a payoff perk's share is PAYOFF\_SHARE (0.05) × pieces^PAYOFF\_EXP (0.8), *placeholders*, by the one power rule (5%, 8.7%, 12%, 15.2%, 18.1% for 1 to 5 pieces; no ceiling), through the augment equation (PerkDef.augments payoff; themeCount and payoffShare in src/ruleset/perks.ts). A piece adds nothing on its own: the count only grows payoff perks and is meant as a condition later perks, classes and achievements can ask for (CN4). **Slots** (CN5 a): 100 and 140 ladder perks. **Patient Knife** (Combat Stealth 100, CN6 a, as an augment): Combat Stealth augments every attack roll made while stealthy (RollRequest.when from\_hiding, set by the attack), by ambush pieces, itself included (a payoff perk carries its own theme); it learns from those rolls. **Protagonist Mentality** (Resolve 60, CN7 a; on 140 because Overwhelm holds Resolve 100): Resolve augments its own rolls, by quirks owned. **Chorus** (Intimidation 120, CN8; Ali picked 120 because Battle Cry holds 140): each shout piece, Chorus included, adds CHORUS\_REACH\_M (1 m) of engagement-aura reach in full, and the base reach grows by the payoff share of those pieces (auraReachM in src/engine/auras.ts). Earlier: Ali (13/11/2026, KB4): "I love this idea". |
| D-261 | Tiered traits that read true skill past the last milestone | 1 | Parked | Ali (13/11/2026, FS9 "later"). Milestones have perks written only to 140 (the series is open past it, CS11); past that only All-150 parts read true skill, and essence points are the only way up. **Suggested method.** An essence trait or a 140 perk with tiers on true skill at 150, 200, 250 and so on (one table, rows added freely), each tier a new effect, not a bigger number. Design it with D-229 (traits) and D-213 (the roll ceiling past 100). |
| D-262 | Carrying a trait onto better points | 1 | Parked | Ali (13/11/2026, FS10 "later"). Replacing an essence loses it whole, so a 4-point early essence with a loved trait blocks an 18-point one. **Suggested method.** When an offer replaces a held essence of the same source table, the player may keep the old trait and take the new points, at the removal price; or a rare service does it (D-250). Matters from skill 100 on. |
| D-263 | Swapping an essence for a random one of the same grade | 1 | Parked | Ali (13/11/2026, FS11 "later"). **Suggested method.** A rare service or item: the held essence is given up and a new row is picked by weight from the same source table at the same grade, its points measured again; it costs the removal price. It softens bad luck without removing commitment. |
| D-264 | Party roles, and pieces that work through allies | 1, 2 | Parked | Ali (13/11/2026, FS12 "later"; KB5, KB11). Waits on companions who fight beside the player. **Suggested method.** A role tag on each class (tank, dealer, healer, scout, none); support perks multiply what an ally does as a share, never add damage of their own; healing past full may pass to a linked ally through healFromPerk; foes and scenarios may punish a missing role. How foes pick targets uses real cues (size, noise, who hurt them), not a threat number (SY10). |
| D-265 | A unique foe's essence, and a source for epic and legendary | 1, 4 | Parked | Ali (13/11/2026, FS6: "we need to suggest a method in a parked item for these consider later items, to make future discussion easier"). The quality roll tops out at rare (Critical Success), so the epic and legendary grades have multipliers but no source. **Suggested method.** A unique template (D-238) lists its own essence row with a fixed grade of epic or legendary, offered with no quality roll when that being is beaten or grants it; one per character, it cannot be let go, and it carries a quirk tied to the being (FS5). Ali allows such an essence to be a plain best choice because it is rare (FS20). Options for the rest: a epic and legendary only from unique beings; b Critical Success steps past the last tier also reach them. |

KB14 was a nice example." **Suggested method.** A few perks or essence traits at 100 or above close one kind of action for good (spells, speech, ranged; KB14) or give up a share of essence slots (FS7), in exchange for a multiplier on what remains; never a multiplier on essence points. Raise it with the action economy rework. |

| D-272 | Perks and classes that nudge personality | 1, 2 | Built | **Built** (PN1-PN6, personality nudges page https://claude.ai/artifact/PidRnFisT7d3wEoe8qmhbB): each piece moves one axis by ACH_PERSONALITY_STEP (+5) while held. A dormant perk adds none. Earlier: Ali (13/11/2026, PG6): "yes that is the intent". In the novel an essence's stats change who the holder is. Achievements already move personality one step per chain level (D-208); perks, classes and essences do not. **Suggested method.** PerkDef, ClassDef and essence rows may carry the same personality field achievements use, one step each; essence traits use it first (D-229). |
| D-273 | A power rating on the character sheet | 1, 2 | Agreed, not built | Ali (13/11/2026, FS17): "make the character rating a power rating that is visible in the detailed character sheet along with how each thing contributes to it when clicked". **Suggested method.** The detailed sheet shows power() as one number; clicking it opens the details pop-up listing each part from the same function (skill rating by group, level, gear at a quarter), with no second formula. |
| D-274 | Essences appear from every beaten foe | 1 | Agreed, not built | Ali (13/11/2026, FS18): "let's make mobs also have a 5% chance to drop an essence and notable foes have 1, so the fail comes from the essence quality roll only." **Method.** The appearance chance becomes 100 for a notable foe and 5 for any other foe; the quality roll is unchanged, so it alone decides whether a notable foe's essence fades. Each foe still rolls once, ever. |
| D-275 | A review of today's hard caps | 1 | Open | Ali (13/11/2026, FP5): "can you also request a review of current hard caps." The standing rule is no caps Ali has not stated, with the halving series doing that job. **Method.** Scan the code and CONFIG for every Math.min, clamp or MAX constant that limits a game number, list each with where it bites at skill 50, 75, 100 and 150+, and ask Ali per item: keep, remove, or replace with a falling series. |
| D-276 | Two more kinds of augment | 1 | Agreed, not built | Ali (13/11/2026). FS15: "there is something to gain here, we only have skills that add to each other, without the negative effect the original's skill checks being more difficult." SY4: "a second type of augment needs to be considered, augment that add a % based on their skill." Two kinds to design on a questions page: a trade converter (the target gains and the source's own checks get harder) and a percentage augment (the target grows by a share that follows the source skill). How two augments on one skill stack stays as built for now (SY4 "ok for now, we will see later"). **Decided** (Ali, 14/11/2026, round 8 of the study page, AK1 to AK4, all as recommended). **Trade augment** (AK1 a, AK2 a): the receiving skill gains a share of the giving skill's true skill and the giving skill's own rolls lose the same number, so points are moved and never made; it is always on while the perk or trait is owned. Placeholder share 0.5, twice a plain augment because it is paid for: a giving skill of 50 moves 25, 75 moves 37.5, 100 moves 50, 150 moves 75. **Percentage augment** (AK3 a): the receiving skill grows by (giving true skill ÷ 100) × a rate of its own true skill, read from true skill like every augment. Placeholder rate 20%: with both skills at 50 it adds 5, at 75 it adds 11, at 100 it adds 20, at 150 it adds 45. Two percentage augments on one skill stack as SY4 says (largest in full, the next at STACK_RATIO); a plain and a percentage augment are different kinds and do not halve each other. **First uses** (AK4 c): the trade kind on essence traits (D-229), the percentage kind on the 120 perks (D-258), where "+5% of its own skill" equals the old +5 at skill 100. Both extend the augment entry that exists (SkillDef.augments, PerkDef.augments), with no second augment equation. |
| D-277 | Kills counted apart from defeats | 1, 2 | Built | Ali (13/11/2026, Barbarian novel study, https://claude.ai/artifact/97hDbTps7FetGoiEuvdoA1, round 7, BA4): "kill should be counted separately from defeats with their own achievement chains." A kill is the player's blow that takes someone else to Dead (applyEffects, tally `kills`); it stays a defeat too, so both tallies move, and a foe someone else kills is not the player's kill. One chain: A Life Taken 1 (level 0), Gravefiller 10 (I), Death Walks Beside You 50 (II), AC194 to AC196, each +1 max morale (D-278). Names, counts and amounts are placeholders. **Not built.** Kill chains per body type or per race, as the defeat chains have; a personality step (Ali names the axis). In the novel the first murder, ten kills and the first player kill each pay the nerve stat and none pays XP. |
| D-278 | Ordeals pay nerve: the max morale reward | 1, 2 | Built | Ali (13/11/2026, BA1): "ok for now". In the novel all nine ordeal rows (near death, exhaustion, killing, losing a companion) pay the one stat that keeps the character alive at 0 HP, never the skill used. Rule for ordeal rows from now on: a flat max morale reward (AchievementDef.maxMorale, summed along a chain like every number reward, read by maxMorale in src/ruleset/morale.ts), never weapon or defence skill. BA10, Ali: "the second rule is not accepted": achievement rewards live in the staying-power lanes (max morale, death's door, rest, rally), but there is NO cap on the true skill achievements may give (the proposed cap of one common essence per skill was turned down). BA14 merges in here: a first companion lost also pays max morale, beside any scar, once companions can die. **The lane guide (round 9, DP8b "a").** Adopted in a softer form, as a guide for choosing the lane and not a ban: the split is between what happened to you and what you did. An ordeal (near death, a morale break, exhaustion, a loss) pays what would have carried you through it: max morale, death's door, rest. A feat done with a skill may pay that skill. A named foe pays an answer to what that foe did (D-281). A place pays its own theme (D-288). Getting hurt then never becomes a way to raise a weapon skill. It is a judgement on each row; no test checks it. |
| D-279 | The survival roll row: depth, not count | 1, 2 | Built | Ali (13/11/2026, BA15): "this is a nice idea for a row". The novel sizes one family by depth, each step about three times the last: health at 2% +1, at 0.1% +3, at 0 and back +10. Here the three depths are a fight survived from Critical (Not Today), back up after going Down (Not Done Yet), and the survival roll passed (D-54). The last now has a row: Woke Up Anyway (AC197, level II, tally `survival_rolls`), +10 max morale (*placeholder*). A foe sparing the player (D-239) is not the roll and does not earn it. **Open, for the achievement trim page.** Whether Not Today / Not Yet / Death Keeps Waiting (counts 1, 3, 5) are reshaped by depth, and whether the two shallower depths also pay max morale (+1 and +3). |
| D-280 | Reward size by reward level | 1 | Parked | Ali (13/11/2026, BA2): "I am willing to explore this idea". The size of a stat reward would come from one config table keyed by the achievement's reward level (−1 to XV), as REWARD_XP_CURVE gives XP, not from a number on each row. **Suggested method.** A table beside REWARD_XP_CURVE (for example REWARD_MORALE) with placeholders I +1, II +2, III +3, IV +5, V +8, VI +12, then +50% a level up to XV; a row asks for its level's amount, and a number on the row still wins. The novel sizes by the depth of the deed (1, 3, 10; D-279), which a level column gives when the deeper deed sits at a higher level. **Options.** a keep numbers on each row (today); b the table for max morale only; c the table for every number reward. |
| D-281 | Named-foe achievements | 1, 2 | Parked | Barbarian study BA5 (Ali, 13/11/2026: "add the note to the parked item"). Defeating a unique named NPC grants a hidden one-off achievement named after it; its reward level comes from the power gap, read from power(), the one measure shared with defeat XP, so it scales from bandit chiefs to godlike beings. **Note.** A named foe's reward answers what that foe did: in the novel the Lord of Chaos, once slain, gives resistance to the mind. **Suggested method.** One row with `by` the foe, granted on its defeat; the reward is written on the foe's own template, and it is claimed in the world (D-283). Waits on D-148. |
| D-282 | Fame, titles and standing with one group | 1, 2 | Parked | Barbarian study BA7 (Ali, 13/11/2026: "add the note to the parked item"). A deed seen by others would spread: the more witnesses, the more people know the player before meeting them. **Note.** The novel keeps three social currencies apart: fame (recognition, offers of work, a warmer first meeting, entry to closed districts), a title given by people, and merit with one temple; merit cannot be bought with fame or rank. We have a piece of each: renown (D-128), worn titles that only those who saw the player know (D-138), and Go-To for {giver}. The line "Fame +1 per witness" could not be found again in any source. **Suggested method.** Keep the three apart: fame as a count of people who hold a belief about the player from hearsay; titles as today; standing as a per-group tally of deeds done for that group that opens its services. |
| D-283 | Hidden rewards claimed in the world | 2 | Agreed, not built | Ali (13/11/2026, BA6 "ok"; principle DP10 in D-257). For a few rows the reward shows as "???" until it is claimed; the claim is a physical act at a place tied to the deed; and it can lapse if the player comes too late. For named-foe rows (D-281) and quest-line rows only. **Suggested method.** The achievement is granted with its reward held back; a quest (the quests thread owns this step) names the place and the act, with a deadline; doing it releases the reward, missing it leaves the achievement without one. |

On the Achievements page, a held level of a chain shows that a higher one exists. What it shows (the next name, its count, its reward or "???") is asked on the achievement trim page (TB5) and built with that page's display work. | D-285 | Achievement rewards that open places | 1, 2 | Agreed, not built | Ali (13/11/2026, BA11 "ok"). In the novel 3 of about 23 achievements pay a right of entry (an archive, a sealed sea, a passage). The key reward (D-125) covers classes, trees, gear and talk; a place would be a new kind of key. **Suggested method.** A reward field naming a zone or way the holder may pass (a barrier that opens for them, a guard who lets them by). Waits on zones and travel. |
| D-286 | World-firsts on a public record | 1, 2 | Parked | Ali (13/11/2026, BA12 "yep"; principle DP11 in D-257: a first is recorded once, and the world changes with it). In the novel a monument writes itself when someone does a thing for the first time ever; it names the doer and those with them; every entry goes with a change to the world for everyone (a new floor, new routes, a new island); six heroes are on it in all of history, and a repeat is never recorded. **Suggested method.** A short written list of firsts: some already held by figures of the world's history, some open. When the player takes an open one it is recorded where people can read it, those who read it learn the player's name (fame, D-282), and the list row names one change to the world. It can start before NPC earning (D-148). |
| D-287 | Wonders witnessed | 1, 5 | Agreed, not built | Ali (13/11/2026, BA16 "nice"). A short chain, x of 5, earned by seeing a hand-placed wonder; no fight needed. In the novel "Mythical Discovery" pays 10, 20, 30 for seeing a giant egg, a great spirit and a dead leviathan's heart. **Suggested method.** Five wonders written by the lore thread, each a thing in a zone; perceiving it clearly tallies the chain; the reward grows in a line. It would be the first exploration chain that is not a count of zones or of lucky event rolls. |
| D-288 | World finds | 1, 2, 5 | Parked | Ali (13/11/2026, BA17): "agreed, but can be true skill." In the novel "hidden pieces" are small permanent bonuses found in places, not earned by deeds; the hero holds 13. Each states its limit (once; up to three times; only the first five count; does not stack), is themed to where it is (the ice cave gives cold resistance, so an ice build goes there first), and is found by a method written down somewhere in the world (a book, a rumour, a guide who hunts them). **Suggested method.** A library table of finds, each with a place, what it gives (true skill allowed), its limit and how it is found; searching (the search rules) turns it up; the sheet lists finds held. For the search and loot threads. |
| D-289 | One blessing from a people | 1, 2 | Parked | Ali (13/11/2026, BA18): "parked". In the novel a deed done for a people earns one blessing: +20% on everything the hero's essences give, raised to 30% by a later find. Only one can be borne, and it has a price (a debt owed, and trouble with his own side). It is the only reward on the whole list that multiplies the build, and it comes from people, not from the system. Ali (FS20) allows rare, very limited pieces to be plain best choices. **Suggested method.** A quest-line reward, one per character for good, written as a percentage on essence points, with a standing cost named on its row. **Options.** a never; b as above; c the same shape on one skill group only. **Round 9 (DP12b "c").** No standing principle about build multipliers is adopted; this parked row is enough. A note for whoever opens it: essence points count as true skill, so a percentage on them moves perk milestones and grows with every slot (four rare essences from skill-100 foes on one skill, 72 points, gain about 14 at +20%); bearing one at a time keeps it from running away. |

| D-292 | Matched critical in a contest | 1, 2 | Built | Asked by Ali (14/11/2026): a one-time achievement for "both you and your enemy roll the same tier of critical success", which unlocks one additional layer of critical + for contest rolls. Perfect Meets Perfect (AC198, rule `matched_crits`, count 1): the player and the other side of a contest land on the same tier, Critical Success or past it (matchedCritFeat in src/engine/feats.ts, from every contest the player is in). Reward: tierUnlocks {roll 'contest', up 1}, so every roll the holder makes as one side of a contest (RollRequest.contest) can reach Critical Success+ by the skill-roll extra band (D-211). Name and reward level I are placeholders. |
| D-293 | Rarer perk milestones past 140 | 1 | Parked | Ali (14/11/2026, leftover scan CS11 b): the milestone and All-step series are open past 140 and 150 in the code (a milestone every 20, an All step every 50, nothing written past them yet), and "we will reduce the frequency of perks past 140, but we will see how to handle it later". **Suggested method.** Change PERK\_MILESTONE\_EVERY into a spacing that widens past 140 (for example 20 to 140, then 30, then 40), one function in src/ruleset/perks.ts, when the first perks past 140 are written. |
| D-294 | Back from the break: a fight won although your morale broke | 1, 2 | Built | Ali (14/11/2026, Barbarian novel study, https://claude.ai/artifact/97hDbTps7FetGoiEuvdoA1, round 9, BA3b): the rewards of the single row "and having a chain after all", with the note that "these achievement are extraordinary, the novel didn't get the chance to experience them multiple times, and nor do I think it will happen in the game, it is to give the truly crazy something to chase." The mind's twin of the survival roll row (D-279); in the novel surviving the countdown is the largest ordeal reward. Tally `won_broken` at the fight's end (fightEnded): every foe is down, fled or yielded, your morale broke during the fight, you are still up and no foe took your yield. Broken stops attacks, so it takes allies, talk, or foes who flee. Chain AC199 to AC201: Back from the Break 1 (level II), Broken, Not Beaten 5 (III), Nothing Left to Fear 25 (IV); each level +5 max morale (D-278) and one Nerve step. The fight still counts as lost for the Loser scars, and the break scars (First Crack and on) stay as they are. Names, levels and amounts are placeholders. |
| D-295 | Temptation refused | 1, 2 | Parked | Ali (14/11/2026, round 9, BA13b "a": park it with its own number and this method). A reward for refusing a strong offer. In the novel it is the largest on the list (refusing a Floor Lord's offer pays twice what killing one does), it happens once, and it is hidden. The flaw to avoid: if refusing reliably pays, it stops being a sacrifice and players learn to always say no. **Suggested method.** (1) The offer is real: accepting gives something strong and lasting, with a price (a rare piece may be a plain best choice). (2) The refusal's reward is hidden (D-283) and of a different kind from the offer (max morale, standing with the offerer's enemies, a personality step), so neither answer is simply better. (3) Hand-written, one row per offer, tied to a unique NPC; never a count. (4) Accepting and refusing both move personality. Waits on offers worth refusing; goal-driven unique NPCs are the natural source. |
| D-296 | Achievement chains that fork | 2 | Parked | Ali (14/11/2026, achievement trim TB1 "a, forks are a parked idea"): a chain that splits at a set row into paths of which you earn only one, like the capstone alternates. Branches that count a slice of the trunk's act (TB1 a) are the agreed kind; forks wait. |
| D-297 | Achievement trim, round 3 | 1, 2 | Built | Ali (14/11/2026, trim page TM4, TM7, TM8, TM25 to TM28). Total defeats 10/50/100/250: Carver of Fifty Graves (AC202) at 50, Thousand-Slayer (AC9) retired; the roll bonus goes by chain position, +1 to +4, and the reward levels I to IV. Aimed hits 10/50/100. Aimed hits by size 1/10/50 with a new first row (AC203); each by-size row takes CONFIG.AIM_EASE_PCT (10 %) off the aim penalty for its size, carried up (10/20/30 %). Clean defenses 1/10/100/500 with a new first row (AC204). Rule: the first of an action is an achievement (TM8, TM27; where it applies is asked as TM29). You down a foe and An ally's battle cry rallies you moved from the fears table (FR6, FR9 retired) to the composure table (CP7, CP8, flat 10 points, ComposureDef.points), so they count toward the Calmed achievements. First-row names are placeholders. |
| D-298 | Achievement branches and the chain page | 2 | Built | Ali (14/11/2026, trim TB1 to TB6). A branch is a chain that counts one slice of its trunk's act (TB1 a); its lowest row names the trunk's lowest row in `branchOf` (or a head with no trunk, ACH_HEADS: healed and calmed by source). A branch carries rewards up only along itself, never inherits the trunk's, and never switches the trunk off (TB2); a branch row is earned as soon as its own count is met (TB3). Nine joins (TB6): total defeats (by body type, by race), past Critical Success (per group), aimed hits (by size), people spotted (per sense), outmatched (per function), quests (per giver), spar wins (cruel spars), classes at max (tier peaks, tiers maxed), healed and calmed (sources). The Achievements page (TB4) lists one line per chain in tag sections: the trunk's current name with ↻N rows earned, its place ("II of IV"), the next row's count (D-284) and when; its side panel unfolds the earlier levels and one line per branch kind with its own place and next count. A by-kind chain with no trunk gets a line per kind. Scars use the same shape. Code: src/ruleset/achChains.ts. The library viewer is to match (asked of its thread). |
| D-299 | Strain on auras | 1 | Parked | Ali (SS9 in the Stamina perks page https://claude.ai/artifact/LEqvuBEYASDsG5gKGU72dR, "c for now. we will deep dive aura later"). Auras (Dread Presence and the other engagement auras) stay free; perk moves keep paying their Action times row's effort tier (Battle Cry All-out). Options for the deep dive: (a) every perk move gets an Action times row with an effort tier (All-out from a 100 perk up) and an aura pays an effort tier (placeholder Hard, 2) for each fight turn it acts on someone; (b) as a, but auras pay Moderate (1); (c) auras stay free, only perk moves pay. Scaling: a fixed cost on a growing line matters less with Stamina but never reaches zero (a Battle Cry is 4% of the line at Stamina 50, 1.6% at 200). |
| D-300 | Short Sleeper's race | 1 | Parked | Ali (SS13 c, Stamina perks page). Short Sleeper (PK141, banked in src/library/perks.ts, not built): sleep clears sleep debt ×1.33 (sleepClear, SHORT\_SLEEPER\_CLEAR, placeholder), from the real DEC2 short-sleep gene (about 6 hours a night against 8). Waits for the lore thread to pick the race born with it. Options: (a) innate for elves; (b) innate for humans; (c) another race the lore picks. |
| D-301 | Stamina perks | 1 | Built | Ali's SS1-SS14 answers (Stamina perks page). Catnap (Stamina 20): a rest also clears sleep debt as CATNAP\_SLEEP\_HOURS (1) hour of sleep would there, by the share of the hour (restRecover in src/engine/fatigue.ts, both rest paths). Iron Lungs (40): +5 Stamina, +1 Stamina (group), +10 Endurance. Forced March (60) was retired with the travel paces (SR5, 14/11/2026; PK134 not reused): the Stamina 60 slot is empty. Deep Lungs (80): line ×DEEP\_LUNGS\_LINE (1.5). Sleepless (100): hours awake add sleep debt ×SLEEPLESS\_NEED (0.2); spilled strain fills the bar as usual and every strain point still counts toward Stamina's marks. Bottomless (120): every strain cost ×BOTTOMLESS\_STRAIN (0.5). Second Breath (Stamina's All-50 part, completes Hard to Kill): once per engagement, when a cost would empty the line it refills to SECOND\_BREATH\_PCT (50%) and nothing spills that time. Quirks Heavy Sleeper (a noise must be HEAVY\_SLEEPER\_STEPS louder than WAKE\_NOISE) and Insomniac (sleepClear ×INSOMNIAC\_CLEAR, 0.75), both tagged "sleep". Sleep perks set PerkDef.sleepNeed or sleepClear, multiplying together in full (SS14, RN5 c). NPC templates may name quirkTags: each spawned NPC rolls one quirk per tag, seeded by its ID (SS12). Overdrive (140, SS15 c): while the short-term line is more than half full, every roll adds OVERDRIVE\_SHARE (0.1) of Stamina through the augment equation (× Stamina's tier) and costs OVERDRIVE\_STRAIN (1) strain more, with worn armour and Bottomless (roll() in src/ruleset/index.ts). It replaced No Rest Needed, which conflicted with Sleepless (SS7). Scaling: all multipliers, so they hold at 50/75/100/150/200; Catnap clears a share of max, which grows with the bar. |

Scaling: none; it is about story structure, the same at any power. |
| D-320 | Doable chores as NPC jobs | 2 | Parked | Ali (BF61 a in the playtest bug fixes, 14/11/2026): jobs NPCs offer are limited to goals the game can check (defeat, deliver, reach, find: QuestGoal kind); everything else is parked as this feature. The feature: a chore is a long action (Action times, busy) at a piece of furniture (zone.furniture), with the tools it needs placed by the game, so an NPC can offer a chore the player can actually do and the code can see it done. Options when it comes back: (a) a new goal kind "work": the Weaver names the furniture and the chore, the game places the tools beside it, and the job is done when the player finishes that long action there (one fixed duration per chore, no roll); (b) a, plus a roll on the chore's skill when it finishes (a tool-use or labour skill): the outcome tier sets the pay and the NPC's stance step, and a failure leaves it half-done to try again; (c) chores as downtime only: an offered chore runs as a time skip between scenarios (like resting) that pays coin and fatigue, with no furniture or tools. Needs before building: which skill a chore rolls (b), the chore table (name, furniture, tools, duration, pay), and how the Weaver may offer one (a catalog row, as for items). |

| D-326 | Strain rebalance | 1 | Built | Ali (14/11/2026, "Strain from action costs" page SR1-SR6, after "strain was barely felt"). The math: costs match real gas-out times (the line empties after 1.1 / 2.2 / 3.3 / 5.6 min of non-stop fighting at Stamina 0 / 50 / 100 / 200), but the line gave no penalty, fights end in a few turns, breath refilled it in 1-2 min and only spill reached the long term, so strain never showed. Answers: SR1 d no penalty from the line; SR2 b defence stays Light; SR3 a costs kept; SR4 a STRAIN\_RESIDUE 10% of every strain point also goes to the long term; SR5 travel paces removed (one TRAVEL\_SPEED\_SHARE, dark speed kept as TRAVEL\_DARK\_SPEED\_SHARE), travel adds TRAVEL\_STRAIN\_PER\_HOUR 1 point an hour to the long term (no armour multiplier), Forced March (SS3, PK134) retired; SR6 a breath kept. Scaling: residue and travel are points, so a bigger bar feels them less, smoothly, with no cap (at 200, 6 h of travel adds 2.4%). |

## Bug log

The play page's Bugs page (Ali, 11/11/2026): Report bug files the player's note as code player_report with the story, turn and last six lines, then sends every new report and open bug to Claude at once (BG-27): a comment sent to Claude that wakes the build thread, which triages each bug to the thread that owns it. The list shows new reports (marked New), newest first, then open bugs, newest number first; Send open bugs to Claude sends them again.

Every bug found or reported, in any thread or in play, gets a fixed number (BG-n). Numbers come from one counter, `bugs_meta/counter` in the play page's db (read the counter, write next + 1 pinned to the version read), so a number is never shared or reused. The game never takes one (BF45, Ali, 14/11/2026): it files each report unnumbered, and Claude numbers it at triage. Each real bug gets one number, and repeats of the same bug share it (the bug row lists the reports it came from in `reports`); each report row then gets status numbered and `as` (its BG-n), or not\_a\_bug. A thread that finds a bug takes the next number itself. Always mention a bug by its BG-n.

The game files a report at `bugs/new-<time>` (status new) for every bug code it logs: any `invalid_` code, and view\_leak, invalid\_target, unlisted\_reveal, planner\_late, agent\_fallback, call\_failed, refused, invalid\_json, weaver\_off\_catalog, weaver\_fallback, weaver\_timeout, routine\_fallback, routine\_skip, reward\_repaired, quest\_goal\_gap and turn\_check (src/save/bugs.ts BUG\_CODES). Plain game outcomes, such as a blocked move or a refused sale, are not bugs. A build thread that reviews a filed bug adds it to this table, sets its db status (open, fixed or wont\_fix), and names the PR that fixes it by its number ("PR #n", written once the PR is open; a test fails on "this PR").

| BG | Bug | Found | Status | Fixed by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| BG-1 | Fight checks that go one way (health fears, crippled-part fear, crowded shots) skipped the player, because the player holds no stances or beliefs; spars didn't count as engaged | Thread | Fixed | PR #361 |
| BG-2 | A sparring blow struck from hiding raised the alarm (SP11) | Thread | Fixed | PR #363 |
| BG-3 | "I await the outcome" after a yield got a clarify ("Nothing is pending"): the Parser had no way to let a moment pass | Play | Fixed | PR #375 |
| BG-4 | "I yield" asked a hostile who never fought the player (waiting in another room), and the sheet's "Deciding" list revealed him; he also counts in the fight log | Play | Fixed | PR #387 (YD1–YD3, YD6, YD7) |
| BG-5 | The player's fight never ended after they left it: the slimes stayed hostile and aware while fighting the guard, so fightingWith kept the engagement open and the end-of-engagement skill-up rolls never came (playtest run 31) | Playtest | Fixed | PR #387 (YD6) |
| BG-6 | Zone placement read "down the street" and "up the road" as stairs, so level street zones were stacked on top of each other | Thread | Fixed | PR #395 |
| BG-7 | The player can't walk out of the tavern front door: zones of different places sit about 36 m apart with no doorway, so the walk heads for a crossing point in the gap, a partial leg leaves the player outside the room, and the next turn resets them to its centre (playtests runs 34, 35) | Playtest | Fixed | PR #408: a zone touches the zone its exit leads from on any free side before it slides out; linked zones that still don't touch are walked edge to edge (the gap is not walked) |
| BG-8 | Scenario planning failed: the Weaver named a tavern place and used its layout's zone keys (tavern.common) without naming the layout, so no zones were built and every link, slot and trigger pointed nowhere (play) | Play | Fixed | PR #412 |
| BG-9 | The Weaver's repair of BG-8 failed the same way, so the scenario fell back to a placeholder area (play) | Play | Fixed | PR #412 |
| BG-10 | BG-8 again on a new story (play) | Play | Fixed | PR #412 |
| BG-11 | BG-9 again on a new story (play) | Play | Fixed | PR #412 |
| BG-12 | The player can't move to a spot inside their zone ("walk to the back of the cellar"): moves only name a zone, so the Parser returned nothing and the turn passed as holding still, five times (playtest run 36). Fixed: a move can name a spot in its zone (move.spot: near someone or a way out, away from one, or a compass side) | Playtest | Fixed | PR #415 |
| BG-13 | The Parser aimed attacks at a slime its view listed as Dead, so the player swung at a corpse while a live slime kept attacking (playtest run 36). Fixed: legality stops an attack on someone the view lists as Dead with "That one is already dead. Who do you mean?", and the Parser is told not to aim at the dead | Playtest | Fixed | PR #415 |
| BG-14 | A level tunnel was laid on top of the cellar on the same floor, because the layout read the tunnel's back exit ("back up into the cellar") as a climb while heights put both at −3 m; their doorway became the cellar's middle, so a slime from the tunnel stood 0 m from the player (playtest run 36). Fixed: once heights are settled, they alone decide whether an exit climbs | Playtest | Fixed | PR #415 |
| BG-15 | "Go up the cellar steps and out the front door into the street" stopped behind the bar: moves could only name a zone next to the player, so the Parser named the first step (playtest run 37). Fixed: a move can name any zone the player knows, or a way out of one it has seen (PlayerView `known`), and the walk goes through the zones between | Playtest | Fixed | PR #418 |
| BG-16 | A move-only turn ("walk behind the bar") was narrated as "You hold your ground and wait": the turn's unused main action made a hold event after the move (playtest run 37). Fixed: no hold after a move unless words go with it | Playtest | Fixed | PR #418 |
| BG-17 | A guard's shout at the gate, four rooms away, reached the player in the cellar word for word: any sound heard, even partly, carried its words (playtest run 36). Fixed by Ali's decision: words need the sound heard clearly | Playtest | Fixed | PR #418 |
| BG-18 | With two slimes both an arm's length away, "attack the nearest slime" and then "the slime on the left" / "on the right" each got the clarify "Which slime do you mean?", two turns running, while both slimes kept attacking (playtest run 38). The Parser has no way to break a tie or use left/right. Fixed: everyone in view has a `side` (ahead, left, right, behind) by the way the player last walked (Actor.facing, engine/position.ts sideFrom); the Parser picks one of equally fitting foes and never asks | Playtest | Fixed | PR #422 |
| BG-19 | "Walk to the back of the cellar" still gave move.spot {awayFrom: the player's own zone} after PR #415 (playtest run 38, as in run 37). Fixed: legality reads awayFrom the zone itself as away from the way the player came in (cameFrom[0]), and the Parser is told so | Playtest | Fixed | PR #422 |
| BG-20 | "Walk to the cellar steps" from behind the bar went down into the cellar: a move to a way out was parsed as a move through it (playtest run 38). Fixed: going to a way out is a spot near it in the zone it leaves from; going through it is a move to the zone beyond | Playtest | Fixed | PR #422 |
| BG-21 | A doorway right where the walker stood (a cellar laid under its counter with no heights) was crossed in 0 m with no move event and no trail entry, so "go back" had nothing to read (found testing BG-19). Fixed: a crossing counts as a move however short | Test | Fixed | PR #422 |
| BG-22 | The narration view's move events said only where each leg ended: "open the tavern door and step inside" (8.4 m along the lane, then into the common room) was told as stepping out into the lane and coming back in, and cellar to kitchen as leaving the common room (playtest run 39). Fixed: a leg that leaves a zone carries `from`, a leg inside a zone is `within` it and names the zone it is `heading` for; Narrator rule 17 reads them | Playtest | Fixed | PR #425 |
| BG-23 | The barkeep, 0.7 m from the player, broke and fled through the kitchen door; perception took the move only where it ended (no sight into the kitchen), so the player only heard something, she dropped from lastSeen, and "cut down the barkeep" next turn asked who that was (Ali's game, turn 6). Fixed: a leg out of a zone is also seen where it set off (`fromPos`), and a clear sight of it remembers the zone she went into | Ali | Fixed | PR #434 |
| BG-24 | "Wrench my sword out and strike" drew the arming sword already in the player's hand, and the draw was logged as invalid gear (Ali's game, turn 12). Fixed: drawing what is already in hand is nothing to do, not a mistake | Ali | Fixed | PR #444 |
| BG-25 | The player swung three times at a woman lying Incapacitated at 0.7 m; every blow was wasted in silence (attacks on anyone down were refused), and the Narrator made up a blade stuck in the floorboards and shaking hands (Ali's game, turns 11 to 14). Fixed: blows on someone down land with no defense, only the dead can't be struck, and a wasted action says why (Narrator rule 18) | Ali | Fixed | PR #444 |
| BG-26 | The autopilot agent kept stopping to check how badly it was hurt ("let me check how bad I'm hurt") with /stats instead of acting (Ali's report, the agent's save, turn 15). | BG-27 | Pressing Report bug only saved the report; the open bugs reached Claude only through a separate "Send open bugs to Claude" button, though Ali had asked that reporting sends them. Fixed: lastSeen keeps everyone seen or heard at the last zone known (Journal lastKnown), and the Parser never asks who someone in it is | Ali | Fixed | PR #453 |

| BG-31 | "Walk to Tavern Lane, the street door" came as a move to the lane with a spot near the common room; the lane was out of view, so the spot couldn't be checked and the player was asked "Where in common room do you want to go?" twice (playtest run 40). | BG-38 | Playtest run 40: Tam the thief hid and could never be found on purpose: a search rolled nothing against anyone hiding, Look around never found a stealthy actor, and an NPC's search rolled nothing either. Handing over needs both grip reaches (straight over a counter); a pick-up needs reach of where the item lies (ItemInstance.at: where it was dropped, where a thrown one landed; one placed with no point lies at its zone's centre). Out of reach, the move slot closes in first (NEEDS in engine/merge.ts is the one approach table: voice, grip, weapon). The equip slot is 'grip:<part>' (was 'hand:'). Siblings with the same gap: a break-free try, a way found shut or opened, a player crossing a frontier or jumping, a dropped walk and a doorway underfoot. Run 98c t19: "walk over to Wren and grab his wrist" after he left was all deferred, and nothing said he was gone. | Using an item one does not carry comes to nothing, with reason no_item; the NPC prompts say a shut container is opened first and use_item names an item carried. notThere also counts someone named in this turn's deferred words. | Fixed in PR #625 |
| BG-172 | Run 98c t28: "I open the side gate and check behind it": a 0.3 m step up to the gate took the move slot, so opening it had none left and came to nothing while the search went on. | A step up to a way (ENTER_M at most) is part of working it: the op keeps the move slot. A longer walk still spends it (BG-86). | Fixed in PR #625 |
| BG-173 | Run 79d t33: Ness was told to open her shut chest first, but open was missing from the NPC action list (the schema and the action list written into the NPC prompts), so she sent use_item with nothing again and again. | open is an NPC action (NPC_ACTIONS), and the prompts build their action list from NPC_ACTIONS, so they never drift from what the schema takes. | Fixed in PR #628 |
| BG-174 | Run 97e t35-38: "walk east with Wen" in Mill Lane, whose east end has no way on: once at the east side, each such move took no step and the turn was a silent wait, five tries running. | A move that takes no step, nothing stopping it, gives the Narrator stayedPut (where the player already stands); Narrator rule 28 says so and names the ways out. | Fixed in PR #628 |
| BG-175 | Run 79e t33-34: Ness's shut chest showed in her view as `container: {}`, and her own wasted use_item and give (no_item) reached her with no reason, so she tried the same again. | A shut container says `shut` in every view; a mind's own wasted event carries its reason in its next view; NPC prompts say to act on that reason. | Fixed in PR #631 |
| BG-176 | Runs 81c t34-40 and 97c: the purse read "coin ×16" in /inv and the gear line the agents read, so a player kept trying to pay four silver. | Every item count reads through countWords, which says coin in coin words (1 silver and 6 copper); also the inventory tooltip. | Fixed in PR #631 |
| BG-177 | Run 97f t31-41: Wen, held in a real fight, said "Enough, I yield" with yields set, but an NPC's yields only left a spar (invalid_spar_yield); the fight went on and Bram killed him. Sibling: grabbing someone one already holds was wasted as already_held and told as someone else's grip. | An NPC's yield in a real fight surrenders, as a surrender from fear does (loseNerve, one function); in a spar it leaves the spar; the NPC prompt offers it. The wasted reason is already_holding. | Fixed in PR #633 |
| BG-178 | Run 97f t44-47: the player searched Wen's body and found a key and coin, left them, searched again and was told nothing, while Bram's search found both; the first find was told as the key in the player's palm. | What a searcher found before and is still there is found again (searchPerson); a find reads as left there for now, never taken. | Fixed in PR #633 |
| BG-179 | Run 79f t35: Brenn sat drinking near people his band is hostile to, in another building; nobody struck anyone, yet the game went into fight turns, which dropped the player's words said beside an action to Ness. | BF14 a: the mode is Tactical only while a fight concerns the player (fightTurns: someone fighting them knows they are there, or they can perceive blows or a spar). Engaged actors elsewhere still get Planner calls. | Fixed in PR #635 |
| BG-180 | Run 79g t44-47: Ness said she was off to fetch rags from her chest, walked there, then stood silent two turns: Planner calls went only to engaged actors and deal parties, so a code-run NPC acted again only when spoken to. | An answer with a deed under way (a move, an open, a lead: anything but words or a hold) gives that NPC Planner calls for ERRAND_TURNS (3) turns, code-run or not; its own hold ends it. Planner rule 6: a promise in its said lines is carried out. | Fixed in PR #638 |
| BG-181 | Run 79g t38-42: Ness bound the player's leg, but nothing recorded it; the story called them unbandaged and bleeding, Ness tried to bind it again with empty hands (no_item twice), her no_item reason left her view a turn later, and "I hand Ness the linen" with none held became action other. | A healing item used on someone marks them tended (condition.tended, shown by item name in every view) until their next wound; Narrator rule 29 (bound only when tended; no bleeding danger). An NPC keeps its last wasted try (self.lastWasted) until it next does that kind of thing. Parser: giving or using what the player does not carry is give or use_item with item null. | Fixed in PR #638 |
| BG-182 | Who's here on Play showed no names, only status words: since PR #514 each chip's name sat inside a code comment. | The name is back as each chip's first word; a smoke test checks a seen person's name shows. | Fixed in PR #662 |

Close-in keeps a move behind the counter and walks to them, and from the near side walks right up to the counter. personalityFrom takes types and values by id or name. The agent's own repeat asks: PR #672. | Fixed in PR #673 |

| BG-187 | Story mwf2lt7l t3 (quest_goal_gap): Corwin's job "find my mother's tin charm and bring it back" became goal other: the charm was real (in Wren's sack) but nothing in Corwin's view showed it, so he could not name it in a deliver goal. | Ali BF18 a: a Weaver reward may name its owner (rewards[].owner); away from them the item is on the owner's self.missing (id and name, never where), and a job's deliver goal may name that exact item (JOB_RULE). views/index.ts missingOf, scenario/apply.ts. | Fixed in PR #684 |
| BG-188 | Ali's report, story mwf2lt7l t11-17: Osric told the man in the patched coat to turn out his pockets and boots; no search happened (Osric only talked), a willing suspect could not be searched hands-on, and turning out one's own pockets had no action. BF17 b: the guard template's Eyesight floor (a search's skill) is 40. | Fixed in PR #684 |
| BG-189 | Action-time playtest run-100-at-rester, turns 9-16: after the fight the player bandaged and rested in 6 s fight turns for about ten turns, so a 40 s bandage looked like it never finished. Wick, who had punched the player and gone out to the lane, stood there silent, out of sight and earshot, but was still engaged with the player; fightInSenses took the player's own engaged pair as "a fight they can hear" by probing it at a loud noise. | The fight-in-senses clause counts only fights between others (engine/mode.ts fightInSenses); the player's own fight counts through the first clause, a foe after them they can see or hear. | Fixed in PR #681 |
| BG-190 | Ali's report, story mwf2lt7l t22: after "I sneak while following the lean man" he had to say it again every turn, "continue" got "Continue with what?", and nothing said whether he still had the lean man or how he lost him.
